| [[_document_ - IDO01, NAD+, KP Pathway, grok\|indoleamine 2,3-dioxygenase]] | Indoleamine 2,3-dioxygenase 1 (IDO1) is a heme-containing enzyme that catalyzes the first and rate-limiting step in the kynurenine pathway (KP) of tryptophan (Trp) catabolism. It oxidizes L-tryptophan (and some other indoleamine substrates) to N-formylkynurenine in an O₂-dependent reaction. N-formylkynurenine is then rapidly converted to kynurenine (Kyn). |
| [[_document_ - Acid_ceramidase_modulates_the_lipid_profile_and_ex\|Acid ceramidase modulates the lipid profile and exacerbates sensitivity to ferroptosis in WI-38 replicative senescent cells]] | Peer-reviewed study (Cell Death and Disease, 2026) demonstrating that acid ceramidase (ASAH1) upregulation in replicatively senescent WI-38 fibroblasts drives a pro-ferroptotic lipid profile and increased ferroptosis sensitivity, independent of GPX4/GSH and iron, and is transmitted to neighboring cells via IL-6/IL-8 SASP factors. |

| [[_document_ - creatine anti-cancer trace\|Creatine anti-cancer trace]] | Synthesized trace of how creatine connects to anti-cancer properties, drawn from the vault wiki and the Creatine in Health and Disease review. |
| [[_document_ - Creatine in Health and Disease\|Creatine in Health and Disease]] | Although creatine has been mostly studied as an ergogenic aid for exercise and sport, several health and therapeutic benefits have been reported. Creatine plays a critical role in cellular metabolism, particularly during metabolically stressed states, and limitations in creatine transport or storage can impair metabolism. This systematic review assesses the evidence for creatine supplementation in promoting general health, managing chronic disease, and aiding recovery from injury. |
| [[_document_ - Fisetin—In Search of Better Bioavailability—From Macro to Nano Modifications A Review\|Fisetin—In Search of Better Bioavailability—From Macro to Nano Modifications: A Review]] | Review of the low bioavailability of the flavonol fisetin and the nanodelivery systems (polymeric nanoparticles, liposomes, nanocrystals, cyclodextrins, probiotics, glycosylation) that improve its solubility, absorption, and therapeutic efficacy. |
| [[_document_ - How Cysteine and Selenocysteine Guard Cells Against Aging and Oxidative Damage\|How Cysteine and Selenocysteine Guard Cells Against Aging and Oxidative Damage]] | Pace et al. (Biomolecules, 2025) narrative review synthesizing how cysteine (glutathione precursor, redox switch) and selenocysteine (catalytic residue of GPx/TrxR) defend cellular redox balance across the lifespan, and how their decline drives aging and neurodegeneration; covers GlyNAC and selenocysteine supplementation evidence. |
| [[_document_ - Humanin a harbinger of mitochondrial-derived peptides?\|Humanin: a harbinger of mitochondrial-derived peptides?]] | Mitochondria have been largely considered as 'end-function' organelles, servicing the cell by producing energy and regulating cell death in response to complex signals. Being cellular entities with vital roles, mitochondria communicate back to the cell and actively engage in determining major cellular policies. These signals, collectively referred to as retrograde signals, are encoded in the nuclear genome, or are secondary products of mitochondrial metabolism. Here, we discuss humanin, the first small peptide of a putative set of mitochondrial-derived peptides, which exhibits strong cytoprotective actions against various stress and disease models. The study of humanin and other mitochondrial-derived retrograde signal peptides will aid in the identification of genes and peptides with therapeutic and diagnostic potential in treating human diseases. |
| [[_document_ - Methylene Blue - StatPearls\|Methylene Blue - StatPearls]] | Methylene blue is a multifaceted therapeutic agent used across a wide range of clinical settings, with its well-established role being the Food and Drug Administration–approved treatment for acquired methemoglobinemia. The unique redox properties of methylene blue enable the restoration of functional hemoglobin and enhanced oxygen delivery, making it a critical intervention in toxicologic emergencies. Beyond this indication, methylene blue has demonstrated clinical utility in perioperative care, critical illness, and select neurologic and oncologic procedures, underscoring the importance of a thorough understanding of its mechanisms, benefits, and potential risks for clinicians across specialties. |

| [[_document_ - Mitochondrial Drivers Stem Cell Aging Inflammaging Bautista 2026\|Mitochondrial drivers of stem cell aging and inflammaging]] | Comprehensive review synthesizing evidence that mitochondrial dysfunction is an upstream driver of stem cell exhaustion and inflammaging, covering mtDNA mutations, quality control failure, NAD+ depletion, and mtDAMP-driven innate immune signaling. |
| [[_document_ - Mitochondrial Fusion and Fission The fine-tune balance for cellular homeostasis\|Mitochondrial Fusion and Fission: The fine-tune balance for cellular homeostasis]] | Mitochondria are highly dynamic, maternally inherited cytoplasmic organelles, which fulfill cellular energy demand through the oxidative phosphorylation system. Besides, they play an active role in calcium and damage-associated molecular patterns ... |
| [[_document_ - Mitochondrial metabolism and epigenetic crosstalk drive SASP\|Mitochondrial metabolism and epigenetic crosstalk drive SASP]] | Research article by Martini, Passos and colleagues (2026) showing that senescent cells upregulate a mitochondrial pyruvate-citrate-acetyl-CoA axis that supplies acetyl-CoA for histone acetylation at SASP loci. Mitochondrial metabolism and mtDNA-cGAS-STING signaling act as independent but complementary inputs that converge to enable SASP gene expression; pharmacological inhibition of SLC25A1 (CTPI2) suppresses SASP-driven inflammation and improves healthspan in aged mice. |

In some cases, these responses persist even after the stress is relieved, leaving ... |
| [[_document_ - mitohormesis, heart rate variability\|Mitohormesis, Heart Rate Variability, and Recovery Tracking]] | A practical framework for visualizing and optimizing mitohormesis and heart rate variability (HRV) using wearables, plus a nutrition and supplementation protocol to support mitochondrial adaptation. |
| [[_document_ - Neuro-Modulating Effects of Honokiol A Review\|Neuro-Modulating Effects of Honokiol: A Review]] | Honokiol is a poly-phenolic compound that exerts neuroprotective properties through GABA-A receptor modulation, preservation of mitochondrial function, and inhibition of NF-κB-mediated inflammation. It has therapeutic potential in anxiety, pain, cerebrovascular injury, epilepsy, and cognitive disorders including Alzheimer's disease. This review summarizes the neuroprotective and anesthetic mechanisms of this Magnolia-derived lignan. |
| [[_document_ - Neuroprotective Actions of Methylene Blue and Its Derivatives\|Neuroprotective Actions of Methylene Blue and Its Derivatives]] | Structure-activity study of methylene blue (MB) and six phenothiazine-related derivatives in HT-22 hippocampal neurons. MB acts as an alternative mitochondrial electron-transfer carrier that bypasses Complexes I-III, reduces mitochondrial superoxide, and is a regenerable mitochondrial antioxidant; side-chain substitution at the 10-nitrogen destroys this activity. |
| [[_document_ - speakman2003_Resting Metabolic Rate\|Physical activity and resting metabolic rate]] | Speakman & Selman (2003) review examining the relationship between physical activity, exercise training, and resting metabolic rate (RMR), integrating animal and human studies on EPOC, NEAT, energy expenditure components, and the obesity epidemic. |
| [[_document_ - Plant flavone apigenin An emerging anticancer agent\|Plant flavone apigenin: An emerging anticancer agent]] | Review of apigenin's chemopreventive and therapeutic anticancer properties across human malignancies, its molecular targets, epidemiological evidence, combination strategies, and clinical/pharmacokinetic limitations. |
| [[_document_ - Rapamycin for longevity opinion article\|Rapamycin for longevity: opinion article]] | Opinion piece arguing that mTOR inhibitors (rapamycin, everolimus) are proven anti-aging drugs whose real side effects are reversible and rare, and that not using them is more dangerous than using them. Advocates intermittent, personalized low-dose scheduling. |
| [[_document_ - Rapamycin for longevity the pros, the cons, and future perspectives\|Rapamycin for longevity: the pros, the cons, and future perspectives]] | Review summarizing the mTOR pathway, rapamycin's effects on aging and epilepsy, and the translational gaps, pitfalls, and ethical concerns of off-label rapamycin use for longevity. Contrasts robust efficacy in monogenic mTORopathies with inconsistent aging data. |
| [[_document_ - repurposing_apigen_senomorphic.09.09.611999v1.full\|Repurposing the plant-derived compound apigenin for senomorphic effect in antiaging pipelines]] | 2024 bioRxiv preprint that screens a natural-product library and identifies apigenin as a senomorphic agent. Demonstrates geroprotection and chemosensitization in vivo. |

| [[_document_ - The Mechanistic Target of Rapamycin (mTOR) Pathway as a Target of Anti-aging Therapies The Role of Rapamycin and Its Analogs in the Regulation of Cellular Processes and Their Impact on Longevity\|The Mechanistic Target of Rapamycin (mTOR) Pathway as a Target of Anti-aging Therapies: The Role of Rapamycin and Its Analogs in the Regulation of Cellular Processes and Their Impact on Longevity]] | Narrative review (Cureus, 2025) of the mTOR pathway as an anti-aging target. Covers rapamycin's mechanisms—autophagy, proteostasis, mitochondrial and immune modulation, stem-cell preservation—preclinical longevity, early human trials (PEARL, topical skin, vaccine response), safety, and ongoing Alzheimer's/cardiac/sarcopenia trials. |

| [[_document_ - Thymic Rejuvenation and Aging\|Thymic Rejuvenation and Aging]] | The thymus is a vital organ for homeostatic maintenance of the peripheral immune system, where T cells develop and are educated before export to the periphery. Thymic involution with age reduces immune efficacy and increases susceptibility to infection, autoimmunity, and cancer. This review discusses thymic rejuvenation strategies including sex steroid ablation, cytokine therapy (IL-7, KGF, IL-22), and hormone therapy (growth hormone, ghrelin). |

| [[AMPK-PGC-1α Pathway\|AMPK-PGC-1α Pathway]] | A master metabolic regulatory axis in which AMPK activation drives PGC-1α to promote mitochondrial oxidative phosphorylation and biogenesis. |

| [[Amyloid Precursor Protein\|Amyloid Precursor Protein]] | A type I transmembrane protein whose proteolytic cleavage produces amyloid-beta peptides implicated in Alzheimer's Disease |
| [[Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis\|Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis]] | Amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS) is a progressive neurodegenerative disease that affects nerve cells in the brain and the spinal cord. Oxidative Stress is heavily implicated, particularly throug... |
| [[Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis (ALS)\|Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis (ALS)]] | A fatal neurodegenerative disease characterized by progressive loss of upper and lower motor neurons, leading to muscle atrophy, paralysis, and respiratory failure. |
| [[Anakinra\|Anakinra]] | Anakinra is a recombinant interleukin-1 receptor antagonist (IL-1Ra) used to treat a range of autoinflammatory and autoimmune conditions by blocking IL-1 signaling. |
| [[Anaplerosis\|Anaplerosis]] | Anaplerosis is the replenishment of TCA cycle intermediates that are withdrawn for biosynthetic purposes; glutamine anaplerosis is the dominant route in proliferating cancer cells. |
| [[Androgen Receptor\|Androgen Receptor]] | A nuclear receptor activated by androgens, central to prostate development and a key driver and therapeutic target in prostate cancer. |
| [[Anemia\|Anemia]] | A condition of reduced red blood cell mass or hemoglobin, causing impaired oxygen delivery; a mild, reversible cytopenia associated with chronic mTOR inhibition. |
| [[Aneuploidy\|Aneuploidy]] | The presence of an abnormal number of chromosomes in a cell. |
| [[Angiogenesis\|Angiogenesis]] | Angiogenesis is the formation of new blood vessels from pre-existing vessels. It is a critical process in Cancer, Wound Healing, and Ischemia-reperfusion Injury adaptation. |
| [[Angiostatin\|Angiostatin]] | Endogenous anti-angiogenic protein fragment generated from plasminogen by MMPs (especially MMP-12), inhibiting endothelial cell proliferation and tumour angiogenesis. |
| [[Angiotensin II Type 1 Receptor\|Angiotensin II Type 1 Receptor]] | The angiotensin II type 1 receptor (AT1R, gene AGTR1) is the principal G-protein-coupled receptor mediating the pressor, pro-inflammatory, and pro-fibrotic actions of angiotensin II, and the target of the angiotensin receptor blocker drug class. |
| [[Anion Exchanger 1\|Anion Exchanger 1]] | AE1/Band 3 (SLC4A1), the most abundant erythrocyte membrane protein: anion exchanger for CO2/HCO3- transport, membrane-cytoskeleton anchor preserving the biconcave shape, senescence neo-antigen for immune clearance, and calpain/caspase-3 substrate during eryptosis. |
| [[Annexin A1\|Annexin A1]] | Anti-inflammatory phospholipid-binding protein (ANXA1) whose tripeptide mimetic Ac2-26 transcriptionally upregulates SIRT3, reducing oxidative damage and promoting mitochondrial biogenesis and mitophagy. |
| [[Antagonistic Pleiotropy\|Antagonistic Pleiotropy]] | An evolutionary concept wherein a trait beneficial to early-life fitness becomes detrimental later in life, proposed as the reason RCM evolved as a short-term survival response that drives aging. |
| [[Antiphospholipid Syndrome\|Antiphospholipid Syndrome]] | Autoimmune thrombophilia defined by antiphospholipid antibodies plus thrombosis or pregnancy morbidity; APS autoantibodies induce eryptosis in healthy red cells, offering a route to a prothrombotic erythrocyte phenotype. |
| [[Anti-inflammatory\|Anti-inflammatory]] | Anti-inflammatory refers to substances, interventions, or mechanisms that reduce Inflammation. This encompasses pharmacological agents (NSAIDs, Corticosteroids, DMARDs, Biologics), dietary compound... |
| [[Antibody\|Antibody]] | An antibody (immunoglobulin) is the Y-shaped soluble immune receptor that binds antigen with high affinity and specificity, coupling antigen recognition to effector functions through the constant region. |
| [[Anticoagulant\|Anticoagulant]] | Anticoagulants are agents that impair blood clot formation by acting on the coagulation cascade — heparin-class agents via antithrombin, vitamin K antagonists via VKORC1, and direct oral agents via thrombin or factor Xa — used to prevent and treat thromboembolism. |
| [[Antidotum Mithridaticum\|Antidotum Mithridaticum]] | Antidotum Mithridaticum, also known as Mithridatium, was a complex potion containing numerous poisonous compounds in small doses, used historically to protect against lethal assaults through the principle of hormesis. |
| [[Antioxidant\|Antioxidant]] | Antioxidants are substances that inhibit oxidation and neutralize reactive oxygen species in biological systems, protecting cells, tissues, and lipoproteins from oxidative damage; key defense against atherosclerosis, aging, and age-related disease. |
| [[Antioxidant Supplementation Paradox\|Antioxidant Supplementation Paradox]] | High-dose exogenous antioxidant supplementation can increase mortality and block beneficial adaptive pathways by suppressing the ROS signaling that triggers endogenous defense systems via NRF2 and mitohormesis. |
| [[Antithrombin III\|Antithrombin III]] | Antithrombin III is the principal endogenous inhibitor of thrombin and factor Xa, a natural anticoagulant whose activity is massively potentiated by heparin and which also binds heparin cofactor II; its deficiency is an inherited thrombophilia. |
| [[AP-1\|AP-1]] | Dimeric bZIP transcription factor (Jun/Fos family) that acts as a pioneer factor opening SASP enhancers, drives inflammatory cytokine expression, and bridges senescence, antioxidant defense, autophagy, and neuroinflammation. |
| [[APC\|APC]] | Tumor suppressor scaffolding the beta-catenin destruction complex; germline loss causes familial adenomatous polyposis. |
| [[APE1\|APE1]] |  |
| [[Apelin\|Apelin]] | Apelin is an endogenous peptide ligand of the APJ receptor (encoded by APLN) with cardiovascular, metabolic, and neuroprotective actions; treatment reversed hippocampal necroptosis and cognitive impairment in diabetic rats. |
| [[Apigenin\|Apigenin]] | A natural flavone (4',5,7-trihydroxyflavone) with CDK-inhibitory, p53-stabilizing, senomorphic/senolytic, and broad anticancer activities across human malignancies. |
| [[ApoE\|ApoE]] | Apolipoprotein E is a 299-residue lipid carrier and the principal ligand for LDL-receptor-family receptors; the human epsilon2/3/4 isoforms differ at residues 112 and 158, with APOE epsilon4 the largest genetic risk factor for late-onset Alzheimer disease. |
| [[APOE3 Christchurch\|APOE3 Christchurch]] | Rare APOE3 R136S variant conferring resilience to Alzheimer's disease and dementia despite high amyloid pathology. |
| [[APOE4\|APOE4]] | Apolipoprotein E epsilon 4 allele, the major genetic risk factor for Alzheimer's disease that amplifies cGAS-STING/IFN-I microglial signaling. |
| [[Apolipoprotein A1\|Apolipoprotein A1]] | Primary protein component of HDL; specific acceptor for ABCA1-mediated cholesterol efflux; initiator of HDL biogenesis. |
| [[Apolipoprotein B\|Apolipoprotein B]] | Apolipoprotein B (ApoB) is the primary apolipoprotein of LDL and LDL. It serves as a ligand for the LDL receptor and is essential for lipoprotein assembly and metabolism. |
| [[Apolipoprotein E\|Apolipoprotein E]] | Apolipoprotein E (ApoE) is a plasma apolipoprotein central to lipoprotein metabolism; ApoE knockout mice are a standard model for atherosclerosis studied in mitochondrial dynamics. |
| [[APP\|APP]] | Amyloid precursor protein, an integral membrane protein whose proteolysis by secretases generates Beta-amyloid. |
| [[aPTT\|aPTT]] | The activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) is a coagulation assay that times plasma clot formation after contact activation, testing the intrinsic and common pathways (factors I, II, V, VIII, IX, X, XI, XII) and used to monitor unfractionated heparin. |
| [[Aquaporins\|Aquaporins]] | A family of integral membrane channel proteins that conduct water (and in some cases small solutes) across cell membranes. |
| [[Arachidonic acid\|Arachidonic acid]] | Biochemical properties: Arachidonic acid is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metab... |
| [[ARE\|ARE (Antioxidant Response Element)]] | ARE (Antioxidant Response Element) is a cis-acting regulatory element in the promoter regions of over 200 cytoprotective genes. It is the primary DNA sequence through which NRF2 drives the expr... |
| [[Arginine Glycine Amidinotransferase\|Arginine Glycine Amidinotransferase]] | First committed enzyme of creatine synthesis, transferring amidino group from arginine to glycine. |
| [[Argonaute\|Argonaute]] | Family of proteins that bind small RNAs (miRNA/siRNA) and execute target repression or cleavage as the catalytic engine of RISC. |
| [[ARH1\|ARH1]] | ARH1 (ADPRH) is the Mg2+-dependent ARH-family hydrolase erasing Arg-linked MARylation |
| [[ARH3\|ARH3]] | ARH3 (ADPRHL2) is a 39-kDa Mg2+-dependent ADP-ribosylhydrolase that erases serine-MARylation and degrades PAR exoglycosidically |
| [[ARIH1\|ARIH1]] | Ariadne RBR E3 ubiquitin protein ligase 1, an E3 ubiquitin ligase that promotes mitophagy and causes cancer drug resistance |
| [[ARN14794\|ARN14794]] | A benzoxazolone carboxamide small-molecule inhibitor of acid ceramidase (ACase, ASAH1), specifically used in the 2026 Soriano-Castell study demonstrating that ACase inhibition protects cells from ferroptosis independently of GPX4/GSH and iron. |
| [[ARN14974\|ARN14974]] | A potent and systemically active benzoxazolone carboxamide inhibitor of acid ceramidase (IC50 79 nM) that reduces ACase activity across multiple organs including brain; developed by Pizzirani et al. and related to ARN14794. |
| [[ARNT\|ARNT]] | Aryl hydrocarbon receptor nuclear translocator; the obligate dimerization partner of HIF1A that forms the active hypoxia-inducible transcription factor complex. |
| [[Arp2-3 Complex\|Arp2-3 Complex]] | The Arp2/3 complex is a seven-subunit actin nucleator (Arp2, Arp3 and ARPC1-5) that builds branched actin networks at a fixed ~70 degree angle off existing filaments; it is intrinsically inactive until nucleation-promoting factors such as WASP and cortactin switch it on. |
| [[Arterial Blood Gas\|Arterial Blood Gas]] | Arterial blood gas (ABG) directly measures blood pH, PaO2, PaCO2, and oxygen saturation; used when pulse oximetry is unreliable (e.g., during methylene blue infusion). |
| [[Arterial Stiffness\|Arterial Stiffness]] | Arterial stiffness refers to the loss of elastic compliance in the large conduit arteries (primarily the aorta and carotid arteries), resulting in reduced ability to buffer the pulsatile output fro... |
| [[Artesunate\|Artesunate]] | Artesunate is an antimalarial artemisinin derivative that exerts anti-cancer and neuroprotective effects partly by inducing ROS-dependent ferroptosis. |
| [[Aryl Hydrocarbon Receptor\|Aryl Hydrocarbon Receptor]] | The aryl hydrocarbon receptor (AhR) is a ligand-activated transcription factor that senses environmental toxins and endogenous metabolites, including kynurenine, regulating immune responses, xenobiotic metabolism, and cell differentiation. |
| [[ASAH1\|ASAH1]] | The gene encoding acid ceramidase (ACase, aCDase), a lysosomal hydrolase that cleaves ceramide into sphingosine and free fatty acid; mutations cause Farber disease and SMA-PME. |
| [[ASC\|ASC]] | Apoptosis-associated speck-like protein containing a CARD; the adaptor that nucleates inflammasome assembly and ASC specks downstream of AIM2/NLRP3. |
| [[ASCT2\|ASCT2]] | ASCT2 (SLC1A5) is a sodium-dependent neutral amino acid transporter that is the principal plasma-membrane importer of glutamine, and a well-characterized metabolic dependency of cancer cells. |
| [[ASF1a\|ASF1a]] | Anti-silencing function 1a histone chaperone that hands off H3.3–H4 to the HIRA complex and is essential for SAHF formation in senescent cells. |
| [[Astrocyte Senescence\|Astrocyte Senescence]] | Acquisition of a senescence-associated phenotype by astrocytes, driven by cGAS-STING signaling in neurodegenerative disease. |
| [[Astrocytes\|Astrocytes]] | Star-shaped glia that support neurons and, when activated via cGAS-STING, adopt neurotoxic A1/senescent states in neurodegeneration. |
| [[ATAC-seq\|ATAC-seq]] | ATAC-seq (Assay for Transposase-Accessible Chromatin using sequencing) is a method for mapping Chromatin Accessibility genome-wide. It uses hyperactive Tn5 Transposase to simultaneously fragmen... |
| [[ATF4\|ATF4]] | ATF4 (Activating Transcription Factor 4) is a stress-induced transcription factor that serves as a central hub for the Integrated Stress Response (ISR) in mammals. |
| [[ATF6α\|ATF6α]] | ATF6α is the transmembrane bZIP transcription factor of the unfolded protein response arm of ER stress signalling; ER stress triggers its Golgi proteolytic release, after which it drives XBP1 and UPRE-dependent target gene expression. |
| [[ATFS-1\|ATFS-1]] | ATFS-1 (Activating Transcription Factor associated with Stress-1) is a basic helix-loop-helix transcription factor in *C. elegans* that is the primary regulator of the Mitochondrial Unfolded Protei... |
| [[Atg\|Atg]] | Atg (autophagy-related) proteins are a family of evolutionarily conserved proteins that orchestrate the Autophagy pathway, from initiation to autophagosome formation, cargo recognition, and lysosom... |
| [[Atg14L\|Atg14L]] | A mammalian-specific subunit of the Vps34–Beclin 1 complex that directs autophagosome formation and endosome–autophagosome fusion to the phagophore. |
| [[Atg18\|Atg18]] | Atg18 is the budding yeast PX-domain protein that binds phosphatidylinositol 3-phosphate on the phagophore and, via Atg2, drives autophagosome size expansion and autophagic flux in S. cerevisiae. |
| [[Atg4B\|Atg4B]] | Catalytic function: Atg4B is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[ATGL\|ATGL]] | Adipose triglyceride lipase (ATGL, PNPLA2) is the rate-limiting triacylglycerol hydrolase that initiates cytoplasmic lipid droplet breakdown, producing diacylglycerol and free fatty acids; it couples sirtuin signaling to lipolysis and acts in parallel to lipophagy in C. elegans. |
| [[Atherosclerosis\|Atherosclerosis]] | Atherosclerosis is a disease in which the inside of an artery narrows due to the build up of plaque. Oxidative Stress and Lipid Peroxidation are key drivers of plaque formation. It often involves E... |
| [[ATLAS\|ATLAS]] | ATLAS is a randomized trial showing urolithin A improves muscle strength, endurance, and mitochondrial biomarkers in middle-aged adults. |
| [[ATM\|ATM]] | ATM (Ataxia-Telangiectasia Mutated) is a ~350 kDa serine/threonine protein kinase belonging to the PI3K-related kinase (PIKK) family. It functions as the master initiator of the DNA Damage Resp... |
| [[ATP\|ATP]] | Adenosine triphosphate (ATP) is a nucleotide composed of adenine, ribose, and three phosphate groups that serves as the universal energy currency of living cells. Hydrolysis of ATP to ADP and P... |
| [[ATP Synthase\|ATP synthase]] | ATP synthase (Complex V) is the mitochondrial enzyme that catalyzes ATP synthesis from ADP and inorganic phosphate using the proton gradient generated by the electron transport chain. |
| [[ATR\|ATR]] | Ataxia Telangiectasia and Rad3-related protein kinase, a central sensor of replication stress and single-stranded DNA that activates the DNA damage checkpoint and drives senescence |
| [[Atrial Fibrillation\|Atrial Fibrillation]] | Atrial fibrillation is the commonest sustained arrhythmia, characterised by chaotic atrial electrical activity and an irregularly irregular ventricular response; it is driven by pulmonary-vein triggers acting on a remodeled atrium and is managed with rate or rhythm control plus stroke prevention. |
| [[Atrial Flutter\|Atrial Flutter]] | Atrial flutter is a re-entrant supraventricular tachyarrhythmia, most commonly a cavotricuspid isthmus-dependent macro-reentrant circuit in the right atrium, producing a regular sawtooth atrial pattern at ~300 bpm that is highly responsive to catheter ablation. |
| [[Atrial natriuretic peptide\|Atrial natriuretic peptide]] | Cardiac hormone (ANP) secreted by atrial cardiomyocytes that promotes natriuresis, diuresis, and vasodilation to lower blood pressure and volume. |
| [[ATRX\|ATRX]] | ATRX (ATRX chromatin remodeler) is an X-linked SWI/SNF family helicase that deposits histone H3.3 at telomeres and pericentric heterochromatin; loss-of-function mutations drive alternative lengthening of telomeres and are enriched in male tumors. |
| [[AUF1\|AUF1]] | AU-rich element RNA-binding protein 1 (hnRNP D), an ARE-mRNA decay factor inactivated by MK2 to stabilize SASP cytokine transcripts. |
| [[Auranofin\|Auranofin]] | Auranofin (Ridaura) is an oral gold(I) coordination compound — the triethylphosphine gold complex of tetraacetylthioglucose — FDA-approved for rheumatoid arthritis (1985) and repurposed as a first-in-class covalent inhibitor of the selenoprotein thioredoxin reductases (TXNRD1/TXNRD2), under clinical investigation as an anticancer, antiparasitic, and antibacterial agent. |
| [[Aurora Kinase\|Aurora Kinase]] | Aurora Kinases (Aurora-A, Aurora-B, Aurora-C) are a family of serine/threonine kinases that regulate mitotic spindle assembly, chromosome segregation, and cytokinesis. Aurora-A and Aurora-B levels rise when SIRT2 is disrupted, linking sirtuins to aneuploidy. |
| [[Autism\|Autism]] | Autism spectrum disorder (ASD) is a heterogeneous neurodevelopmental condition characterized by persistent deficits in social communication and interaction alongside restricted, repetitive behaviors, arising from highly polygenic susceptibility interacting with prenatal environment and, possibly, immune mechanisms. |
| [[Autoimmune Disease\|Autoimmune Disease]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Autoimmune Disease is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, ... |
| [[Autoinflammation\|Autoinflammation]] | Autoinflammation refers to dysregulated innate immune activation without identifiable adaptive immune involvement, driven by inflammasome hyperactivation and excessive IL-1β production. |
| [[Autonomic Nervous System\|Autonomic Nervous System]] | The involuntary branch of the peripheral nervous system (sympathetic and parasympathetic) that regulates visceral function and, via HRV, mirrors recovery capacity. |
| [[Axon Degeneration\|Axon Degeneration]] | Axon degeneration is the pathological loss of axonal integrity in response to injury, disease, or metabolic stress, with SARM1-mediated Wallerian degeneration as its best-characterized execution pathway. |
| [[B Cells\|B Cells]] | Antibody-producing adaptive lymphocytes that infiltrate the CNS in neurodegeneration and are shaped by cGAS-STING signaling. |
| [[B-cell Immunoglobulin-derived Gene 1\|B-cell Immunoglobulin-derived Gene 1]] | A disambiguation entry for the historical gene name BIG1, which in the vault resolves to GBF1, the Golgi-resident Sec7-domain Arf1 guanine nucleotide exchange factor that drives COPI coat recruitment and secretory-pathway trafficking. The full name "B-cell immunoglobulin-derived gene 1" is not a current HGNC or NCBI Gene symbol. |
| [[B2M\|B2M]] | Beta-2 microglobulin, the invariant light chain of MHC class I molecules; a senescence surface marker used for targeted senolytic delivery and a plasma protein surrogate in the DNAmGrimAge epigenetic clock. |
| [[BACE1\|BACE1]] | Catalytic function: BACE1 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Bacteroidetes\|Bacteroidetes]] | Bacteroidetes is a phylum of Gram-negative, non-spore-forming, anaerobic or aerobic, and rod-shaped bacteria that are widely distributed in the environment, including the human gut. |
| [[BAFF\|BAFF]] | B-cell activating factor (TNFSF13B), a TNF-superfamily cytokine secreted by senescent cells that reinforces the SASP and links senescence to adaptive immunity. |
| [[Bafilomycin A1\|Bafilomycin A1]] | Chemical properties: Bafilomycin A1 is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[Bardoxolone methyl\|Bardoxolone methyl]] | Chemical properties: Bardoxolone methyl is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[Barrier-to-Autointegration Factor 1\|Barrier-to-Autointegration Factor 1]] | A chromatin-associated protein that binds the nuclear envelope and regulates cGAS localization and activity. |
| [[Base Excision Repair\|Base Excision Repair]] | Definition: Base Excision Repair is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways... |
| [[BCG\|BCG]] | Bacillus Calmette-Guérin, a live attenuated vaccine strain of Mycobacterium bovis used as the standard intravesical adjuvant for high-risk non-muscle-invasive bladder cancer, and one of the earliest demonstrations that deliberate infection can generate antitumour immunity. Its mechanisms remain incompletely defined. |
| [[BCMA\|BCMA]] | B-cell maturation antigen (TNFRSF17, CD269) is a TNF receptor superfamily member on plasma cells that binds BAFF and transduces NF-κB survival signalling; it is the target of BCMA-directed CAR T cells and antibody-drug conjugates in multiple myeloma. |
| [[BCR-ABL\|BCR-ABL]] | Oncogenic fusion kinase driving chronic myeloid leukemia; target of imatinib and successors. |
| [[Benign Tumor\|Benign Tumor]] | A benign tumor (benign neoplasm) is a localized, non-metastatic proliferation; its relationship to longevity is dual — some benign lesions are tumor-suppressive fail-safes, while others (hamartomas) reflect pathological growth. |
| [[Beta Blockers\|Beta Blockers]] | Beta blockers are adrenergic receptor antagonists that block beta-adrenergic signalling, chiefly slowing heart rate, reducing contractility and lowering blood pressure; they are cornerstone drugs in heart failure, ischaemic heart disease and arrhythmia, and their antimicrobial and anti-longevity reputations are not supported by trial data. |
| [[Beta Diversity\|Beta Diversity]] | Beta diversity is a measure of the difference in species composition between different communities or samples, allowing for comparisons across various stages of aging or environmental conditions. |
| [[Beta-Adrenergic Receptor\|Beta-Adrenergic Receptor]] | G protein-coupled receptors for catecholamines (norepinephrine and epinephrine) comprising β1, β2, and β3 subtypes. They mediate sympathetic nervous system control of heart rate, smooth-muscle relaxation, lipolysis, thermogenesis, and post-exercise metabolic responses. |
| [[Beta-amyloid\|Beta-amyloid]] | Peptides of 36–43 amino acids that are the main component of the amyloid plaques found in the brains of people with Alzheimer's Disease. |
| [[Bicarbonate\|Bicarbonate]] | Bicarbonate (hydrogencarbonate, HCO3-) is the conjugate base of carbonic acid and the principal extracellular buffer of the blood, linked to carbon dioxide transport through the carbonic anhydrase reaction; it is also the substrate for cytosolic bicarbonate transporters that feed de novo nucleotide synthesis. |
| [[Bicuspid Aortic Valve\|Bicuspid Aortic Valve]] | A congenital cardiac defect in which two of the three aortic valve leaflets fuse so that a functional bicuspid valve persists, affecting roughly 1% of adults and predisposing to early calcific aortic stenosis, aortic regurgitation and ascending aortic aneurysm. |
| [[Bifidobacterium\|Bifidobacterium]] | Bifidobacterium is a genus of Gram-positive, non-motile, often branched anaerobic bacteria that are common inhabitants of the gastrointestinal tract and are often used as probiotics. |
| [[Bilirubin\|Bilirubin]] | Biochemical properties: Bilirubin is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic fl... |
| [[Bioavailability\|Bioavailability]] | Bioavailability is the fraction of an administered dose of a compound that reaches systemic circulation unchanged and is available at the site of action to produce a biological effect. |
| [[Biological Aging\|Biological Aging]] | The functional decline of physiological systems with time, distinguishable from chronological age and measurable by multi-system aging clocks. |
| [[Biomarker\|Biomarker]] | A biomarker (biological marker) is a measurable indicator of a biological state, condition, or process. Biomarkers are used for Diagnosis, Prognosis, Risk Stratification, and monitoring Therape... |
| [[Biomarkers\|Biomarkers]] | In the field of Geroscience, biomarkers are essential tools for measuring the physiological state of an organism and predicting its future health outcomes. Epigenetics-based biomarkers, particu... |
| [[BiP\|BiP]] | BiP (GRP78, HSPA5) is the endoplasmic-reticulum-resident Hsp70 chaperone that binds misfolded proteins to keep the three unfolded protein response sensors repressed and is the primary transcriptional target of the UPR. |
| [[Biphasic Dose-Response Curve\|Biphasic Dose-Response Curve]] | A non-monotonic dose-response relationship in which the slope changes sign, producing an inverted-U (or J-shaped) profile—the quantitative signature of hormesis. |
| [[BIRC2\|BIRC2]] | The BIRC2 gene encoding cIAP1 — a RING E3 ubiquitin ligase that ubiquitinates RIPK1 in TNFR1 complex I and is destroyed by Smac-mimetic-induced auto-ubiquitination. |
| [[Bleomycin\|Bleomycin]] | A genotoxic glycopeptide antibiotic used to induce cellular senescence (therapy-induced senescence, TIS) in senescence research and as a clinical chemotherapeutic. |
| [[Blood-Brain Barrier\|Blood-Brain Barrier]] | The neurovascular interface that restricts CNS immune infiltration, dynamically regulated by cell-type-specific cGAS-STING signaling. |
| [[BMAL1\|BMAL1]] | Core circadian clock transcription factor (ARNTL); heterodimerizes with CLOCK to drive rhythmic gene expression and is deacetylated/activated by SIRT1. |
| [[BMP\|BMP]] | Bone morphogenetic proteins, a subfamily of TGFβ-superfamily growth factors that act as developmental SASP factors contributing to patterning, morphogenesis, and tissue growth. |
| [[BNIP3\|BNIP3]] | BNIP3 is a BH3-only BCL-2 family member and outer mitochondrial membrane mitophagy receptor that binds LC3 through an LIR motif; it is transcriptionally induced by HIF-1α under hypoxia, where it can also drive permeability transition and delayed neuronal death. |
| [[BNIP3L\|BNIP3L]] | BCL2/adenovirus E1B interacting protein 3-like (BNIP3L/NIX), an outer mitochondrial membrane mitophagy receptor for hypoxia-induced mitophagy |
| [[BNP\|BNP]] | B-type natriuretic peptide, a cardiac hormone released in response to myocardial stretch and used as a heart-failure biomarker. |
| [[Body Mass Index\|Body Mass Index]] | A simple anthropometric index calculated as body weight in kilograms divided by height in metres squared (kg/m2), used to classify underweight, normal weight, overweight, and obesity in populations and clinical practice. |
| [[Bone Marrow\|Bone Marrow]] | Bone Marrow is a semi-solid tissue found within the spongy (trabecular) portions of bones. It is the primary site of Hematopoiesis — the production of all blood cell lineages — in adult mammals. |
| [[Bone marrow-derived macrophages\|Bone marrow-derived macrophages]] | Macrophages differentiated in vitro from bone marrow progenitors; widely used model for inflammasome and innate immune studies. |
| [[Bortezomib\|Bortezomib]] | A first-in-class reversible proteasome inhibitor used to treat multiple myeloma and mantle cell lymphoma. |
| [[BRAF\|BRAF]] | BRAF (B-Raf Proto-Oncogene, Serine/Threonine Kinase) is a protein kinase in the RAS-RAF-MEK1/2-ERK1/2 (MAPK/ERK pathway) signaling cascade that regulates Cell Proliferation, Differentiation, and Su... |
| [[Brain\|Brain]] | The Brain is the central organ of the nervous system, coordinating cognition, sensation, movement, and autonomic control. |
| [[Brain Cancer\|Brain Cancer]] | Malignant primary tumours of the central nervous system — chiefly glioblastoma, astrocytoma, oligodendroglioma, ependymoma and medulloblastoma — plus the far more common brain metastases; a group of diseases with poor prognosis and limited treatment options. |
| [[Branched-Chain Amino Acids\|Branched-Chain Amino Acids]] | Leucine, isoleucine, and valine — the three essential branched-chain amino acids. High circulating levels are associated with obesity, insulin resistance, and mortality; dietary restriction recapitulates many benefits of protein restriction. |
| [[BRCA2\|BRCA2]] | BRCA2 (Breast Cancer Type 2 Susceptibility Protein) is a Tumor Suppressor protein critical for Homologous Recombination repair of Double-Strand Breaks. It interacts with RAD51 to mediate the loadin... |
| [[BRD4\|BRD4]] | Bromodomain-containing protein 4, an epigenetic reader recruited to senescence-activated enhancers to promote SASP. |
| [[BrdU\|BrdU]] | 5-bromo-2'-deoxyuridine (BrdU) is a thymidine analog incorporated into replicating DNA, used experimentally as a proliferation/DNA-synthesis marker; reduced BrdU incorporation is a readout of senescence-associated growth arrest. |
| [[Breast Cancer\|Breast Cancer]] | Breast Cancer is a major malignancy affecting breast tissue, primarily in women. It is the most common cancer in women worldwide. |
| [[BRG1\|BRG1]] | ATPase subunit of SWI/SNF chromatin remodelers controlling differentiation and tumor suppression. |
| [[Bronchopulmonary Dysplasia\|Bronchopulmonary Dysplasia]] | Chronic lung disease of prematurity affecting alveolar and vascular development |
| [[Brown Adipose Tissue\|Brown Adipose Tissue]] | Mitochondria-rich thermogenic fat that expends energy as heat via UCP1; activated by cold exposure, a classic mitohormetic stimulus. |
| [[BubR1\|BubR1]] | BubR1 (BUB1B) is a mitotic checkpoint serine/threonine kinase essential for the spindle assembly checkpoint; SIRT2 extends lifespan in BubR1 hypomorphic progeroid mice. |
| [[Butyrate\|Butyrate]] | Butyrate is a four-carbon short-chain fatty acid produced by gut bacteria, known for its anti-inflammatory properties and its role as a primary energy source for colonocytes. |
| [[c-FLIP\|c-FLIP]] | FLICE-like inhibitory protein (c-FLIP/CFLAR) — an enzymatically inactive caspase-8 homolog whose isoforms set the apoptose/necroptose/pyroptose switch by tuning caspase-8 activity inside death receptor and inflammasome complexes. |
| [[c-Fos\|c-Fos]] | An immediate-early transcription factor (AP-1 subunit) driving proliferation; its mRNA is stabilized by HuR, and loss of cytosolic HuR during senescence represses c-Fos translation, aiding proliferative arrest. |
| [[c-Jun\|c-Jun]] | Jun proto-oncogene (JUN), an AP-1 family pioneer transcription factor that opens senescence-associated SASP enhancers and partially enforces the senescence growth arrest. |
| [[c-KIT\|c-KIT]] | Receptor tyrosine kinase (CD117, KIT) involved in mast-cell, GIST, and melanoma biology. |
| [[c-Myc\|c-Myc]] | A proto-oncogene and transcription factor (MYC) that regulates cell cycle progression, apoptosis, and cellular transformation. In the context of mitochondrial stress, c-Myc can influence metabolic ... |
| [[C11-BODIPY\|C11-BODIPY]] | A fluorescent fatty acid analogue used as a probe for detecting lipid peroxidation in live cells by flow cytometry or microscopy. |
| [[C57BL-6\|C57BL/6]] | The most widely used inbred laboratory mouse strain, serving as the reference background for genetic and aging research. |
| [[C9orf72\|C9orf72]] | C9orf72, an ALS/FTD-associated gene whose repeat expansions drive cGAS-STING activation via nucleocytoplasmic transport defects and DNA damage. |
| [[Ca2+ overload\|Ca2+ overload]] | Cytosolic Ca2+ overload is the calcium-flooded intermediate linking ATP collapse to calpain and mPTP execution in necrosis. |
| [[Carbonic Anhydrase\|Carbonic Anhydrase]] | Zinc enzyme hydrating CO2 to bicarbonate; in erythrocytes it completes ~23% of CO2 carriage together with anion exchanger 1 in the chloride shift. |
| [[Cachexia\|Cachexia]] | A multifactorial syndrome of severe body-weight loss, muscle and fat wasting driven by systemic inflammation and catabolic signaling, complicating chronic disease. |
| [[Caffeic Acid Phenethyl Ester\|Caffeic Acid Phenethyl Ester]] | CAPE is the caffeic acid–phenethyl alcohol ester found in propolis and several plants; in vitro and animal models it activates NRF2/HO-1, suppresses NF-kB and is reported as a radiosensitiser, but it has no established clinical efficacy. |
| [[Caffeine\|Caffeine]] | Caffeine is a methylxanthine alkaloid and the most widely consumed psychoactive drug; it acts mainly as an antagonist at adenosine receptors, and at typical doses produces alerting, reduced fatigue and a modest reduction in Parkinson's disease risk. |
| [[Calcium\|Calcium]] | A divalent cation (Ca2+) that serves as a ubiquitous second messenger in cellular signaling, playing critical roles in mitochondrial function, muscle contraction, neurotransmission, and cell death pathways. |
| [[Calcium Channel Blockers\|Calcium Channel Blockers]] | Calcium channel blockers (CCBs) are a drug class that inhibit L-type voltage-gated calcium channels, divided into dihydropyridines (amlodipine, nifedipine) acting on vascular smooth muscle and non-dihydropyridines (verapamil, diltiazem) acting additionally on cardiac pacemaker and contractile tissue. |
| [[Calcium Signaling\|Calcium Signaling]] | Cellular Ca2+ signaling, especially at ER-mitochondria contact sites, regulating mitochondrial dynamics and fission. |
| [[Calmodulin\|Calmodulin]] | Calmodulin is a 148-residue four-EF-hand Ca2+ sensor that, on calcium binding, exposes a hydrophobic surface and activates hundreds of targets including CaM kinases, calcineurin, and NOS, making it the cell's most-used calcium decoder. |
| [[Caloric Restriction\|Caloric Restriction]] | Caloric restriction (CR)—reducing calorie intake without malnutrition—is the most robust non-genetic intervention known to delay Aging. It exerts its effects largely by remodeling the Epigenome, ef... |
| [[Calpain\|Calpain]] | Calpain is a Ca2+-dependent cysteine protease; calpain I is a candidate mediator of AIF release in parthanatos and cleaves BID to route Ca2+ signals through BAX, though its role is dispensable in some models. |
| [[Calpain-2\|Calpain-2]] | Calcium-dependent cysteine protease (CAPN2/m-calpain) recruited by the phosphorylated caspase-8 scaffold to drive focal-adhesion turnover, migration and therapy resistance. |
| [[Calreticulin\|Calreticulin]] | Calreticulin (CRT, CALR) is an ER calcium-binding chaperone with three domains that also functions as a cell-surface 'eat-me' signal driving phagocytic clearance, a plasminogen receptor on the tumor surface, and a regulator of both MeCP2 and NRF2 activity. |
| [[Canakinumab\|Canakinumab]] | Canakinumab is a human monoclonal antibody that selectively targets interleukin-1 beta (IL-1β), used in autoinflammatory conditions and studied for cardiovascular risk reduction. |
| [[Cancer Chemoprevention\|Cancer Chemoprevention]] | The use of natural or synthetic agents to prevent, reverse, or delay carcinogenesis before invasive cancer develops. |
| [[CANTOS Trial\|CANTOS Trial]] | Landmark trial proving IL-1beta blockade with canakinumab lowers cardiovascular events independent of lipids. |
| [[cap-dependent translation\|cap-dependent translation]] | Definition: cap-dependent translation is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pat... |
| [[Cardiac Development\|Cardiac Development]] | Cardiogenesis is the process by which the heart forms from mesoderm through induction, tube formation, looping, septation and maturation, controlled by combinatorial transcription factor codes and sequential waves of signalling. |
| [[Cardiac Fibrosis\|Cardiac Fibrosis]] | Pathological process of excessive extracellular matrix deposition in the myocardium, driven by TGF-β-mediated myofibroblast differentiation |
| [[Cardiolipin\|Cardiolipin]] | Cardiolipin is a diphosphatidylglycerol lipid uniquely concentrated in the inner mitochondrial membrane, essential for electron transport chain function and a key damage-associated molecular pattern (DAMP) when released into the cytosol. |
| [[Cardiomyocyte\|Cardiomyocyte]] | Cardiomyocytes are striated, contractile muscle cells of the heart responsible for generating pump force throughout life. |
| [[Cardiomyocyte Toxicity\|Cardiomyocyte Toxicity]] | Cardiomyocyte toxicity (cardiotoxicity) refers to damage or dysfunction of Cardiomyocyte caused by drugs, environmental toxins, or disease states. It is a major dose-limiting Adverse Effect of ... |
| [[Cardiomyopathy\|Cardiomyopathy]] | A group of diseases of the heart muscle that impair its ability to pump blood, leading to heart failure and arrhythmia. |
| [[Cardiovascular Disease\|Cardiovascular Disease]] | Cardiovascular disease (CVD) is a class of diseases that involve the heart or blood vessels. Major types include coronary artery disease (e.g., Atherosclerosis), which is often preceded by Endothel... |
| [[Cardiovascular System\|Cardiovascular System]] | Organ system responsible for blood circulation; its hemodynamic parameters are a domain of biological aging and a target of inflammaging. |
| [[Carmofur\|Carmofur]] | A 5-fluorouracil prodrug clinically approved in Japan for colorectal cancer since 1981; a potent acid ceramidase inhibitor (IC50 29 nM) that crosses the blood-brain barrier and is a repurposing candidate for glioblastoma and senolytic therapy. |
| [[Carnosine\|Carnosine]] | Carnosine (beta-alanyl-L-histidine) is a dipeptide found in high concentrations in muscle and brain tissues, known for its antioxidant, pH-buffering, and anti-glycation properties. |
| [[Carotenoids\|Carotenoids]] | Carotenoids are C40 isoprenoid pigments — carotenes (β-, α-, γ-carotene, lycopene) and xanthophylls (lutein, zeaxanthin, β-cryptoxanthin) — that color plants, quench singlet oxygen and lipid radicals in membranes, and, for three of them, serve as provitamin A precursors converted to retinol with feedback control. |
| [[Catalase\|Catalase]] | Catalase is a key antioxidant enzyme that catalyzes the decomposition of Hydrogen Peroxide into water and oxygen. It is a critical component of the cell's defense against Oxidative Stress. |
| [[Cataract\|Cataract]] | Cataract is a clouding of the eye lens that leads to decreased vision. It is driven by Oxidative Stress to lens proteins, protein carbonylation, and aggregation of crystallins. |
| [[Catechin\|Catechin]] | Catechin is a flavan-3-ol flavonoid abundant in tea, with antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and senotherapeutic properties. |
| [[Catechol\|Catechol]] | Catechol (pyrocatechol, 1,2-dihydroxybenzene) is an organic ortho-diphenol compound, the parent scaffold of the catecholamine family and a key intermediate in melanin formation and adrenochrome research. |
| [[Catecholamine-induced cardiomyopathy\|Catecholamine-induced cardiomyopathy]] | Myocardial injury and ventricular dysfunction caused by excess catecholamines from stress, pheochromocytoma, or exogenous sympathomimetics. |
| [[Catecholamines\|Catecholamines]] | Catecholamines are a class of aromatic amines that serve as hormones and neurotransmitters. They are characterized by a catechol group (a benzene ring with two hydroxyl groups) and an amine sid... |
| [[Cathepsin L1\|Cathepsin L1]] | Lysosomal cysteine protease (CTSL) that cleaves the N-terminal tail of histone H3.3, generating the senescence-associated H3.3cs1 fragment linked to SASP and growth arrest. |
| [[CB-839\|CB-839]] | CB-839 (telaglenastat) — a first-in-class, orally bioavailable glutaminase (GLS1) inhibitor that starves glutamine-addicted tumors of TCA anaplerosis; limited monotherapy efficacy, but a synthetic-lethality partner for Complex I inhibition in MYC-high tumors. |
| [[CBP\|CBP]] | CBP (CREB-binding protein / CREBBP) is a 2442-residue transcriptional coactivator and lysine acetyltransferase that scaffolds enhancer-bound transcription factors to the Mediator complex and acetylates histones and non-histone targets including p53. |
| [[CCCP\|CCCP]] | Carbonyl cyanide m-chlorophenylhydrazone, a mitochondrial uncoupler that depletes mitochondria via Parkin-mediated mitophagy; in irradiated senescent fibroblasts it abrogates senescence markers and the SASP, showing mitochondria are central to senescence. |
| [[CCL18\|CCL18]] | C-C motif chemokine ligand 18 (PARC), a SASP chemokine associated with mortality and used as a senescence biomarker. |
| [[CCL2\|CCL2]] | C-C motif chemokine ligand 2 (MCP-1), a prototypical SASP chemokine that recruits monocytes and macrophages via CCR2. |
| [[CCL5\|CCL5]] | C-C motif chemokine ligand 5 (RANTES), a SASP chemokine that binds CCR1/3/5 and promotes cancer progression and reproductive aging. |
| [[CCN1\|CCN1]] | Cellular communication network factor 1 (CYR61), a matricellular SASP protein that promotes wound healing, fibroblast senescence, and tissue regeneration through integrin signaling. |
| [[CCR2\|CCR2]] | C-C chemokine receptor type 2, the principal receptor for the monocyte chemoattractant MCP-1 (CCL2). Mediates monocyte/macrophage recruitment in inflammation, including SASP-driven immune infiltration in aged tissues. |
| [[CD206\|CD206]] | Mannose receptor (MRC1), a C-type lectin on alternatively activated M2 macrophages involved in endocytosis and immune tolerance. |
| [[CD36\|CD36]] | Cluster of differentiation 36, a scavenger receptor essential for NF-κB activation and SASP signaling downstream of lipid ligands. |
| [[CD38\|CD38]] | CD38 is an ectoenzyme that degrades NAD+ and its precursors like NMN, with its levels and activity increasing during aging and contributing to mitochondrial dysfunction. Also regulates extracellular NMN availability and is neuroprotective in axon injury models. |
| [[CD4\|CD4]] | Co-receptor marking helper T cells that recognize MHC class II and orchestrate adaptive immunity. |
| [[CD4+ T Cells\|CD4+ T Cells]] | CD4-positive T helper cells are central coordinators of adaptive immunity, orchestrating immune responses through cytokine production and helper functions. |
| [[CD40L\|CD40L]] | CD40L (CD154, TNFSF5) is a trimeric TNF-superfamily type II transmembrane cytokine expressed on activated CD4+ T cells that engages CD40 on B cells and antigen-presenting cells to drive germinal centre formation, class-switch recombination, and dendritic cell maturation. |
| [[CD44\|CD44]] | CD44 variant isoforms (CD44v) partner with MUC1-C and xCT to sustain GSH and ferroptosis resistance in triple-negative breast cancer. |
| [[CD47\|CD47]] | CD47 is a ubiquitously expressed immunoglobulin-superfamily receptor that pairs with SIRP-alpha on myeloid cells to deliver a "don't eat me" signal suppressing phagocytosis; blockade of CD47 is a cancer and fibrosis immunotherapy strategy. |
| [[CD73\|CD73]] | CD73 is an ectoenzyme that can degrade NMN into Nicotinamide Riboside (NR), thereby sustaining NAD+ synthesis. |
| [[CD8 T cells\|CD8 T cells]] | Cytotoxic CD8+ T lymphocytes that mediate pathogen-specific and anti-tumor immunity; their memory subset is increased by mTOR inhibition, improving vaccine responses. |
| [[CD86\|CD86]] | Co-stimulatory B7-family transmembrane receptor (B7-2) on antigen-presenting cells that engages CD28/CTLA-4 to regulate T-cell activation. |
| [[CD9\|CD9]] | Tetraspanin transmembrane protein that organizes membrane microdomains and mediates cell adhesion, migration, and vesicle trafficking. |
| [[CD94\|CD94]] | CD94, the invariant signaling subunit that pairs with NKG2A (KLRC1) to form the inhibitory HLA-E-recognizing receptor on NK cells and a subset of T cells. |
| [[CDH5\|CDH5]] | Vascular endothelial cadherin (VE-cadherin), the principal adhesion molecule at endothelial adherens junctions |
| [[CDK\|CDK]] | Cyclin-dependent kinases (CDKs) are the CMGC-family serine/threonine kinases whose catalytic activity is gated by cyclin binding; the 11 mammalian members fall into cell-cycle CDKs (CDK1/2/4/6), transcriptional CDKs (CDK8/9/12/13) and CDK5, which has no canonical activating cyclin. |
| [[CDK2\|CDK2]] | CDK that regulates G1/S transition and S phase progression in complex with cyclin E/A. |
| [[CDK4 6\|CDK4 6]] | CDK4 and CDK6 are closely related Cyclin-Dependent Kinases that regulate the G1 Phase-to-S Phase phase transition of the Cell Cycle. They form active complexes with Cyclin D (D1, D2, D3) to... |
| [[CDK5\|CDK5]] | CDK5 (Cyclin-dependent kinase 5) is a proline-directed serine/threonine kinase that, despite its name, is not primarily involved in cell cycle regulation. It is activated by p35 or p39 and play... |
| [[CDKN2A\|CDKN2A]] | CDKN2A is a single 9p21.3 locus encoding two unrelated tumor suppressors from alternative first exons: p16INK4a, which inhibits CDK4/6 to hold Rb in a hypophosphorylated state, and p14ARF, which binds MDM2 to stabilise p53. |
| [[CDO1\|CDO1]] | CDO1 (cysteine dioxygenase type 1) promotes ferroptosis in gastric cancer by competitively consuming cysteine and restricting GSH synthesis. |
| [[CEBPα\|CEBPα]] | CCAAT/enhancer-binding protein alpha is a bZIP transcription factor that binds senescence-activated enhancers and cooperates with NF-κB to drive SASP gene expression. |
| [[CEBPβ\|CEBPβ]] | CEBPβ (CCAAT/enhancer-binding protein beta) is a transcription factor belonging to the C/EBP family of basic leucine zipper (bZIP) proteins. It regulates genes involved in Inflammation, Metabolism,... |
| [[Cell Adhesion\|Cell Adhesion]] | The specific, non-covalent binding of a cell to another cell or to the extracellular matrix through dedicated cell-surface adhesion molecules — cadherins, integrins, selectins and immunoglobulin-superfamily receptors — governing tissue architecture, migration, and cancer invasion. |
| [[Cell Cycle\|Cell Cycle]] | The Cell Cycle is the series of events that take place in a cell leading to its division and duplication of its DNA (DNA replication) to produce two daughter cells. It is tightly regulated by Cycli... |
| [[Cell Division\|Cell Division]] | Cell division is the process by which a parent cell divides into two or more daughter cells. In Eukaryotic Cells, it comprises Mitosis (nuclear division) and Cytokinesis (cytoplasmic division). Pro... |
| [[Cell Membranes\|Cell Membranes]] | Cell membranes (biological membranes) are phospholipid bilayers that define the boundaries of cells and organelles. They are composed primarily of Phospholipids, Cholesterol, and Membrane Proteins,... |
| [[Cell Migration\|Cell Migration]] | Directed cell movement via actin remodeling, adhesion turnover, and growth-factor guidance. |
| [[Cell Proliferation\|Cell Proliferation]] | Cell proliferation is the process by which cells grow and divide to produce daughter cells, tightly regulated by Growth Factor signaling, Cell Cycle checkpoints, and Cell Division machinery. It is ... |
| [[Cell Signaling\|Cell Signaling]] | The process by which cells detect and respond to chemical and physical cues through receptors, second messengers and amplified signal-transduction cascades; includes autocrine, paracrine, juxtacrine and endocrine signalling modes. |
| [[Cell Stress\|Cell Stress]] | Definition: Cell Stress is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and envi... |
| [[Cell-Penetrating Peptide\|Cell-Penetrating Peptide]] | A short (roughly 5-40 residue), typically cationic or amphipathic peptide that crosses biological membranes and escorts covalently linked or non-covalently complexed cargo into the cytosol, most often by macropinocytosis with endosomal escape. |
| [[Cellular Homeostasis\|Cellular Homeostasis]] | Cellular homeostasis refers to the dynamic equilibrium maintained by cells through coordinated regulation of metabolism, signaling, gene expression, and organelle function. It encompasses the balan... |
| [[Cellular Senescence\|Cellular Senescence]] | A state of stable cell cycle arrest accompanied by metabolic reprogramming, macromolecular damage, and a pro-inflammatory secretory phenotype; central to aging and age-related diseases. |
| [[CENP-A\|CENP-A]] | CENP-A is the centromere-specific histone H3 variant whose centromere targeting domain uniquely replaces H3 in centromeric nucleosomes, defining centromere identity epigenetically and licensing kinetochore assembly for chromosome segregation. |
| [[Centenarians\|Centenarians]] | Centenarians are individuals who have reached the age of 100 years or more, serving as models for successful aging and extreme longevity. |
| [[Central Nervous System\|Central Nervous System]] | The brain and spinal cord, the anatomical compartment in which cGAS-STING signaling regulates glial activation, neuronal homeostasis, and neuroinflammation. |
| [[Central Sensitization\|Central Sensitization]] | A state of enhanced pain hypersensitivity produced by activity-dependent plasticity in central nociceptive pathways, in which excitatory transmission is amplified and descending and local inhibition is reduced, so that normally non-painful input is perceived as painful. |
| [[Centromere\|Centromere]] | The centromere is a specialized Chromosome region where Kinetochore assembles and Spindle Microtubules attach during Mitosis and Meiosis. It is defined by the presence of the Histone H3 variant CEN... |
| [[Ceramide\|Ceramide]] | A central sphingolipid composed of sphingosine linked to a fatty acid; a pro-apoptotic/senescence-associated lipid whose catabolism by acid ceramidase releases free fatty acids that feed pro-ferroptotic membrane remodeling. |
| [[Cerebrospinal Fluid\|Cerebrospinal Fluid]] | Clear fluid filling CNS ventricles and subarachnoid space that conveys cGAMP and inflammatory mediators between brain and periphery. |
| [[Ceruloplasmin\|Ceruloplasmin]] | Ceruloplasmin is a 1065-residue, six-domain plasma multicopper oxidase that carries 6–7 copper atoms and oxidises Fe2+ to Fe3+ for transferrin loading, while serving as the major copper transport protein and an acute-phase reactant. |
| [[CESD\|CESD]] | Cholesteryl ester storage disease — the milder, later-onset phenotype of lysosomal acid lipase deficiency caused by biallelic LIPA mutations, in which cholesteryl esters accumulate in liver, spleen and gut without the infantile fulminant course of Wolman disease. |
| [[cGAMP\|cGAMP]] | 2'3'-cyclic GMP-AMP; a cyclic dinucleotide second messenger produced by cGAS that activates STING |
| [[cGAS\|cGAS]] | Cyclic GMP-AMP synthase that senses cytosolic DNA to activate the cGAS-STING innate immune pathway, linking genome stress, senescence, and inflammaging. |
| [[cGAS Inhibitors\|cGAS Inhibitors]] | Experimental small molecules that suppress cGAS enzymatic activity to dampen cGAS-STING–driven neuroinflammation. |
| [[cGAS-STING Pathway\|cGAS-STING Pathway]] | The cGAS-STING complex is an innate immune sensor that detects cytoplasmic DNA and activates inflammatory interferon signalling, playing a crucial role in SASP activation and age-related inflammation. |
| [[Charcot-Marie-Tooth Disease Type 2A\|Charcot-Marie-Tooth Disease Type 2A]] | Autosomal dominant axonal neuropathy caused by MFN2 mutations impairing axonal mitochondrial transport. |
| [[Checkpoint Inhibitor\|Checkpoint Inhibitor]] | A class of cancer immunotherapeutics (notably anti-CTLA-4, anti-PD-1, anti-PD-L1 agents) that block inhibitory immune-checkpoint receptors to reinvigorate T-cell anti-tumor immunity. |
| [[Chemokines\|Chemokines]] | Chemokines are a family of small chemotactic cytokines that direct immune cell migration and are key components of the senescence-associated secretory phenotype (SASP). |
| [[Chemoresistance\|Chemoresistance]] | Resistance of cancer cells to chemotherapy; in the apigenin study, senescence-associated secretory phenotype (SASP) factors from therapy-damaged stromal cells confer chemoresistance that apigenin reverses. |
| [[Chemotherapy\|Chemotherapy]] | Chemotherapy refers to pharmacological treatment of Cancer using cytotoxic or cytostatic drugs that target rapidly dividing cells. Major classes include Alkylating Agents, Antimetabolites, Topoisom... |
| [[Chemotherapy-Induced Peripheral Neuropathy\|Chemotherapy-Induced Peripheral Neuropathy]] | Chemotherapy-induced peripheral neuropathy (CIPN) is a dose-limiting toxic effect of taxane and platinum agents, driven partly by SARM1-dependent axonal NAD+ depletion and targetable by SARM1 inhibitors. |
| [[CHK1\|CHK1]] | CHK1 (CHEK1) is the CMGC-family serine/threonine checkpoint kinase activated by ATR at stalled replication forks; it delays cell-cycle progression by inhibiting CDC25 phosphatases and activating WEE1, and is a major oncology drug target. |
| [[CHK2\|CHK2]] | CHK2 (Checkpoint Kinase 2, CHEK2) is a Serine/Threonine Kinase that functions as a DNA Damage checkpoint protein in the Cell Cycle. It is activated by ATM in response to Double-Strand Breaks (DSBs)... |
| [[Chloride\|Chloride]] | Chloride (Cl-) is the dominant extracellular anion and a physiological inhibitor of the oxygen-sensitive prolyl hydroxylase enzymes that gate HIF-1alpha degradation. It is also the required co-substrate for myeloperoxidase-derived hypochlorous acid. |
| [[Chloroquine\|Chloroquine]] | Chloroquine is an autophagy inhibitor and antimalarial drug that blocks autophagosome-lysosome fusion by raising lysosomal pH; it is used in combination cancer therapy and is a rare trigger of methemoglobinemia, with higher risk in G6PD deficiency. |
| [[Chlorpromazine\|Chlorpromazine]] | Chlorpromazine is the first antipsychotic phenothiazine; its position-10 side chain destroys methylene blue-like mitochondrial electron-transfer activity, leaving only weak, pro-oxidative neuroprotection. |
| [[Cholesterol\|Cholesterol]] | A sterol lipid essential for cell membrane integrity, steroid hormone synthesis, and signaling microdomains; elevated levels drive atherosclerosis; oxidation yields oxysterols and oxidized LDL. |
| [[Cholesterol Efflux\|Cholesterol Efflux]] | The process by which excess cholesterol is removed from cells (especially macrophages) to lipid acceptors like apoA-1 and HDL; first step in reverse cholesterol transport. |
| [[Cholesterol Influx\|Cholesterol Influx]] | The movement of cholesterol from the extracellular environment into cells; opposite of cholesterol efflux; reduced by resveratrol. |
| [[Choline\|Choline]] | Choline is an essential quaternary ammonium nutrient that serves as the precursor for phosphatidylcholine and sphingomyelin synthesis, for acetylcholine, and — via betaine — as a methyl donor that regenerates methionine in the hepatic methylation cycle. |
| [[Chondrocytes\|Chondrocytes]] | The resident cells of cartilage responsible for synthesizing and maintaining the extracellular matrix. |
| [[CHOP\|CHOP]] | CHOP (DDIT3, GADD153) is a stress-inducible bZIP transcription factor of the C/EBP family, induced downstream of eIF2alpha phosphorylation and ATF4, that couples ER stress to apoptosis by repressing survival genes and activating pro-death targets. |
| [[ChREBP\|ChREBP]] | Carbohydrate-responsive element-binding protein, a lipogenic transcription factor activated by glucose that drives de novo lipogenesis |
| [[Christensenellaceae\|Christensenellaceae]] | Christensenellaceae is a family of bacteria in the phylum Firmicutes, found to be highly heritable and associated with a lean body mass index and healthy aging. |
| [[Chromaffin cells\|Chromaffin cells]] | Neuroendocrine cells of the adrenal medulla and sympathetic ganglia that synthesize, store, and secrete catecholamines into the bloodstream. |
| [[Chromatin\|Chromatin]] | Chromatin is the complex of DNA and proteins (primarily histones) that packages the genome within the cell nucleus. In young, healthy cells, chromatin is organized into a precise landscape of t... |
| [[Chromatin Modifiers\|Chromatin Modifiers]] | Proteins that establish and maintain chromatin states (sirtuins, HDACs, PARP1, writers/readers/erasers) and relocalize to DNA repair sites in the RCM hypothesis. |
| [[Chromatin Remodeling\|Chromatin Remodeling]] | Definition: Chromatin Remodeling is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways... |
| [[Chromosome\|Chromosome]] | Packaged DNA-protein structure carrying genetic information; segregated during mitosis and meiosis. |
| [[Chronic Fatigue Syndrome (CFS)\|Chronic Fatigue Syndrome (CFS)]] | A complex, debilitating disorder (also myalgic encephalomyelitis) defined by profound fatigue lasting >6 months, post-exertional malaise, and cognitive, sleep, and pain disturbances of unknown etiology. |
| [[Chronic Inflammation\|Chronic Inflammation]] | Sustained, non-resolving inflammatory signaling lasting weeks to years, driven by persisting triggers and defective resolution programs; it is a core mechanism of inflammaging, atherosclerosis, autoimmune disease, and inflammation-driven tumorigenesis. |
| [[Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia\|Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia]] | Chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL), the most common adult leukemia in Western populations, characterized by accumulation of mature CD5+ B cells; SIRT3-mediated ROS buffering contributes to chemoresistance. |
| [[Chrysin\|Chrysin]] | A natural flavone (5,7-dihydroxyflavone) found in honey and propolis with antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and anticancer properties that synergize with apigenin. |
| [[Cigarette Smoke\|Cigarette Smoke]] | A complex inhalant mixture of free radicals and oxidants that raises tissue oxidant load and impairs sirtuin-mediated anti-inflammatory defenses. |
| [[Circadian Rhythm\|Circadian Rhythm]] | ~24h endogenous oscillator driven by BMAL1/CLOCK; gated by SIRT1 via circadian NAD+ (NAMPT) oscillations. |
| [[CISD1\|CISD1]] | CISD1 (mitoNEET) is a mitochondrial outer-membrane iron-sulfur protein that protects against ferroptosis by limiting mitochondrial iron and lipid ROS. |
| [[CISD2\|CISD2]] | CISD2 is a mitochondrial iron-sulfur protein that suppresses ferroptosis; its loss raises mitochondrial iron and lipid ROS, promoting ferroptosis in head and neck cancer. |
| [[Cisplatin\|Cisplatin]] | Cisplatin is a platinum-based chemotherapy drug widely used to treat various types of Cancer, whose therapeutic efficacy can be enhanced by Intermittent Fasting. |
| [[cit-H3\|cit-H3]] | Citrullinated histone H3 — the PAD4-generated immunodetection signature of NETosis, read together with extracellular DNA and MPO–DNA complexes. |
| [[Citrate\|Citrate]] | Tricarboxylic acid intermediate of the TCA cycle and substrate for mitochondrial citrate carrier SLC25A1. Exported to the cytosol, citrate is converted by ACLY to acetyl-CoA for lipid synthesis and histone acetylation. |
| [[Citrate Synthase\|Citrate Synthase]] | The first and rate-limiting enzyme of the tricarboxylic acid (TCA) cycle, catalysing condensation of acetyl-CoA with oxaloacetate to form citrate. Citrate synthase activity is a marker of mitochondrial content and is induced in muscle by endurance training. |
| [[Citric Acid Cycle\|Citric Acid Cycle]] | The central mitochondrial metabolic pathway (Krebs/TCA cycle) that generates NADH and FADH2 for OXPHOS; its dysregulation contributes to the altered NAD+/NADH ratio and bioenergetic defects of senescent cells. |
| [[CK2\|CK2]] | Casein kinase 2 is a ubiquitous serine/threonine protein kinase that restrains NF-κB and senescence. |
| [[Cl-amidine\|Cl-amidine]] | A pan-peptidylarginine deiminase inhibitor that blocks PAD4-mediated histone citrullination and NET release — the pharmacological probe for PAD4-dependent NETosis. |
| [[Clathrin\|Clathrin]] | Clathrin is a three-legged scaffold protein that polymerises into the lattice of clathrin-coated pits and vesicles, the entry route for receptor-mediated endocytosis at the plasma membrane and at endosomes. |
| [[Clear cell renal cell carcinoma\|Clear cell renal cell carcinoma]] | Clear cell renal cell carcinoma (ccRCC) is a kidney cancer exquisitely dependent on glutathione/GPX4 defenses, making it highly sensitive to ferroptosis induction. |
| [[CLEAR Network\|CLEAR Network]] | The Coordinated Lysosomal Expression and Regulation network, a transcriptional program centered on TFEB/TFE3 that coordinates lysosomal biogenesis and autophagy gene expression. |
| [[CLOCK\|CLOCK]] | Core circadian clock transcription factor; heterodimerizes with BMAL1 to drive rhythmic gene expression, itself a SIRT1 deacetylation target. |
| [[Clofarabine\|Clofarabine]] | Clofarabine (Clolar/Evoltra) is a second-generation purine nucleoside antimetabolite FDA-approved in 2004 for relapsed or refractory pediatric acute lymphoblastic leukemia; its triphosphate inhibits DNA polymerases and chain-terminates DNA, while its diphosphate inhibits ribonucleotide reductase (IC50 ~65 nM), depleting dNTP pools and driving apoptosis. |
| [[Clonal Hematopoiesis\|Clonal Hematopoiesis]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Clonal Hematopoiesis is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic... |
| [[Clostridium sporogenes\|Clostridium sporogenes]] | Anaerobic, spore-forming gut commensal and the prototypical auxotrophic tryptophan fermenter; primary producer of indole-3-propionic acid (IPA) in the mammalian gut. |
| [[CML\|CML]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: CML is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, environmental, ... |
| [[CMS121\|CMS121]] | A fatty-acid-synthase (FASN) inhibitor from the Maher lab that protects against lipid peroxidation/inflammation and alleviates cognitive loss in Alzheimer's-disease models; reached Phase 1 for AD. |
| [[CNKSR2\|CNKSR2]] | CNKSR2 (Connector Enhancer of Kinase Suppressor of Ras 2) is an X-linked synaptic Ras/MAPK pathway regulator and putative tumor suppressor enriched for loss-of-function mutations in male cancers. |
| [[Cobalamin\|Cobalamin]] | Cobalamin is the cobalt corrinoid vitamin B12, an essential cofactor for methylmalonyl-CoA mutase and methionine synthase. It is synthesised exclusively by bacteria and archaea, and its only human cofactor roles are one-carbon remethylation and rearrangements of 1,2-carbon skeletons. |
| [[Cocaine\|Cocaine]] | Cocaine is an alkaloid stimulant and Schedule II drug that blocks reuptake at the DAT, NET and SERT with roughly equal affinity and additionally acts as a use-dependent sodium-channel blocker; the monoamine effect produces reward and the sodium-channel effect produces cardiotoxicity. |
| [[Cochlea\|Cochlea]] | Spiral-shaped auditory organ of the inner ear that converts sound vibrations into neural signals via hair cells; a site of cumulative oxidative damage in age-related hearing loss and a target of SIRT3-dependent protection. |
| [[Coenzyme A\|Coenzyme A]] | Coenzyme A is the universal thiol-containing acyl carrier of metabolism, a cofactor for over 100 enzyme reactions including acetyl-CoA generation, fatty acid synthesis and oxidation, and the TCA cycle. It is synthesised from pantothenic acid and degraded by pantothenate kinase. |
| [[Cognition\|Cognition]] | Cognition refers to the mental action or process of acquiring knowledge and understanding through thought, experience, and the senses. |
| [[Cognitive Decline\|Cognitive Decline]] | Age-related reduction in cognitive function, associated with inflammaging, neurodegeneration, and chronic inflammation. |
| [[Cognitive Performance\|Cognitive Performance]] | Definition: Cognitive Performance is a scientific concept that describes fundamental principles governing biological or chemical systems. |
| [[COL1A1\|COL1A1]] | Collagen type I alpha 1 chain gene, the principal fibrillar collagen subunit of the extracellular matrix in myocardium and other tissues; a pro-fibrotic target suppressed by SIRT3-mediated deacetylation. |
| [[COL3A1\|COL3A1]] | COL3A1 encodes the alpha-1 chain of type III collagen, a homotrimeric fibrillar collagen of the reticular fibres of hollow organs; dominant mutations cause vascular Ehlers-Danlos syndrome. |
| [[COL5A2\|COL5A2]] | COL5A2 encodes the alpha-2 chain of type V collagen, a low-abundance fibrillar collagen that nucleates and regulates the diameter of type I fibrils; dominant mutations cause classical Ehlers-Danlos syndrome. |
| [[Colchicine\|Colchicine]] | Alkaloid from Colchicum autumnale that inhibits tubulin polymerization; used in gout and as a microtubule research tool. |
| [[Collagen\|Collagen]] | Collagen is the main structural protein in the extracellular matrix of various connective tissues in the body. |
| [[Colloidal Silver\|Colloidal Silver]] | Colloidal silver is a suspension of silver nanoparticles in water, marketed as a broad-spectrum antimicrobial with no approved therapeutic indication. Its antimicrobial plausibility has not been supported by clinical evidence, and chronic ingestion causes irreversible argyria. |
| [[Colorectal Cancer\|Colorectal Cancer]] | Colorectal cancer (CRC) is a common malignant tumor affecting the colon or rectum. |
| [[Complement System\|Complement System]] | The complement system is a network of roughly 30 soluble and membrane-bound proteins that provides the fastest-acting antimicrobial defence, links innate and adaptive immunity, and is a major driver of inflammatory and autoimmune disease when dysregulated. |
| [[Complex I\|Complex I]] | Complex I (NADH:ubiquinone oxidoreductase) is the first and largest enzyme complex of the mitochondrial respiratory chain. |
| [[Complex II\|Complex II]] | Succinate dehydrogenase, the only TCA-cycle enzyme also embedded in the electron transport chain, oxidizing succinate and feeding electrons to ubiquinone. |
| [[Complex III\|Complex III]] | Complex III (cytochrome bc1 complex, ubiquinol-cytochrome c oxidoreductase) is the third complex of the Electron Transport Chain in the inner Mitochondria membrane. It catalyzes the transfer of ele... |
| [[Complex IV\|Complex IV]] | Complex IV (cytochrome c oxidase) is the terminal enzyme of the Mitochondria electron transport chain. It catalyzes the transfer of electrons from cytochrome c to oxygen, reducing O₂ to H₂O and building the proton gradient. |
| [[COMT Inhibitors\|COMT Inhibitors]] | Chemical properties: COMT Inhibitors is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[Condensin\|Condensin]] | The SMC2-SMC4 condensin complex that binds B-to-A compartment-switching regions in senescent cells, where its enrichment coincides with upregulation of SASP genes. |
| [[Conjugated Dienes\|Conjugated Dienes]] | Products of lipid peroxidation formed during LDL and HDL oxidation; markers of oxidative stress; measured at 234 nm absorbance. |
| [[Connective Tissue Growth Factor\|Connective Tissue Growth Factor]] | CTGF is a matricellular protein and key SASP factor that promotes fibrosis, tumor progression, and extracellular matrix remodeling |
| [[COPD\|COPD]] | Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD) is a common chronic respiratory disease characterized by persistent respiratory symptoms and progressive airflow obstruction. It is the fourth leadi... |
| [[COPI\|COPI]] | COPI is the heptameric coatomer complex that mediates retrograde vesicular transport from the Golgi to the endoplasmic reticulum and within Golgi cisternae, recruited to membranes by Arf1 GTP and selected for dilysine-tagged cargo. |
| [[Copper\|Copper]] | Copper is an essential trace metal cofactor for oxidases including cytochrome c oxidase and superoxide dismutase, and a transition-metal catalyst for free radical generation via the Fenton-type Haber-Weiss reaction. |
| [[Coronary Artery Disease\|Coronary Artery Disease]] | Narrowing or blockage of the coronary arteries, typically from atherosclerosis, leading to myocardial ischemia and infarction |
| [[Coronary Heart Disease\|Coronary Heart Disease]] | Coronary Heart Disease (CHD), also known as Coronary Artery Disease (CAD), is a primary manifestation of Cardiovascular Disease. Epigenetics mechanisms serve as a critical bridge between... |
| [[Cortactin\|Cortactin]] | A Src-family kinase substrate encoded by CTTN on chromosome 11q13 that binds F-actin and the Arp2/3 complex, stabilising branched actin networks at cell protrusions and thereby driving migration, invadopodia formation and membrane trafficking. |
