| [[Cortisol\|Cortisol]] | The primary glucocorticoid stress hormone released by the adrenal cortex that mobilizes energy and, when chronically elevated, suppresses HRV and recovery. |
| [[Coumadin\|Coumadin]] | The Bristol-Myers Squibb brand name for warfarin sodium, the oral coumarin-derived vitamin K antagonist that inhibits VKORC1 and blocks gamma-carboxylation of the vitamin K-dependent clotting factors; the branded product was discontinued in 2020 and generic warfarin remains available. |
| [[COVID-19\|COVID-19]] | Infectious respiratory disease caused by SARS-CoV-2, ranging from asymptomatic infection to severe pneumonia, ARDS, and multi-organ involvement |
| [[COX-2\|COX-2]] | Catalytic function: COX-2 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[CPEO\|CPEO]] | Chronic progressive external ophthalmoplegia, a mitochondrial myopathy associated with mtDNA abnormalities |
| [[CpG Island\|CpG Island]] | CpG islands are regions of the genome with a high frequency of CpG sites (cytosine-guanine dinucleotides), often located in the promoter regions of genes. |
| [[CpG Islands\|CpG Islands]] | Genomic regions rich in unmethylated CpG dinucleotides; mitochondrial DNA contains unmethylated CpG islands recognized by TLR9, linking mtDNA release in senescence to innate immune SASP activation. |
| [[CPT1\|CPT1]] | CPT1 (carnitine palmitoyltransferase 1) is the outer-mitochondrial-membrane enzyme that transfers long-chain acyl groups to carnitine, the rate-limiting and allosterically malonyl-CoA-regulated gatekeeper of mitochondrial fatty acid beta-oxidation. |
| [[CPT2\|CPT2]] | Carnitine palmitoyltransferase 2, a mitochondrial inner-membrane enzyme that regenerates acyl-CoA from acylcarnitine in the matrix, the second step of the carnitine shuttle enabling long-chain fatty acid β-oxidation; a SIRT5 desuccinylation target linked to cardiac metabolism. |
| [[Creatine\|Creatine]] | Creatine is a guanidino compound synthesized from arginine, glycine, and S-adenosylmethionine that buffers cellular ATP via the phosphocreatine system; its synthesis consumes ~40% of body methyl groups. |
| [[Creatine Phosphate Shuttle\|Creatine Phosphate Shuttle]] | The creatine phosphate (phosphocreatine/creatine kinase) shuttle is a subcellular energy-transport system that buffers and redistributes ATP from mitochondria to sites of high ATP consumption via phosphocreatine diffusion. |
| [[Creatine Supplementation\|Creatine Supplementation]] | Exogenous creatine monohydrate intake — the most evidence-backed ergogenic and neuroprotective supplement; augments the phosphocreatine energy buffer for exercise performance, sarcopenia, and neurological disease support. |
| [[Creatine Synthesis Deficiency\|Creatine Synthesis Deficiency]] | A group of rare inborn errors of metabolism caused by mutations in enzymes or transporters of the creatine biosynthesis/transport pathway (AGAT, GAMT, SLC6A8), resulting in depleted cerebral creatine and a spectrum of neurological impairment. |
| [[Creatine Transporters (CRTR)\|Creatine Transporters (CRTR)]] | Sodium- and chloride-dependent transporters of the SLC6 family (notably SLC6A8) that mediate cellular uptake of creatine across plasma and blood-brain barriers; their deficiency causes cerebral creatine depletion. |
| [[Creatinine\|Creatinine]] | A breakdown product of creatine phosphate in muscle, produced at a relatively constant rate and excreted by the kidneys; serum creatinine is the most widely used clinical biomarker of renal function. |
| [[Cristae\|Cristae]] | Cristae, the inner membrane invaginations of mitochondria where oxidative phosphorylation complexes are organized |
| [[Crohn's Disease\|Crohn's Disease]] | A chronic, relapsing, transmural inflammatory bowel disease that can affect any part of the gastrointestinal tract; the intestinal pathology most closely resembling the spontaneous RIPK3-dependent necroptosis seen when FADD/caspase-8 are deleted from intestinal epithelium. |
| [[Cross-linking\|Cross-linking]] | Protein cross-linking is the covalent bridging of adjacent protein molecules — accelerated by glycation and Advanced Glycation End Products — that stiffens the extracellular matrix and is proposed to drive aging and diabetic complications. |
| [[Crown-like Structures\|Crown-like Structures]] | Histological structures in obese adipose tissue formed by macrophages surrounding dead or dying adipocytes; hallmark of adipose tissue inflammation |
| [[CRTC2\|CRTC2]] | CREB-regulated transcriptional coactivator 2 (CRTC2, also TORC2) is a coactivator that drives hepatic gluconeogenesis; it is regulated by phosphorylation (SIK/AMPK) and by sirtuin deacetylation. |
| [[CtIP\|CtIP]] | C-terminal binding protein interacting protein (RBBP8); a DNA-end resection factor required for homologous recombination repair of DNA double-strand breaks; deacetylated by SIRT6. |
| [[CTLA-4\|CTLA-4]] | Inhibitory T-cell checkpoint competing with CD28 for CD80/CD86; target of ipilimumab immunotherapy. |
| [[CTPI2\|CTPI2]] | Third-generation pharmacological inhibitor of the mitochondrial citrate carrier SLC25A1 with high binding affinity and specificity. Suppresses the SASP in vitro and in aged mice, improving frailty, muscle function, and healthspan without clearing senescent cells. |
| [[Curcumin\|Curcumin]] | Curcumin is a polyphenolic compound derived from _Curcuma longa_ (turmeric) with pleiotropic biological activities including Histone Acetyltransferase (HAT) inhibition, NF-κB suppression, and antio... |
| [[CUT&Tag\|CUT&Tag]] | CUT&Tag (Cleavage Under Targets and Tagmentation) is a method for genome-wide mapping of Histone Modifications, Transcription Factor binding, and Chromatin-associated proteins using an antibody... |
| [[CXCL1\|CXCL1]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 1, an ELR+ SASP chemokine that binds CXCR2 and recruits neutrophils, amplifying senescence paracrine signaling. |
| [[CXCL10\|CXCL10]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 10 (IP-10), an IFN-inducible ELR− SASP chemokine that binds CXCR3 and recruits NK cells and T cells for immune surveillance. |
| [[CXCL11\|CXCL11]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 11 (I-TAC), an IFN-inducible ELR− SASP chemokine that binds CXCR3 and promotes pro-tumorigenic paracrine signaling from senescent endothelial cells. |
| [[CXCL12\|CXCL12]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 12 (SDF-1), a homeostatic ELR− SASP chemokine that binds CXCR4/CXCR7 and cooperates with MMPs to drive cancer metastasis. |
| [[CXCL14\|CXCL14]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 14 (BRAK), a homeostatic chemokine of the early p53/p21/Rb-dependent SASP that recruits myeloid and NK cells for immunosurveillance. |
| [[CXCL2\|CXCL2]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 2, an ELR+ SASP chemokine that binds CXCR2 and recruits neutrophils, reinforcing senescence in liver and pancreas. |
| [[CXCL3\|CXCL3]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 3, an ELR+ SASP chemokine that binds CXCR2 and recruits neutrophils, reinforcing senescence in liver and pancreas. |
| [[CXCL5\|CXCL5]] | C-X-C motif chemokine ligand 5 (ENA-78), an ELR+ SASP chemokine that binds CXCR2 and contributes to reproductive aging and neutrophil-driven inflammation. |
| [[CXCR2\|CXCR2]] | C-X-C chemokine receptor 2, the principal receptor for ELR+ CXC chemokines (CXCL1-3/5-8) that mediates neutrophil recruitment and reinforces paracrine senescence. |
| [[CXCR3\|CXCR3]] | C-X-C chemokine receptor 3, the receptor for ELR− CXC chemokines CXCL9/10/11 that recruits T and NK cells for SASP immune surveillance and can promote cancer. |
| [[CXCR4\|CXCR4]] | C-X-C chemokine receptor type 4 (CD184), the principal receptor for CXCL12 (SDF-1), implicated in stem cell homing, cancer metastasis, HIV entry, and vascular aging. |
| [[CxxC Motif\|CxxC Motif]] | The CxxC (Cys-X-X-Cys) motif is a conserved redox-active tetrapeptide — two cysteines separated by two variable residues — that forms the catalytic core of thioredoxin-fold oxidoreductases such as thioredoxins, glutaredoxins, and protein disulfide isomerases, mediating thiol-disulfide exchange and dithiol-disulfide redox catalysis. |
| [[Cyanosis\|Cyanosis]] | A bluish or grayish discoloration of skin and mucous membranes caused by increased deoxygenated or abnormal hemoglobin in the blood. |
| [[Cyclic Dinucleotide\|Cyclic Dinucleotide]] | Second messengers (e.g., 2′3′-cGAMP) and STING agonists that activate STING to drive type I interferon responses. |
| [[Cyclic Guanosine Monophosphate\|Cyclic Guanosine Monophosphate]] | Cyclic guanosine monophosphate (cGMP) is a second messenger generated by guanylyl cyclases downstream of nitric oxide, mediating vasodilation, platelet inhibition, and smooth-muscle relaxation. |
| [[Cyclin A\|Cyclin A]] | An S/G2-phase cyclin that drives entry into and progression through S phase with CDK2; its mRNA is stabilized by HuR, and loss of cytosolic HuR during senescence represses Cyclin A translation, aiding proliferative arrest. |
| [[Cyclin B\|Cyclin B]] | The G2/M cyclin that partners CDK1 to drive mitotic entry; its mRNA is stabilized by HuR, and loss of cytosolic HuR during senescence represses Cyclin B translation, contributing to cell-cycle arrest. |
| [[Cyclin D\|Cyclin D]] | Cyclin D (CCND1/CCND2/CCND3) is the family of G1/S-specific regulatory subunits that activate CDK4 and CDK6 to phosphorylate Retinoblastoma Protein, release E2F, and commit cells to S phase; CCDN1 amplification and overexpression are among the most common oncogenic lesions in human cancer. |
| [[Cyclin E\|Cyclin E]] | G1/S cyclin that partners with CDK2 to drive S-phase entry; frequently amplified in cancer. |
| [[Cyclodextrin\|Cyclodextrin]] | Torus-shaped oligosaccharides that form inclusion complexes to solubilize hydrophobic molecules. |
| [[Cyclodextrins\|Cyclodextrins]] | Cyclodextrins are cyclic oligosaccharides with a hydrophobic cavity and hydrophilic exterior that form inclusion complexes, enhancing the solubility and bioavailability of lipophilic guests such as fisetin. |
| [[Cyclooxygenase\|Cyclooxygenase]] | Cyclooxygenase (COX, prostaglandin-endoperoxide synthase, PTGS) is the heme peroxidase family that converts arachidonic acid to prostaglandin H2, committing it to the prostanoid branch of eicosanoid metabolism. COX-1 is constitutive and supports gastric cytoprotection and platelet aggregation; COX-2 is largely inducible and drives inflammatory prostaglandin synthesis, which is why NSAIDs are both analgesic and ulcerogenic. |
| [[Cyclophilin A\|Cyclophilin A]] | Cyclophilin A (PPIA) is a peptidyl-prolyl cis-trans isomerase and secreted pro-inflammatory factor; in parthanatos it is proposed to form an AIF-cyclophilin A-H2AX complex that degrades DNA. |
| [[Cyclophilin D\|Cyclophilin D]] | Cyclophilin D (CypD) is a mitochondrial matrix peptidyl-prolyl isomerase that serves as the primary positive regulator of the mitochondrial permeability transition pore (mPTP), playing a key role in calcium homeostasis, cell death, and senescence. |
| [[Cyclosporine A\|Cyclosporine A]] | Immunosuppressant that potently inhibits the mitochondrial permeability transition via cyclophilin D binding. |
| [[CYLD\|CYLD]] | The K63/M1 deubiquitinase cylindromatosis that strips ubiquitin from RIPK1, TRAF2 and TRAF6 — the enzymatic switch converting TNFR1 complex I survival signaling into complex II death signaling. |
| [[CYP2C9\|CYP2C9]] | Catalytic function: CYP2C9 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[CYP2D6\|CYP2D6]] | Catalytic function: CYP2D6 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[CYP3A4\|CYP3A4]] | Catalytic function: CYP3A4 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Cystatin C\|Cystatin C]] | Cysteine protease inhibitor and kidney-function biomarker integrated into GrimAge epigenetic clocks. |
| [[Cysteine\|Cysteine]] | Cysteine is a semi-essential proteinogenic amino acid with a reactive thiol group, serving as the rate-limiting precursor for glutathione synthesis. |
| [[Cytochrome b5 reductase\|Cytochrome b5 reductase]] | - Methemoglobinemia: Caused by a deficiency in this enzyme. |
| [[Cytokine Storm\|Cytokine Storm]] | Systemic hyperinflammatory syndrome in which innate immune cells release pro-inflammatory cytokines (IL-6, IL-1, TNF-alpha, IFN-gamma) at levels that overwhelm normal regulatory feedback, driving endothelial activation, capillary leak, hypotension and multiorgan failure. Classically described in severe sepsis, cytokine release syndrome after CAR-T or checkpoint blockade, and in viral infections such as COVID-19. |
| [[Cytokines\|Cytokines]] | Cytokines are small signaling proteins released by cells, especially immune cells such as Microglia and Lymphocytes, that mediate intercellular communication during inflammation and immune responses. |
| [[Cytokinesis\|Cytokinesis]] | Cytokinesis is the physical division of the cytoplasm that completes cell division, executed by an actomyosin contractile ring constricting the cleavage furrow followed by ESCRT-mediated abscission of the intercellular bridge. |
| [[Cytoplasmic Chromatin Fragments\|Cytoplasmic Chromatin Fragments]] | Cytoplasmic chromatin fragments (CCFs) are nuclear DNA fragments released into the cytosol of senescent cells due to Lamin B1 downregulation and nuclear envelope collapse, serving as key ligands for cGAS-STING-dependent SASP activation. |
| [[Cytoskeleton\|Cytoskeleton]] | Actin, microtubule, and intermediate-filament networks governing shape, adhesion, and migration. |
| [[Cytosol\|Cytosol]] | The cytosol is the aqueous intracellular fluid that fills the cytoplasmic compartment of Eukaryotic Cells, excluding the contents of membrane-bound organelles such as the Nucleus, Mitochondria,... |
| [[CZ-48\|CZ-48]] | CZ-48 (sulfo-ara-F-NMN) is a cell-permeant NMN analog that exhibits opposite pharmacological effects on the two major NAD+ glycohydrolases — it activates SARM1 while inhibiting CD38 — making it a unique probe of the NAD+ consumer network. |
| [[D-dimer\|D-dimer]] | A cross-linked fibrin degradation product formed when plasmin degrades fibrin that has been cross-linked by factor XIII. Its plasma concentration is a marker of simultaneous coagulation and fibrinolysis and is the most-used laboratory test to exclude venous thromboembolism, at the cost of very poor positive predictive value. |
| [[DACH1\|DACH1]] | Dachshund homolog 1, a transcriptional regulator gene; a long noncoding RNA at the DACH1 locus interacts with SIRT3 to regulate mitochondrial oxidative damage and cell death in heart failure. |
| [[DAI\|DAI]] | RHIM-containing DNA sensor identical to ZBP1 that nucleates a RIPK1-independent necrosome with RIPK3. |
| [[Dalteparin\|Dalteparin]] | A low-molecular-weight heparin fragment given subcutaneously as a once- or twice-daily anticoagulant for venous thromboembolism prophylaxis and treatment. Its longer half-life and predictable, weight-based non-monitored dosing distinguish it from unfractionated heparin, and the CLOT trial made it the preferred first-line agent for extended VTE treatment in cancer. |
| [[Damage-Associated Molecular Patterns\|Damage-Associated Molecular Patterns]] | Endogenous danger signals (e.g., mtDNA, cardiolipin, TFAM) released by stressed/dying cells that activate innate immunity; in senescence, mitochondrial DAMPs drive cGAS-STING/TLR9/NLRP3 signaling and the SASP. |
| [[DAPK\|DAPK]] | Death-associated protein kinase (DAPK) is the calcium/calmodulin-dependent Ser/Thr kinase family DAPK1, DAPK2/DRP-1, and DAPK3/ZIP-kinase. DAPK1 is a tumour suppressor that promotes apoptosis and autophagy, is frequently silenced by promoter hypermethylation, and phosphorylates RIPK1, Beclin1, TSC2, and Pin1. |
| [[Dasatinib\|Dasatinib]] | Chemical properties: Dasatinib is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[DAT\|DAT]] | Overview: DAT is a membrane transport protein that facilitates the movement of molecules across biological membranes. It mediates substrate translocation through conformational changes driven b... |
| [[Dauer Larva\|Dauer Larva]] | An alternative, non-feeding, stress-resistant third-stage larval state of Caenorhabditis elegans entered when conditions are poor. Dauer entry is a rich and reversible state with a thickened cuticle, arrested development and altered metabolism, made possible by the skin and the ability to store and re-use internal energy. |
| [[DAXX\|DAXX]] | DAXX (Death Domain Associated Protein) is the obligate H3.3 chaperone partner of ATRX for telomeric and pericentric heterochromatin deposition. |
| [[DdCBE\|DdCBE]] | DddA-derived cytosine base editors, a class of programmable base-editing tools that make targeted C-to-T edits in mitochondrial DNA without creating double-strand breaks; an emerging approach to correct pathogenic mtDNA mutations. |
| [[DDX3X\|DDX3X]] | DDX3X (DEAD-box helicase 3 X-linked) is an X-linked RNA helicase involved in translation initiation and mRNA splicing; recurrently mutated in cancer with male-enriched loss-of-function mutations. |
| [[DDX3Y\|DDX3Y]] | DDX3Y (DEAD-box helicase 3 Y-linked) is the Y homolog of DDX3X with overlapping but non-identical RNA-helicase functions and partial tumor-suppressor compensation. |
| [[Deep Vein Thrombosis\|Deep Vein Thrombosis]] | Deep vein thrombosis is clot formation in a deep vein, most often below the knee, arising where venous stasis, endothelial injury and hypercoagulability converge; it is the primary cause of pulmonary embolism and a major preventable cause of morbidity and mortality. |
| [[Deferoxamine\|Deferoxamine]] | Deferoxamine (DFO) is an iron-chelating drug that inhibits ferroptosis by depriving cells of the labile iron required for Fenton-driven lipid peroxidation. |
| [[Deiodinase\|Deiodinase]] | Iodothyronine deiodinases (DIO1–DIO3) are selenium-dependent selenoenzymes that activate and inactivate thyroid hormone by removing specific iodine atoms, controlling local triiodothyronine availability. |
| [[DELE1\|DELE1]] | Death ligand signal enhancer 1, a mitochondrial stress sensor that relays inner-membrane stress to the cytosolic integrated stress response via HRI. |
| [[Dendritic cells\|Dendritic cells]] | Professional antigen-presenting cells that activate naive T lymphocytes; their activity is enhanced by low-dose mTOR inhibition, improving vaccine and anti-tumor immunity. |
| [[Denham Harman\|Denham Harman]] | American biochemist who proposed the free radical theory of aging in 1955 and later the mitochondrial free radical theory of aging in 1972. |
| [[Deoxycholic Acid\|Deoxycholic Acid]] | Deoxycholic acid (DCA) is a secondary bile acid produced by gut bacterial metabolism that induces cellular senescence in hepatic stellate cells, linking obesity-associated gut dysbiosis to hepatocellular carcinoma. |
| [[Deoxycytidine Kinase\|Deoxycytidine Kinase]] | Deoxycytidine kinase (DCK, dCK) is the rate-limiting nucleoside kinase that phosphorylates deoxycytidine and its analogs, most importantly gemcitabine and cytarabine; low DCK expression is a well-established mechanism of acquired gemcitabine resistance. |
| [[Depression\|Depression]] | Depression (major depressive disorder) is a common and serious mood disorder characterized by persistent sadness, loss of interest, and impaired function. It has complex neurobiological underpi... |
| [[Dextromethorphan\|Dextromethorphan]] | Dextromethorphan is a cough-suppressant with serotonergic (NMDA-antagonist) activity; risky with MAO inhibitors. |
| [[DHPS\|DHPS]] | Deoxyhypusine synthase, the NAD-dependent enzyme that performs the first step of hypusine biosynthesis, transferring the 4-aminobutyl moiety of spermidine to a specific lysine (Lys50) on eukaryotic translation initiation factor 5A to form deoxyhypusine. Biallelic loss-of-function variants cause a neurodevelopmental disorder with seizures and hypotonia. |
| [[Diabetes Mellitus\|Diabetes Mellitus]] | Diabetes Mellitus is a group of metabolic diseases characterized by high blood sugar levels (Hyperglycemia) over a prolonged period. Chronic hyperglycemia leads to increased production of Reactive ... |
| [[Diabetic Cardiomyopathy\|Diabetic Cardiomyopathy]] | Cardiac dysfunction occurring in diabetic patients independent of coronary artery disease and hypertension, driven by mitochondrial dysfunction, oxidative stress, and metabolic inflexibility. |
| [[Diabetic nephropathy\|Diabetic nephropathy]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Diabetic nephropathy is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic... |
| [[Diabetic Neuropathy\|Diabetic Neuropathy]] | Most prevalent complication of diabetes, a progressive disorder of peripheral nerves caused by chronic hyperglycemia |
| [[Diabetic Retinopathy\|Diabetic Retinopathy]] | Microvascular complication of diabetes causing damage to the retinal capillaries, leading to capillary apoptosis, neovascularization, and vision loss |
| [[Diamine Oxidase\|Diamine Oxidase]] | Diamine oxidase (DAO) is the intestinal enzyme that degrades dietary histamine. Aminoguanidine potently inhibits DAO, causing histamine intolerance when taken orally. |
| [[Dicer\|Dicer]] | Cytoplasmic RNase III enzyme that cleaves pre-miRNA hairpins and long dsRNA into mature miRNA/siRNA duplexes for loading onto Argonaute in RISC. |
| [[Dimethyl fumarate\|Dimethyl fumarate]] | Chemical properties: Dimethyl fumarate is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[Dimethyl Sulfoxide\|Dimethyl Sulfoxide]] | Polar aprotic solvent used as a vehicle to improve solubility and bioavailability of poorly water-soluble drugs. |
| [[Disease-Associated Microglia\|Disease-Associated Microglia]] | A defined microglial activation signature in neurodegeneration, distinct from the cGAS-driven IFN-I-enriched microglial state. |
| [[Disulfiram\|Disulfiram]] | Disulfiram is an FDA-approved alcohol-aversion drug repurposed as a first-in-class covalent SARM1 inhibitor that irreversibly blocks the TIR-domain NADase active site, protecting axons from Wallerian degeneration. |
| [[DMT1\|DMT1]] | DMT1 (SLC11A2) is the divalent metal-ion transporter that moves endosomal Fe2+ into the cytosol, supplying the labile iron pool that drives ferroptosis. |
| [[DNA\|DNA]] | DNA (Deoxyribonucleic acid) is the hereditary material in humans and almost all other organisms, which encodes the genetic instructions for life. |
| [[DNA Damage\|DNA Damage]] | DNA damage refers to any chemical or physical alteration to the structure of DNA that deviates from the canonical Watson-Crick base pairing. Unlike DNA Mutation, which are heritable sequence ch... |
| [[DNA Helicase\|DNA Helicase]] | Enzymes that unwind double-stranded DNA during replication, repair, and transcription. |
| [[DNA Ligase IV\|DNA Ligase IV]] | DNA ligase IV (LIG4), the ligase that seals DNA double-strand breaks in non-homologous end joining; interacts with SIRT3-associated DNA repair complexes in mitochondrial and nuclear genome maintenance. |
| [[DNA Polymerase\|DNA Polymerase]] | Family of enzymes that synthesize DNA by adding deoxynucleotides to a growing strand during replication and repair. |
| [[DNA Repair\|DNA Repair]] | DNA repair refers to the collection of processes by which a cell identifies and corrects damage to the DNA molecules that encode its genome. |
| [[DNA-PK\|DNA-PK]] | DNA-dependent protein kinase catalytic subunit (PRKDC) central to non-homologous end joining. |
| [[DNA-SCARS\|DNA-SCARS]] | Persistent nuclear DNA damage foci (DNA segments with chromatin alterations reinforcing senescence) that sustain growth arrest and inflammatory cytokine secretion in senescence. |
| [[DNASE2\|DNASE2]] | Acid endolysosomal DNase whose downregulation in senescence permits cytosolic DNA accumulation and cGAS-STING activation. |
| [[DNM2\|DNM2]] | Dynamin 2, a dynamin-like GTPase recruited to DRP1-constricted mitochondria to complete division. |
| [[dNTP\|dNTP]] | Deoxyribonucleoside triphosphates (dNTPs — dATP, dGTP, dCTP, dTTP) are the four deoxyribose building blocks of DNA, produced de novo by ribonucleotide reductase; balanced dNTP pools are essential for high-fidelity DNA replication and repair, and both depletion and imbalance drive replication stress, mutagenesis, and genomic instability. |
| [[Docosahexaenoic acid\|Docosahexaenoic acid]] | An omega-3 polyunsaturated fatty acid highly enriched in neuronal membranes, with six double bonds making it highly susceptible to lipid peroxidation. |
| [[DOHH\|DOHH]] | Deoxyhypusine hydroxylase, the nonheme diiron monooxygenase that catalyses the second and final step of hypusine biosynthesis, hydroxylating deoxyhypusine on eIF5A to hypusine while reducing molecular oxygen. Reported human DOHH variants cause a neurodevelopmental disorder with hypotonia and intellectual disability, and DOHH is a proposed anti-cancer target. |
| [[Dominant Optic Atrophy\|Dominant Optic Atrophy]] | Most common inherited optic neuropathy caused by OPA1 mutations impairing IMM fusion. |
| [[Dopamine\|Dopamine]] | Dopamine is a critical catecholamine neurotransmitter in the brain and peripheral nervous system, playing key roles in reward, motivation, motor control, and executive function. |
| [[Dopamine Transporter\|Dopamine Transporter]] | The Dopamine Transporter (also known as DAT) is a membrane protein responsible for the reuptake of Dopamine from the synaptic cleft back into the presynaptic neuron, terminating the signal and recy... |
| [[Dopaminergic Neurons\|Dopaminergic Neurons]] | Dopaminergic Neurons are the primary source of the neurotransmitter Dopamine in the mammalian central nervous system. They play a critical role in the control of metabolic processes, executive ... |
| [[Dopaminochrome\|Dopaminochrome]] | Dopaminochrome is an aminochrome intermediate formed by the Oxidation of Dopamine. It is the structural and biochemical analog of Adrenochrome in the dopaminergic pathway. |
| [[Dorsal Root Ganglion\|Dorsal Root Ganglion]] | Cluster of sensory neuron somata whose cGAS-STING activation contributes to neuropathic pain. |
| [[DOT1L\|DOT1L]] | Disruptor of telomeric silencing 1-like (KMT4), the sole H3K79 methyltransferase whose inhibition suppresses SASP without releasing the senescence arrest. |
| [[Double-Strand Break\|Double-Strand Break]] | A lesion in which both strands of the DNA duplex are severed, triggering coordinated DNA damage responses and the recruitment of chromatin modifiers to repair sites. |
| [[Double-stranded RNA-binding domain\|Double-stranded RNA-binding domain]] | Conserved protein module (~65–70 aa) that binds double-stranded RNA; present in DGCR8, Dicer, and other RNase III partners for substrate recognition. |
| [[Doubly-Labelled Water\|Doubly-Labelled Water]] | A stable-isotope method for measuring total daily energy expenditure in free-living subjects over 1–3 weeks by tracking the differential elimination of 2H and 18O from body water as CO2 and water, respectively. The criterion field method in human and animal energetics. |
| [[Down Syndrome\|Down Syndrome]] | Trisomy 21 disorder with accelerated aging, APP overdosage, and early Alzheimer pathology. |
| [[Doxorubicin\|Doxorubicin]] | Doxorubicin (also known as Adriamycin) is a powerful anthracycline chemotherapy drug used to treat various types of cancer, including Breast Cancer, bladder cancer, and leukemia. |
| [[DPP4\|DPP4]] | DPP4 (CD26) is a protease whose plasma-membrane localization promotes ferroptosis in colorectal cancer through a p53-dependent mechanism. |
| [[DPPH\|DPPH]] | 1,1-diphenyl-2-picryl-hydrazyl; a stable free radical used to measure antioxidant capacity via free radical scavenging assays. |
| [[DRP1\|DRP1]] | Dynamin-Related Protein 1 is the cytosolic dynamin-like GTPase that mediates mitochondrial fission, recruited to mitochondria by MFF/MID49/MID51/FIS1 adaptors. |
| [[Drug Resistance\|Drug Resistance]] | Acquired or intrinsic reduction in cancer cell sensitivity to therapeutic agents. |
| [[DSRM-3716\|DSRM-3716]] | DSRM-3716 is a next-generation non-covalent small-molecule SARM1 inhibitor that occupies the NAD+-binding pocket of the TIR dimer with nanomolar potency and high selectivity, under development for axon-degeneration disorders. |
| [[Duffy Antigen Receptor for Chemokines\|Duffy Antigen Receptor for Chemokines]] | DARC/ACKR1 chemokine scavenger on erythrocytes, fine-tuning leukocyte recruitment and serving as the invasion receptor for Plasmodium vivax and P. knowlesi. |
| [[DunedinPACE\|DunedinPACE]] | A methylation-based estimator of the pace of biological aging that predicts aging-related decline and disease from a single timepoint. |
| [[Dysbiosis\|Dysbiosis]] | Disruption of the composition and functional balance of the commensal microbiota, linked to inflammation, metabolic disease, and accelerated aging. |
| [[Dyspnea\|Dyspnea]] | Dyspnea is the subjective sensation of breathlessness, a presenting symptom of methemoglobinemia and respiratory distress. |
| [[Dystrophin\|Dystrophin]] | A 427 kDa rod-shaped cytoskeletal protein encoded by the DMD gene on Xp21, located at the inner surface of the muscle fibre sarcolemma where it links the actin cytoskeleton to the dystrophin-associated glycoprotein complex in the extracellular matrix. Its absence causes Duchenne muscular dystrophy; its reduction causes Becker muscular dystrophy. |
| [[E-cadherin\|E-cadherin]] | Epithelial cadherin (CDH1), a calcium-dependent cell-cell adhesion protein whose loss marks epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition; referenced in the SASP tumorigenesis module. |
| [[E-Selectin\|E-Selectin]] | E-selectin (SELE, CD62E, ELAM-1) is an inducible endothelial adhesion molecule of the selectin family that mediates leukocyte tethering and rolling on inflamed endothelium; its soluble form serves as a circulating biomarker of endothelial activation. |
| [[E2F1\|E2F1]] | E2F transcription factor 1 (E2F1) is a protein that regulates the expression of genes involved in the cell cycle and Apoptosis. |
| [[E3 Ubiquitin Ligase\|E3 Ubiquitin Ligase]] | The third and substrate-selecting enzyme of the ubiquitination cascade, of which more than 600 are encoded in the human genome. E3 ligases determine which protein is modified and with what ubiquitin chain topology, and fall into structurally distinct families (RING, RBR, HECT) that transfer ubiquitin either directly or via a covalent enzyme intermediate. |
| [[ECM\|ECM]] | The Extracellular Matrix (ECM) is a complex network of extracellular macromolecules, such as collagen, enzymes, and glycoproteins like Fibronectin and Vitronectin, that provide structural and bioch... |
| [[Ectoparasite\|Ectoparasite]] | A parasite that lives on the external surface of its host or in the superficial layers of the skin, relying on the host for sustenance, maturation and reproduction. Clinically this covers lice, scabies mites, fleas and ticks - a group of neglected tropical diseases associated with substantial morbidity, stigma and secondary bacterial infection. |
| [[EDTA\|EDTA]] | EDTA (Ethylenediaminetetraacetic acid) is a chelating agent used to bind metal ions such as iron and copper. |
| [[EEA1\|EEA1]] | Early endosome antigen 1, a 1413-residue FYVE-domain-containing tethering protein that localises exclusively to Rab5-positive early endosomes via binding to phosphatidylinositol 3-phosphate, and couples endosomal membrane docking to SNARE-mediated fusion. |
| [[eEFSec\|eEFSec]] | eEFSec (EEFSEC) is the selenocysteine-specific eukaryotic translation elongation factor that, bound to GTP and tRNA[Ser]Sec, delivers selenocysteine to the ribosomal A site at recoded UGA codons; recruited by SECISBP2. |
| [[EF24\|EF24]] | Synthetic curcumin analog with enhanced stability that suppresses oxidative stress-induced epithelial senescence and PTEN/PI3K signaling. |
| [[EGCG\|EGCG]] | EGCG (Epigallocatechin gallate) is a type of catechin and the most abundant polyphenol in Green tea. It is known for its potent antioxidant and potential health-promoting properties. |
| [[EGF\|EGF]] | Epidermal growth factor is a 53-amino-acid, 6 kDa secreted peptide of the EGF module family and the founding ligand of the EGFR/ErbB receptor tyrosine kinase system; it drives proliferation, differentiation and survival in epithelia, and its dysregulation feeds both SASP secretion and tumour growth. |
| [[EGFR\|EGFR]] | Epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) is a transmembrane receptor tyrosine kinase that regulates cell proliferation, survival, and differentiation. It is frequently overexpressed or mutated in nu... |
| [[Ehlers-Danlos Syndrome\|Ehlers-Danlos Syndrome]] | Ehlers-Danlos syndromes are a heterogeneous group of heritable connective tissue disorders caused by defects in collagen structure or processing, characterised by joint hypermobility, skin hyperextensibility, tissue fragility and, in the vascular type, arterial and organ rupture. |
| [[eIF4G\|eIF4G]] | eIF4G is the large scaffolding subunit of the eIF4F cap-binding complex, binding eIF4E, eIF4A, eIF3 and poly(A)-binding protein to assemble the 43S preinitiation complex, and acting as the convergence point for mTORC1 and MNK1 control of translation. |
| [[Elastic Fibers\|Elastic Fibers]] | Elastic fibers are insoluble extracellular matrix assemblies in which a cross-linked amorphous elastin core is ensheathed within a fibrillin-based microfibrillar scaffold; they provide reversible elastic recoil in large arteries, lung, skin and ligaments and also sequester TGF-beta growth factors. |
| [[Elastin\|Elastin]] | Highly elastic structural protein of connective tissue ECM, providing resilience and recoil to skin, lungs, arteries, and ligaments; degraded primarily by MMP-12. |
| [[Elastokines\|Elastokines]] | Bioactive peptides generated by proteolytic degradation of Elastin that signal through the Elastin-Binding Protein receptor to amplify MMP expression and promote inflammation. |
| [[Electron Transport Chain\|Electron Transport Chain]] | The Electron Transport Chain (ETC) is a series of protein complexes in the inner Mitochondria membrane that couples electron transfer to proton pumping, establishing the Proton Motive Force used by... |
| [[Elephantiasis\|Elephantiasis]] | The advanced end-stage of chronic lymphedema, in which long-standing lymphatic dysfunction produces massive swelling, thickened hyperkeratotic skin and fibrosis of a limb or the genitalia. The term describes a syndrome rather than a disease - lymphatic filariasis is the commonest tropical cause, with podoconiosis a close second. |
| [[ELK1\|ELK1]] | ELK1 is an ETS-domain ternary complex factor transcription factor activated by ERK/MAPK phosphorylation. It is the founding member of the TCF subfamily that also includes ELK3 and ELK4 (SAP-1). |
| [[Embryogenesis\|Embryogenesis]] | The formation and early development of an embryo, from fertilisation through cleavage, gastrulation, neurulation and organogenesis. It is the period in which the three germ layers and body axes are established, and is the developmental window during which most birth defects and most spontaneous abortions originate. |
| [[Embryoid Bodies\|Embryoid Bodies]] | Three-dimensional aggregates formed by embryonic stem cells in suspension that自发 differentiate into derivatives of all three germ layers. |
| [[Emphysema\|Emphysema]] | Emphysema is a chronic obstructive lung disease characterized by irreversible destruction of alveolar walls and loss of elastic recoil, driven by protease–antiprotease imbalance and oxidative stress. |
| [[EndMT\|EndMT]] | EndMT is an acronym covering two related mesenchymal transitions of endothelial origin - endocardial-to-mesenchymal transition during heart development, and the pathological endothelial-to-mesenchymal transition that supplies fibroblast-like cells to fibrosis. |
| [[Endocytosis\|Endocytosis]] | Endocytosis is the vesicle-mediated internalisation of plasma membrane, extracellular fluid and membrane-bound cargo into a cell, delivering it to endosomes for sorting to recycling, retrograde transport, or lysosomal degradation. |
| [[Endogenous Retrovirus\|Endogenous Retrovirus]] | Endogenous retroviruses (ERVs), germline-integrated retroviral relics that are epigenetically silenced in youth and derepressed in senescence and aging to drive innate immune inflammation. |
| [[Endoplasmic Reticulum\|Endoplasmic Reticulum]] | The endoplasmic reticulum (ER) is a continuous membrane network—rough and smooth—that performs protein synthesis and folding, lipid synthesis, calcium storage, and detoxification, and initiates the unfolded protein response under stress. |
| [[Endoplasmic Reticulum Stress\|Endoplasmic Reticulum Stress]] | Definition: Endoplasmic Reticulum Stress is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling ... |
| [[endostatin\|Endostatin]] | 20 kDa anti-angiogenic C-terminal fragment of collagen XVIII, an endogenous inhibitor of endothelial proliferation and tumor vascularization with metabolic and aging relevance. |
| [[Endosymbiosis\|Endosymbiosis]] | Theory that mitochondria descended from an ancient prokaryote engulfed by early eukaryotes. |
| [[Endothelial Cells\|Endothelial Cells]] | Cerebrovascular lining cells that express cGAS-STING and, when activated, disrupt the blood-brain barrier via pyroptosis and inflammation. |
| [[Endothelial Dysfunction\|Endothelial Dysfunction]] | Endothelial dysfunction is a systemic pathological state of the vascular endothelium characterized by reduced bioavailability of Nitric Oxide, impaired vasodilation, increased Inflammation adhe... |
| [[Endothelial Nitric Oxide Synthase\|Endothelial Nitric Oxide Synthase]] | Endothelial nitric oxide synthase (eNOS, NOS3) is the constitutive isoform that produces nitric oxide for normal vascular tone and homeostasis. |
| [[Endothelial-to-Mesenchymal Transition\|Endothelial-to-Mesenchymal Transition]] | A transdifferentiation process by which endothelial cells lose their characteristic endothelial traits and acquire mesenchymal/contractile features, implicated in vascular aging and fibrosis. |
| [[Energy Balance\|Energy Balance]] | The steady-state relationship between energy intake (food) and energy expenditure (resting metabolism, thermic effect of food, physical activity). Positive energy balance over time drives fat storage and obesity; negative balance drives weight loss. |
| [[Energy Expenditure\|Energy Expenditure]] | Total energy used by an organism per unit time, partitioned into resting metabolic rate, the thermic effect of food, and the energy cost of physical activity. Measured directly by calorimetry or indirectly by the doubly-labelled water method. |
| [[Enhancer-Promoter Looping\|Enhancer-Promoter Looping]] | Physical chromatin contacts that bring enhancers into proximity with gene promoters, rewired in senescence to drive SASP gene transcription within TADs. |
| [[eNOS\|eNOS]] | eNOS (Endothelial Nitric Oxide Synthase, NOS3) is a constitutively expressed, calcium/calmodulin-dependent isoform of Nitric Oxide Synthase that produces low, pulsatile fluxes of Nitric Oxide c... |
| [[Enoxaparin\|Enoxaparin]] | Enoxaparin is a low-molecular-weight heparin of roughly 4.5 kDa mean molecular weight, obtained by partial enzymatic depolymerisation of heparin, that inhibits factor Xa predominantly and is dosed once or twice daily by subcutaneous injection. |
| [[Entacapone\|Entacapone]] | A nitrocatechol-class COMT inhibitor used as an adjunct to levodopa therapy in Parkinson's disease. |
| [[Enterocloster\|Enterocloster]] | Enterocloster is a gut bacterial genus contributing to ellagitannin conversion toward urolithin A. |
| [[Enzalutamide\|enzalutamide]] | Enzalutamide is a second-generation, nonsteroidal androgen-receptor (AR) signaling inhibitor used in castration-resistant prostate cancer (CRPC) for which drug resistance is a major clinical problem; preclinical studies show ivermectin can overcome enzalutamide resistance and synergize with docetaxel. |
| [[Efferocytosis\|Efferocytosis]] | PS-dependent phagocytic clearance of apoptotic cells; in erythrocytes it removes eryptotic cells within minutes and reprograms macrophages toward an antiinflammatory, heme-oxygenase-1-high phenotype. |
| [[Eosinophils\|Eosinophils]] | Eosinophils are IL-5-driven granulocytes of myeloid lineage that degranulate a cationic, peroxidase-rich granule arsenal onto helminths and onto host tissue, making them simultaneously essential for parasite defence and drivers of allergic and fibrotic disease. |
| [[EP300\|EP300]] | E1A binding protein p300, a histone acetyltransferase whose inhibition by spermidine derepresses autophagy. |
| [[Epacadostat\|Epacadostat]] | Epacadostat (INCB024360) is a selective, orally available inhibitor of IDO1 that was developed for cancer immunotherapy, often combined with checkpoint inhibitors. |
| [[Ephrin Type A Receptor 2\|Ephrin Type A Receptor 2]] | EPHA2, a receptor tyrosine kinase activated by exosomes from senescent cells to drive cancer proliferation and SASP-mediated tumor promotion. |
| [[Epigenetic Aging\|Epigenetic Aging]] | Age-related, largely stochastic drift in DNA methylation and chromatin marks that can be quantified as epigenetic clocks predictive of biological age. |
| [[Epigenetic Alterations\|Epigenetic Alterations]] | Definition: Epigenetic Alterations is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathwa... |
| [[Epigenetic Landscape\|Epigenetic Landscape]] | The ensemble of chromatin states, histone modifications, and DNA methylation that defines cellular identity and gene-expression potential; proposed to erode progressively with age. |
| [[Epigenome\|Epigenome]] | The Epigenome consists of the chemical compounds and proteins that can attach to DNA and direct such actions as turning genes on or off, controlling the production of proteins in particular cel... |
| [[Epilepsy\|Epilepsy]] | Neurological disorder characterized by recurrent, unprovoked seizures |
| [[Epinephrine\|Epinephrine]] | Epinephrine, also known as Adrenaline, is a hormone and neurotransmitter involved in the body''s fight-or-flight response. It is produced primarily by the adrenal glands and certain neurons. |
| [[Epithelial cells\|Epithelial cells]] | Epithelial cells are polarized, tightly adhered sheets that line surfaces and cavities, providing barrier, secretory, absorptive, and transport functions and serving as the origin of most carcinomas. |
| [[Epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition\|Epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition]] | A developmental/programmable process in which epithelial cells lose polarity and adhesion, acquiring mesenchymal, migratory and invasive traits; inducible by the SASP. |
| [[EPR spectroscopy\|EPR spectroscopy]] | Electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) spectroscopy, also known as electron spin resonance (ESR), is a technique that detects species with unpaired Electron (paramagnetic species), including Fre... |
| [[Epstein-Barr virus\|Epstein-Barr virus]] | Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) is a double-stranded DNA virus of the Herpesviridae family (subfamily Gammaherpesvirinae). It infects >90% of the adult human population worldwide and establishes lifel... |
| [[ER Stress\|ER Stress]] | ER stress is a cellular condition caused by the accumulation of misfolded or unfolded proteins in the endoplasmic reticulum lumen, triggering the unfolded protein response (UPR). It is intimate... |
| [[EREG\|EREG]] | Epiregulin, a transmembrane EGFR ligand and SASP factor secreted by senescent stromal cells that drives cancer chemoresistance; circulating EREG is reversed by apigenin in vivo. |
| [[ERGIC\|ERGIC]] | ER–Golgi intermediate compartment where activated STING transits and assembles TBK1–IRF3 signaling complexes. |
| [[ERK2\|ERK2]] | Extracellular regulated protein kinase 2 (ERK2) is a kinase that phosphorylates TFEB at S142, contributing to its cytosolic retention. |
| [[ERMIT\|ERMIT]] | Erythroid mitophagy; the programmed elimination of mitochondria during erythrocyte maturation, driven primarily by BNIP3L/NIX and BIM, essential for red blood cell function. |
| [[ERRalpha\|ERRalpha]] | ERRα (estrogen-related receptor alpha, encoded by ESRRA) is an orphan Nuclear Receptor that functions as a master regulator of Mitochondrial Biogenesis, Oxidative Phosphorylation, and Fatty aci... |
| [[Erythrocyte Senescence\|Erythrocyte Senescence]] | The ~120-day red-cell aging program (density rise, desialylation, cation loss, oxidative damage, CD47 loss, AE1 neo-antigen) cleared over days by macrophage/NOAb immunity — distinct from minutes-fast eryptosis. |
| [[Erythropoietin\|Erythropoietin]] | The renal hypoxia-induced hematopoietic cytokine (EPO) driving erythropoiesis — reported protective against eryptosis, though direct anti-eryptotic action is inferred rather than demonstrated. |
| [[Erythropoiesis\|Erythropoiesis]] | The marrow line from hematopoietic stem cells to mature erythrocytes, ending in enucleation and organelle clearance — which is why mature red cells retain only a truncated death machinery. |
| [[Essential Amino Acids\|Essential Amino Acids]] | The nine amino acids that cannot be synthesized de novo by humans and must be obtained from the diet (histidine, isoleucine, leucine, lysine, methionine, phenylalanine, threonine, tryptophan, valine). Their dietary restriction underlies the benefits of protein and amino-acid restriction. |
| [[Estrogen\|Estrogen]] | Estrogen is a category of sex hormones responsible for the development and regulation of the female reproductive system and secondary sex characteristics. |
| [[Estrogen Receptor\|Estrogen Receptor]] | Nuclear receptor transcription factors (ERα/ERβ) that mediate estrogen signaling and are key targets in breast and other hormone-sensitive cancers; ERβ in particular gates female-restrained inflammasome/cell-death programs and is an emerging sex-stratified therapeutic axis. |
| [[Etoposide\|Etoposide]] | Etoposide (also known as VP-16) is a chemotherapy medication used for the treatment of a number of types of cancer, including testicular cancer, lung cancer, Lymphoma, and leukemia. |
| [[ETS1\|ETS1]] | ETS proto-oncogene 1, a pioneer-like ETS-family transcription factor regulating invasion, angiogenesis, and immune genes; its promoter is controlled by SIRT3 via H3K27 crotonylation in metastatic cancer. |
| [[Euchromatin\|Euchromatin]] | Euchromatin is the lightly packed, transcriptionally active form of Chromatin. It is characterized by high levels of Histone Acetylation and low levels of DNA methylation, which keep the DNA ac... |
| [[Everolimus\|Everolimus]] | Chemical properties: Everolimus is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[Excess Post-Exercise Oxygen Consumption\|Excess Post-Exercise Oxygen Consumption]] | The transient elevation of oxygen consumption (and thus energy expenditure) above resting levels after an exercise bout. EPOC has a rapid component lasting < 2 h and a prolonged component lasting up to 48 h, and can represent 80–90 % of total energy cost for short high-intensity efforts. |
| [[Excitotoxicity\|Excitotoxicity]] | Excitotoxicity is the process by which excessive glutamatergic stimulation, particularly at NMDA and AMPA receptors, causes pathological Ca2+ influx, mitochondrial failure, and neuronal death—central to stroke, TBI, and neurodegeneration. |
| [[Executive Function\|Executive Function]] | Definition: Executive Function is a scientific concept that describes fundamental principles governing biological or chemical systems. |
| [[Exercise\|Exercise]] | Exercise is planned, structured physical activity that improves or maintains physical fitness. It is one of the most effective interventions for extending healthspan, reducing Oxidative Stress,... |
| [[EXITS\|EXITS]] | EXITS (Escape from X-Inactivation Tumor Suppressors) is a framework identifying X-linked tumor suppressor genes that escape X-inactivation and are loss-of-function mutated more frequently in male cancers. |
| [[Exportin-5\|Exportin-5]] | RanGTP-dependent nuclear export receptor that transports pre-miRNA hairpins from the nucleus to the cytoplasm for Dicer processing. |
| [[Extracellular Vesicles\|Extracellular Vesicles]] | Membrane-bound particles that package cGAMP and mtDNA to propagate cGAS-STING activation between CNS cells. |
| [[EZH2\|EZH2]] | Catalytic function: EZH2 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Factor XIIIa\|Factor XIIIa]] | Factor XIIIa is the activated plasma transglutaminase of the coagulation cascade that covalently cross-links fibrin monomers into an insoluble clot, a step that fixes clot architecture and resistance to fibrinolysis. |
| [[FAD\|FAD]] | Biochemical properties: FAD is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic flux and... |
| [[Faecalibacterium\|Faecalibacterium]] | Faecalibacterium is a genus of bacteria, with Faecalibacterium prausnitzii being a key species, known for its high production of butyrate and its anti-inflammatory effects in the gut. |
| [[FAK\|FAK]] | Focal adhesion kinase (PTK2); a non-receptor cytoplasmic tyrosine kinase that integrates integrin and growth factor signals to regulate cell survival, proliferation, and migration, and is recruited by phosphorylated Caspase-8 to promote metastasis. |
| [[Farber disease\|Farber disease]] | A rare autosomal recessive lysosomal storage disorder caused by loss-of-function mutations in ASAH1 resulting in <5% residual acid ceramidase activity, characterized by painful joint deformities, subcutaneous nodules, and vocal hoarseness. |
| [[Fascia\|Fascia]] | The continuous sheet-like connective-tissue continuum that wraps, separates, and connects muscles, bones, vessels, nerves, and organs; transmits force, senses mechanical tension, and remodels on a months-to-years timescale. |
| [[FASN\|FASN]] | FASN is the human fatty acid synthase, a single 251 kDa multi-enzyme polypeptide that condenses one acetyl-CoA and seven malonyl-CoA plus 14 NADPH into palmitate, the committed enzyme of de novo lipogenesis. |
| [[Fasting\|Fasting]] | Fasting is the voluntary abstinence from food and caloric beverages for a defined period. It induces a metabolic switch from glucose-based to ketone-based energy, activates stress-resistance pathwa... |
| [[Fat Oxidation\|Fat Oxidation]] | The catabolism of fatty acids via beta-oxidation and the TCA cycle to generate ATP. Fat oxidation is elevated during low-intensity exercise and during the prolonged phase of post-exercise oxygen consumption (EPOC). |
| [[Fat-Free Mass\|Fat-Free Mass]] | The total mass of the body minus stored fat — comprising skeletal muscle, bone, organs, and connective tissue. Fat-free mass is the principal determinant of resting metabolic rate, accounting for 50–70 % of inter-individual variation. |
| [[Fatty acid\|Fatty acid]] | Fatty acids are carboxylic acids with aliphatic chains (typically 4–28 carbons) that serve as key components of membrane phospholipids, energy storage molecules, and signaling precursors in biological systems. |
| [[Fatty acid oxidation\|Fatty acid oxidation]] | Definition: Fatty acid oxidation is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways... |
| [[Fatty Acid Synthase\|Fatty Acid Synthase]] | A multifunctional enzyme (FASN) that catalyzes de novo long-chain fatty acid synthesis and is overexpressed in many cancers, making it a metabolic therapeutic target. |
| [[FBXO7\|FBXO7]] | F-box protein that functions as a scaffolding protein to accelerate PINK1-mediated Parkin recruitment to damaged mitochondria in mitophagy. |
| [[FCCP\|FCCP]] | FCCP (carbonyl cyanide-4-trifluoromethoxy phenylhydrazone) is a mitochondrial uncoupler used to maximally stimulate cellular oxygen consumption in Seahorse bioenergetic assays of methylene blue. |
| [[Fenofibrate\|Fenofibrate]] | Fenofibrate is a fibrate and orally absorbed PPAR-alpha agonist prodrug whose active metabolite fenofibric acid lowers triglycerides and VLDL by driving lipoprotein lipase and suppressing apoC-III; it does not reduce cardiovascular events in diabetes trials. |
| [[Fenton Reaction\|Fenton Reaction]] | The Fenton Reaction is a chemical process in which Iron (Fe²⁺) reduces hydrogen peroxide (H₂O₂) to generate the highly reactive hydroxyl radical (•OH), a potent Reactive Oxygen Species. In the brai... |
| [[Ferroportin\|Ferroportin]] | Ferroportin (FPN/SLC40A1) is the sole cellular iron exporter; its activity limits the labile iron pool and thus modulates ferroptosis sensitivity. |
| [[FGF\|FGF]] | Fibroblast growth factor family of paracrine and endocrine signals driving proliferation and repair. |
| [[FGF21\|FGF21]] | Fibroblast growth factor 21, an endocrine hormone of the FGF19 subfamily that regulates glucose and lipid metabolism, ketogenesis, and energy expenditure. Induced by mitochondrial stress and acts as a Mitokine. |
| [[Fibrin\|Fibrin]] | Fibrin is the insoluble gel polymer formed when thrombin cleaves fibrinopeptides A and B from fibrinogen; it is cross-linked by factor XIIIa into the structural backbone of a blood clot and also serves as a scaffold for cell adhesion and tissue repair. |
| [[Fibrinolysis\|Fibrinolysis]] | The physiological and pharmacological breakdown of fibrin clots by plasmin, controlled by the balance between tissue-type and urokinase-type plasminogen activators and their inhibitor PAI-1; the counterpart of coagulation and the target of thrombolytic therapy. |
| [[Fibroblast\|Fibroblast]] | Mesenchymal cell that synthesizes the extracellular matrix and collagen, builds tissue stroma, and drives wound healing and fibrosis; when senescent in IPF, acquires a profibrotic SASP-driving phenotype. |
| [[Fibromyalgia\|Fibromyalgia]] | A chronic pain syndrome characterized by widespread musculoskeletal pain, fatigue, sleep disturbances, and cognitive difficulties, with emerging evidence of bioenergetic dysfunction linking it to creatine metabolism. |
| [[FIN56\|FIN56]] | FIN56 is a Class III ferroptosis inducer (FIN) that activates squalene synthase, diverting mevalonate-pathway flux away from CoQ10 and depleting the FSP1 substrate, while also promoting GPX4 degradation via acetyl-CoA carboxylases. |
| [[Firmicutes\|Firmicutes]] | Firmicutes is a dominant phylum of Gram-positive bacteria in the human gut, encompassing Bacilli, Clostridia, and Lactobacilli, and contributing to short-chain fatty acid production and metabolic health. |
| [[FIS1\|FIS1]] | Mitochondrial fission 1 protein (FIS1) is a outer mitochondrial membrane protein that recruits DRP1 to initiate mitochondrial fission, playing a key role in mitochondrial dynamics and quality control. |
| [[Fisetin\|Fisetin]] | Fisetin is a plant Flavonoids and antioxidant found in many fruits and vegetables, such as strawberries and apples. It has been identified as a potent senolytic agent, selectively inducing apoptosi... |
| [[FKBP12\|FKBP12]] | FKBP12 (FKBP1A) is a 12 kDa peptidyl-prolyl isomerase of the FKBP family whose rapamycin/FK506-binding pocket couples ligand occupancy to allosteric inhibition of the FRB domain of mTOR. It is the intracellular receptor for rapamycin and for the calcineurin inhibitors FK506 and tacrolimus. |
| [[FKBP38\|FKBP38]] | FK506 binding protein 38 (FKBP38/FKBP8), an OMM protein that induces Parkin-independent mitophagy through LC3A aggregation |
| [[FKBP8\|FKBP8]] | Parkin-independent mitophagy receptor with a unique escape mechanism that avoids proteasomal degradation and preferentially binds LC3A. |
| [[Flavan-3-ols\|Flavan-3-ols]] | Flavan-3-ols (flavanols) are a subclass of flavonoid polyphenols comprising monomeric catechins/epicatechins and their gallates, which polymerize into proanthocyanidins; abundant in tea, cocoa, and fruit with antioxidant and vascular benefits. |
| [[Flavin-containing Monooxygenase\|Flavin-containing Monooxygenase]] | Family of FAD-dependent enzymes (FMO1-FMO5) that oxygenate nucleophilic heteroatoms; contributes to drug metabolism. |
| [[Flavone\|Flavone]] | The parent scaffold of flavonoids such as apigenin (4',5,7-trihydroxyflavone); the chemical class to which the senomorphic apigenin belongs. |
| [[Flavones\|Flavones]] | A subclass of flavonoids lacking a 3-hydroxyl group, including apigenin, luteolin, and chrysin, with antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and anticancer activities. |
| [[Flavonoid\|Flavonoid]] | A large class of polyphenolic plant secondary metabolites with antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and senolytic properties. |
| [[Flavonoids\|Flavonoids]] | Flavonoids are a diverse group of phytonutrients (plant chemicals) found in almost all fruits and vegetables. |
| [[Flavonol\|Flavonol]] | Flavonols are a subclass of flavonoid polyphenols characterized by a 3-hydroxyflavone backbone; fisetin and quercetin are prominent members with senotherapeutic and antioxidant activity. |
| [[FLICA\|FLICA]] | Fluorescent labeled inhibitors of caspases — cell-permeant activity probes (e.g. FAM-YVAD-FMK for caspase-1) used to report active caspase-1 in pyroptosis. |
| [[Flippase\|Flippase]] | ATP-dependent P4-ATPase lipid transporters that maintain phosphatidylserine on the inner membrane leaflet — the asymmetry keepers antagonized by scramblases in eryptosis and apoptosis. |
| [[Flow-Mediated Dilation\|Flow-Mediated Dilation]] | Flow-mediated dilation is the standard non-invasive measurement of endothelial function, quantitating the percent widening of the brachial artery by ultrasound after reactive hyperemia from cuff occlusion; it is largely nitric-oxide mediated and independently predicts cardiovascular events. |
| [[Flubendazole\|Flubendazole]] | Benzimidazole anthelmintic with reported microtubule-disrupting anticancer activity distinct from vinca alkaloids. |
| [[FN1\|FN1]] | FN1 encodes fibronectin, the high-molecular-weight extracellular matrix glycoprotein whose integrin-binding type III repeats carry cell adhesion, mechanotransduction, and both pro- and anti-fibrotic signalling, and whose plasma isoform drives fibrin clot retraction. |
| [[FNDC5\|FNDC5]] | Fibronectin type III domain-containing protein 5, a type I transmembrane protein that is proteolytically cleaved to release the myokine irisin; its expression is driven by PGC-1α in response to exercise and cold exposure. |
| [[Foam Cells\|Foam Cells]] | Macrophages loaded with cholesterol and lipids; hallmark of early atherosclerotic lesions. Formed when cholesterol efflux is impaired and macrophages engulf modified (glycated/oxidized) LDL. |
| [[FokI\|FokI]] | A type IIS restriction endonuclease from Flavobacterium okeonokoites whose catalytic cleavage domain is the obligate-dimeric nuclease module used in zinc finger nucleases, TALENs, and mitoTALENs to introduce site-specific double-strand breaks. |
| [[Folate\|Folate]] | Biochemical properties: Folate is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic flux ... |
| [[Fondaparinux\|Fondaparinux]] | Fondaparinux is the first synthetic anticoagulant, a 1,728-Da pentasaccharide that mimics the antithrombin-binding sequence of heparin to accelerate factor Xa inactivation without inhibiting thrombin and without binding platelet factor 4. |
| [[Foodborne disease\|Foodborne disease]] | Foodborne disease is any illness acquired from contaminated food or drink, spanning bacterial, viral and parasitic infections plus preformed toxins, and estimated by WHO at roughly 866 million cases and 1.5 million deaths annually. |
| [[FOS\|FOS]] | FOS proto-oncogene, a basic leucine zipper transcription factor that dimerizes with JUN family members to form AP-1 complexes driving proliferation, inflammation, and fibrosis genes. |
| [[FoxM1\|FoxM1]] | Forkhead box protein M1 (FoxM1) is a proliferation-associated transcription factor that drives cell-cycle genes, DNA repair, and senescence bypass; it is frequently overexpressed in cancer and is regulated by sirtuins. |
| [[FOXO\|FOXO]] | A family of transcription factors (Forkhead box O) that regulate the expression of genes involved in cell cycle, Apoptosis, and Autophagy. |
| [[FoxO1\|FoxO1]] | Transcription factor regulated by Akt that controls gluconeogenesis, adipogenesis, and beta-cell function. |
| [[FOXO3a\|FOXO3a]] | Forkhead box O3 (FOXO3A) is a transcription factor involved in regulating genes related to apoptosis, cell cycle progression, and oxidative stress resistance. Deacetylation of FOXO3A by sirtuins li... |
| [[FOXO4\|FOXO4]] | FOXO4 (Forkhead Box O4) is a transcription factor belonging to the FOXO family, involved in cellular senescence, stress response, and Autophagy. |
| [[FPRL1\|FPRL1]] | A formyl-peptide receptor-like G-protein-coupled receptor that serves as a functional receptor for Humanin |
| [[Frailty\|Frailty]] | A clinical syndrome of decreased reserve and vulnerability in older adults, linked to chronic inflammation and inflammaging. |
| [[Frailty Index\|Frailty Index]] | Quantified measure of healthspan and vulnerability to adverse outcomes based on accumulated health deficits (e.g., the 31-parameter mouse clinical frailty index of Whitehead et al.). |
| [[Free Radical Theory of Aging\|Free Radical Theory of Aging]] | The theory proposed by Denham Harman in 1955 postulating that reactive oxygen species (ROS) produced during aerobic respiration cause cumulative oxidative damage to cellular components, driving the aging process. |
| [[French Paradox\|French Paradox]] | The epidemiological observation that French populations exhibit relatively low rates of cardiovascular disease and atherosclerosis despite a diet rich in saturated fat; attributed to red wine and polyphenols such as resveratrol and the Mediterranean Diet. |
| [[FSCV\|FSCV]] | Fast-scan cyclic voltammetry (FSCV) is an electrochemical technique used to detect rapid changes in Neurotransmitter concentration in real time, typically in living brain tissue. It employs a c... |
| [[FSP1\|FSP1]] | Ferroptosis suppressor protein 1 (FSP1, formerly AIFM2) is a plasma-membrane flavoprotein that uses NAD(P)H to reduce ubiquinone (CoQ10) to ubiquinol, a lipophilic radical-trapping antioxidant, acting as a GPX4-independent, parallel brake on ferroptosis. |
| [[FTO\|FTO]] | FTO is the founding m6A RNA demethylase, an Fe(II)/2-oxoglutarate-dependent dioxygenase that oxidatively demethylates N6-methyladenosine in RNA and is best known as the gene harbouring the commonest obesity-associated variant in humans. |
| [[FTSJ1\|FTSJ1]] | FTSJ1 is an X-linked tRNA 2′-O-methyltransferase that installs the Um34 modification on selenocysteine tRNA (tRNA[Ser]Sec), a modification required for efficient UGA recoding of selenocysteine during translation. |
| [[FUNDC1\|FUNDC1]] | Outer mitochondrial membrane protein that functions as a receptor for ubiquitin-independent mitophagy, particularly under hypoxic conditions. |
| [[FYCO1\|FYCO1]] | FYCO1 is an LC3-binding selective autophagy receptor that tethers autophagosomes to kinesin-1 motors for transport along microtubules toward the lysosome, where it also promotes cargo release. |
| [[Fzo\|Fzo]] | Drosophila fuzzy onion protein, the ancestral ortholog of mammalian MFN1 and MFN2. |
| [[G Protein-Coupled Receptor\|G Protein-Coupled Receptor]] | GPCRs are the largest family of cell-surface membrane receptors, built from seven transmembrane helices that activate heterotrimeric G proteins; roughly a third of all approved drugs act on them. |
| [[G3BP1\|G3BP1]] | Ras-GAP SH3-domain-binding protein 1, a stress-granule scaffold that assists cGAS recognition of cytoplasmic chromatin fragments to trigger SASP via cGAS–STING. |
| [[G6PD\|G6PD]] | Glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase, the rate-limiting enzyme of the pentose phosphate pathway that also stabilizes PINK1 and regulates mitophagy. |
| [[GABA_A receptor\|GABA_A receptor]] | A ligand-gated chloride channel and the principal inhibitory receptor in the CNS; target of benzodiazepines, barbiturates, and neuroactive phytochemicals such as honokiol and magnolol. |
| [[GABPβ1\|GABPβ1]] | GA-binding protein subunit beta-1 (GABPβ1) is the DNA-binding beta subunit of the GABP (GA-binding protein) ETS transcription factor complex that drives expression of nuclear-encoded mitochondrial genes. |
| [[GADD45\|GADD45]] | GADD45 (Growth Arrest and DNA Damage-inducible) comprises α/β/γ stress-response proteins that integrate p53 and FOXO signaling into DNA repair, cell-cycle arrest, and senescence regulation. GADD45α is a downstream effector of the SIRT1–FOXO4 antioxidant axis. |
| [[Galangin\|Galangin]] | Galangin is 3,5,7-trihydroxyflavone, a flavonol found in galangal rhizomes and poplar-type propolis, reported to act as an aryl hydrocarbon receptor antagonist with antioxidant, anti-inflammatory and anticancer activity largely in preclinical models. |
| [[Galectin-9\|Galectin-9]] | A member of the galectin family of β-galactoside-binding lectins with dual roles in immune regulation and tumor biology |
| [[GAP\|GAP]] | GAP (GTPase-activating protein) is a family of regulatory proteins that accelerate intrinsic GTP hydrolysis on small GTPases, switching them from GTP- to GDP-bound and thereby terminating their signalling. The clearest cases in this vault are the TSC1/TSC2 heterodimer acting on Rheb to restrain mTORC1, and NF1/neurofibromin acting on Ras. |
| [[Gap Junction\|Gap Junction]] | Connexin-formed intercellular channels that permit direct transfer of cGAMP to amplify cGAS-STING signaling. |
| [[GAPDH\|GAPDH]] | Glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate dehydrogenase (GAPDH) is the NAD+-dependent glycolytic enzyme catalyzing the GAP to 1,3-BPG conversion, and a moonlighting protein that senses oxidative stress and translocates to the nucleus to promote apoptosis. |
| [[Gardos Channel\|Gardos Channel]] | KCa3.1 (KCNN4), the Ca2+-activated K+ channel whose K+ efflux produces the cell shrinkage of eryptosis; blocked by charybdotoxin and clotrimazole. |
| [[Gastrointestinal Tract\|Gastrointestinal Tract]] | The gastrointestinal tract (GIT) is the continuous muscular tube from mouth to anus responsible for ingestion, digestion, nutrient absorption, and waste elimination, and a key interface with the gut microbiome and immune system. |
| [[GATA3\|GATA3]] | GATA3 is a zinc-finger pioneer transcription factor that drives luminal differentiation in breast epithelium, is mutated at high frequency in luminal breast cancer, and is widely used as an immunohistochemical marker of breast and urothelial origin. |
| [[GATA4\|GATA4]] | GATA binding protein 4, a zinc-finger transcription factor of the GATA family essential for cardiac development and the hypertrophic response, that also functions as a stress-responsive regulator of the senescence-associated secretory phenotype (SASP) cooperating with NF-κB and C/EBPβ. |
| [[Gaucher Disease\|Gaucher Disease]] | Gaucher disease is the most common lysosomal storage disorder, an autosomal recessive deficiency of lysosomal glucocerebrosidase that causes glucosylceramide accumulation in monocyte-macrophages, producing hepatosplenomegaly, cytopenias, bone disease and, in rare types, neurological involvement. |
| [[GbetaL\|GbetaL]] | GβL (also known as mLST8) is a core subunit of both mTORC1 and mTORC2 complexes. It binds the mTOR Kinase Domain, stabilizing catalytic activity and promoting complex assembly. GβL belongs ... |
| [[GCN2\|GCN2]] | General control nonderepressible 2 (GCN2) is a serine/threonine kinase that phosphorylates eIF2α in response to amino acid starvation, including tryptophan depletion mediated by IDO1. |
| [[GDF15\|GDF15]] | Growth differentiation factor 15, a TGF-beta superfamily stress-responsive cytokine/mitokine secreted under mitochondrial and senescent stress, increasingly recognized as a circulating biomarker of cellular senescence and age-related frailty. |
| [[Geminin\|Geminin]] | Geminin (GMNN) is a replication-licensing inhibitor that blocks MCM2-7 helicase loading onto chromatin by binding CDT1. It accumulates in S/G2 and is destroyed at the metaphase-anaphase transition, licensing exactly one round of DNA replication per cell cycle; biallelic GMNN mutations cause Meier-Gorlin syndrome 6. |
| [[Gene\|Gene]] | A gene is a genomic DNA sequence transcribed into a functional RNA — the basic unit of inheritance and the unit of heredity; humans have roughly 20,000 protein-coding genes occupying about 1.5% of the genome. |
| [[Genomic Imprinting\|Genomic Imprinting]] | Parent-of-origin gene expression controlled by germline DNA methylation at imprinting control regions. |
| [[Genomic Instability\|Genomic Instability]] | Genomic Instability is a hallmark of Aging characterized by an increased tendency of the genome to acquire mutations, deletions, and structural alterations. It is both a cause and a consequence... |
| [[Genotoxic Stress\|Genotoxic Stress]] | Genotoxic stress encompasses cellular damage to DNA from chemical agents, radiation, and reactive oxygen species, triggering DNA damage response pathways that can lead to senescence, apoptosis, or genomic instability. |
| [[Genotype\|Genotype]] | A genotype is the inherited DNA sequence repertoire of a cell or organism that, together with epigenetic and environmental influences, determines phenotypic traits and disease susceptibility. |
| [[Geraldol\|Geraldol]] | A 3'-O-methylated flavonoid and COMT-generated metabolite of fisetin with uncharacterized senolytic and antioxidant pharmacology. |
| [[Geroprotectors\|Geroprotectors]] | Compounds or interventions that slow aging and extend healthspan by targeting the conserved hallmarks of aging. |
| [[Geroscience\|Geroscience]] | Geroscience is an interdisciplinary field that seeks to understand the biological relationship between Aging and age-related diseases. The central hypothesis of geroscience is that because agin... |
| [[Ghrelin\|Ghrelin]] | A metabolic hormone that regulates food intake and has immunomodulatory properties; promotes thymopoiesis and reduces age-associated inflammation. |
| [[GKRP\|GKRP]] | Glucokinase regulatory protein that regulates hepatic glucose uptake by controlling glucokinase localization; SIRT2 deacetylation target |
| [[Glaucoma\|Glaucoma]] | Glaucoma is an optic neuropathy featuring progressive retinal ganglion cell axon degeneration, in which SARM1-dependent NAD+ depletion contributes to retinal ganglion cell loss. |
| [[Glioblastoma\|Glioblastoma]] | The most common and malignant primary brain tumor in adults; acid ceramidase (ASAH1) is an independent poor-prognosis marker and therapeutic target in glioblastoma stem-like cells. |
| [[Glioma\|Glioma]] | Most common and malignant primary CNS tumor, especially glioblastoma, with sirtuin expression differentially regulated in tumor tissue |
| [[Glucagon\|Glucagon]] | Pancreatic alpha-cell peptide hormone that antagonizes insulin and raises blood glucose via hepatic glycogenolysis and gluconeogenesis. |
| [[Glucocorticoids\|Glucocorticoids]] | Steroid hormones that block pro-inflammatory SASP by impairing IL-1α-mediated NF-κB activity via the glucocorticoid receptor. |
| [[Glucokinase\|Glucokinase]] | Catalytic function: Glucokinase is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Gluconeogenesis\|Gluconeogenesis]] | Definition: Gluconeogenesis is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and ... |
| [[Glucose Intolerance\|Glucose Intolerance]] | A pre-diabetic state of impaired glucose handling in which blood glucose remains above normal but below the diabetic threshold; a recognized metabolic effect of chronic mTOR inhibition. |
| [[Glucose Oxidase\|Glucose Oxidase]] | Glucose oxidase (GOx) is an enzyme that generates extracellular H2O2 from glucose; its HT-22 neurotoxicity model is a direct oxidative-stress insult that methylene blue cannot protect against. |
| [[Glucose uptake\|Glucose uptake]] | Cellular transport of glucose across the plasma membrane, principally via GLUT transporters. |
| [[Glucose-6-Phosphate Dehydrogenase\|Glucose-6-Phosphate Dehydrogenase]] | Glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase (G6PD) is the rate-limiting enzyme of the pentose phosphate pathway, generating NADPH that regenerates reduced glutathione to protect erythrocytes from oxidative stress. |
| [[GLUT4\|GLUT4]] | GLUT4 (SLC2A4) is the insulin-responsive glucose transporter that translocates from intracellular vesicles to the plasma membrane of muscle and adipose cells to enable postprandial glucose uptake. |
| [[Glutamate\|Glutamate]] | Major excitatory neurotransmitter whose excessive synaptic signaling drives excitotoxicity, mitochondrial dysfunction, and redox injury in neurodegeneration. |
| [[Glutamate-Cysteine Ligase\|Glutamate-Cysteine Ligase]] | Catalytic function: Glutamate-Cysteine Ligase is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Glutamine Synthetase\|Glutamine Synthetase]] | Glutamine synthetase (GS, EC 6.3.1.2) is the enzyme that condenses glutamate and ammonia to form glutamine at the cost of ATP. In mammals it resides in the cytosol, the mitochondrial matrix (where it completes the urea cycle) and the perinuclear membrane of astrocytes, where it is the principal ammonia and glutamate scavenger. |
| [[Glutaminolysis\|Glutaminolysis]] | Glutaminolysis is the metabolic pathway of glutamine catabolism through glutaminase to glutamate and then α-ketoglutarate for TCA cycle entry; a key anaplerotic route in cancer. |
| [[Glutaredoxin\|Glutaredoxin]] | Glutaredoxins (Grx) are a family of small (~9–14 kDa) glutathione-dependent thiol oxidoreductases that reduce protein disulfides and protein-glutathione mixed disulfides, using electrons from reduced glutathione instead of thioredoxin reductase; they constitute the GSH-coupled half of the cellular disulfide-reducing system that sustains ribonucleotide reductase, peroxiredoxins, and redox signaling. |
| [[Glutathione\|Glutathione]] | Glutathione (GSH) is the most abundant endogenous thiol antioxidant, a tripeptide of glutamate, cysteine, and glycine that buffers cellular redox state, reduces peroxides via GPX enzymes, and conjugates electrophiles for excretion. |
| [[Glutathione Peroxidase\|Glutathione Peroxidase]] | Glutathione peroxidase (GPx) is an enzyme family with peroxidase activity whose main biological role is to protect the organism from oxidative damage. |
| [[Glutathione Reductase\|Glutathione Reductase]] | Catalytic function: Glutathione Reductase is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Glutathione S-Transferase\|Glutathione S-Transferase]] | A family of phase II detoxifying enzymes that conjugate glutathione to electrophilic substrates, regulated by the NRF2–ARE pathway and implicated in cellular antioxidant defense and mitophagy. |
| [[Glutathione Synthetase\|Glutathione Synthetase]] | Catalytic function: Glutathione Synthetase is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Glycation\|Glycation]] | Glycation is a spontaneous non-enzymatic reaction of free reducing sugars with free amino groups of proteins, DNA, and lipids. |
| [[Glyceraldehyde 3-Phosphate\|Glyceraldehyde 3-Phosphate]] | The glycolytic triose phosphate converted to 1,3-bisphosphoglycerate by GAPDH using NAD+; its flux is constrained when senescence lowers the NAD+/NADH ratio, limiting glycolysis and ATP. |
| [[Glycine\|Glycine]] | Glycine is the smallest amino acid and a versatile metabolite that serves as a building block for glutathione, creatine, heme, purines, and collagen, and acts as an inhibitory neurotransmitter. |
| [[Glycolysis\|Glycolysis]] | Glycolysis is the metabolic pathway that converts glucose into pyruvate. A byproduct of this pathway is the formation of Methylglyoxal, a potent precursor for Advanced Glycation End Products. |
| [[Glycosaminoglycan\|Glycosaminoglycan]] | Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are long, linear, repeating disaccharide polysaccharides built from a uronic acid and an N-acetylated hexosamine. Sulfated GAGs are covalently attached to core proteins to form proteoglycans; hyaluronan is the exception, being non-sulfated and core-protein-free. |
| [[Glyoxalase I\|Glyoxalase I]] | Catalytic function: Glyoxalase I is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[GM-CSF\|GM-CSF]] | Granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor is a SASP cytokine that recruits and activates myeloid cells and serves as a senolytic-therapy response biomarker. |
| [[Gnathostoma\|Gnathostoma]] | Gnathostoma is a genus of parasitic nematodes (gnathostomes, "jaw worms") of which G. spinigerum is the principal cause of human gnathostomiasis. Third-stage infective larvae penetrate the gut wall after ingestion and then migrate through tissues, producing recurrent migratory swellings and occasionally eosinophilic meningitis. |
| [[Golgi apparatus\|Golgi apparatus]] | Overview: Golgi apparatus is a specialized cellular or anatomical structure with defined functions in tissue organization and physiology. |
| [[Gout\|Gout]] | Gout is an inflammatory arthritis caused by deposition of monosodium urate crystals in joints and soft tissue, resulting from chronic hyperuricemia due to overproduction or underexcretion of uric acid. |
| [[gp130\|gp130]] | A transmembrane glycoprotein that serves as a common signal transducer for the IL-6 family of cytokines |
| [[Gram-Negative Bacteria\|Gram-Negative Bacteria]] | Bacteria whose outer membrane contains lipopolysaccharide (LPS/endotoxin); major drivers of innate immune activation, inflammation, and mitochondrial oxidative stress relevant to aging. |
| [[Gram-positive bacteria\|Gram-positive bacteria]] | Gram-positive bacteria are those whose thick, multilayered peptidoglycan cell wall retains crystal violet-iodine complex, giving a purple Gram stain. They possess a single cytoplasmic membrane, no outer membrane and no lipopolysaccharide, and instead are decorated with anionic teichoic acid and lipoteichoic acid polymers. |
| [[Growth Factor\|Growth Factor]] | Growth factors are signaling molecules (typically Peptides or Proteins) that stimulate Cell Proliferation, Differentiation, Survival, and Migration by binding to Receptor Tyrosine Kinases or G Prot... |
| [[Growth Factor Receptor\|Growth Factor Receptor]] | Growth factor receptors are cell-surface receptors — almost all receptor tyrosine kinases — that bind secreted peptide growth factors such as EGF, FGF, VEGF, PDGF and insulin-like factors. Ligand binding induces oligomerisation and trans-autophosphorylation on cytoplasmic tyrosines, generating docking sites that trigger RAS–MAPK, PI3K–AKT and PLCγ signalling. |
