| [[GRP75\|GRP75]] | Glucose-regulated protein 75 (mortalin/HSPA9), a mitochondrial Hsp70-family chaperone that bridges the VDAC1/IP3R complex at mitochondria-associated ER membranes, connecting ER calcium release to mitochondrial calcium uptake. |
| [[GSIS\|GSIS]] | Glucose-stimulated insulin secretion (GSIS) is the process by which pancreatic beta cells sense rising glucose and exocytose insulin, maintaining glucose homeostasis; its failure underlies type 2 diabetes. |
| [[GSK-428\|GSK-428]] | GSK-428 is a quinazoline-scaffold SARM1 inhibitor with orthosteric, high-potency (nanomolar IC50) activity that blocks the TIR-domain NADase, developed for axon-degeneration and neurodegenerative indications. |
| [[GSK3\|GSK3]] | Glycogen synthase kinase 3, a serine/threonine protein kinase that regulates numerous signaling pathways, including Wnt signaling and mTOR. |
| [[GSK840\|GSK840]] | GSK840 (GSK'840) is a small-molecule RIPK3 kinase inhibitor used alongside GSK872 as a necroptosis-blocking tool compound. |
| [[GSK872\|GSK872]] | GSK872 is a small-molecule RIPK3 kinase inhibitor used as a necroptosis-blocking tool compound. |
| [[GTP\|GTP]] | Guanosine triphosphate, a purine nucleotide substrate required by cGAS to synthesize 2'3'-cGAMP. |
| [[Guanidinoacetate (GAA)\|Guanidinoacetate (GAA)]] | Guanidinoacetate is the immediate biosynthetic precursor of creatine, formed from arginine and glycine by AGAT and methylated by GAMT; it also serves as a supplemental ergogenic and therapeutic agent. |
| [[Guanidinoacetate N-methyltransferase\|Guanidinoacetate N-methyltransferase]] | The enzyme that catalyzes the final step of creatine biosynthesis, methylating guanidinoacetate to form creatine using S-adenosyl methionine as the methyl donor; deficiency causes a treatable form of cerebral creatine deficiency syndrome. |
| [[Guanylate Cyclase\|Guanylate Cyclase]] | Guanylate cyclase enzymes (soluble sGC and particulate pGC) convert GTP to cyclic GMP (cGMP); the NO-activated soluble isoform is central to vascular relaxation, and its inhibition by methylene blue underlies vasoconstrictive rescue. |
| [[Gut Microbiome\|Gut Microbiome]] | The gut microbiome is the collective community of bacteria, archaea, viruses, and fungi residing in the gastrointestinal tract, which shapes immunity, metabolism, and aging through microbial metabolites and barrier crosstalk. |
| [[Gut-Brain Axis\|Gut-Brain Axis]] | The gut-brain axis is the bidirectional communication network linking the central nervous system and the gastrointestinal tract through neural, endocrine, immune, and microbial metabolite pathways. |
| [[Gyrate Atrophy\|Gyrate Atrophy]] | A progressive chorioretinal degeneration caused by ornithine aminotransferase deficiency, characterized by circular atrophic lesions in the peripheral retina and secondary creatine depletion leading to muscle atrophy. |
| [[H1.2\|H1.2]] | H1.2 (HIST1H1C) is a linker histone variant that, when PARylated, carries poly(ADP-ribose) out of the nucleus during parthanatos; its PAR-dependent ubiquitination by Iduna blocks the death signal. |
| [[H2A.J\|H2A.J]] | H2A histone variant that accumulates in senescent cells and at persistent DNA damage sites, where it promotes chromatin accessibility and drives SASP gene expression. |
| [[H2A.X\|H2A.X]] | H2A histone variant whose Ser139-phosphorylated form (γ-H2A.X) marks DNA double-strand breaks and is required for DNA-damage-induced senescence and SASP. |
| [[H3.3\|H3.3]] | Replication-independent H3 histone variant that accumulates with age and in senescent cells, where it is deposited by HIRA and can be cleaved to drive a senescence program. |
| [[H3K122ac\|H3K122ac]] | Acetylation of histone H3 at lysine 122, a mark within the H3 core that loosens nucleosomes and is enriched at senescence-activated super-enhancers driving SASP. |
| [[H3K18ac\|H3K18ac]] | Acetylation of histone H3 at lysine 18, an enhancer/promoter acetyl mark enriched at senescence-activated super-enhancers that helps drive SASP expression. |
| [[H3K27ac\|H3K27ac]] | Acetylation of histone H3 at lysine 27, a hallmark active-enhancer mark that is remodeled at TAD scale in senescence and recruits BRD4/CEBPα to drive SASP. |
| [[H3K36me3\|H3K36me3]] | Trimethylation of histone H3 at lysine 36, a transcription-coupled mark of active gene bodies whose redistribution in senescence accompanies SASP and DDR gene expression. |
| [[H3K4me1\|H3K4me1]] | Monomethylation of histone H3 at lysine 4, a mark of active and primed enhancers that is enriched at senescence-activated enhancers and super-enhancers driving SASP. |
| [[H3K4me3\|H3K4me3]] | Trimethylation of histone H3 at lysine 4, an activating promoter mark enriched at active gene promoters whose redistribution in senescence impacts SASP and DDR gene regulation. |
| [[H3K79me2\|H3K79me2]] | Dimethylation of histone H3 at lysine 79, a gene-body mark deposited by DOT1L whose methylation (including H3K79me3) is elevated at active SASP loci in senescence. |
| [[H4K5ac\|H4K5ac]] | Acetylation of histone H4 at lysine 5, an early acetyl mark associated with active enhancers and promoters, enriched at senescence-activated super-enhancers driving SASP. |
| [[HAF-1\|HAF-1]] | HAF-1 is an inner mitochondrial membrane ABC transporter in C. elegans that exports peptides from the mitochondrial matrix to the cytosol, initiating the mitochondrial unfolded protein response via ATFS-1. |
| [[Hair Cells\|Hair Cells]] | Specialized mechanosensory epithelial cells of the cochlea that transduce sound into neural signals; highly vulnerable to oxidative stress, and preserved by SIRT3-dependent antioxidant defense under caloric restriction. |
| [[Hallmarks of Aging\|Hallmarks of Aging]] | The hallmarks of aging are the interconnected molecular and cellular changes that drive progressive loss of physiological integrity across organisms. |
| [[Hamartoma\|Hamartoma]] | A hamartoma is a benign tumor-like lesion composed of disorganized mature cells native to the tissue of origin; in mTORopathies it reflects mTORC1 hyperactivation rather than a protective arrest. |
| [[Hannum Clock\|Hannum Clock]] | A blood-based epigenetic clock estimating chronological age from DNA methylation, trained on whole-blood samples. |
| [[HDAC inhibitors\|HDAC inhibitors]] | Chemical properties: HDAC inhibitors is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[HDAC3\|HDAC3]] | Histone deacetylase 3, a class I HDAC that partners with the reader HDAC4 to restrain AP-1-driven SASP enhancer activation in senescence. |
| [[HDAC4\|HDAC4]] | Histone deacetylase 4, a catalytically inactive class IIa HDAC that acts as an epigenetic reader of H3K27ac and normally buffers the AP-1-driven SASP program until it is degraded upon senescence entry. |
| [[HDAC6\|HDAC6]] | Histone deacetylase 6 that deacetylates MFN1 to promote fusion under glucose deprivation. |
| [[HDL\|HDL]] | High-density lipoprotein; anti-atherogenic lipoprotein that mediates reverse cholesterol transport; protects LDL against oxidation; carries PON1. |
| [[Head and neck cancer\|Head and neck cancer]] | Head and neck cancer (HNC) is sensitive to ferroptosis inducers (GPX4 inhibitors, artesunate) and to CISD2 suppression that raises mitochondrial iron and lipid ROS. |
| [[Health and Retirement Study\|Health and Retirement Study]] | Longitudinal, nationally representative U.S. panel study of Americans over 50, used to assess aging, health, and economic outcomes. |
| [[Healthspan\|Healthspan]] | Healthspan refers to the period of life during which an individual remains generally healthy and free from serious disease, disability, or functional decline. It is distinguished from Lifespan ... |
| [[Heart\|Heart]] | Muscular pump organ whose cardiomyocytes, conduction, and natriuretic signaling set systemic perfusion. |
| [[Heart Failure\|Heart Failure]] | Heart failure is a complex clinical syndrome where the heart is unable to pump sufficiently to maintain blood flow. It is a common end-stage of Cardiovascular Disease and Hypertension. |
| [[Heart Rate Variability\|Heart Rate Variability]] | The beat-to-beat variation in interbeat intervals, a noninvasive marker of autonomic balance and recovery capacity used to guide hormetic stress dosing. |
| [[Haptoglobin\|Haptoglobin]] | Plasma glycoprotein that binds hemoglobin released by hemolysis and clears the complex via CD91 without liberating iron; low haptoglobin marks intravascular hemolysis. |
| [[Heat Shock Proteins\|Heat Shock Proteins]] | Conserved chaperones induced by proteotoxic and thermal stress that refold misfolded proteins and support mitochondrial and cellular proteostasis. |
| [[HEK293\|HEK293]] | A human embryonic kidney cell line widely used for recombinant protein expression, transfection, and virology. |
| [[HeLa\|HeLa]] | The first immortal human cell line, derived from cervical adenocarcinoma, foundational to modern cell biology. |
| [[HeLa Cells\|HeLa Cells]] | Immortalized human cervical carcinoma epithelial cell line; widely used in molecular and virology research including miRNA biogenesis assays. |
| [[Helminth\|Helminth]] | Helminths are parasitic worms — nematodes, trematodes and cestodes — that have co-evolved with vertebrate hosts over tens of millions of years. Most infections are chronic and asymptomatic, and helminths exert a strongly T helper 2 / regulatory T cell immunomodulatory effect that is the mechanistic basis of the hygiene hypothesis. |
| [[Hematopoiesis\|Hematopoiesis]] | Hematopoiesis is the lifelong process of blood-cell formation from hematopoietic stem cells in the bone marrow, governed by transcription factors, cytokines, and the niche, and declining with age. |
| [[Hematopoietic Stem Cell\|Hematopoietic Stem Cell]] | Multipotent stem cells that give rise to all blood cell types through haematopoiesis |
| [[Hemoglobin\|Hemoglobin]] | - Methemoglobin: An oxidized form of hemoglobin (Fe³⁺) that cannot transport oxygen. |
| [[Hemopexin\|Hemopexin]] | Plasma heme-binding glycoprotein delivering heme to CD91 receptors for HO-1 degradation; its depletion marks intravascular hemolysis and unbuffered heme becomes a DAMP. |
| [[Heparin-Induced Thrombocytopenia\|Heparin-Induced Thrombocytopenia]] | Heparin-induced thrombocytopenia is an immune-mediated, highly prothrombotic adverse drug reaction to heparin in which IgG antibodies against platelet factor 4-heparin complexes activate platelets via Fc gamma RIIa, causing thrombocytopenia with paradoxically high rates of venous and arterial thrombosis. |
| [[Hepatic Steatosis\|Hepatic Steatosis]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Hepatic Steatosis is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, e... |
| [[Hepatic Stellate Cells\|Hepatic Stellate Cells]] | Hepatic stellate cells (HSC) are liver-specific pericytes that store vitamin A, regulate extracellular matrix turnover, and when senescent secrete SASP factors driving obesity-associated hepatocellular carcinoma. |
| [[Hepatitis B Virus\|Hepatitis B Virus]] | Hepatotropic DNA virus causing acute and chronic hepatitis, cirrhosis, and hepatocellular carcinoma |
| [[Hepatosplenomegaly\|Hepatosplenomegaly]] | Hepatosplenomegaly is the simultaneous enlargement of both liver and spleen. It is a sign rather than a disease, produced by any process that congests, infiltrates, or increases the workload of the reticuloendothelial system — most commonly portal hypertension, chronic infection, infiltrative disease, or haematological malignancy. |
| [[Herpes Zoster\|Herpes Zoster]] | Reactivation of latent varicella zoster virus causing a painful vesicular rash (shingles); risk rises with aging and immunodeficiency. |
| [[Hesperetin\|Hesperetin]] | Citrus flavonoid with antioxidative, antifibrotic, and senescence-modulating activity via autophagic flux restoration and ROS reduction. |
| [[Heterochromatin\|Heterochromatin]] | Heterochromatin is a tightly packed form of DNA or condensed Chromatin, which is typically transcriptionally inactive. It plays a vital role in maintaining the structure of chromosomes and prot... |
| [[Heteroplasmy\|Heteroplasmy]] | The coexistence of wild-type and mutant mitochondrial DNA molecules within a single cell, tissue, or individual; the fraction of mutant genomes determines disease penetrance and is the target of mtDNA-editing therapies. |
| [[Hexokinase-1\|Hexokinase-1]] | Hexokinase-1 (HK1) is the neuronal glycolytic gate whose PAR binding and VDAC dissociation couples PARP1 hyperactivation to glycolytic collapse in parthanatos. |
| [[HG-9-91-01\|HG-9-91-01]] | HG-9-91-01 is a salt-inducible kinase (SIK) inhibitor that suppresses necroptosis by disrupting the RIPK3/MLKL interaction. |
| [[HGF\|HGF]] | Hepatocyte growth factor (HGF, HPTA, scatter factor) is a secreted trypsin-like serine protease zymogen that acts as the single ligand for the MET receptor tyrosine kinase. It drives epithelial motility and mitogenesis, is essential for liver regeneration and embryonic development, and is a major paracrine driver of MET-addicted cancer. |
| [[HGPS\|HGPS]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: HGPS is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, environmental,... |
| [[HIF-1α\|HIF-1α]] | Hypoxia-inducible factor 1 alpha; master transcriptional regulator of the hypoxic response, repressed by SIRT6 and de-repressed by SIRT3 loss. |
| [[HIF-2α\|HIF-2α]] | Hypoxia-inducible factor 2-alpha (HIF-2α, EPAS1) is an oxygen-regulated PAS-domain transcription factor that, with HIF-1α, controls hypoxic gene programs; SIRT1 deacetylates and modulates HIF-2α. |
| [[High-fat Diet\|High-fat Diet]] | A high-fat diet (HFD) is an experimental diet supplying 40-70% of calories from fat, most commonly lard-based at 60% kcal fat. In C57BL/6 mice fed for 12-24 weeks it produces obesity, insulin resistance, dyslipidaemia and hepatic steatosis, with mild steatohepatitis but minimal fibrosis. |
| [[HIIT\|HIIT]] | High-intensity interval training, a potent mitohormetic stimulus that generates an acute ROS and lactate spike to trigger PGC-1α and mitochondrial biogenesis. |
| [[Hippo Pathway\|Hippo Pathway]] | Definition: Hippo Pathway is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and en... |
| [[HIRA\|HIRA]] | Histone chaperone that, with ASF1a and UBN1, deposits the H3.3 histone variant and is essential for senescence-associated heterochromatin foci (SAHF) formation. |
| [[Histamine\|Histamine]] | Biochemical properties: Histamine is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic fl... |
| [[Histamine Intolerance\|Histamine Intolerance]] | Histamine intolerance is a condition of histamine excess from impaired degradation (often DAO deficiency or inhibition), causing flushing, headache, hives, and GI symptoms after histamine-rich foods. |
| [[Histone\|Histone]] | Histones are basic nuclear proteins that package DNA into Nucleosomes, the fundamental unit of Chromatin. The core histones (Histone H2A, Histone H2B, Histone H3, Histone H4) form an octamer wrappe... |
| [[Histone Acetylation\|Histone Acetylation]] | Histone acetylation is a dynamic epigenetic modification where an acetyl group is added to lysine residues on histone tails, typically leading to a more open chromatin structure and increased g... |
| [[Histone Crotonylation\|Histone Crotonylation]] | Reversible lysine crotonylation (Kcr) of histones, a non-acetyl acylation mark deposited on chromatin from crotonyl-CoA that regulates gene expression; targeted by SIRT3 lysine decrotonylase activity. |
| [[Histone Demethylase\|Histone Demethylase]] | Enzyme that removes methyl groups from histone lysine or arginine residues, dynamically regulating chromatin state and gene expression. |
| [[Histone H2AX\|Histone H2AX]] | Histone H2AX is a variant of the core H2A histone that constitutes roughly 2-25% of total H2A depending on cell type, and whose phosphorylation at Ser139 (gamma-H2AX) is the earliest and most widely used molecular marker of DNA double-strand breaks. |
| [[Histone H3\|Histone H3]] | Histone H3 is one of the five core histone proteins that form the Nucleosome structure in eukaryotic chromatin. Along with Histone H4, Histone H2A, and Histone H2B, it packages DNA into structural ... |
| [[Histone Methylation\|Histone Methylation]] | Histone methylation adds methyl groups to lysine or arginine residues on histones, activating or repressing transcription depending on the site and degree of methylation. |
| [[Histone Modification\|Histone Modification]] | Histone Modification refers to the various post-translational modifications (PTMs) that occur on the ''tails'' of histone proteins. These modifications are a central part of the Epigenetics, dict... |
| [[HIV\|HIV]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: HIV is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, environmental, ... |
| [[HMG-CoA reductase\|HMG-CoA reductase]] | HMG-CoA reductase is the rate-limiting enzyme of the mevalonate pathway; its inhibition by statins lowers CoQ10/ubiquinone availability, converging on FSP1 and sensitizing cells to ferroptosis. |
| [[HMGA1\|HMGA1]] | High-mobility-group AT-hook chromatin architectural protein, downregulated during senescence, that contributes to heterochromatin formation and the senescence-associated growth arrest. |
| [[HMGB1\|HMGB1]] | High Mobility Group Box 1, a nuclear alarmin and potent SASP factor released from senescent cells that drives inflammation and immune activation via TLR and RAGE signaling |
| [[HMGB2\|HMGB2]] | High-mobility-group-box chromatin architectural protein that is depleted upon senescence entry and that protects SASP gene loci from repressive heterochromatinization in oncogene-induced senescence. |
| [[HMGCS2\|HMGCS2]] | 3-hydroxy-3-methylglutaryl-CoA synthase 2 (HMGCS2) is the rate-limiting mitochondrial enzyme of ketogenesis, converting acetyl-CoA to HMG-CoA; it is activated by SIRT5 desuccinylation. |
| [[HO-1\|HO-1]] | Heme oxygenase 1 (HO-1) is a stress-inducible enzyme that catalyzes the rate-limiting step in heme degradation, producing biliverdin, carbon monoxide (CO), and ferrous iron (Fe²⁺). It is a key ... |
| [[Homer1\|Homer1]] | Scaffold protein at excitatory synapses whose expression is reduced in PINK1 knockout neurons, contributing to impaired synaptic plasticity. |
| [[Homocysteine\|Homocysteine]] | Biochemical properties: Homocysteine is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic... |
| [[Homologous Recombination\|Homologous Recombination]] | Definition: Homologous Recombination is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling path... |
| [[Hormesis\|Hormesis]] | Hormesis is a biological phenomenon where a beneficial effect (improved health, stress tolerance, growth, or longevity) results from exposure to low doses of an agent that is otherwise toxic or let... |
| [[Hormesis in Non-Mitochondrial Systems\|Hormesis in Non-Mitochondrial Systems]] | Beyond mitohormesis, many biological processes follow the same biphasic dose-response pattern where a mild stressor triggers adaptive benefit. This note synthesizes the cross-domain evidence and shared molecular architecture. |
| [[Hormetic Window\|Hormetic Window]] | The Hormetic Window is the dose range over which a stressor or toxicant produces adaptive, beneficial effects rather than damage. It is the quantitative boundary separating Hormesis from toxici... |
| [[Horvath Clock\|Horvath Clock]] | A pan-tissue epigenetic clock estimating chronological age from DNA methylation at 353 CpG sites, applicable across the human body. |
| [[HP1alpha\|HP1alpha]] | Heterochromatin protein 1 alpha (CBX5), a chromatin reader of H3K9me3 that, with KAP1 and SIRT6, silences LINE-1 retrotransposons whose derepression fuels SASP in senescence. |
| [[HRI\|HRI]] | Heme-regulated inhibitor (HRI, EIF2AK1) is a serine/threonine kinase that phosphorylates eIF2α to repress translation and initiate the integrated stress response; it is activated by heme deficiency and by mitochondrial stress via DELE1. |
| [[Hrk\|Hrk]] | Hrk (DP5, harakiri, BID3) is a pro-apoptotic BH3-only member of the Bcl-2 family. It is 91 residues long, localises to mitochondria, is transcriptionally induced by p53 and by neuronal stress, and kills by selectively binding the anti-apoptotic proteins Bcl-2 and Bcl-xL rather than by directly activating Bax or Bak. |
| [[hs-CRP\|hs-CRP]] | High-sensitivity C-reactive protein, a sensitive circulating marker of low-grade systemic inflammation and a proxy for recovery and mitohormetic status. |
| [[HSC70\|HSC70]] | HSC70 (HSPA8, also HSP73) is the constitutively expressed Hsp70 family chaperone. Unlike inducible HSP70, HSC70 is present at high basal level and performs housekeeping proteostasis, and it is the obligatory cytosolic receptor that delivers KFERQ-motif substrates to LAMP2A for chaperone-mediated autophagy. |
| [[HSF1\|HSF1]] | HSF1 (heat shock factor 1, HSTF1) is the mammalian stress-activated transcription factor that trimerises on heat and other proteotoxic stress, binds inverted NGAAN repeats in heat shock element promoters, and induces the chaperone network. It is held inactive by Hsp90 and is a validated cancer dependency. |
| [[HSL\|HSL]] | Hormone-sensitive lipase (HSL, LIPE) is the second-step adipocyte lipase that hydrolyzes diacylglycerol and cholesteryl esters downstream of ATGL, mobilizing fatty acids during lipolysis. |
| [[HSP27\|HSP27]] | Small heat-shock protein (HSPB1) chaperone; stabilizes SASP mRNAs via the p38/MK2 axis and marks senescence. |
| [[HSP70\|HSP70]] | HSP70 (Heat Shock Protein 70 kDa) is a family of highly conserved molecular chaperones that assist in Protein Folding, prevent Protein Aggregation, and facilitate Proteostasis. Major family members... |
| [[HSPA8\|HSPA8]] | Heat shock cognate protein 70 (HSPA8 / HSC70), a constitutively expressed HSP70 family chaperone that bridges ATM and p38 MAPK signaling and is the interaction node disrupted by apigenin to suppress the SASP in senescent cells. |
| [[HTT\|HTT]] | Gene encoding huntingtin, a large scaffold protein whose CAG repeat expansion causes Huntington's disease. |
| [[Human Endogenous Retrovirus\|Human Endogenous Retrovirus]] | HERV, ancient retroviral sequences constituting ~8% of the human genome that are derepressed in senescence by DNA demethylation and heterochromatin decompaction, driving SASP and paracrine senescence spread. |
| [[Human Growth Hormone\|Human Growth Hormone]] | A pituitary peptide hormone that promotes somatic growth; indirectly activates mTOR and is considered a pro-aging hormone when elevated exogenously. |
| [[Human Serum Albumin\|Human Serum Albumin]] | Human serum albumin (HSA) is the most abundant plasma protein and a biocompatible nanocarrier used for drug delivery, including fisetin-loaded HSA nanoparticles. |
| [[Humanin\|Humanin]] | Humanin is a 24-amino acid peptide encoded by a cryptic open reading frame within the mitochondrial 16S ribosomal RNA gene. It is considered a founding member of the class of Mitokines or mitochond... |
| [[Huntingtin\|Huntingtin]] | The protein encoded by the HTT gene, whose mutated form with expanded polyglutamine tracts causes Huntington's Disease. |
| [[Huntington's Disease\|Huntington's Disease]] | Huntington''s Disease (HD) is an inherited neurodegenerative disorder characterized by motor dysfunction, cognitive decline, and psychiatric symptoms. |
| [[Huntington's Disease (HD)\|Huntington's Disease (HD)]] | An autosomal dominant neurodegenerative disorder caused by CAG repeat expansion in HTT, producing mutant huntingtin and progressive motor, cognitive, and psychiatric decline. |
| [[HuR\|HuR]] | HuR (ELAVL1, Hu antigen R) is a ubiquitously expressed RNA-binding protein of the ELAVL family with three RNA-recognition motifs that binds AU-rich elements in 3-prime UTRs and stabilises the bound mRNAs. It is an m6A reader that cooperates with IGF2BP1, and it couples ambient temperature to expression of heat-shock and metabolic mRNAs. |
| [[Hutchinson-Gilford Progeria Syndrome\|Hutchinson-Gilford Progeria Syndrome]] | Hutchinson-Gilford Progeria Syndrome (HGPS) is an extremely rare, fatal genetic condition characterized by an appearance of dramatic, rapid Aging beginning in childhood. It serves as a critical... |
| [[Hydrocele\|Hydrocele]] | A hydrocele is an abnormal accumulation of serous fluid in a body cavity lined by peritoneum or a tunica, most commonly the tunica vaginalis of the scrotum. Adult hydrocele is overwhelmingly a consequence of lymphatic filariasis worldwide, where it is the commonest chronic manifestation of Wuchereria bancrofti infection. |
| [[Hydrogen Peroxide\|Hydrogen Peroxide]] | Hydrogen Peroxide ($H_2O_2$) is a non-radical but highly reactive oxygen species (ROS). It is a key signaling molecule in cells but can also contribute to oxidative damage. |
| [[Hydrogen Sulfide\|Hydrogen Sulfide]] | Hydrogen sulfide (H2S) is a colourless, water-soluble gas and the third member of the mammalian gasotransmitter family alongside nitric oxide and carbon monoxide. It is generated by the transsulfuration enzymes CBS and CSE and acts largely by S-sulfhydrating protein cysteines to hydropersulfides. |
| [[Hydroxychloroquine\|Hydroxychloroquine]] | Hydroxychloroquine is a 4-aminoquinoline antimalarial and immunomodulator that also inhibits autophagy by raising lysosomal pH. |
| [[Hydroxyeicosatetraenoic acids\|Hydroxyeicosatetraenoic acids]] | HETEs are monohydroxylated derivatives of arachidonic acid produced by lipoxygenases, cytochrome P450 enzymes, or non-enzymatic lipid peroxidation; bioactive oxylipins and markers of oxidative stress. |
| [[Hyperfunction Theory of Aging\|Hyperfunction Theory of Aging]] | Blagosklonny's theory that aging is the quasi-programmed, mTOR-driven continuation of developmental growth programs after growth should have stopped; rapamycin extends lifespan by suppressing growth. |
| [[Hyperlipidemia\|Hyperlipidemia]] | A metabolic disorder characterized by elevated concentrations of lipids (cholesterol and/or triglycerides) in the blood, commonly induced by chronic mTOR inhibition with rapamycin. |
| [[Hypertension\|Hypertension]] | Hypertension is a chronic medical condition characterized by persistently elevated arterial blood pressure. It is a major risk factor for Cardiovascular Disease, Stroke, Chronic Kidney Disease,... |
| [[Hypotension\|Hypotension]] | Hypotension is abnormally low blood pressure that impairs tissue perfusion; severe refractory vasoplegic hypotension is rescued with vasopressors and methylene blue, which inhibits guanylate cyclase and NO signaling. |
| [[Hypothalamus\|Hypothalamus]] | Small diencephalic brain structure that integrates autonomic, endocrine, and behavioral responses; regulates feeding, sleep, thermoregulation, and reproduction through nuclei such as the dorsomedial hypothalamus (DMH) and lateral hypothalamus (LH). |
| [[Hypoxia\|Hypoxia]] | Hypoxia is a condition of low oxygen levels in tissues that acts as a stressful stimulus, often triggering Macroautophagy as a survival mechanism in tumor cells. |
| [[Iba1\|Iba1]] | Ionized calcium-binding adaptor molecule 1 (AIF1), a microglial/macrophage actin-crosslinking protein used as a marker of myeloid cells. |
| [[IC50\|IC50]] | The half-maximal inhibitory concentration—the drug concentration producing 50% of maximal inhibition on a monotonic sigmoidal dose-response curve; contrasted with hormetic/biphasic curves where a single inflection point is undefined. |
| [[ICAM-1\|ICAM-1]] | ICAM-1 (CD54) is a single-pass type I cell-surface glycoprotein of the immunoglobulin superfamily and the principal ligand for the beta2 integrins LFA-1 and Mac-1. It mediates leukocyte firm adhesion and transendothelial migration, is induced by inflammatory cytokines, and doubles as the entry receptor for major-group rhinoviruses. |
| [[IDH2\|IDH2]] | Mitochondrial isocitrate dehydrogenase 2; deacetylated and activated by SIRT3 to regenerate NADPH and reduced glutathione, suppressing mitochondrial ROS; also required for SIRT3-dependent antioxidant responses during dietary restriction. |
| [[Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis\|Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis]] | Progressive age-related lung fibrosis driven by senescent fibroblast accumulation and a pro-fibrotic SASP (IL-11, leukotrienes). |
| [[IDO1\|IDO1]] | Indoleamine 2,3-dioxygenase 1 (IDO1) is a heme-containing enzyme that catalyzes the first and rate-limiting step in the kynurenine pathway of tryptophan catabolism, converting tryptophan to N-formylkynurenine. |
| [[IDO2\|IDO2]] | Indoleamine 2,3-dioxygenase 2 (IDO2) is a less active paralog of IDO1 that catalyzes the same tryptophan-to-N-formylkynurenine reaction but with much lower catalytic efficiency, and may act as a negative regulator of IDO1. |
| [[Iduna\|Iduna]] | Iduna (RNF146) is a PAR-dependent E3 ubiquitin ligase whose WWE domain binds poly(ADP-ribose); it ubiquitinates PARylated proteins such as H1.2 for proteasomal degradation and protects against parthanatos. |
| [[IFITM3\|IFITM3]] | Interferon-induced transmembrane protein 3, an antiviral restriction factor enriched in plasma exosomes of elderly individuals that induces paracrine senescence. |
| [[IFN-β\|IFN-β]] | Interferon beta is a type I interferon and a primary output of the cGAS-STING pathway in senescent cells, reinforcing arrest and driving sterile inflammaging. |
| [[IFNAR1\|IFNAR1]] | Interferon alpha/beta receptor subunit 1, the type I interferon receptor subunit that transmits microglial IFN-I signaling to neurons. |
| [[Ifosfamide\|Ifosfamide]] | Ifosfamide is a nitrogen-mustard alkylating chemotherapeutic prodrug whose neurotoxic metabolite chloroacetaldehyde can cause encephalopathy, effectively reversed by methylene blue. |
| [[iFSP1\|iFSP1]] | iFSP1 is the first-in-class, human-specific small-molecule inhibitor of FSP1 (ferroptosis suppressor protein 1), binding the F360 pocket (IC50 ≈ 103 nM) and synergizing with GPX4 inhibitors to induce ferroptosis. |
| [[IgE\|IgE]] | Allergy-driving antibody isotype produced after IL-4/IL-13 class switching; triggers mast cell degranulation. |
| [[IGF-Akt Signaling\|IGF-Akt Signaling]] | A pro-survival, pro-hypertrophic growth signaling axis centered on insulin-like growth factor receptor activation of Akt, repressed by SIRT6 to protect against cardiac hypertrophy. |
| [[IGF1\|IGF1]] | Insulin-like growth factor 1 is a hormone downstream of growth hormone that promotes cell survival, growth, and immune reconstitution while suppressing autophagy; low IGF1 signaling is associated with lifespan extension. |
| [[IGF1R\|IGF1R]] | IGF1R (CD221) is a ubiquitously expressed receptor tyrosine kinase of the insulin receptor family. It is a disulfide-linked alpha2beta2 tetramer that signals through IRS/Shc to PI3K-AKT-mTOR and RAS-MAPK, is obligatory for prenatal growth, and is a driver and validated-on-target-but-hard-to-drug cancer dependency. |
| [[IGFBP3\|IGFBP3]] | IGF-binding protein 3 is a direct p53 target gene and p53-arm SASP factor that modulates IGF signaling and supports immunosurveillance of senescent cells. |
| [[IGFBP5\|IGFBP5]] | Insulin-like growth factor-binding protein 5, a SASP factor that modulates IGF signaling and contributes to senescence reinforcement, developmental patterning, and tissue remodeling. |
| [[IkappaB\|IkappaB]] | IκB (inhibitor of NF-κB) proteins are a family of cytoplasmic inhibitors that sequester NFκB transcription factors in the Cytosol, preventing their nuclear translocation. The best-characterized... |
| [[IkappaBalpha\|IkappaBalpha]] | IκBα (encoded by NFKBIA) is the prototypical member of the IkappaB family of NFκB inhibitors. It is the primary negative regulator of the canonical NFκB signaling pathway, controlling the activation kinetics and duration of inflammatory and immune responses. |
| [[IKK complex\|IKK complex]] | The IκB kinase complex that phosphorylates IκB inhibitors to activate NF-κB signaling. |
| [[IKKalpha\|IKKalpha]] | IKKα (IKK1, CHUK) — catalytic IκB kinase subunit of the NF-κB complex; partner of IKKβ in the canonical pathway and, as an IKKα homodimer, the engine of non-canonical NF-κB signaling, with additional chromatin-regulatory roles. |
| [[IKKbeta\|IKKbeta]] | IKKβ (IκB kinase β) is the primary catalytic subunit of the IKK complex — the signal integrator for NFκB activation. It phosphorylates IkappaBalpha (and other IkappaB proteins) at Ser32/Ser36, ... |
| [[IKKepsilon\|IKKepsilon]] | Inducible I kappa B kinase (IKKε); phosphorylates XIAP at Ser430, triggering autoubiquitination and degradation to sensitize cells to apoptosis. |
| [[IL-1 Receptor\|IL-1 Receptor]] | The interleukin-1 receptor (IL-1R1) is the primary signaling receptor for IL-1α and IL-1β, activating MyD88-dependent NF-κB and MAPK pathways to drive inflammatory gene transcription. |
| [[IL-10\|IL-10]] | Interleukin-10 is an anti-inflammatory cytokine that suppresses pro-inflammatory signaling and limits tissue damage during immune responses. |
| [[IL-12\|IL-12]] | Interleukin-12 is a pro-inflammatory cytokine that drives Th1 differentiation and IFN-gamma production in cell-mediated immunity. |
| [[IL-13\|IL-13]] | Interleukin-13 is a Th2-associated cytokine that drives allergic inflammation, mucus production, and alternative macrophage activation. |
| [[IL-18\|IL-18]] | Interleukin-18, a pro-inflammatory cytokine of the IL-1 family implicated in inflammasome signaling and senescence. |
| [[IL-1β\|IL-1b]] | Interleukin-1 beta (IL-1β) is a potent pro-inflammatory cytokine and a key component of the Senescence-Associated Secretory Phenotype (SASP). It is processed by the NLRP3 inflammasome and activates NF-κB signaling to drive chronic inflammation in aging and disease. Elevated IL-1β contributes to inflammaging, atherosclerosis, and neurodegenerative conditions. |
| [[IL-1RaCP\|IL-1RaCP]] | The interleukin-1 receptor accessory protein (IL-1RAcP, IL1RAP) is a shared co-receptor for IL-1R1, IL-33's receptor ST2, and IL-18R1, recruiting MyD88 to the receptor TIR domains to drive NF-κB and MAPK signaling. |
| [[IL-1α\|IL-1α]] | Interleukin-1 alpha is a cell-associated pro-inflammatory cytokine and master upstream initiator of the SASP via NF-κB and mTOR-regulated translation. |
| [[IL-22\|IL-22]] | An IL-10 family cytokine that drives thymic epithelial cell regeneration and thymic recovery after injury; produced by TH17 cells and innate lymphoid cells. |
| [[IL-4\|IL-4]] | Interleukin-4 is a Th2 cytokine that drives B cell class switching to IgE, alternative macrophage activation, and type 2 immune responses. |
| [[IL-6\|IL-6]] | Interleukin-6 is a pleiotropic pro-inflammatory cytokine and a hallmark component of the SASP, acting via IL-6R/gp130 and JAK/STAT3. |
| [[IL-6R\|IL-6R]] | Interleukin-6 receptor (CD126), the ligand-binding subunit of the gp130-family receptor complex for IL-6 that activates JAK–STAT3 signalling — the receptor arm of the autocrine loop that sustains the senescence-associated secretory phenotype. |
| [[IL-7\|IL-7]] | A 25 kDa glycoprotein cytokine critical for T cell survival, differentiation, and homeostatic proliferation; produced by thymic and bone marrow stromal cells. |
| [[IL-8\|IL-8]] | IL-8 (Interleukin-8, CXCL8) is a pro-inflammatory Chemokine that recruits Neutrophils and other immune cells to sites of Inflammation and infection. It is produced by Macrophages, Epithelial Cells,... |
| [[Imatinib\|Imatinib]] | First-generation BCR-ABL and KIT/PDGFR tyrosine-kinase inhibitor used in CML and GIST. |
| [[Imiquimod\|Imiquimod]] | A synthetic imidazoquinoline TLR7 agonist used topically to treat anogenital warts and actinic keratoses, working by provoking a local interferon-alpha-dominant innate immune response against HPV-infected or transformed keratinocytes. |
| [[Immune Evasion\|Immune Evasion]] | The capacity of senescent and tumor cells to escape cytotoxic immune clearance via checkpoint ligands and immunosuppressive microenvironments. |
| [[Immune Surveillance\|Immune Surveillance]] | The process by which immune cells detect and eliminate threats including senescent cells, pathogens, and malignant cells. |
| [[Immune System\|Immune System]] | The immune system is a complex network of cells, tissues, and organs that work together to defend the body against harmful pathogens and maintain overall health. |
| [[Immunotherapy\|Immunotherapy]] | Cancer treatment harnessing the immune system via checkpoint blockade, cell therapies, and cancer vaccines. |
| [[Incoherent Bivalent Motif\|Incoherent Bivalent Motif]] | A minimal three-node network motif in which a drug-inhibited Target relays two opposing (incoherent) signals to a downstream Output—proposed as the universal structural basis of drug hormesis. |
| [[Indirect Calorimetry\|Indirect Calorimetry]] | A method for measuring energy expenditure from oxygen consumption and carbon dioxide production, typically using a respiratory quotient to convert gas exchange into energy units. The standard approach for measuring resting metabolic rate and exercise energy cost. |
| [[Indole-3-propionic acid\|Indole-3-propionic acid]] | Gut microbiota-derived tryptophan metabolite with potent antioxidant, AhR/PXR-mediated anti-inflammatory and barrier-protective effects; potentiates SIRT3 via AhR and the NAD salvage pathway to protect against HFpEF diastolic dysfunction. |
| [[Induced Pluripotent Stem Cells\|Induced Pluripotent Stem Cells]] | iPSCs are somatic cells reprogrammed to pluripotency by defined factors (OSKM), avoiding embryo use; patient-derived iPSC lines model human cGAS-STING signaling in neurons, microglia, and motor neurons. |
| [[Inducible Nitric Oxide Synthase\|Inducible Nitric Oxide Synthase]] | Inducible nitric oxide synthase (iNOS, NOS2) is the cytokine-induced isoform of NOS that produces high-output nitric oxide during inflammation; selectively inhibited by aminoguanidine. |
| [[INF2\|INF2]] | ER-bound inverted formin 2 that regulates actin assembly for mitochondrial constriction before DRP1 recruitment. |
| [[Inflammaging\|Inflammaging]] | Inflammaging is a state of chronic, low-grade, systemic inflammation that develops with advanced age and is a major risk factor for many age-related diseases. |
| [[Inflammation\|Inflammation]] | Inflammation is the body''s complex biological response to harmful stimuli, such as pathogens, damaged cells, or irritants. It is a key context for the production of Adrenochrome. |
| [[Inflammatory Bowel Disease\|Inflammatory Bowel Disease]] | Chronic, relapsing inflammatory disorders of the gastrointestinal tract, principally ulcerative colitis and Crohn's disease; intestinal-epithelial necroptosis via RIPK3 contributes to pathology when the FADD–caspase-8–c-FLIP suppressive complex is lost. |
| [[Inflammatory Diseases\|Inflammatory Diseases]] | Chronic diseases driven by unresolved inflammation, from arthritis and IBD to psoriasis and atherosclerosis. |
| [[Information Theory of Aging\|Information Theory of Aging]] | A framework, advanced by David Sinclair, proposing that aging is primarily a loss of epigenetic/nuclear information ("software") rather than irreversible hardware damage such as mutations. |
| [[Innate Immune System\|Innate Immune System]] | Definition: Innate Immune System is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways... |
| [[Inner Mitochondrial Membrane\|Inner Mitochondrial Membrane]] | Highly folded inner membrane of mitochondria that forms cristae and houses the OXPHOS complexes. |
| [[INR\|INR]] | International Normalized Ratio, a dimensionless standardization of the prothrombin time that corrects for the sensitivity of the thromboplastin reagent used; the primary monitoring parameter for warfarin anticoagulation. |
| [[Insulin\|Insulin]] | Pancreatic anabolic peptide hormone regulating glucose uptake, lipid synthesis, and growth; central to metabolic health, mTOR/AMPK balance, and aging. |
| [[Insulin Receptor\|Insulin Receptor]] | The INSR-encoded receptor tyrosine kinase that binds insulin, IGF-I and IGF-II and is the obligate first step in glucose homeostasis; activation triggers IRS-PI3K-AKT signalling and is opposed by PTP1B dephosphorylation. |
| [[Insulin Secretion\|Insulin Secretion]] | Definition: Insulin Secretion is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways an... |
| [[Insulin Signaling\|Insulin Signaling]] | Insulin signaling is a conserved growth factor pathway that regulates metabolism, growth, and lifespan. It is a central suppressor of Autophagy via PI3K-Akt Signaling-dependent activation of mT... |
| [[Integrase\|Integrase]] | HIV-1 integrase, the retroviral endonuclease that inserts viral DNA into the host chromosome via 3'-processing and strand transfer; the target of the INSTI class of antiretroviral drugs including raltegravir and dolutegravir. |
| [[Integrated Stress Response\|Integrated Stress Response]] | A common adaptive pathway in eukaryotic cells (ISR) triggered by various stressors (ER stress, amino acid starvation, heme deficiency, viral infection). It centers on the phosphorylation of EIF2A, ... |
| [[Interferon\|Interferon]] | Interferon (IFN) — family of antiviral and immunomodulatory cytokines (types I, II, III) induced downstream of TLR3/TRIF, RIG-I/MDA5, and cGAS-STING sensing. |
| [[Interferon-gamma\|Interferon-gamma]] | Interferon-gamma (IFNγ) is a pro-inflammatory cytokine that plays a central role in innate and adaptive immunity, potently inducing IDO1 expression and activating macrophages. |
| [[Interferon-Stimulated Genes\|Interferon-Stimulated Genes]] | Collection of genes transcriptionally induced by type I interferon signaling that encode antiviral and inflammatory effector proteins. |
| [[Interleukin 11\|Interleukin 11]] | Interleukin-11 is a senescence-induced gp130/STAT3-driving osteokine and pro-fibrotic SASP factor linked to osteoporosis, pulmonary and cardiac fibrosis. |
| [[Interleukin 33\|Interleukin 33]] | Interleukin-33 is an IL-1-family alarmin and IL-1β-dependent SASP factor secreted by senescent hepatic stellate cells that promotes hepatocellular carcinoma. |
| [[Intermedin\|Intermedin]] | Adrenomedullin 2 (intermedin), a calcitonin-family peptide hormone that enhances mitochondrial bioenergetics via AMPK/SIRT3-mediated cristae remodeling in vascular calcification. |
| [[Intervertebral Disc Degeneration\|Intervertebral Disc Degeneration]] | Age-related deterioration of the intervertebral disc, involving nucleus pulposus cell loss, oxidative stress, and inflammation |
| [[Intestinal Ischemia-Reperfusion Injury\|Intestinal Ischemia-Reperfusion Injury]] | Injury to the intestinal mucosa upon reperfusion after ischemia, characterized by epithelial ROS accumulation, apoptosis, and barrier disruption |
| [[Intestinal Stem Cell\|Intestinal Stem Cell]] | Intestinal stem cells (ISCs) are LGR5+ stem cells at the base of intestinal crypts that drive epithelial renewal. They are vulnerable to mtDNA mutations and NAD+ depletion, which impair their regenerative capacity through UPRmt activation. |
| [[Intestine\|Intestine]] | The gastrointestinal tube from stomach to anus that completes digestion and absorption, hosts the densest resident microbial community in the body, and is a major immune organ and endocrine organ in its own right. |
| [[Intrinsically Disordered Region\|Intrinsically Disordered Region]] | Intrinsically disordered regions (IDRs) are protein segments that lack a fixed three-dimensional structure under physiological conditions, yet mediate critical protein-protein interactions, phase separation, and signaling. |
| [[Inulin\|Inulin]] | Inulin is a non-digestible fructan oligosaccharide used as a prebiotic and as a nanodimensional carrier that enhances the solubility and stability of hydrophobic bioactives such as fisetin. |
| [[Iodoacetic Acid\|Iodoacetic Acid]] | Iodoacetic acid (IAA) is an alkylating glycolytic inhibitor that depletes ATP and elevates mitochondrial ROS, used as a neuronal oxidative-stress insult in methylene blue neuroprotection assays. |
| [[IP3R2\|IP3R2]] | Inositol 1,4,5-trisphosphate receptor type 2 located on the endoplasmic reticulum that interacts with FUNDC1 at mitochondria-associated membranes to regulate calcium signaling. |
| [[IRAK\|IRAK]] | Interleukin-1 receptor-associated kinase, a small family of serine/threonine kinases of which IRAK4 is the obligatory upstream kinase for Toll-like receptor and IL-1 receptor signalling into NF-kappaB and AP-1. |
| [[IRAK1\|IRAK1]] | Interleukin-1 receptor-associated kinase 1, an upstream kinase in IL-1R/TLR signaling whose inhibition by apigenin contributes to SASP suppression. |
| [[IRAK4\|IRAK4]] | Interleukin-1 receptor-associated kinase 4, an upstream kinase in IL-1R/TLR signaling whose inhibition by apigenin contributes to SASP suppression. |
| [[IRE1\|IRE1]] | ER-stress sensor splicing XBP1 to launch the unfolded protein response; links proteostasis to autophagy. |
| [[IRF3\|IRF3]] | Interferon regulatory factor 3 is a transcription factor that mediates type I interferon and SASP production. |
| [[IRF7\|IRF7]] | Interferon regulatory factor 7 is the master transcriptional regulator of type I and type III interferon responses, acting downstream of pattern-recognition receptors via TBK1 and cooperating with IRF3 at interferon-stimulated response elements. |
| [[Irisin\|Irisin]] | Exercise-induced myokine cleaved from the FNDC5 membrane protein that drives browning of white adipose tissue, promotes bone formation, enhances BDNF-dependent neuroprotection, and exerts cardiovascular and SIRT3-dependent redox effects. |
| [[Iron\|Iron]] | Iron is an essential metal that plays a vital role in many biological processes, including oxygen transport and enzymatic reactions. |
| [[IRS-2\|IRS-2]] | Insulin receptor substrate 2 (IRS2) is a cytosolic adapter phosphorylated by the insulin/IGF-1 receptor that bridges insulin signaling to PI3K–Akt pathways controlling glucose homeostasis and β-cell function. |
| [[IRS1\|IRS1]] | IRS1 (insulin receptor substrate 1) is a 1242-residue cytoplasmic signalling scaffold with a PH domain, a PTB domain, and a large unstructured tail bearing dozens of phosphotyrosine and phosphoserine motifs. It transduces insulin and IGF1R signals to PI3K-AKT-mTOR and RAS-MAPK, and its serine phosphorylation by S6K is the canonical mechanism of nutritionally induced insulin resistance. |
| [[Ischemia\|Ischemia]] | Ischemia is the restriction of blood supply causing hypoxia, ATP depletion, and necrotic or regulated cell death on reperfusion. |
| [[Ischemia-reperfusion Injury\|Ischemia-reperfusion Injury]] | Ischemia-reperfusion Injury occurs when blood supply returns to tissue after a period of ischemia, often triggering oxidative stress and modulating Autophagy pathways. |
| [[Ischemic Stroke\|Ischemic Stroke]] | Ischemic stroke is acute brain injury from arterial occlusion and hypoxia-ischemia; methylene blue has investigational neuroprotective relevance. |
| [[ISG15\|ISG15]] | Interferon-stimulated gene 15, an ubiquitin-like ISG induced by p16 overexpression that encodes an interferon-type SASP factor. |
| [[Isoleucine\|Isoleucine]] | Essential branched-chain amino acid that has emerged as a key regulator of metabolism and aging. Dietary isoleucine correlates with body mass index and mortality risk; isoleucine restriction consistently improves metabolic health, extends lifespan, reduces frailty, and rejuvenates aged mice. |
| [[ITPR2\|ITPR2]] | Gene encoding the inositol 1,4,5-trisphosphate receptor type 2 (IP3R2), an intracellular calcium channel on the endoplasmic reticulum. |
| [[IκBα\|IκBα]] | The inhibitory subunit of the NF-κB complex; degraded upon senescence stress and reversed by apigenin/VER155008, curtailing NF-κB activation and the SASP. |
| [[IκBζ\|IκBζ]] | Inhibitor of NF-κB zeta, a selective co-activator of NF-κB for SASP genes like IL-6 and IL-8. |
| [[J147\|J147]] | A curcumin-derived small molecule from the Maher lab that potently inhibits ferroptosis/lipid peroxidation; completed Phase 1 clinical trials for Alzheimer's disease. |
| [[JAK\|JAK]] | Janus kinase is a family of intracellular tyrosine kinases that transduce cytokine signals. |
| [[JAK-STAT Signaling\|JAK-STAT Signaling]] | A cytokine-responsive signaling axis (notably JAK-STAT3) that promotes a pro-inflammatory SASP and is a target of senomorphic interventions. |
| [[JC Virus\|JC Virus]] | JC polyomavirus (JCV), a ubiquitous human DNA virus held in latent renal and lymphoid infection since childhood, which causes progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy and granule cell neuronopathy by lytic infection of oligodendrocytes when host immunity wanes. |
| [[JMJD3\|JMJD3]] | Jumonji domain-containing protein 3 (KDM6B), an H3K27me3 demethylase that derepresses IL-6 and IL-8 SASP loci in senescent cells. |
| [[JNK\|JNK]] | c-Jun N-terminal kinase (stress-activated protein kinase); a stress-activated MAPK that regulates apoptosis, proliferation, and inflammation by phosphorylating BAX, Bim, Mcl-1, and c-Jun, with context-dependent (pro-apoptotic vs pro-survival) roles in cancer. |
| [[Kaempferol\|Kaempferol]] | A natural flavonol (3,5,7,4′-tetrahydroxyflavone) with antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and anticancer activities, frequently studied alongside apigenin and quercetin. |
| [[KAP1\|KAP1]] | KRAB-associated protein 1 (TRIM28/TIF1β), a transcriptional corepressor mono-ADP-ribosylated by SIRT6 to silence LINE-1 retrotransposons via HP1α in aging and senescence. |
| [[Kaposi sarcoma\|Kaposi sarcoma]] | A vascular spindle-cell tumour driven by HHV-8/KSHV, occurring in four epidemiological forms (classic, endemic African, iatrogenic post-transplant, and AIDS-associated) and strongly dependent on impaired cell-mediated immunity for its clinical expression. |
| [[Kupffer Cell\|Kupffer Cell]] | Hepatic sinusoidal macrophage; the liver's clearance arm for PS-exposing (eryptotic and senescent) erythrocytes and RBC-derived vesicles, forming erythrophagolysosomes and driving hepatic iron recycling. |
| [[KDM4\|KDM4]] | Lysine demethylase 4 family, JmjC histone demethylases that erase H3K9me3/me2 to open chromatin and promote SASP factor expression. |
| [[KDM5C\|KDM5C]] | KDM5C (Lysine Demethylase 5C, also called JARID1C) is an X-linked H3K4 demethylase that regulates gene expression and X-chromosome inactivation; loss-of-function mutations drive male-biased tumorigenesis. |
| [[KDM6A\|KDM6A]] | KDM6A (Lysine Demethylase 6A, also called UTX) is an X-linked H3K27me3 demethylase that opens repressive chromatin and escapes X-inactivation in females; loss-of-function mutations are enriched in male tumors. |
| [[Keratinocyte Growth Factor\|Keratinocyte Growth Factor]] | An FGF family growth factor (FGF7) that stimulates thymic epithelial cell proliferation and differentiation; used clinically as Palifermin for thymic regeneration. |
| [[Kidney\|Kidney]] | The paired organs that filter blood, regulate fluid and electrolyte balance, and produce hormones. |
| [[Kidney Diseases\|Kidney Diseases]] | The umbrella of acute and chronic disorders of renal structure or function, dominated by diabetes and hypertension, defined by a sustained abnormality in filtration or structure lasting over three months, and the leading cause of cardiovascular morbidity and mortality worldwide. |
| [[Kidney Fibrosis\|Kidney Fibrosis]] | Pathological accumulation of extracellular matrix in the kidney leading to loss of renal function, the final common pathway of chronic kidney disease |
| [[Kidney Stones\|Kidney Stones]] | Crystalline deposits that form in the kidney, most commonly calcium oxalate, causing pain and urinary obstruction |
| [[Kinase\|Kinase]] | Kinases are enzymes that catalyze the transfer of the gamma-phosphate of ATP to substrate hydroxyl groups (kinase activity), regulating nearly every signaling pathway. Protein kinases share an adenosine-binding site that can be exploited by kinase inhibitors repurposed against sirtuins. |
| [[Kinase Inhibitor\|Kinase Inhibitor]] | A small-molecule drug that inhibits a protein kinase; the dominant class of targeted cancer and signaling therapies, and the class in which drug hormesis (biphasic dose response) is frequently observed. |
| [[Kinesin\|Kinesin]] | Microtubule motor protein that drives axonal mitochondrial transport via the MFN2-Miro-Milton complex. |
| [[Klotho\|Klotho]] | An anti-aging transmembrane and secreted protein (hormone) whose deficiency drives premature aging; rapamycin improves function in Klotho-deficient models via stem-cell protection. |
| [[Knockout mouse\|Knockout mouse]] | A genetically engineered mouse in which a specific gene has been inactivated (knocked out) to study gene function in vivo. |
| [[Kynurenic Acid\|Kynurenic Acid]] | Kynurenic acid (KynA) is a neuroactive metabolite of the kynurenine pathway that acts as an NMDA receptor antagonist, providing neuroprotection that counterbalances the excitotoxic effects of quinolinic acid. |
| [[Kynurenine\|Kynurenine]] | Kynurenine (Kyn) is a central metabolite of the kynurenine pathway produced from tryptophan, acting as an immunosuppressive signaling molecule via AhR activation and as a precursor for downstream metabolites including quinolinic acid and kynurenic acid. |
| [[Kynurenine Pathway\|Kynurenine Pathway]] | The kynurenine pathway (KP) is the major catabolic route for tryptophan degradation, accounting for ~95% of Trp metabolism outside protein synthesis and serotonin production, ultimately producing NAD+. |
| [[L-Carnitine\|L-Carnitine]] | L-Carnitine is the biologically active enantiomer of carnitine, an endogenous quaternary ammonium compound required for shuttling long-chain fatty acids into mitochondria for β-oxidation. |
| [[Lactate\|Lactate]] | Biochemical properties: Lactate is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic flux... |
| [[Lactobacillus\|Lactobacillus]] | Lactobacillus is a genus of Gram-positive, lactic-acid-fermenting bacteria abundant in fermented foods, the vaginal tract, and the gut, where it supports barrier integrity and metabolic health. |
| [[Lactylation\|Lactylation]] | Lysine lactylation (Kla), a lactate-derived post-translational modification of histones and other proteins that couples metabolic state to gene expression; implicated in SIRT3-dependent ferroptosis in intervertebral disc degeneration. |
| [[Lamin A\|Lamin A]] | Lamin A is a nuclear-envelope intermediate-filament protein encoded by LMNA; defective processing produces progerin and drives progeroid syndromes. |
| [[Lamin B1\|Lamin B1]] | Nuclear lamina protein whose loss is a conserved, cell-autonomous marker of senescence, linked to nuclear envelope defects, chromatin reorganization, and SAHF formation. |
| [[Lamivudine\|Lamivudine]] | A nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor (3TC) repurposed as a senomorphic agent that suppresses the SASP by blocking LINE-1 retrotransposition. |
| [[LAMP-2A\|LAMP-2A]] | Lysosomal membrane receptor for chaperone-mediated autophagy. LAMP-2A is one of three alternatively spliced LAMP-2 isoforms; it binds HSC70-delivered KFERQ-containing cytosolic proteins at the lysosomal surface and releases them into the lysosome for degradation. |
| [[Lands cycle\|Lands cycle]] | A phospholipid remodeling pathway in which free fatty acids released from ceramide catabolism are exchanged into the sn-2 position of membrane phospholipids, linking acid ceramidase activity to the enrichment of PUFA-containing phospholipids and ferroptosis sensitivity. |
| [[Lapatinib\|Lapatinib]] | Lapatinib is a reversible ATP-competitive small-molecule tyrosine kinase inhibitor that targets the intracellular domains of EGFR/HER1 and HER2, used in HER2-positive metastatic breast cancer. |
| [[LC3\|LC3]] | LC3 (Microtubule-associated protein 1A/1B light chain 3B) is a central protein in the Autophagy pathway. It is the mammalian homologue of yeast Atg8 and serves as the most widely used marker fo... |
| [[LC3B\|LC3B]] | Microtubule-associated protein 1B light chain 3 (MAP1LC3B), the most abundant mammalian ATG8-family protein. LC3B is lipidated to the inner autophagosomal membrane and acts as a membrane-bound binding platform for cargo receptors through LIR motif recognition; it is the standard biochemical marker of autophagic flux. |
| [[LDHA\|LDHA]] | Lactate dehydrogenase A, the muscle-type M subunit of the LDH tetramer, which catalyses pyruvate-to-lactate conversion and regenerates NAD+ under anaerobic conditions; upregulated in tumours, where it sustains the Warburg effect. |
| [[LDL\|LDL]] | Low-density lipoprotein (LDL) is a lipoprotein particle that transports cholesterol and triglycerides from the liver to peripheral tissues. Elevated LDL is a major risk factor for Atheroscleros... |
| [[LDL Receptor\|LDL Receptor]] | The LDL receptor (LDLR) is the cell-surface, clathrin-mediated endocytic receptor that clears LDL from plasma by binding apolipoprotein B; it is the rate-limiting step of receptor-mediated cholesterol uptake and the mutated gene in most familial hypercholesterolaemia. |
| [[Left Ventricular Hypertrophy\|Left Ventricular Hypertrophy]] | Thickening of the left ventricular wall associated with increased DRP1 and reduced MFN2; Mdivi-1 ameliorates it. |
| [[Legionella\|Legionella]] | Legionella is a genus of Gram-negative bacteria, chiefly L. pneumophila, transmitted by aerosolised water and causing Legionnaires' disease, a severe atypical pneumonia. |
| [[Leishmania\|Leishmania]] | Leishmania is a genus of kinetoplastid protozoan parasites of the order Trypanosomatida, transmitted by phlebotomine sandflies, whose intracellular amastigotes replicate within host macrophages. |
| [[Lens Crystallins\|Lens Crystallins]] | Long-lived eye-lens proteins whose glycation and racemization cause age-related cataract. |
| [[Leptin\|Leptin]] | Biochemical properties: Leptin is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic flux ... |
| [[Leptin Signaling\|Leptin Signaling]] | Leptin signalling is the JAK2-STAT3 cascade initiated by leptin binding the LepRb cytokine receptor, transmitted in the hypothalamus to regulate appetite, energy expenditure and glucose homeostasis. |
| [[Leucine\|Leucine]] | An essential branched-chain amino acid that acts as a potent nutrient signal activating mTORC1 to stimulate muscle protein synthesis. |
| [[LeucoMB\|LeucoMB]] | The reduced (leuco) form of methylene blue generated during its catalytic redox cycling; LeucoMB donates electrons to complex IV, regenerating methylene blue and enhancing mitochondrial function to delay senescence. |
| [[Leukemia inhibitory factor\|Leukemia inhibitory factor]] | Cytokine that maintains embryonic stem cell pluripotency and self-renewal; withheld to permit differentiation in vitro. |
| [[Leukopenia\|Leukopenia]] | A reduction in circulating white blood cell count; a mild, reversible cytopenia associated with chronic mTOR inhibition. |
| [[Leukotrienes\|Leukotrienes]] | Leukotrienes are lipid mediators synthesised from arachidonic acid via the 5-lipoxygenase pathway; LTB4 acts mainly through BLT receptors to recruit neutrophils, while the cysteinyl leukotrienes LTC4, LTD4 and LTE4 act at CysLT1 and CysLT2 receptors to cause bronchoconstriction, mucus secretion and vascular permeability. |
| [[Levodopa\|Levodopa]] | Chemical properties: Levodopa is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[LHRH\|LHRH]] | Luteinizing hormone-releasing hormone; a hypothalamic peptide that controls gonadotropin release and is targeted by analogues for sex steroid ablation in thymic rejuvenation. |
| [[LINE-1\|LINE-1]] | Long Interspersed Nuclear Element-1 (LINE-1 or L1) is a common retrotransposable element in the human genome that can replicate and insert itself into new locations. |
| [[Lipid Metabolism\|Lipid Metabolism]] | Synthesis, storage, and oxidation of lipids; regulated by SIRT1 via PGC-1α, LXR, FOXO, and CREB deacetylation. |
| [[Lipid Nanoparticles\|Lipid Nanoparticles]] | Lipid nanoparticles are self-assembling nanoscale lipid assemblies that encapsulate nucleic acids and proteins, using ionizable lipids to enable endosomal escape and cytosolic delivery of mRNA. |
| [[Lipid Rafts\|Lipid Rafts]] | Lipid rafts are cholesterol- and sphingolipid-enriched, more-ordered microdomains of the plasma membrane that concentrate signalling proteins into platform-like assemblies; caveolae are the best-characterised raft subtype. |
| [[Lipid Synthesis\|Lipid Synthesis]] | Definition: Lipid Synthesis is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and ... |
| [[LIPIN1\|LIPIN1]] | Lipin-1, a bifunctional enzyme and transcription coactivator that acts as phosphatidic acid phosphatase 1 in triacylglycerol synthesis and as a coactivator of PPAR-alpha for fatty acid oxidation; loss causes lipodystrophy. |
| [[Lipopolysaccharide\|Lipopolysaccharide]] | Gram-negative bacterial outer-membrane endotoxin (LPS) that activates Toll-like receptor 4 and drives sterile and septic inflammation, redox stress, and inflammaging. |
| [[Liposomes\|Liposomes]] | Liposomes are self-assembled spherical vesicles of one or more phospholipid bilayers that encapsulate hydrophilic and hydrophobic cargo for drug delivery, improving solubility and bioavailability. |
| [[Lipotoxicity\|Lipotoxicity]] | Lipotoxicity is the tissue damage caused by accumulation of lipid intermediates (especially saturated fatty acids and ceramides) in non-adipose tissues, driving insulin resistance, ER stress, and inflammation. |
| [[Lipoxygenase\|Lipoxygenase]] | Lipoxygenases (LOXs) are iron-containing dioxygenases that oxidize polyunsaturated fatty acids to bioactive hydroperoxides and signaling lipids such as leukotrienes, linking lipid peroxidation to inflammation and ferroptosis. |
| [[Liquid-Liquid Phase Separation\|Liquid-Liquid Phase Separation]] | A physical process by which intrinsically disordered proteins assemble into membraneless condensates, exploited by noncanonical cGAS for nuclear scaffolding. |
| [[Liquiritigenin\|Liquiritigenin]] | Flavanone from licorice (Glycyrrhiza) that induces Nrf2 translocation to potentiate SIRT3 activity, supporting mitochondrial biogenesis and suppressing apoptosis in acute kidney injury. |
| [[LIR Motif\|LIR Motif]] | The LC3-interacting region, a short hydrophobic peptide motif that directs autophagy receptors and cargo proteins to LC3/GABARAP family proteins on the phagophore. |
| [[Liraglutide\|Liraglutide]] | Liraglutide, a GLP-1 receptor agonist used for diabetes and obesity that reduces cardiac fibrosis by promoting mitophagy |
| [[LIT-927\|LIT-927]] | A CXCL12 neutraligand (bispecific antibody) that neutralizes CXCL12 signaling through both CXCR4 and ACKR3 receptors |
| [[Lithium\|Lithium]] | Lithium is an alkali-metal ion used as a mood stabilizer that inhibits GSK3β, modulates inositol signaling, and induces autophagy, with emerging neuroprotective and pro-longevity interest alongside a narrow therapeutic index. |
| [[Liver\|Liver]] | Overview: Liver is a specialized cellular or anatomical structure with defined functions in tissue organization and physiology. |
| [[Liver Ischemia-Reperfusion Injury\|Liver Ischemia-Reperfusion Injury]] | Hepatic damage occurring upon restoration of blood flow to ischemic liver, driven by oxidative stress, inflammation, and mitochondrial dysfunction |
| [[Liver X Receptor\|Liver X Receptor]] | Nuclear receptor (LXRα/LXRβ) regulating cholesterol and lipid homeostasis; deacetylated and modulated by SIRT1. |
| [[Livin\|Livin]] | Livin (BIRC7/ML-IAP) is an inhibitor of apoptosis protein that binds and inhibits caspases, promoting cancer cell survival; its expression is a biomarker and potential therapeutic target in multiple malignancies. |
| [[LKB1\|LKB1]] | Liver kinase B1 (STK11) is a tumor suppressor serine/threonine kinase and the primary upstream kinase activating AMPK under energy stress; deacetylated by SIRT1 and SIRT3 to coordinate metabolic adaptation, autophagy induction, and tumour suppression. |
| [[LMNA Gene\|LMNA Gene]] | The LMNA Gene (Lamin A/C) provides instructions for making several proteins called lamins, primarily Lamin A and Lamin C. These proteins are structural components of the nuclear lamina, a meshw... |
| [[Locked Nucleic Acid\|Locked Nucleic Acid]] | Locked Nucleic Acid (LNA), a nucleic acid analog with a 2'-O,4'-C methylene bridge that confers high binding affinity and nuclease resistance. |
| [[Long COVID\|Long COVID]] | Long COVID (post-acute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 infection) is a multisystem chronic condition with prominent fatigue, cognitive, and autonomic symptoms; mitochondrial dysfunction is a hypothesized driver. |
| [[Long-Term Potentiation\|Long-Term Potentiation]] | Long-term potentiation (LTP) is a persistent, input-specific strengthening of synaptic transmission following brief high-frequency activity, widely treated as the leading cellular substrate for learning and memory. |
| [[Longevity\|Longevity]] | Longevity refers to the capacity of an organism to survive beyond the average lifespan, often influenced by a combination of genetic, environmental, and lifestyle factors. |
| [[Loss of Y Chromosome\|Loss of Y Chromosome]] | Mosaic loss of the Y chromosome in blood with age; male-specific driver of fibrosis, immune dysregulation, cancer, and cardiovascular risk. |
| [[Lovastatin\|Lovastatin]] | A statin (HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor) that acts as an SHP2 agonist to promote Parkin activation. |
| [[LPCAT3\|LPCAT3]] | LPCAT3 is a lysophosphatidylcholine acyltransferase that remodels membrane phospholipids with polyunsaturated fatty acids, supplying ferroptosis substrates. |
| [[LPS\|LPS]] | Lipopolysaccharide, a major component of the outer membrane of Gram-negative bacteria and a potent inducer of Macrophage activation and TFE3 translocation. |
| [[LRO\|LRO]] | Lysosome-related organelles, a family of secretory and storage compartments with a lysosome-like acidic lumen but specialised cargo, best characterised as the C. elegans gut granules and including human melanosomes and platelet dense granules. |
| [[LRRK2\|LRRK2]] | Kinase mutated in familial Parkinson's disease that regulates mitophagy, lysosomal function, and vesicle sorting. |
| [[Luteoloside\|Luteoloside]] | Flavonoid glycoside with antifibrotic and senescence-modulating activity via autophagic flux restoration and ROS reduction. |
| [[Lymphedema\|Lymphedema]] | Lymphedema is the accumulation of protein-rich interstitial fluid from impaired lymphatic drainage, causing chronic tissue swelling, fibrosis and recurrent cellulitis. |
| [[Lysosomal Biogenesis\|Lysosomal Biogenesis]] | Lysosomal biogenesis is the process of synthesizing and assembling lysosomal components—including acid hydrolases, membrane proteins (LAMP1, LAMP2, V-ATPase subunits), and regulatory proteins—i... |
| [[Lysosomal Dysfunction\|Lysosomal Dysfunction]] | Impaired lysosomal acidification, enzyme activity, or autophagic flux — a hallmark of aging and neurodegeneration that allows proteotoxic aggregates (α-synuclein, lipofuscin) to accumulate and sustains inflammatory DAMP signaling. |
| [[Lysosomal Membrane Permeabilization\|Lysosomal Membrane Permeabilization]] | The disruption of lysosomal membrane integrity that releases cathepsins and other hydrolases into the cytosol, triggering controlled cell death and inflammatory signaling. |
| [[Lysosomal Storage Diseases\|Lysosomal Storage Diseases]] | A group of about 50 rare inherited metabolic disorders that result from defects in lysosomal function. |
| [[Lysosome\|Lysosome]] | The Lysosome is a membrane-bound organelle containing acidic hydrolases responsible for the degradation and recycling of cellular waste. It serves as a central degradative center and signaling ... |
| [[Lysosome Biogenesis\|Lysosome Biogenesis]] | Definition: Lysosome Biogenesis is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways ... |
| [[M1dA\|M1dA]] | 1-methyladenine is a cytotoxic DNA base lesion formed by N1-alkylation of adenine; it blocks replication and transcription and is removed by dedicated glycosylases. |
| [[MacroD1\|MacroD1]] | MacroD1 (LRP16) is a mitochondrial macrodomain hydrolase erasing Asp/Glu-MARylation and deacetylating OAADPr |
| [[MacroD2\|MacroD2]] | MacroD2 is a cytosolic/nuclear macrodomain hydrolase erasing Asp/Glu-MARylation and regulating DNA-damage signaling |
| [[macroH2A\|macroH2A]] | H2A histone variant that accumulates in senescent and aged cells, stabilizing SAHF, reinforcing proliferation arrest, and helping time SASP expression. |
| [[Macrophage\|Macrophage]] | Large mononuclear phagocytes that are key innate immune cells, performing phagocytosis, antigen presentation, and cytokine production, with critical links to Autophagy and inflammatory regulation. |
| [[Macrophage Polarization\|Macrophage Polarization]] | Functional reprogramming of macrophages toward proinflammatory (M1) or anti-inflammatory/tissue-repair (M2) phenotypes in response to environmental cues |
| [[Macrophages\|Macrophages]] | Overview: Macrophages is a specialized cellular or anatomical structure with defined functions in tissue organization and physiology. |
| [[MAGEC3\|MAGEC3]] | MAGEC3 (Melanoma-Associated Antigen Family Member C3) is an X-linked cancer-testis antigen and putative tumor suppressor enriched for loss-of-function mutations in male tumors. |
| [[Magnesium\|Magnesium]] | Magnesium is an essential mineral involved in over 300 biochemical reactions in the body. |
| [[Magnolol\|Magnolol]] | A biphenyl neolignan from Magnolia species, structural analog of honokiol, with neuroprotective, anxiolytic, anti-inflammatory, antibacterial, and antioxidant activities. |
| [[Malaria\|Malaria]] | Plasmodium infection of erythrocytes; oxidative stress opens cation channels and drives eryptosis, clearing infected cells and parasites, while G6PD deficiency and other erythrocyte defects confer partial protection. |
| [[Malate\|Malate]] | The four-carbon TCA-cycle intermediate produced from oxaloacetate by malate dehydrogenase; a node linking the citric acid cycle, the malate-aspartate shuttle, and cytosolic-mitochondrial NAD+/NADH balance in senescence. |
| [[Malate Dehydrogenase\|Malate Dehydrogenase]] | Enzyme catalyzing the reversible NAD+-dependent interconversion of oxaloacetate and malate in the TCA cycle and the malate-aspartate shuttle; cytosolic MDH1 loss lowers NAD+/NADH and can induce senescence. |
| [[Malate-Aspartate Shuttle\|Malate-Aspartate Shuttle]] | The principal cytosolic-mitochondrial shuttle that transfers glycolytic NADH into mitochondria for OXPHOS; inhibiting it with aminooxyacetate lowers cytosolic NAD+/NADH and induces senescence. |
| [[Malonyl-CoA\|Malonyl-CoA]] | Malonyl-CoA is the three-carbon intermediate of de novo fatty acid synthesis made by ACC1/ACC2, and the key metabolic switch that represses mitochondrial fatty acid oxidation by inhibiting CPT1. |
| [[MAML1\|MAML1]] | Mastermind-like 1, the transcriptional co-activator that nucleates the NOTCH–CSL/RBPJ ternary complex and, with the Notch intracellular domain, forms part of the nuclear control layer of SASP induction. |
| [[Manganese\|Manganese]] | Manganese is a transition metal that can exhibit high binding affinity for Neuromelanin and is associated with a Parkinsonian-like syndrome known as Manganism. |
| [[MnSOD\|Manganese Superoxide Dismutase (MnSOD/SOD2)]] | Manganese Superoxide Dismutase (MnSOD/SOD2) is the primary mitochondrial antioxidant enzyme responsible for dismutating Superoxide (O₂⁻) to hydrogen peroxide (H₂O₂) and oxygen. It is encoded by the SOD2 gene and imported into the mitochondrial matrix. |
| [[MAP2\|MAP2]] | Microtubule-associated protein 2, a dendritic cytoskeletal protein of neurons important for microtubule stabilization and neuronal morphology. |
| [[MAPK\|MAPK]] | The MAPK (Mitogen-Activated Protein Kinase) pathway is a series of proteins in the cell that communicates a signal from a receptor on the surface of the cell to the DNA in the nucleus. It is a ... |
| [[MAPKAPK2\|MAPKAPK2]] | Mitogen-activated protein kinase-activated protein kinase 2, a key regulator of SASP mRNA stability. |
| [[MARylation\|MARylation]] | MARylation is mono-ADP-ribosylation, the transfer of a single ADP-ribose moiety from NAD+ to a serine residue, used by PARP1, PARP2 and PARG to produce a reversible DNA-break signal. |
| [[Mass Drug Administration\|Mass Drug Administration]] | Mass drug administration is the public-health strategy of giving a whole eligible population, or a defined subset, a single dose of a therapeutic drug on a recurrent schedule regardless of individual infection status; it is the core tool of WHO elimination campaigns for lymphatic filariasis, onchocerciasis, schistosomiasis and soil-transmitted helminths. |
| [[Matrine\|Matrine]] | Quinolizidine alkaloid from Sophora flavescens with anti-inflammatory, antioxidant, and cardiorenal protective activity; protects against cisplatin nephrotoxicity via the SIRT3/OPA1 axis. |
| [[Matrix Metalloproteinase\|Matrix Metalloproteinase]] | Zinc-dependent endopeptidases (MMPs) that degrade extracellular matrix; MMP-2 and MMP-9 mediate tumor invasion, metastasis, and angiogenesis. |
| [[MBOAT1\|MBOAT1]] | MBOAT1 is an estrogen-receptor-driven lyso-phospholipid acyltransferase suppressing ferroptosis via PE-MUFA membrane remodeling. |
| [[MBOAT2\|MBOAT2]] | MBOAT2 is an androgen-receptor-driven lyso-phospholipid acyltransferase suppressing ferroptosis via PE-MUFA membrane remodeling. |
| [[MCC950\|MCC950]] | Selective small-molecule inhibitor of the NLRP3 inflammasome (NLRP3-IN-1) that blocks ASC speck formation and IL-1β release. |
| [[MCT Oil\|MCT Oil]] | Medium-chain triglyceride oil (caprylic acid / C8) providing a rapid mitochondrial fuel that raises ketones and acts as a signaling molecule for mitohormesis. |
| [[MCU\|MCU]] | The mitochondrial calcium uniporter (MCU) is a highly selective ion channel in the inner mitochondrial membrane that mediates calcium uptake into the mitochondrial matrix, regulating energy metabolism, cell death, and senescence. |
| [[MDA5\|MDA5]] | MDA5 (IFIH1) is a cytosolic RIG-I-like receptor that senses long double-stranded RNA and signals through MAVS to IRF3/IRF7-dependent type I interferon production. |
| [[MDC1\|MDC1]] | MDC1 is a large BRCT-domain scaffold protein that binds gamma-H2AX at DNA double-strand breaks and orchestrates RNF8/RNF168-mediated ubiquitin signalling and downstream repair factor recruitment. |
| [[MDH1\|MDH1]] | Cytosolic malate dehydrogenase, an enzyme in the malate-aspartate shuttle that converts malate to oxaloacetate while reducing NAD+ to NADH. |
| [[MDH2\|MDH2]] | Mitochondrial malate dehydrogenase, an enzyme in the TCA cycle and malate-aspartate shuttle that converts malate to oxaloacetate while reducing NAD+ to NADH. |
| [[Mdivi-1\|Mdivi-1]] | Selective DRP1 inhibitor that reduces atherosclerosis, LV hypertrophy, and cancer metastasis. |
| [[MdmX\|MdmX]] | MDM2/MDM4 regulator of p53 (MDMX), a 491-residue RING-finger protein homologous to MDM2 that binds and inhibits p53 transactivation and is largely E3-ligase-inert on its own, functioning instead through heterodimerisation with MDM2. |
| [[ME2\|ME2]] | Malic Enzyme 2, the mitochondrial isoform of malic enzyme that converts malate to pyruvate while generating NADPH; a SIRT3 delactylation and SIRT5 desuccinylation substrate at the nexus of tumor redox balance and metabolic plasticity. |
| [[MEF\|MEF]] | Mouse embryonic fibroblasts, primary cells isolated from mouse embryos and widely used in cell biology and senescence studies. |
| [[MEF2C\|MEF2C]] | Myocyte enhancer factor 2C, a transcription factor orchestrating neuronal resilience and axon morphology gene networks suppressed by cGAS-STING/IFN-I signaling. |
| [[Meiosis\|Meiosis]] | Meiosis is a specialized form of Cell Division that produces Gametes (Sperm and Egg cells) with half the chromosome number (haploid). It consists of two sequential divisions: Meiosis I (reductional... |
| [[MEK1_2\|MEK1/2]] | MEK1 (MAP2K1) and MEK2 (MAP2K2) are dual-specificity kinases that phosphorylate and activate ERK1/2 (MAPK3/MAPK1) in the MAPK/ERK pathway. They are activated by Raf kinases (BRAF, ARAF, RAF1) and i... |
| [[Melanocyte\|Melanocyte]] | Melanocytes are pigment-producing cells of the skin, hair, and eye that are the cell of origin for melanoma. |
| [[MELAS\|MELAS]] | Mitochondrial encephalomyopathy with lactic acidosis and stroke-like episodes, a maternally inherited mitochondrial disorder |
| [[Melatonin\|Melatonin]] | Melatonin is a hormone produced by the pineal gland that regulates the sleep-wake cycle. |
| [[Membrane Trafficking\|Membrane Trafficking]] | The directed, coat- and motor-protein-dependent transport of lipids, proteins and cargo between membrane compartments and to the plasma membrane, organised by Rab GTPases, tethering complexes and SNARE-mediated fusion. |
| [[Meningeal Lymphatic System\|Meningeal Lymphatic System]] | Dural lymphatic vessels that drain CNS antigens and cGAS-STING–derived inflammatory products to cervical lymph nodes. |
| [[Merck & Co. Inc\|Merck & Co. Inc.]] | Merck & Co., Inc. (known as MSD outside the US and Canada) is an American multinational pharmaceutical company. It is one of the largest pharmaceutical companies in the world and played a key role ... |
| [[MERCS\|MERCS]] | Mitochondria-ER contact sites (MERCS) are physical connections between the endoplasmic reticulum and mitochondria that facilitate calcium transfer, lipid synthesis, and signaling, with important roles in cellular senescence and neurodegenerative disease. |
| [[Mesenchymal Stem Cells\|Mesenchymal Stem Cells]] | Multipotent stromal cells that can donate healthy mitochondria to reverse senescence; their mesenchymal gene program is also acquired by endothelial cells undergoing EndoMT in vascular aging. |
| [[Messenger RNA\|Messenger RNA]] | Messenger RNA (mRNA), the ribonucleic acid transcript that conveys genetic information from DNA to the ribosome for protein synthesis. |
| [[MET\|MET]] | MET is the gene encoding c-Met, the hepatocyte growth factor receptor tyrosine kinase, a proto-oncogene and therapeutic target in gastric, lung and liver cancers. |
| [[Metabolic Flexibility\|Metabolic Flexibility]] | The capacity of cells and organisms to switch between fuel sources (glucose vs fatty acids) in response to nutrient availability and energy demand |
