| [[Metabolic Plasticity\|Metabolic Plasticity]] | The capacity of cancer cells (and stem cells) to dynamically switch between glycolysis and oxidative phosphorylation (OXPHOS) in response to tumor type, developmental stage, and microenvironmental cues — a key axis of the SIRT3-mediated redox-metabolic interplay. |
| [[Metabolic Syndrome\|Metabolic Syndrome]] | Cluster of interconnected metabolic abnormalities — central obesity, insulin resistance, hypertension, and dyslipidemia — increasing risk of diabetes and cardiovascular disease |
| [[Metabolic Syndromes\|Metabolic Syndromes]] | Metabolic Syndromes refer to a cluster of conditions—including increased blood pressure, high blood sugar, excess body fat around the waist, and abnormal cholesterol or triglyceride levels—that... |
| [[Metabolism\|Metabolism]] | Metabolism is the set of life-sustaining chemical reactions in organisms, involving the conversion of food to energy and the building of cellular components. |
| [[Metabolites\|Metabolites]] | Metabolites are the small-molecule intermediates and end products of enzyme-catalyzed metabolism; their collective profile (the metabolome) shifts with age and microbiome composition. |
| [[Metabolome\|Metabolome]] | The metabolome is the complete set of small-molecule chemicals—metabolic intermediates, hormones, and signaling metabolites—present in a biological sample, cell, or organism at a given time. |
| [[Metabotypes\|Metabotypes]] | Data-driven subgroups of individuals defined by distinct metabolic response or phenotype profiles derived from multi-omic clustering. |
| [[Metachromatic Leukodystrophy\|Metachromatic Leukodystrophy]] | An autosomal recessive lysosomal storage disorder caused by arylsulfatase A deficiency, leading to sulfatide accumulation in oligodendrocytes and Schwann cells and progressive demyelination and neurodegeneration. |
| [[Metastasis\|Metastasis]] | Definition: Metastasis is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and envir... |
| [[Metastatic colonization\|Metastatic colonization]] | Metastatic colonization is the final, rate-limiting step of metastasis — the growth of disseminated tumor cells into macroscopic lesions at a distant site; it is restricted by oxidative stress and supported by selenocysteine translation. |
| [[Meteorin-like\|Meteorin-like]] | Meteorin-like protein (Metrnl), an exercise- and cold-induced myokine/adipokine that activates the PGC-1alpha/SIRT3 axis to preserve mitochondrial ultrastructure and regulate energy metabolism in diabetic nephropathy. |
| [[Metformin\|Metformin]] | Metformin is the world''s most widely prescribed medication for type 2 Diabetes Mellitus. Beyond its role in controlling blood sugar, metformin has gained significant attention in Geroscience as... |
| [[Methemoglobin\|Methemoglobin]] | Methemoglobin is a form of Hemoglobin in which the iron in the heme group is in the Fe³⁺ (ferric) state, not the Fe²⁺ (ferrous) of normal hemoglobin. Methemoglobin cannot bind oxygen, which mea... |
| [[Methemoglobin Reductase\|Methemoglobin Reductase]] | NADH-cytochrome b5 reductase (methemoglobin reductase, cytochrome b5 reductase) is the enzyme that normally reduces methemoglobin back to hemoglobin; methylene blue is reduced by it (via NADPH) to leucomethylene blue. |
| [[Methemoglobinemia\|Methemoglobinemia]] | Methemoglobinemia is a condition caused by elevated levels of Methemoglobin in the blood. |
| [[Methionine\|Methionine]] | Essential sulfur-containing amino acid and precursor of S-adenosylmethionine, the universal methyl donor. Its dietary restriction (methionine restriction) extends lifespan and recapitulates many benefits of protein restriction through FGF21-dependent and methyl-donor-dependent mechanisms. |
| [[Methionine Restriction\|Methionine Restriction]] | Dietary restriction of the sulfur-containing essential amino acid methionine. Methionine restriction extends lifespan of many species, reduces body weight, improves metabolic function, and reduces frailty, partially through FGF21 and effects on methyl-donor availability. |
| [[Methionine Synthase\|Methionine Synthase]] | Catalytic function: Methionine Synthase is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Methotrexate\|Methotrexate]] | A folate antagonist antimetabolite that inhibits dihydrofolate reductase, used as a first-line disease-modifying antirheumatic drug and as a cytotoxic agent in leukemia, lymphoma and osteosarcoma. |
| [[Methyl Donor\|Methyl Donor]] | Methyl donors are compounds that provide a labile methyl group (−CH₃) for methylation reactions in One-Carbon Metabolism. The principal methyl donor is SAMe (S-adenosylmethionine), which transfers ... |
| [[Methyl-beta-cyclodextrin\|Methyl-beta-cyclodextrin]] | Cyclodextrin derivative used as a solubilizing vehicle to increase water solubility of poorly soluble drugs. |
| [[Methylation\|Methylation]] | Methylation is a fundamental biochemical and epigenetic process involving the transfer of a methyl group to a substrate. It is critical for the regulation of gene expression, neurotransmitter m... |
| [[Methylation Cycle\|Methylation Cycle]] | The methylation cycle (also known as the one-carbon metabolism cycle) is a biochemical pathway that transfers methyl groups for DNA Methylation, Epigenetic regulation, neurotransmitter synthesis, a... |
| [[Methylene blue\|Methylene blue]] | Methylene blue (methylthioninium chloride) is an FDA-approved first-line treatment for acquired methemoglobinemia and a redox-cycling mitochondrial electron carrier with off-label uses in vasoplegic shock, neuroprotection, and low-dose cognitive/mitochondrial support. |
| [[Methylglyoxal (MG)\|Methylglyoxal (MG)]] | A highly reactive endogenous α-oxoaldehyde byproduct of glycolysis that glycates proteins (advanced glycation end-products) and, at controlled doses, exhibits selective anticancer cytotoxicity augmented by creatine. |
| [[METTL14\|METTL14]] | Methyltransferase-like 14, core subunit of the m6A writer complex (with METTL3) that also promotes SASP gene expression via enhancer–promoter looping. |
| [[METTL3\|METTL3]] | Methyltransferase-like 3, the catalytic subunit of the m6A writer complex that regulates RNA fate and autophagy flux via TFEB, and also reshapes enhancer-promoter contacts to drive SASP in senescence. |
| [[Mevalonate pathway\|Mevalonate pathway]] | The mevalonate pathway regulates ferroptosis by controlling selenocysteine tRNA maturation and CoQ10/isoprenoid production that modulate GPX4 and antioxidant defenses. |
| [[MFF\|MFF]] | Mitochondrial Fission Factor, an OMM adaptor that recruits DRP1 to initiate fission. |
| [[MFN1\|MFN1]] | Mitofusin 1 is an outer-mitochondrial-membrane dynamin-related GTPase that mediates mitochondrial outer-membrane fusion through GTPase-domain oligomerization. |
| [[MFN2\|MFN2]] | Mitofusin 2 is an outer-mitochondrial-membrane dynamin-related GTPase that mediates mitochondrial fusion and ER–mitochondria tethering; mutations cause CMT2A. |
| [[Mgm1\|Mgm1]] | Yeast mitochondrial dynamin-like GTPase, ortholog of mammalian OPA1, essential for fusion. |
| [[MHC type II\|MHC type II]] | Major histocompatibility complex class II molecules, involved in antigen presentation. |
| [[Michaelis-Menten Kinetics\|Michaelis-Menten Kinetics]] | The classical enzyme-kinetic framework describing reaction rate as a function of substrate concentration via Vmax and the Michaelis-Menten constant Km; central to the saturation requirement for drug hormesis. |
| [[Microbiome\|Microbiome]] | The collective community of commensal microbes and their metabolites that regulate host immunity, redox tone, and aging via the gut-brain and gut-NAD+ axes. |
| [[Microglia\|Microglia]] | Microglia originate from yolk-sac progenitors during early embryogenesis and are maintained throughout life by local self-renewal, independent of circulating monocytes. |
| [[Micronuclei\|Micronuclei]] | Aberrant nuclear structures formed from missegregated chromosomes that serve as cGAS-STING DNA-sensing platforms. |
| [[Microprocessor complex\|Microprocessor complex]] | Nuclear ribonucleoprotein complex of Drosha and DGCR8 that initiates canonical microRNA biogenesis by cleaving pri-miRNA into pre-miRNA. |
| [[MicroRNA\|MicroRNA]] | MicroRNA (miRNA) is a class of small, non-coding RNA molecules (typically 21-25 nucleotides long) that play a crucial role in the post-transcriptional regulation of gene expression. They functi... |
| [[MICU1\|MICU1]] | Mitochondrial calcium uptake regulator that gates the MCU pore, preventing Ca2+ uptake at low matrix Ca2+ and enabling it at high cytosolic Ca2+. |
| [[MICU2\|MICU2]] | Mitochondrial calcium uptake regulator that cooperates with MICU1 to keep the MCU pore closed at low cytosolic Ca2+, reinforcing the uptake threshold. |
| [[MID49\|MID49]] | Mitochondrial dynamics protein 49 (MID49/MIEF2), a DRP1 adaptor protein on the OMM that recruits DRP1 for mitochondrial fission |
| [[MID51\|MID51]] | Mitochondrial dynamics protein 51 (MID51/MIEF1), a DRP1 adaptor protein on the OMM; can inhibit DRP1 GTPase activity stimulated by MFF |
| [[MIF\|MIF]] | MIF is a moonlighting protein acting both as a pro-inflammatory cytokine and as the PAAN nuclease executing parthanatotic chromatinolysis. |
| [[Milbemycin Oxime\|Milbemycin Oxime]] | Milbemycin-class macrocyclic lactone (Interceptor, Sentinel) used for monthly heartworm prevention and intestinal-worm control in dogs and cats; the widest-margin ivermectin-like option and the preferred heartworm preventive for dogs with the MDR1 (ABCB1-1Δ) mutation. |
| [[Miltefosine\|Miltefosine]] | A zwitterionic alkylphosphocholine that is the only oral drug licensed for visceral leishmaniasis, killing the parasite by disrupting membrane lipid organisation and impairing lipid signalling. |
| [[Milton\|Milton]] | Adaptor protein that links MFN2 to kinesin via Miro for mitochondrial trafficking. |
| [[Minority MOMP\|Minority MOMP]] | Sublethal, partial mitochondrial outer membrane permeabilization that releases mtDNA and drives the SASP without committing the cell to apoptosis. |
| [[MIP-2\|MIP-2]] | Macrophage inflammatory protein 2 (CXCL2), a CXC chemokine that recruits neutrophils to sites of inflammation; its expression is suppressed downstream of SIRT3 activation. |
| [[miR-137\|miR-137]] | miR-137 is a microRNA that negatively regulates ferroptosis in melanoma by directly targeting the glutamine transporter SLC1A5. |
| [[miR-34a\|miR-34a]] | Tumor-suppressive microRNA induced by p53 that represses SIRT1 translation; a node in the p53–miR-34a–SIRT1 senescence feedback loop. |
| [[miR-494-3p\|miR-494-3p]] | MicroRNA that targets SIRT3 under oxidative stress, driving a senescence feedback loop systemically and accelerating cellular senescence in small airway epithelial cells in COPD. |
| [[miRNA\|miRNA]] | MicroRNAs, ~22-nt non-coding RNAs that post-transcriptionally regulate SASP factors and mediate intercellular senescence communication via exosomes. |
| [[Miro\|Miro]] | OMM protein that with Milton links MFN2 to kinesin motors for axonal mitochondrial transport. |
| [[MiT\|MiT]] | MiT (Microphthalmia/TFE family) refers to a family of basic helix-loop-helix leucine zipper transcription factors that includes MiTF (MITF), TFEB, TFE3, and TFEC. These proteins regulate Lysosomal ... |
| [[Mithridates VI\|Mithridates VI]] | Mithridates VI, King of Pontus (c. 134–63 BCE), practiced gradual poison exposure (Mithridatism), a historical precursor to the modern concept of Hormesis. |
| [[Mitochondria\|Mitochondria]] | Double-membrane organelles that generate ATP via oxidative phosphorylation and regulate apoptosis, calcium, and redox signaling; their dysfunction is central to aging and is mitigated by mTOR inhibition. |
| [[Mitochondria-Associated Membranes\|Mitochondria-Associated Membranes]] | Mitochondria-ER contact sites (MERCs) are regulatory hubs for Ca2+ signaling, lipid exchange, mitochondrial dynamics, and stress adaptation. Their remodeling during aging weakens inter-organelle communication and contributes to stem cell decline and inflammaging. |
| [[Mitochondrial Biogenesis\|Mitochondrial Biogenesis]] | The process by which cells increase mitochondrial mass and number through growth and division of preexisting mitochondria, driven by PGC-1α, NRF1/2, and TFEB, and coupled to sirtuin/NAD+ signaling, exercise, and caloric restriction. |
| [[Mitochondrial DNA\|Mitochondrial DNA]] | 16.5 kb circular DNA encoding 13 OXPHOS proteins, 2 rRNAs, and 22 tRNAs; mutations linked to disease. |
| [[Mitochondrial Dynamics\|Mitochondrial Dynamics]] | Mitochondrial dynamics refers to the continuous processes of mitochondrial fusion and mitochondrial fission that regulate mitochondrial morphology, number, and function within a cell. |
| [[Mitochondrial Dysfunction\|Mitochondrial Dysfunction]] | Mitochondrial dysfunction is a central feature of Parkinson's Disease and is thought to be both a cause and a consequence of other pathological processes. |
| [[Mitochondrial Dysfunction-Associated Senescence\|Mitochondrial Dysfunction-Associated Senescence]] | A form of cellular senescence triggered by mitochondrial dysfunction, driven by NAD+/NADH collapse and AMPK–p53 signaling, and characterized by a p53-dependent SASP with an attenuated IL-1-dependent inflammatory arm. |
| [[Mitochondrial Electron Transport Chain\|Mitochondrial Electron Transport Chain]] | The mitochondrial electron transport chain (ETC) is the series of protein complexes in the inner mitochondrial membrane that transfers electrons to generate the proton gradient driving ATP synthesis. |
| [[Mitochondrial Fission\|Mitochondrial Fission]] | Mitochondrial fission is the DRP1-mediated division of a mitochondrion into two, required for organelle segregation, quality control via mitophagy, and apoptosis. |
| [[Mitochondrial Free Radical Theory of Aging\|Mitochondrial Free Radical Theory of Aging]] | A refinement of the free radical theory of aging proposed by Denham Harman in 1972, specifically implicating mitochondrial ROS production as the primary driver of aging through a vicious cycle of oxidative damage. |
| [[Mitochondrial Function\|Mitochondrial Function]] | The integrated capacity of mitochondria to carry out oxidative phosphorylation, metabolite and ion handling, redox balance, and stress signaling; declining mitochondrial function is central to aging, senescence, and metabolic disease. |
| [[Mitochondrial Fusion\|Mitochondrial Fusion]] | Mitochondrial fusion is the GTPase-driven joining of two mitochondria at the outer and inner membranes, enabling content mixing, stress resilience, and quality control. |
| [[Mitochondrial Integrity\|Mitochondrial Integrity]] | Maintenance of mitochondrial structure, function, and homeostasis via balanced fusion and fission. |
| [[Mitochondrial Intermembrane Space\|Mitochondrial Intermembrane Space]] | The space between the outer and inner mitochondrial membranes where MFN HR2 and S-OPA1 domains face. |
| [[Mitochondrial Matrix\|Mitochondrial Matrix]] | The compartment enclosed by the inner mitochondrial membrane, site of the TCA cycle and mtDNA. |
| [[Mitochondrial Membrane Potential\|Mitochondrial Membrane Potential]] | The mitochondrial membrane potential (ΔΨm) is the proton-motive force across the inner mitochondrial membrane; its glutamate-induced collapse is attenuated by methylene blue and neuroprotective phenothiazines. |
| [[Mitochondrial outer membrane permeabilization\|Mitochondrial outer membrane permeabilization]] | The pivotal event in the intrinsic apoptotic pathway where BAX/BAK pores permeabilize the outer mitochondrial membrane; sublethal (minority) MOMP drives senescence. |
| [[Mitochondrial Permeability Transition\|Mitochondrial Permeability Transition]] | Pathological increase in inner and outer mitochondrial membrane permeability causing swelling, ROS, NAD+ loss, and necrotic death. |
| [[Mitochondrial Permeability Transition Pore\|Mitochondrial Permeability Transition Pore]] | The Mitochondrial Permeability Transition Pore is a calcium- and ROS-gated inner-membrane channel whose prolonged opening collapses energetics and releases pro-death factors. |
| [[Mitochondrial Processing Peptidase\|Mitochondrial Processing Peptidase]] | Matrix protease (MPP) that cleaves N-terminal mitochondrial targeting sequences from imported proteins, including the PINK1 MTS. |
| [[Mitochondrial Quality Control\|Mitochondrial Quality Control]] | The collective cellular mechanisms that maintain mitochondrial homeostasis, including mitochondrial dynamics (fission/fusion), mitophagy, mitochondrial biogenesis, and proteostasis within the organelle. |
| [[Mitochondrial Respiration\|Mitochondrial Respiration]] | Mitochondrial respiration is oxygen consumption coupled to ATP synthesis by the electron transport chain; methylene blue can enhance it when complexes are impaired. |
| [[Mitochondrial ROS\|Mitochondrial ROS]] | Mitochondrial ROS (mtROS) are Reactive Oxygen Species generated primarily at Complex I and Complex III of the electron transport chain; they drive oxidative damage at high levels but mediate adaptive signaling at low levels. |
| [[Mitochondrial Superoxide\|Mitochondrial Superoxide]] | Mitochondrial superoxide is the primary reactive oxygen species generated at the electron transport chain; methylene blue can reduce its production by bypassing damaged complexes. |
| [[Mitochondrial Targeting Sequence\|Mitochondrial Targeting Sequence]] | N-terminal sequence on OPA1 isoforms that targets the protein to the IMM and is cleaved on import. |
| [[Mitochondrial Translation\|Mitochondrial Translation]] | The process by which the ~13 protein-coding genes of the mitochondrial genome are transcribed and translated by mitochondrial ribosomes within the organelle; essential for oxidative phosphorylation and a target of quality-control and aging-related regulation. |
| [[Mitochondrial Trifunctional Protein α\|Mitochondrial Trifunctional Protein α]] | Mitochondrial trifunctional protein alpha subunit (MTPα, HADHB) is the alpha subunit of the mitochondrial trifunctional protein catalyzing the last three steps of long-chain fatty acid β-oxidation. |
| [[Mitochondrial Uncoupling\|Mitochondrial Uncoupling]] | Dissipation of the proton motive force across the inner mitochondrial membrane, decoupling electron transport from ATP synthesis and releasing heat. |
| [[Mitochondrial Unfolded Protein Response\|Mitochondrial Unfolded Protein Response]] | The Mitochondrial Unfolded Protein Response (UPRmt) is a form of retrograde signaling that allows Mitochondria to communicate with the nucleus in response to proteotoxic stress, specifically the ac... |
| [[Mitochondrial-Derived Peptide\|Mitochondrial-Derived Peptide]] | Small bioactive peptides encoded within the mitochondrial genome that function as retrograde signals and endocrine factors |
| [[mitoCPR\|mitoCPR]] | The mitochondrial compromised protein import response is a stress pathway activated when import through the TOM translocon stalls, clearing precursors via PDR3, Cis1, and Msp1/ATAD1. |
| [[Mitohormesis\|Mitohormesis]] | Mitohormesis is a biological process where low-level mitochondrial stress triggers adaptive cytoprotective responses that increase resilience and lifespan. Key mitohormetic interventions include exercise, caloric restriction, and metformin. The response is mediated through the Integrated Stress Response (DELE1-OMA1-HRI), UPRmt, and mitokine signaling via GDF15 and FGF21. |
| [[Mitokines\|Mitokines]] | Mitokines are soluble factors (peptides or proteins) secreted by cells undergoing mitochondrial stress that can trigger stress responses, such as the Mitochondrial Unfolded Protein Response, in dis... |
| [[Mitophagy\|Mitophagy]] | Mitophagy is a specialized form of Selective Autophagy that targets damaged or dysfunctional Mitochondria for degradation. It is a critical quality control mechanism for maintaining mitochondri... |
| [[mitoRQC\|mitoRQC]] | Mitochondrial ribosome quality control is a surveillance pathway that recognizes and degrades nascent polypeptides stalled on mitochondrial ribosomes, preventing proteotoxic accumulation. |
| [[Mitosis\|Mitosis]] | Mitosis is the process of Cell Division in which a single Eukaryotic Cell divides its duplicated Chromosomes into two identical daughter nuclei. It consists of stages: Prophase, Prometaphase, Metap... |
| [[MitoStores\|MitoStores]] | Cytosolic granules that serve as temporary storage depots for mitochondrial precursor proteins when mitochondrial import is impaired or saturated. |
| [[mitoTAD\|mitoTAD]] | Mitochondrial protein translocation-associated degradation. A quality control pathway that extracts stalled mitochondrial precursor proteins from the TOM/TIM translocons and targets them for proteasomal degradation. |
| [[mitoTALENs\|mitoTALENs]] | Mitochondria-targeted transcription activator-like effector nucleases that cleave mutant mtDNA to shift heteroplasmy toward wild-type genomes; an experimental gene-editing strategy for mitochondrial disease. |
| [[Mitotane\|Mitotane]] | Mitotane is an adrenolytic agent used in adrenocortical carcinoma whose toxicity profile may be improved by ferroptosis-inducing alternatives. |
| [[mitoZFNs\|mitoZFNs]] | Mitochondria-targeted zinc finger nucleases that selectively cleave mutant mtDNA to shift heteroplasmy toward wild-type genomes; the first demonstrated mtDNA-editing approach for mitochondrial disease. |
| [[MJ33\|MJ33]] | A selective inhibitor of the iPLA2 (acidic calcium-independent phospholipase A2) activity of peroxiredoxin 6 (PRDX6); phenocopies apigenin's senomorphic SASP suppression. |
| [[MLH1\|MLH1]] | Core mismatch-repair gene; germline loss causes Lynch syndrome and microsatellite instability. |
| [[MLL1\|MLL1]] | Histone-lysine N-methyltransferase 2A (KMT2A), the major H3K4me3 methyltransferase that regulates development and hematopoiesis, drives MLL-rearranged leukemias, and restrains SASP by sustaining the DNA damage response in senescent cells. |
| [[MMP-12\|MMP-12]] | Matrix metalloproteinase-12, a zinc-dependent endopeptidase primarily secreted by macrophages, with major roles in elastin degradation, ECM remodelling, inflammation resolution, and SASP-driven tissue ageing. |
| [[MMP-13\|MMP-13]] | Matrix metalloproteinase 13 (collagenase-3), a SASP protease that degrades type II collagen and remodels the extracellular matrix, contributing to osteoarthritis and age-related tissue pathology. |
| [[MMP-2\|MMP-2]] | Matrix metalloproteinase-2 (gelatinase A), a zinc-dependent SASP protease and mortality-associated senescence biomarker. |
| [[MMP-3\|MMP-3]] | Matrix metalloproteinase-3 (stromelysin-1), a SASP protease that remodels the ECM and promotes EMT, tumorigenesis and tissue ageing. |
| [[MMP-7\|MMP-7]] | Matrix metalloproteinase 7 (matrilysin), a secreted zinc-dependent endopeptidase that degrades ECM components and sheds substrates; implicated in epithelial-mesenchymal transition and renal fibrosis downstream of beta-catenin. |
| [[MMP-9\|MMP-9]] | MMP-9 (Matrix Metalloproteinase 9, Gelatinase B) is a zinc-dependent endopeptidase that degrades Extracellular Matrix components including Collagen IV, Gelatin, and Elastin. It is produced as a zym... |
| [[MMP1\|MMP1]] | Catalytic function: MMP1 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[MODY1\|MODY1]] | MODY1 is maturity-onset diabetes of the young type 1, caused by heterozygous pathogenic variants in HNF4A, producing a progressive, sulfonylurea-responsive form of monogenic diabetes. |
| [[Mon1-Ccz1\|Mon1-Ccz1]] | A heterodimeric Rab7 guanine nucleotide exchange factor that executes the Rab5-to-Rab7 switch at endosomes and autophagosomes, recruiting Rab7 and driving late-endosome and lysosome identity. |
| [[Monoacylglycerol Lipase\|Monoacylglycerol Lipase]] | Monoacylglycerol lipase (MGL, MGLL) is the final-step adipocyte lipase that hydrolyzes monoacylglycerol to glycerol and free fatty acid, completing triglyceride catabolism during lipolysis. |
| [[Monoamine oxidase\|Monoamine oxidase]] | Monoamine oxidase (also known as MAO) is a family of enzymes that catalyze the oxidation of monoamines. |
| [[Monoamine Oxidase Inhibitor\|Monoamine Oxidase Inhibitor]] | Monoamine oxidase inhibitors (MAOIs) block the enzymatic breakdown of monoamine neurotransmitters (serotonin, dopamine, norepinephrine), raising synaptic levels; methylene blue acts as a potent MAO-A inhibitor. |
| [[Monocytes\|Monocytes]] | Circulating mononuclear leukocytes of the innate immune system that differentiate into macrophages and dendritic cells upon tissue infiltration, serving as key effectors of inflammation and tissue remodelling. |
| [[Mosquito\|Mosquito]] | Blood-feeding dipteran insects of the family Culicidae, the principal vectors of arboviruses, malaria and filarial nematodes, and the focus of vector-control interventions including Wolbachia and Wolbachia-free approaches. |
| [[Motor Neurons\|Motor Neurons]] | Motor neurons are the cholinergic upper and lower neurons of the motor system, forming a two-neuron descending pathway that terminates on skeletal muscle; lower motor neurons subdivide into alpha, beta and gamma types whose properties are matched to the muscle fibres they innervate. |
| [[MOTS-c\|MOTS-c]] | MOTS-c is a 16-amino-acid micropeptide encoded by a short open reading frame in the mitochondrial 12S rRNA gene (MT-RNR1). Exercise-induced, it acts on AMPK to improve insulin sensitivity, promote mitochondrial function, and protect muscle and bone against age-related decline. |
| [[MPC\|MPC]] | Mitochondrial pyruvate carrier, a heterodimeric complex of MPC1 and MPC2 in the inner mitochondrial membrane that imports pyruvate into the matrix for the TCA cycle. Upregulated in cellular senescence; its inhibition suppresses the SASP without affecting cell cycle arrest. |
| [[MPEG-PCL\|MPEG-PCL]] | Monomethyl poly(ethylene glycol)-poly(ε-caprolactone) (MPEG-PCL) is an amphiphilic block copolymer that self-assembles into polymeric micelles for delivering hydrophobic drugs like fisetin. |
| [[mPOS\|mPOS]] | Mitochondrial precursor over-accumulation stress. A cellular stress condition arising when mitochondrial precursor proteins accumulate excessively in the cytosol, leading to proteotoxicity and cell death. |
| [[MRN Complex\|MRN Complex]] | The MRN complex is the MRE11-RAD50-NBS1 heterotrimer, the primary sensor of DNA double-strand breaks and the activator of ATM, coordinating end resection for homologous recombination repair, alternative end joining, and telomere maintenance. |
| [[Mst1\|Mst1]] | Mammalian STE20-like kinase 1 (STK4), a Hippo pathway kinase regulating apoptosis, autophagy, and cell proliferation; its phosphorylation is inhibited by melatonin to protect the diabetic heart via SIRT3. |
| [[mtDNA\|mtDNA]] | Mitochondrial DNA that can be released into the cytosol to activate innate immune signaling via cGAS-STING, acting as a DAMP in senescence and aging. |
| [[MTFP1\|MTFP1]] | Mitochondrial fission process 1 (MTFP1/MTP18), an inner mitochondrial membrane protein essential for mitochondrial division and cardiac function |
| [[MTHFD2\|MTHFD2]] | MTHFD2 (methylenetetrahydrofolate dehydrogenase 2) is the mitochondrial bifunctional enzyme of one-carbon metabolism that generates NADPH; deacetylated by SIRT3 and linked to tumor redox balance, chemoresistance, and metabolic plasticity. |
| [[MTHFR\|MTHFR]] | MTHFR (Methylenetetrahydrofolate Reductase) is a critical enzyme in the Methylation Cycle. It is responsible for converting 5,10-methylenetetrahydrofolate to 5-methyltetrahydrofolate, the prima... |
| [[mTOR\|mTOR]] | mTOR (Mechanistic Target of Rapamycin) is a protein kinase that serves as a central ''master regulator'' of cellular metabolism. It integrates signals from nutrients, growth factors, and energy s... |
| [[mTOR Inhibitor\|mTOR Inhibitor]] | A pharmacological agent that suppresses the mechanistic target of rapamycin, inducing autophagy and mimicking caloric restriction. |
| [[mTORC1\|mTORC1]] | mTORC1 (mechanistic target of rapamycin complex 1) is a multi-protein signaling complex that integrates nutrient, energy, and growth factor signals to coordinate cell growth, protein synthesis, and metabolism. |
| [[mTORC2\|mTORC2]] | mTOR complex 2 is a multi-protein signaling complex built around the mTOR kinase with rictor, responsible for phosphorylating AKT, SGK, and PKC; insensitively regulated by acute rapamycin but trapped by long-term rapamycin exposure. |
| [[mTORopathies\|mTORopathies]] | A class of neurological and developmental disorders driven by hyperactivation of the mTOR signaling pathway, including tuberous sclerosis complex, focal cortical dysplasia, and GATOR1-related epilepsies. |
| [[MUC1\|MUC1]] | MUC1-C is a transmembrane oncoprotein that complexes with xCT and CD44v to maintain GSH and ferroptosis resistance in triple-negative breast cancer. |
| [[Mucositis\|Mucositis]] | Inflammation and ulceration of the mucous membranes (gastrointestinal tract and oral cavity); a dose-limiting adverse effect of mTOR inhibitor therapy, closely related to stomatitis. |
| [[MUL1\|MUL1]] | E3 ubiquitin ligase located on the outer mitochondrial membrane that functions as a parallel and compensatory pathway to Parkin-mediated mitophagy. |
| [[Multiple Myeloma\|Multiple Myeloma]] | Multiple myeloma is a cancer that forms in a type of white blood cell called a plasma cell. |
| [[Murine embryonic fibroblast\|Murine embryonic fibroblast]] | Primary fibroblast cultured from mouse embryos, used as feeder layers to support embryonic stem cell growth. |
| [[Mus musculus\|Mus musculus]] | The house mouse, a widely used mammalian model organism in biomedical research, including senescence and aging studies. |
| [[Muscle\|Muscle]] | Contractile tissue spanning skeletal, cardiac, and smooth types; major metabolic and creatine reservoir. |
| [[Muscle Atrophy\|Muscle Atrophy]] | The pathological loss of skeletal muscle mass and function resulting from accelerated protein degradation (UPS, autophagy-lysosome) and suppressed protein synthesis (mTORC1/FOXO), relevant to disuse, aging, and disease. |
| [[Muscle Hypertrophy\|Muscle Hypertrophy]] | The increase in skeletal muscle cross-sectional area and mass through gains in contractile protein, driven by mechanical loading and anabolic signaling. |
| [[Muscular Dystrophies (MD)\|Muscular Dystrophies (MD)]] | A group of inherited disorders characterized by progressive skeletal muscle weakness and degeneration due to defects in structural muscle proteins. |
| [[Mutations\|Mutations]] | Permanent alterations to the DNA sequence; contrasted with reversible epigenetic information loss in the Information Theory of Aging and RCM hypothesis. |
| [[Myelin\|Myelin]] | Myelin is a multilamellar lipid-protein sheath that wraps around axons in the Nervous System, produced by Oligodendrocytes in the central nervous system (CNS) and Schwann Cells in the periphera... |
| [[Myelin Basic Protein\|Myelin Basic Protein]] | Myelin basic protein is the second most abundant protein of CNS myelin, an intrinsically disordered ~18.5 kDa protein whose net positive charge lets it collapse the cytoplasmic leaflets into the major dense line, and whose citrullination and isoform switching mark the demyelination of multiple sclerosis. |
| [[Myelodysplastic Syndrome\|Myelodysplastic Syndrome]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Myelodysplastic Syndrome is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves gen... |
| [[Myeloperoxidase\|Myeloperoxidase]] | Myeloperoxidase (MPO) is a heme-containing enzyme primarily found in the azurophilic granules of Neutrophils. It plays a critical role in the innate immune system''s ability to kill pathogens. |
| [[Myocardial Ischemia-Reperfusion Injury\|Myocardial Ischemia-Reperfusion Injury]] | Damage to cardiac muscle that occurs when blood flow is restored to ischemic myocardium, paradoxically worsening injury through oxidative stress and inflammation |
| [[Myofibroblast\|Myofibroblast]] | A contractile, activated fibroblast state characterised by alpha-smooth muscle actin stress fibres and increased matrix production, generated by TGF-beta signalling and responsible both for wound contraction and for the progressive matrix deposition of fibrosis. |
| [[Myosin\|Myosin]] | Actin-based motor protein powering muscle contraction, cytokinesis, and vesicle transport. |
| [[Myostatin\|Myostatin]] | A secreted TGF-β family member (GDF-8) that acts as a negative regulator of skeletal muscle mass; its inhibition promotes muscle growth. |
| [[Myricetin\|Myricetin]] | Myricetin is a flavonol polyphenol with antioxidant and senotherapeutic activity, tested alongside fisetin in a 10-flavonoid senolytic screen. |
| [[Myristoylation\|Myristoylation]] | Myristoylation is an N-terminal covalent attachment of a myristoyl group that tethers FSP1 to the inner leaflet of the plasma membrane, positioning it to regenerate the ubiquinol radical trap where lipid peroxidation is initiated. |
| [[Myxomatous Mitral Valve Disease\|Myxomatous Mitral Valve Disease]] | Age-dependent valve disease in dogs (and humans) where senescent valve interstitial cells have impaired autophagic flux; rapamycin restores flux and reverses senescent phenotype via p62-mediated p16/p21 degradation. |
| [[N-Acetylcysteine\|N-Acetylcysteine]] | N-Acetylcysteine (NAC) is the N-acetyl derivative of L-cysteine; it acts as a mucolytic and, more importantly, as a precursor that replenishes intracellular Glutathione and supports antioxidant defense. |
| [[N-Formylkynurenine\|N-Formylkynurenine]] | N-Formylkynurenine is the immediate product of IDO1 and TDO activity on tryptophan, which is rapidly deformylated to kynurenine by kynurenine formamidase. |
| [[N6-methyladenosine\|N6-methyladenosine]] | The most abundant internal mRNA methylation mark (m6A), deposited by the METTL3-METTL14 writer complex and implicated in senescence-associated transcriptomic and 3D-genome remodeling. |
| [[NAC\|NAC]] | N-acetylcysteine, a peroxidase/antioxidant inhibitor used as a comparator showing apigenin's SASP suppression is independent of PRDX6 peroxidase activity. |
| [[NAD+\|NAD+]] | NAD+ (Nicotinamide Adenine Dinucleotide) is a critical coenzyme found in every cell, essential for energy Metabolism and a vital signaling molecule. It serves as an obligatory co-substrate for all ... |
| [[NAD+ Biosynthesis\|NAD+ Biosynthesis]] | Enzymatic routes that generate nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide, spanning de novo, salvage, and Preiss-Handler pathways that fuel sirtuins, PARPs, and redox metabolism. |
| [[NAD+ Salvage Pathway\|NAD+ Salvage Pathway]] | The primary biosynthetic route for maintaining cellular NAD+ levels, recycling nicotinamide back to NAD+ via the rate-limiting enzyme NAMPT. |
| [[NADH\|NADH]] | Biochemical properties: NADH is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic flux an... |
| [[NADH-dependent methemoglobin reductase\|NADH-dependent methemoglobin reductase]] | - Methemoglobin: The substrate reduced by this enzyme. |
| [[NADP+\|NADP+]] | NADP+ is the oxidized cousin of NAD+ that accepts electrons to form NADPH, fueling reductive biosynthesis and glutathione-dependent antioxidant defense. |
| [[NADPH\|NADPH]] | NADPH is the reduced nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide phosphate, a principal cellular reducing equivalent that fuels FSP1 (ubiquinone reduction) and glutathione reductase (GSH recycling), linking redox and ferroptosis defense. |
| [[NADPH-cytochrome P450 reductase\|NADPH-cytochrome P450 reductase]] | Catalytic function: NADPH-cytochrome P450 reductase is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[NAFLD\|NAFLD]] | Non-alcoholic fatty liver disease, the most common liver disease worldwide and the hepatic manifestation of metabolic syndrome; renamed MASLD in the 2023 multisociety Delphi consensus. |
| [[Naked Mole Rat\|Naked Mole Rat]] | A long-lived, cancer-resistant rodent model exhibiting muted cGAS-STING signaling despite genomic instability. |
| [[NAMPT\|NAMPT]] | NAMPT (Nicotinamide phosphoribosyltransferase) is a key enzyme in the salvage pathway of NAD+ biosynthesis, whose levels decrease significantly in various tissues during aging. |
| [[Nanocrystals\|Nanocrystals]] | Nanocrystals are carrier-free aggregates of pure drug molecules in the nanometer range that increase surface area and dissolution velocity of poorly soluble compounds. |
| [[Nanoemulsions\|Nanoemulsions]] | Nanoemulsions are thermodynamically unstable but kinetically stable oil-in-water or water-in-oil dispersions (20–200 nm) that enhance the solubility, permeability, and bioavailability of poorly soluble drugs. |
| [[Nanoparticles\|Nanoparticles]] | Nanoparticles are sub-micron (10–1000 nm) carriers — polymeric, lipid, protein, or metallic — that improve the solubility, stability, and bioavailability of poorly water-soluble bioactive compounds. |
| [[Nanotechnology\|Nanotechnology]] | Nanotechnology in drug delivery refers to the use of nanoscale carriers (nanoparticles, liposomes, nanoemulsions, nanocrystals) to improve the solubility, bioavailability, and therapeutic index of bioactive compounds. |
| [[NAPRT\|NAPRT]] | Nicotinic Acid Phosphoribosyltransferase; enzyme in the Preiss-Handler pathway converting nicotinic acid to nicotinic acid mononucleotide for NAD+ biosynthesis |
| [[NASH\|NASH]] | Inflammatory and progressive form of non-alcoholic fatty liver disease, characterized by hepatic steatosis with hepatocyte injury, inflammation, and fibrosis |
| [[Natural Killer Cells\|Natural Killer Cells]] | Innate lymphoid cells with cytotoxic activity against virus-infected and tumour cells, regulated by activating and inhibitory receptors that recognise MHC-I and stress ligands. |
| [[Navitoclax\|Navitoclax]] | Navitoclax (ABT-263) is an orally bioavailable BH3-mimetic that inhibits BCL-2/BCL-xL/BCL-w, acting as a senolytic and anticancer agent limited by thrombocytopenia. |
| [[NB-4746\|NB-4746]] | NB-4746 is a brain-penetrant, clinical-stage SARM1 inhibitor developed by Nura Bio for axon-degeneration disorders, including chemotherapy-induced peripheral neuropathy and traumatic brain injury. |
| [[NBR1\|NBR1]] | Neighbor of BRCA1 gene 1, a cytoplasmic selective autophagy receptor that mediates mitophagy alongside p62 and NDP52 |
| [[NBS1\|NBS1]] | Nijmegen breakage syndrome 1 protein; core subunit of the MRE11–RAD50–NBS1 (MRN) complex for DNA double-strand-break sensing, deacetylated and stabilized by SIRT1. |
| [[NCOA4\|NCOA4]] | Nuclear receptor coactivator 4 (also called androgen receptor-associated protein 70, ARA70). Best known as the receptor mediating ferritinophagy, the ATG5-ATG7-dependent autophagic delivery of ferritin heavy and light chains to lysosomes, which releases free iron and thereby licenses ferroptosis. |
| [[NDP52\|NDP52]] | Autophagy receptor that serves as a primary mitophagy receptor in the PINK1/Parkin pathway, bridging ubiquitin-coated mitochondria to LC3 on the phagophore. |
| [[NDUFA10\|NDUFA10]] | A subunit of Mitochondrial Complex I (NADH:ubiquinone oxidoreductase) phosphorylated by PINK1 to regulate bioenergetics. |
| [[NDUFS1\|NDUFS1]] | NADH:ubiquinone oxidoreductase core subunit S1, the 75 kDa Fe-S cluster subunit of mitochondrial complex I. It is a core catalytic subunit of the N-module, and biallelic NDUFS1 variants cause Leigh syndrome and other complex I deficiencies. |
| [[Neglected Tropical Diseases\|Neglected Tropical Diseases]] | A WHO-defined portfolio of 20 parasitic and fungal diseases of the tropics that disproportionately affect the poorest populations, prioritised for control and elimination through mass drug administration. |
| [[Nematode\|Nematode]] | Roundworms of the phylum Nematoda — unsegmented round-bodied pseudocoelomate animals ranging from free-living soil worms to major human parasites, and including the model organism C. elegans. |
| [[NEMO\|NEMO]] | NF-κB essential modulator (IKKγ); regulatory subunit of the IKK complex that relays DNA damage response and cytokine signals to NF-κB activation. |
| [[Neonatal Adrenoleukodystrophy\|Neonatal Adrenoleukodystrophy]] | Neonatal Adrenoleukodystrophy (NALD) is an intermediate form of Zellweger Spectrum Disorders (ZSD), a group of autosomal recessive peroxisomal biogenesis disorders resulting from PEX gene mutations. |
| [[NET\|NET]] | Overview: NET is a membrane transport protein that facilitates the movement of molecules across biological membranes. It mediates substrate translocation through conformational changes driven b... |
| [[NETosis\|NETosis]] | Regulated neutrophil death with extrusion of neutrophil extracellular traps (NETs) — decondensed chromatin plus histones, elastase, and myeloperoxidase — via largely PAD4-mediated histone citrullination. |
| [[Neuroblastoma\|Neuroblastoma]] | Embryonal tumor of the sympathetic nervous system arising from neural crest cells |
| [[Neurodegeneration\|Neurodegeneration]] | Neurodegeneration is the progressive loss of structure or function of neurons, including death of neurons. It is a key feature of diseases like Alzheimer's Disease and Parkinson's Disease, often li... |
| [[Neurodegenerative Disease\|Neurodegenerative Disease]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Neurodegenerative Disease is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves ge... |
| [[Neurodegenerative Diseases\|Neurodegenerative Diseases]] | Neurodegenerative Diseases are a group of disorders characterized by the progressive loss of structure or function of neurons, including death of neurons. Aging is the single most significant r... |
| [[Neurofibrillary tangles\|Neurofibrillary Tangles]] | Intracellular aggregates of hyperphosphorylated tau protein, a hallmark pathology of Alzheimer's disease and other tauopathies. |
| [[Neurogenesis\|Neurogenesis]] | Neurogenesis is the process by which nervous system cells (neurons) are produced from Neural Stem Cell and Progenitor Cell. It occurs during embryonic development and persists in specific adult... |
| [[Neuroinflammation\|Neuroinflammation]] | Immune response within the central nervous system that drives pathology in neurodegenerative disease and brain injury. |
| [[Neuron\|Neuron]] | Excitable eukaryotic cell of the nervous system specialized for receiving, integrating, and transmitting electrical and chemical signals. |
| [[Neuroprotection\|Neuroprotection]] | Neuroprotection refers to the mechanisms and strategies used to protect the central nervous system from injury and neurodegeneration. |
| [[Neurotoxicity\|Neurotoxicity]] | The property of an agent or biological state to damage neurons, here relevant to SIRT2 overactivity and to adrenochrome-derived oxidative stress hypotheses. |
| [[Neurotransmitters\|Neurotransmitters]] | Neurotransmitters are endogenous chemical messengers that transmit signals across a Synapse from one neuron to another. |
| [[Neutral Red\|Neutral Red]] | Neutral Red is a phenazine (sulfur-to-nitrogen substituted) methylene blue analog; it enhances cellular oxygen consumption but lacks alternative electron-transfer function and shows weak neuroprotection. |
| [[Neutrophil Elastase\|Neutrophil Elastase]] | The azurophil-granule serine protease (ELANE) that degrades chromatin packaging and decorates NETs — effector and marker of NETosis alongside MPO and cit-H3. |
| [[Neutrophils\|Neutrophils]] | Neutrophils (also known as polymorphonuclear leukocytes or granulocytes) are the most abundant type of white blood cell and serve as the first line of defense in the innate immune system. |
| [[NF-κB\|NF-κB]] | Nuclear factor kappa-light-chain-enhancer of activated B cells (NF-κB) is a protein complex that controls transcription of DNA, cytokine production, and cell survival. It plays a central role in immunity, inflammation, and redox signaling. |
| [[NFATc2\|NFATc2]] | Nuclear factor of activated T-cells 2, a calcium-responsive transcription factor deacetylated by SIRT2 in cardiac hypertrophy |
| [[NFS1\|NFS1]] | NFS1 is a mitochondrial cysteine desulfurase whose inhibition promotes iron starvation and sensitizes cells to ferroptosis under oxidative stress. |
| [[Niacin\|Niacin]] | Biochemical properties: Niacin is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic flux ... |
| [[Nicotinamide Mononucleotide\|Nicotinamide Mononucleotide]] | Nicotinamide Mononucleotide (NMN) is a direct precursor to NAD+ and a key intermediate in the biosynthesis of NAD+ in cells, used to enhance mitochondrial energy production and support longevit... |
| [[Nicotinamide nucleotide transhydrogenase\|Nicotinamide nucleotide transhydrogenase]] | Mitochondrial inner-membrane enzyme (NNT) that transfers hydride from NADH to NADP+, coupling to proton translocation. |
| [[Nicotinamide Riboside\|Nicotinamide Riboside]] | Nicotinamide Riboside (NR) is a naturally occurring pyridine nucleoside form of vitamin B3 (niacin) and a direct precursor to NAD+ (nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide). NR is phosphorylated by nicot... |
| [[Nitric Oxide\|Nitric Oxide]] | Nitric Oxide ($NO^{\bullet}$) is a gaseous signaling molecule that\ \ plays critical roles in the cardiovascular, nervous, and immune systems. It is\ \ also a key player in the development of 'nitrat... |
| [[Nitroimidazole\|Nitroimidazole]] | Class of nitrogen-containing heterocyclic compounds with antimicrobial and hypoxia-selective radiosensitizing properties. |
| [[NIX\|NIX]] | Bcl-2/adenovirus E1B interacting protein 3-like (BNIP3L/NIX), a mitophagy receptor on the outer mitochondrial membrane |
| [[NKG2A\|NKG2A]] | NKG2A (KLRC1), an inhibitory C-type lectin-like receptor that pairs with CD94 to recognize HLA-E and deliver ITIM-mediated stop signals to NK cells and a subset of T cells. |
| [[NLRP3\|NLRP3]] | NOD-like receptor pyrin domain-containing protein 3 (NLRP3, cryopyrin), the sensor component of the NLRP3 inflammasome. It is a tripartite PYD-NACHT-LRR protein activated by a two-step priming-then-activation process that culminates in caspase-1 activation and pyroptosis. |
| [[NMDA receptor\|NMDA receptor]] | A glutamate-gated ion channel central to excitatory neurotransmission, synaptic plasticity, and Ca2+ signaling; implicated in seizures, stroke, and excitotoxicity, and a target of honokiol. |
| [[NMN\|NMN (Nicotinamide Mononucleotide)]] | Nicotinamide Mononucleotide (NMN) is an endogenous nucleotide derived from vitamin B3 (niacin) and a direct biosynthetic precursor to NAD+ (nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide). NMN serves as a key i... |
| [[NMNAT\|NMNAT]] | NMNAT (Nicotinamide mononucleotide adenylyltransferase) is a family of enzymes catalyzing the final step in NAD+ biosynthesis, with three compartment-specific mammalian isoforms and a neuroprotective chaperone function. |
| [[NMNAT2\|NMNAT2]] | NMNAT2 (Nicotinamide Mononucleotide Adenylyl Transferase 2) is a neuronal NAD+ synthase with a short half-life that maintains axonal NAD+ and suppresses SARM1; its injury-dependent loss is the primary trigger of Wallerian degeneration. |
| [[NMRK1\|NMRK1]] | NMRK1 (Nicotinamide riboside kinase 1), also known as NRK1, is an enzyme that directly converts Nicotinamide riboside (NR) to Nicotinamide mononucleotide (NMN), bypassing the need for NAMPT in NAD+... |
| [[NMRK2\|NMRK2]] | NMRK2 (Nicotinamide riboside kinase 2), also known as NRK2, is an enzyme predominantly expressed in cardiac and skeletal muscle that converts Nicotinamide riboside (NR) to Nicotinamide mononucleoti... |
| [[NNMT\|NNMT]] | Nicotinamide N-methyltransferase (NNMT) is a cytosolic enzyme that methylates nicotinamide to 1-methylnicotinamide, diverting nicotinamide away from the NAD+ salvage pathway and thereby shaping cellular NAD+ availability. |
| [[Nociceptor\|Nociceptor]] | Sensory neurons whose cGAS-STING signaling drives chronic neuropathic pain and regulates axonal regeneration. |
| [[NOD-like Receptors\|NOD-like Receptors]] | A family of ~20 cytosolic innate immune receptors built on a nucleotide-binding NACHT domain, encompassing signal-transducing NOD1/NOD2 and inflammasome-forming NLRs such as NLRP1, NLRP3, and the NAIP-NLRC4 inflammasome. |
| [[Non-alcoholic Fatty Liver Disease\|Non-alcoholic Fatty Liver Disease]] | Most common liver disease, a spectrum of hepatic steatosis to steatohepatitis, fibrosis, and cirrhosis in the absence of significant alcohol consumption |
| [[Non-coding RNA\|Non-coding RNA]] | Non-coding RNA (ncRNA) refers to RNA molecules that are not translated into proteins. Once thought to be ''junk,'' ncRNAs are now recognized as powerful regulators of the Epigenome and are involv... |
| [[Non-Exercise Activity Thermogenesis\|Non-Exercise Activity Thermogenesis]] | The energy expended on all physical activity other than volitional sporting exercise — fidgeting, posture maintenance, standing, walking, and general ambulatory activity. NEAT is a highly variable component of daily energy expenditure implicated in resistance to fat gain. |
| [[Non-homologous End Joining\|Non-homologous End Joining]] | DNA double-strand break repair pathway that directly ligates broken ends without a homologous template; dominant repair route in G1 phase and in post-mitotic cells. |
| [[Nonalcoholic Steatohepatitis\|Nonalcoholic Steatohepatitis]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Nonalcoholic Steatohepatitis is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves... |
| [[Norepinephrine\|Norepinephrine]] | A Catecholamines that functions as both a hormone and a neurotransmitter. |
| [[Notch\|Notch]] | Notch signaling pathway that gates the early-to-late SASP switch and establishes paracrine senescence. |
| [[Notch Signaling\|Notch Signaling]] | Definition: Notch Signaling is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and ... |
| [[NPAT\|NPAT]] | Nuclear protein, ataxia-telangiectasia locus (NPAT), a transcription factor coordinating replication-dependent histone gene expression that declines with the histone-biosynthesis drop in senescence. |
| [[NPC1\|NPC1]] | Niemann-Pick C1, a lysosomal/cholesterol-trafficking protein that restrains cGAS-STING activation at the nuclear envelope. |
| [[NPC1L1\|NPC1L1]] | Niemann-Pick C1-like 1, the intestinal and hepatic cholesterol uptake transporter that is the molecular target of ezetimibe and a validated human genetic target for lowering LDL cholesterol. |
| [[NPM1\|NPM1]] | Nucleophosmin, a nucleolar chaperone identified as a shared ATM/p38 MAPK interactor in senescent cells; unlike HSPA8, its interaction with these kinases is not disrupted by apigenin. |
| [[NRF1\|NRF1]] | Nuclear respiratory factor 1 (NFE2L1), a bZIP transcription factor and homodimer that binds MCB elements in the promoters of nuclear-encoded mitochondrial genes, coordinating mitochondrial biogenesis, respiration and heme synthesis with the mitochondrial genome. |
| [[NRF2\|Nrf2]] | Transcription factor (NF-E2-related factor 2) that orchestrates the antioxidant and detoxification response by binding ARE/EpRE elements. |
| [[NSAIDs\|NSAIDs]] | NSAIDs (nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs) are a chemically diverse drug class that reduce pain, fever and inflammation by inhibiting cyclooxygenase and suppressing prostaglandin synthesis, carrying class risks of gastrointestinal bleeding, renal injury, blood pressure elevation and thrombotic cardiovascular events. |
| [[nsp3 Macrodomain\|nsp3 Macrodomain]] | The nsp3 macrodomain (Mac1, or Nsp3b in SARS-CoV-2) is a conserved ~130-residue domain in coronavirus nonstructural protein 3 that binds and hydrolyzes mono-ADP-ribose, reversing host PARP-mediated MARylation and thereby countering antiviral innate immunity. |
| [[NUCKS1\|NUCKS1]] | Nuclear casein kinase and cyclin-dependent kinase substrate 1, an abundant intrinsically disordered nuclear phosphoprotein with HMG-box-like DNA/RNA binding that regulates chromatin organization, transcription, DNA repair, and metabolism; linked to cellular senescence through SIRT7-dependent deacetylation. |
| [[Nuclear Envelope\|Nuclear Envelope]] | The double-membrane boundary of the nucleus that anchors cGAS via BAF1 and hosts STING and NPC1 regulation of cGAS activity. |
| [[Nuclear Pore Complex\|Nuclear Pore Complex]] | Overview: Nuclear Pore Complex is a specialized cellular or anatomical structure with defined functions in tissue organization and physiology. |
| [[Nuclear Receptor\|Nuclear Receptor]] | Members of a superfamily of ligand-activated transcription factors that bind hormone, retinoid, vitamin D, fatty acid or xenobiotic ligands and regulate target gene transcription through a zinc-finger DNA-binding domain, a ligand-binding domain and two activation functions. |
| [[Nucleoside Reverse Transcriptase Inhibitor\|Nucleoside Reverse Transcriptase Inhibitor]] | NRTI, a class of antiretroviral drugs that inhibit retroviral reverse transcriptase and are repurposed to suppress LINE-1/HERV-driven inflammaging and SASP in senescence. |
| [[Nucleosome\|Nucleosome]] | The Nucleosome is the fundamental structural unit of Chromatin. It consists of a segment of DNA (approximately 147 base pairs) wound around a core of eight histone proteins (two each of H2A, H2... |
| [[Nucleotide Excision Repair\|Nucleotide Excision Repair]] | Definition: Nucleotide Excision Repair is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pa... |
| [[Nucleus\|Nucleus]] | The membrane-bound organelle housing chromatin, where cGAS exerts noncanonical functions in genome stability, RNA metabolism, and chromatin organization. |
| [[Nucleus Accumbens\|Nucleus Accumbens]] | The nucleus accumbens (NAc) is a ventral striatal brain region central to reward, motivation, reinforcement learning, and the actions of drugs of abuse. |
| [[Nucleus Pulposus\|Nucleus Pulposus]] | Gelatinous inner core of the intervertebral disc, composed of notochordal-derived cells embedded in a proteoglycan-rich matrix; its degeneration is central to intervertebral disc degeneration. |
| [[Nudix Hydrolases\|Nudix Hydrolases]] | Nudix phosphodiesterases cleave free ADP-ribose into AMP and ribose-5-phosphate, linking PAR turnover to AMPK signaling and NAD+ salvage cost in parthanatos. |
| [[NUMTs\|NUMTs]] | Nuclear insertions of mitochondrial DNA origin, also known as nuclear mitochondrial DNA transfer |
| [[Nutlin 3a\|Nutlin 3a]] | MDM2 antagonist that stabilizes p53; a senolytic/senescence-inducing and senomorphic agent. |
| [[O-GlcNAcylation\|O-GlcNAcylation]] | O-GlcNAcylation is the reversible post-translational modification of serine and threonine residues with O-linked β-N-acetylglucosamine, catalyzed by OGT and removed by OGA; a nutrient-sensitive regulator of signaling, transcription, and mitochondrial dynamics. |
| [[Obesity\|Obesity]] | Obesity is a complex chronic disease characterized by excessive adipose tissue accumulation, affecting over 603.7 million adults globally. Obesity is associated with chronic inflammation and in... |
| [[Olaparib\|Olaparib]] | First-in-class PARP inhibitor exploiting synthetic lethality in BRCA-deficient cancers. |
| [[Oligomycin\|Oligomycin]] | Oligomycin is a macrolide inhibitor of mitochondrial ATP synthase (Complex V) used in Seahorse assays to dissect the oxygen-consumption and glycolytic effects of methylene blue. |
| [[OMA1\|OMA1]] | OMA1 is a mitochondrial inner-membrane zinc metalloprotease activated by mitochondrial stress; it cleaves OPA1 and DELE1 to couple dynamics, mitophagy, and the integrated stress response. |
| [[OMA1-DELE1-HRI axis\|OMA1-DELE1-HRI axis]] | A mitochondrial stress-signaling axis where OMA1 cleaves DELE1 to activate HRI and trigger the integrated stress response. |
| [[Oncogene\|Oncogene]] | An oncogene is a Gene that has the potential to cause Cancer when mutated, overexpressed, or constitutively activated. Oncogenes arise from Proto-Oncogenes, which normally regulate Cell Proliferati... |
| [[Oncogene Activation\|Oncogene Activation]] | Oncogene activation refers to the processes by which a Proto-Oncogene is converted into an Oncogene, acquiring the ability to promote Cancer. Mechanisms include point mutations (e.g., RAS G12V, BRA... |
| [[One-Carbon Metabolism\|One-Carbon Metabolism]] | A network of interconnected folate- and methionine-cycle reactions that transfer one-carbon units (methyl, methylene, methenyl, formyl, formimino) between metabolites, supporting nucleotide biosynthesis, amino acid homeostasis, methylation, and redox balance. |
| [[OPA1\|OPA1]] | Optic Atrophy Protein 1 is an inner-mitochondrial-membrane dynamin-related GTPase that mediates inner-membrane fusion with cardiolipin; mutations cause dominant optic atrophy. |
| [[Opioids\|Opioids]] | Opioids are a class of analgesic drugs acting at opioid receptors; several (e.g., tramadol, meperidine) have serotonergic activity and pose serotonin-syndrome risk with MAO inhibitors such as methylene blue. |
| [[Opportunistic infection\|Opportunistic infection]] | An opportunistic infection is a disease caused by a normally harmless organism that becomes pathogenic because the host's defences are impaired; the classic setting is advanced HIV/AIDS, but iatrogenic immunosuppression, transplant, ageing, and biologicals are now comparably important causes. |
| [[Optineurin\|Optineurin]] | Optineurin, a cytoplasmic selective autophagy receptor involved in mitophagy and linked to glaucoma and ALS |
| [[OPTN\|OPTN]] | Autophagy receptor that functions as a primary mitophagy receptor in the PINK1/Parkin pathway and also mediates anterograde axonal mitochondrial transport. |
| [[Orexin\|Orexin]] | Hypothalamic neuropeptide (also called hypocretin) that promotes wakefulness, arousal, feeding, and reward; signals through two G-protein-coupled receptors, OX1R (HCRTR1) and OX2R (HCRTR2). |
| [[Ornithine\|Ornithine]] | Ornithine is a non-proteinogenic amino acid that serves as a urea cycle intermediate and as the precursor for polyamine biosynthesis, and it is the substrate of ornithine aminotransferase whose deficiency causes gyrate atrophy. |
| [[Ornithine Aminotransferase (OAT) Deficiency\|Ornithine Aminotransferase (OAT) Deficiency]] | A rare autosomal recessive disorder of the urea cycle and ornithine metabolism caused by mutations in the OAT gene, leading to gyrate atrophy of the choroid and retina with secondary creatine depletion. |
| [[ORP1L\|ORP1L]] | Oxysterol-binding protein-related protein 1L (ORP1L, gene OSBPL1) is a Rab7 effector and sterol-sensing lipid transfer protein that couples cholesterol levels in late endosomes and autophagosomes to the formation of ER membrane contact sites, thereby positioning autophagosomes for lysosomal fusion. |
| [[Osteoarthritis\|Osteoarthritis]] | Most common joint disease, a degenerative disorder of articular cartilage characterized by chondrocyte apoptosis, ECM degradation, and subchondral bone remodeling |
| [[Osteoporosis\|Osteoporosis]] | Systemic skeletal disease of low bone mass and bone fragility, arising from imbalance between bone resorption and formation |
| [[Outer Mitochondrial Membrane\|Outer Mitochondrial Membrane]] | The outer membrane of mitochondria, the site of mitofusin-mediated fusion and DRP1-driven fission. |
| [[OX2R\|OX2R]] | Orexin receptor type 2 (HCRTR2); a G-protein-coupled receptor for the hypothalamic orexin/hypocretin neuropeptides, regulating arousal, wakefulness, feeding, and energy balance; upregulated by SIRT1 in the hypothalamus and implicated in SIRT1-dependent lifespan extension. |
| [[Oxaloacetate\|Oxaloacetate]] | The four-carbon TCA-cycle intermediate converted to malate by malate dehydrogenase; central to the oxaloacetate-malate redox couple that sets cytosolic NAD+/NADH and influences senescence. |
| [[Oxidation\|Oxidation]] | Oxidation is a concept related to Adrenochrome and catecholamine metabolism. |
| [[Oxidative Phosphorylation\|Oxidative Phosphorylation]] | Definition: Oxidative Phosphorylation is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pat... |
| [[Oxidized LDL\|Oxidized LDL]] | Oxidized LDL (oxLDL) is low-density lipoprotein that has undergone oxidative modification, primarily by Reactive Oxygen Species and Myeloperoxidase. It is a key driver of Atherosclerosis and En... |
| [[P-glycoprotein\|P-glycoprotein]] | P-glycoprotein (ABCB1/MDR1) is a full-length ATP-binding cassette transporter that pumps a very broad range of hydrophobic drugs and xenobiotics out of cells using ATP hydrolysis; its activity underlies both multidrug resistance in cancer and xenobiotic protection at the gut, kidney, and blood-brain barrier. |
| [[P-gp\|P-gp]] | Overview: P-gp is a membrane transport protein that facilitates the movement of molecules across biological membranes. It mediates substrate translocation through conformational changes driven ... |
| [[p15INK4b\|p15INK4b]] | A cyclin-dependent kinase inhibitor encoded by CDKN2B that enforces G1 arrest by blocking CDK4/6 and maintaining Rb in its hypophosphorylated state. |
| [[p16\|p16]] | Cyclin-dependent kinase inhibitor 2A, a critical tumor suppressor and cell cycle regulator that enforces senescence via the p16/RB pathway |
| [[p21 CIP1\|p21 CIP1]] | p21CIP1 (encoded by CDKN1A) is a cyclin-dependent kinase inhibitor that mediates DNA damage-induced cell cycle arrest and supports an early immunosurveillance SASP profile. |
| [[p22-FLIP\|p22-FLIP]] | The N-terminal p22 fragment of c-FLIP generated by caspase-8 cleavage at Asp198/Asp196 — a FADD-independent DED1-DED2 module that binds NEMO/IKKgamma and potently activates NF-kB. |
| [[p22phox\|p22phox]] | Membrane subunit of NADPH oxidases essential for ROS production; mutated in chronic granulomatous disease. |
| [[p27\|p27]] | p27Kip1 (CDKN1B) is a 198-residue Cip/Kip-family cyclin-dependent kinase inhibitor that restrains cyclin E-CDK2 and cyclin D-CDK4/6 activity at the G1-S transition. Its abundance is set less by transcription than by proteolysis and subcellular localisation, and low nuclear p27 is one of the most widely reproduced prognostic markers in solid tumours. |
| [[P2X7 Receptor\|P2X7 Receptor]] | P2X7 (P2RX7) is an ATP-gated, non-selective cation channel that functions as a death receptor in many cell types, driving potassium efflux, NLRP3 inflammasome assembly and cytokine release; it is an emerging drug target in neuroinflammation and cancer. |
| [[P300\|P300]] | P300 (EP300) is a transcriptional coactivator and histone acetyltransferase (HAT) that acetylates histone H3 (K18, K27) and non-histone proteins including TFEB, p53, NRF2, and Atg4B. |
| [[p38 MAPK\|p38 MAPK]] | p38 mitogen-activated protein kinase (p38 MAPK) is a signaling molecule that regulates TFEB by phosphorylating it at S211 (inhibitory) or S401 (activating), depending on the cellular context. |
| [[p43-FLIP\|p43-FLIP]] | The N-terminal p43 cleavage fragment of c-FLIP long form generated by caspase-8 at Asp376 — a DISC-retained heterodimer partner that amplifies procaspase-8 recruitment and NF-kB signaling. |
| [[p47phox\|p47phox]] | Cytosolic organizer subunit activating NOX2 NADPH oxidase; mutated in chronic granulomatous disease. |
| [[p50\|p50]] | p50 is the 361-residue mature product of the NFKB1 gene, generated by constitutive ubiquitin-proteasome processing of the 968-residue precursor p105. It supplies the Rel homology DNA-binding and dimerisation domain of canonical NF-kB complexes and has no transactivation domain of its own, so it acts as a cofactor of Rel-family partners and as a repressor when homodimerised. |
| [[p53\|p53]] | p53 (also known as tumor protein P53 or TP53) is a crucial tumor suppressor protein that regulates the cell cycle and prevents cancer formation. It is often referred to as the ''guardian of the ... |
| [[p62\|p62]] | p62/SQSTM1 (sequestosome-1) is a multidomain scaffold protein that delivers ubiquitinated cargo to autophagosomes via its LC3-interacting region and serves as a key autophagy substrate and signaling hub. |
| [[p65\|p65]] | p65 (RELA) is the 551-residue Rel-family subunit of NF-kB that supplies the transactivation domains. It is the dominant transcription factor of canonical NF-kB signalling, held inactive in the cytoplasm by I-kB, and released by IKK-mediated phosphorylation and proteasomal destruction of the inhibitor. Activity is gated principally by reversible lysine acetylation. |
| [[P66shc\|P66shc]] | 66 kDa Src-homology-2-domain-containing isoform of the ShcA adaptor; a mitochondrial ROS generator and longevity regulator negatively controlled by SIRT1. |
| [[PAD4\|PAD4]] | Peptidylarginine deiminase 4 — the calcium-dependent histone-citrullinating enzyme that drives chromatin decondensation in NETosis and marks cit-H3-positive inflammatory death. |
| [[PAI-1\|PAI-1]] | Plasminogen activator inhibitor-1 (SERPINE1), a key pro-fibrotic SASP factor and leading plasma senescence biomarker. |
| [[PAK2\|PAK2]] | p21-activated kinase 2; a serine/threonine kinase that phosphorylates and inhibits Caspase-7, contributing to chemotherapy resistance in breast cancer. |
| [[PALB2\|PALB2]] | BRCA1/2 scaffold enabling homologous recombination; mutated in breast, pancreatic, and Fanconi anemia disease. |
| [[Palbociclib\|Palbociclib]] | Palbociclib (PD0332991) is a CDK4/CDK6 inhibitor that induces lysosomal biogenesis by inhibiting the nuclear export of TFEB. |
| [[Pancreatic Cancer\|Pancreatic Cancer]] | Pancreatic cancer, particularly pancreatic duct adenocarcinoma (PDA), involves dysregulated TFEB activity which supports tumor growth by controlling glutamine metabolism. |
| [[Pancreatitis\|Pancreatitis]] | Inflammation of the pancreas, ranging from acute necrotizing pancreatitis to chronic inflammation-associated fibrosis and EMT |
| [[PANoptosis\|PANoptosis]] | An innate-immune inflammatory cell-death program that combines pyroptosis, apoptosis, and necroptosis in one complex (the PANoptosome) — typically assembled around ZBP1 or TAK1 sensing and unable to be compensated by blocking any single pathway. |
| [[PANoptosome\|PANoptosome]] | The PANoptosome is the multiprotein complex that co-assembles pyroptosis, apoptosis, and necroptosis — nucleated by sensors such as ZBP1, AIM2, or loss of TAK1 — producing PANoptosis that single-pathway inhibitors cannot block. |
| [[PANX1\|PANX1]] | Pannexin-1 channel that releases cGAMP to the extracellular space for contact-independent intercellular cGAS-STING propagation. |
| [[PAR\|PAR]] | PAR is the toxic poly(ADP-ribose) polymer death signal linking PARP1 hyperactivation to mitochondrial AIF release. |
| [[Paraquat\|Paraquat]] | Paraquat is a chemical compound and toxic herbicide that induces cellular oxidative stress and reactive oxygen species (ROS) production. |
| [[Parasympathetic Nervous System\|Parasympathetic Nervous System]] | The "rest, digest, and repair" branch of the autonomic nervous system, governed by the vagus nerve, that raises HRV and drives recovery from hormetic stress. |
| [[PARG\|PARG]] | PARG is the primary poly(ADP-ribose) glycohydrolase erasing PAR; its dosage bidirectionally sets parthanatos and stroke outcome. |
| [[PARG53\|PARG53]] | PARG53 is a catalytically active ~53-kDa splice isoform of PARG (exon 1–8); its existence revises the PARG55/60 annotation and helps explain discrepancies between PARG knockout and PARG inhibitor experiments in parthanatos. |
| [[Parkin\|Parkin]] | An E3 ubiquitin ligase that plays a critical role in Mitophagy. It is recruited to damaged mitochondria by PINK1 and labels them for degradation by the lysosome. |
| [[Parkinson's Disease\|Parkinson's Disease]] | A progressive neurodegenerative disorder defined by loss of dopaminergic neurons in the substantia nigra, causing resting tremor, rigidity, bradykinesia, and postural instability. Linked to cellular senescence, inflammaging, and a distinguishable circulating SASP signature. |
| [[PARL\|PARL]] | Presenilin-associated rhomboid-like protein, a mitochondrial inner membrane protease that cleaves PINK1 for degradation in healthy mitochondria |
| [[Paromomycin\|Paromomycin]] | Paromomycin is an aminoglycoside antibiotic with poor systemic absorption used to treat intestinal protozoal infections and, systemically, visceral leishmaniasis; it binds the 30S ribosomal subunit, suppresses post-translational glycosylation in Leishmania, and carries class risks of ototoxicity and nephrotoxicity. |
| [[Pattern Recognition Receptors\|Pattern Recognition Receptors]] | Germline-encoded innate immune sensors that detect pathogen-derived and endogenous nucleic acids and danger signals, initiating inflammatory responses. |
| [[Pazopanib\|Pazopanib]] | Multi-targeted receptor tyrosine kinase inhibitor (VEGFR/PDGFR) with antiangiogenic activity. |
| [[PCNA\|PCNA]] | Proliferating cell nuclear antigen is a homotrimeric ring-shaped sliding clamp loaded onto DNA by RFC that gives replicative polymerases their processivity and acts as a mobile docking platform whose ubiquitin and SUMO modifications switch the cell between translesion synthesis and template switching. |
| [[PCSK9\|PCSK9]] | Proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9 (PCSK9) is a secreted protease that drives LDL receptor degradation, raising plasma LDL; its dysregulation by SIRT6 is linked to atherosclerosis. |
| [[PD-1\|PD-1]] | Programmed cell death protein 1 (PD-1; CD279), an inhibitory immune-checkpoint receptor expressed on T cells that dampens effector responses upon binding PD-L1/PD-L2. |
| [[PD-L1\|PD-L1]] | Programmed death-ligand 1 (CD274/B7-H1); a transmembrane protein that inhibits T-cell function by binding PD-1, serving as a key immune checkpoint in cancer and a target modulated by SASP signaling in the senescent tumor microenvironment. |
| [[PDA\|PDA]] | Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma, the most common type of pancreatic cancer. |
| [[PDE5 Inhibitors\|PDE5 Inhibitors]] | A class of drugs that inhibit phosphodiesterase type 5, used for erectile dysfunction, pulmonary arterial hypertension, and benign prostatic hyperplasia; noted for multi-indication anti-aging potential. |
| [[PDGFAA\|PDGFAA]] | Platelet-derived growth factor AA is a SASP growth factor that induces myofibroblast differentiation and is required for optimal wound healing. |
| [[PDGFR\|PDGFR]] | Platelet-derived growth factor receptor, a family of type III receptor tyrosine kinases comprising PDGFRA and PDGFRB. Dimeric PDGF ligands bind the extracellular Ig-like domain, triggering trans-autophosphorylation on the intracellular kinase domain and recruitment of PI3K, SHP2 and PLCγ adaptors. |
| [[PDK1\|PDK1]] | 3-Phosphoinositide-dependent protein kinase 1 (gene PDPK1), a PH-domain-containing serine/threonine kinase of the AGC/CAMK family that phosphorylates Akt at Thr308 and acts as the master upstream activator of AGC-family kinases. Distinct from pyruvate dehydrogenase kinase 1 (PDHK1), which shares the abbreviation. |
| [[PECAM1\|PECAM1]] | Platelet endothelial cell adhesion molecule 1 (CD31), a transmembrane glycoprotein expressed on endothelial cells and immune cells |
| [[Pembrolizumab\|Pembrolizumab]] | Anti-PD-1 immune checkpoint inhibitor monoclonal antibody used in multiple cancers. |
| [[Pentamidine\|Pentamidine]] | Pentamidine is a diamidine antiprotozoal used by injection for Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia and leishmaniasis; it disrupts the parasite's mitochondrial membrane potential and DNA topology, and its substantial toxicity — including often-irreversible pancreatic beta-cell injury — confines it to second-line or prophylaxis use. |
| [[Pentose Phosphate Pathway\|Pentose Phosphate Pathway]] | The pentose phosphate pathway generates NADPH and ribose-5-phosphate; its oxidative branch fuels glutathione reduction, defending erythrocytes from oxidants. |
| [[Pericytes\|Pericytes]] | Perivascular cells of the neurovascular unit whose cGAS activation destabilizes the blood-brain barrier in tauopathy. |
| [[Periodontitis\|Periodontitis]] | Chronic inflammatory disease of the tooth-supporting tissues (gingiva, periodontal ligament, alveolar bone) driven by dysbiotic biofilm and host immune responses, leading to alveolar bone resorption and tooth loss. |
| [[PERK\|PERK]] | Protein kinase RNA-activated-like ER kinase (encoded by the EIF2AK3 gene). PERK is a key sensor of the integrated stress response that phosphorylates EIF2A, leading to a global reduction in protein... |
| [[Permethrin\|Permethrin]] | Permethrin is a synthetic pyrethroid whose insecticidal action is delay of voltage-gated sodium channel inactivation; as a 5% cream it is first-line for scabies and as a 1% liquid or shampoo for head lice, with rising kdr resistance in some regions. |
| [[Peroxiredoxin\|Peroxiredoxin]] | Peroxiredoxins (Prx) are a family of thioredoxin-dependent antioxidant peroxidases that reduce Hydrogen Peroxide, peroxynitrite, and organic hydroperoxides, guarding redox homeostasis. |
| [[Peroxiredoxin 3\|Peroxiredoxin 3]] | Mitochondrial 2-Cys peroxiredoxin that reduces hydrogen peroxide and peroxynitrite using thioredoxin-2 as electron donor. |
| [[Peroxisomes\|Peroxisomes]] | Peroxisomes are single-membrane-bound organelles present in virtually all eukaryotic cells that perform essential functions in lipid metabolism, reactive oxygen species (ROS) metabolism, and si... |
| [[PEX Genes\|PEX Genes]] | PEX genes encode the peroxins, a family of proteins required for Peroxisome biogenesis, assembly, and function. There are at least 14 known PEX genes in humans (PEX1–PEX14), each encoding a per... |
| [[PEX10\|PEX10]] | Peroxisomal RING-finger ubiquitin ligase partnering PEX2/PEX12 in receptor recycling. |
| [[PEX12\|PEX12]] | Peroxisomal RING-finger ubiquitin ligase completing the PEX2/PEX10 importomer complex. |
| [[PEX13\|PEX13]] | PEX13 encodes an integral peroxisomal membrane protein that forms part of the docking complex for the PTS1 and PTS2 receptors (PEX5 and PEX7), essential for peroxisomal matrix protein import. |
| [[PEX14\|PEX14]] | PEX14 encodes an integral peroxisomal membrane protein that serves as the primary docking site for the PTS1 receptor (PEX5) and a critical component of the peroxisomal protein import machinery. |
| [[PEX19\|PEX19]] | PEX19 encodes a predominantly cytosolic chaperone and import receptor for peroxisomal membrane proteins (PMPs), essential for the insertion of newly synthesized PMPs into the Peroxisome membrane during biogenesis and growth. |
| [[PEX2\|PEX2]] | Peroxisomal RING-finger ubiquitin ligase of the importomer; mutated in Zellweger spectrum disorders. |
| [[PEX5\|PEX5]] | PEX5 encodes the PTS1 receptor, a cytosolic shuttling receptor that recognizes and transports peroxisomal matrix proteins bearing the C-terminal peroxisomal targeting signal 1 (PTS1) to the peroxisomal membrane docking complex. |
| [[PEX6\|PEX6]] | PEX6 is a peroxisomal AAA+ ATPase that, as a heterohexamer with PEX1, extracts ubiquitinated PEX5 from the peroxisomal membrane to recycle the import receptor and prevent pexophagy; PEX6 mutations cause Zellweger-spectrum peroxisome biogenesis disorders. |
| [[PEX7\|PEX7]] | PEX7 encodes the PTS2 receptor, a cytosolic shuttling receptor that recognizes and imports peroxisomal matrix proteins bearing the N-terminal peroxisomal targeting signal 2 (PTS2) into the Peroxisome. |
| [[Pexophagy\|Pexophagy]] | Pexophagy is the selective autophagic degradation of peroxisomes, maintaining peroxisome quality, number, and redox homeostasis. |
| [[PGAM5\|PGAM5]] | Phosphoglycerate mutase family member 5 (PGAM5) — a mitochondrial serine/threonine phosphatase at the crossroads of mitophagy and necroptosis; it stabilizes PINK1 for mitophagy and anchors the necrosome to activate Drp1-driven fission and cell rupture. |
| [[PGC-1α\|PGC-1α]] | PGC-1α (PPARGC1A) is a transcriptional coactivator that serves as the master regulator of mitochondrial biogenesis, oxidative phosphorylation, and antioxidant defense. It links external stimuli to mitochondrial and metabolic gene programs. |
| [[Phagophore Assembly Site\|Phagophore Assembly Site]] | The dedicated subcellular locus at which the autophagy machinery nucleates and elongates the phagophore, the precursor membrane of the autophagosome. |
| [[Phagosome\|Phagosome]] | A phagosome is the membrane-bound compartment a phagocyte forms around an ingested particle; its maturation through sequential fusion with early, intermediate, and late endosomes and lysosomes — driven by Rab5 to Rab7 GTPase switching — delivers the cargo to degradative proteases and acid. |
| [[PHB2\|PHB2]] | Inner mitochondrial membrane protein that functions as a mitophagy receptor when exposed upon OMM damage, binding LC3 to initiate autophagy. |
| [[Phenelzine\|Phenelzine]] | Phenelzine is a non-selective, irreversible monoamine oxidase inhibitor (MAOI) antidepressant; its MAO-blocking mechanism parallels the low-dose MAO-A inhibition of methylene blue. |
| [[PhenoAge\|PhenoAge]] | A second-generation epigenetic clock trained on a composite of clinical blood biomarkers to estimate biological age and all-cause mortality risk. |
| [[Phenothiazine\|Phenothiazine]] | Phenothiazine is the sulfur- and nitrogen-containing tricyclic heterocyclic scaffold that launched modern psychopharmacology and underlies methylene blue's neuroprotective, redox-cycling activity. |
| [[Phenotype\|Phenotype]] | A phenotype is the set of observable characteristics or traits of an organism—morphological, developmental, biochemical, physiological, and behavioral—shaped by genotype and environment. |
| [[Pheochromocytoma\|Pheochromocytoma]] | A rare catecholamine-secreting tumor of the adrenal medulla that causes episodic hypertension, palpitations, and elevated oxidative stress. |
| [[Phosphatidylethanolamine\|Phosphatidylethanolamine]] | Phosphatidylethanolamine is a major membrane phospholipid whose polyunsaturated (arachidonate/adrenate) species are the key peroxidation substrates that execute ferroptosis. |
| [[Phosphatidylserine\|Phosphatidylserine]] | Phosphatidylserine is an anionic aminophospholipid confined to the inner leaflet of the plasma membrane in healthy cells; its exposure on the outer leaflet is a universal "eat-me" signal that drives efferocytosis, and it is also the required negative charge for membrane curvature and for Ca2+-dependent clotting complex assembly. |
| [[Phospholipase A2\|Phospholipase A2]] | A family of lipolytic enzymes that hydrolyze phospholipids to release arachidonic acid; the iPLA2 activity of PRDX6 is the apigenin-inhibited domain underlying SASP suppression. |
| [[Phospholipase C\|Phospholipase C]] | Phospholipase C is the family of inositol lipid hydrolases (PLCβ, γ, δ, ε, ζ, η) that cleave phosphatidylinositol 4,5-bisphosphate into IP3 and DAG, converting receptor activation into calcium release and protein kinase C activation; PLCζ is the sperm factor that triggers egg activation. |
