| [[Phospholipid\|Phospholipid]] | Glycerophospholipids forming cellular membranes; their polyunsaturated fatty acid (PUFA) content at the sn-2 position determines susceptibility to lipid peroxidation and ferroptosis. |
| [[Photobiomodulation\|Photobiomodulation]] | Red/near-infrared light therapy that stimulates mitochondrial cytochrome c oxidase (Complex IV) to enhance ATP production and recovery. |
| [[Physical Activity Level\|Physical Activity Level]] | The ratio of total daily energy expenditure to resting metabolic rate (DEE:RMR). Western adults average ~1.66, whereas free-living wild mammals average ~2.6, expressing the extent to which energy above rest is expended on activity. |
| [[PI3K\|PI3K]] | Phosphoinositide 3-kinase (PI3K) is a family of enzymes involved in cellular functions such as cell growth, proliferation, differentiation, motility, survival, and intracellular trafficking. |
| [[Piceatannol\|Piceatannol]] | Piceatannol (3,3',4,5'-tetrahydroxystilbene) is a natural stilbenoid polyphenol, structurally related to resveratrol, that activates multiple sirtuin isoforms. |
| [[Piezo1\|Piezo1]] | Mechanosensitive nonselective cation channel transducing mechanical stress into Ca2+ influx in erythrocytes, linking shear and surface-accumulating nanomaterials to eryptosis. |
| [[Pin1\|Pin1]] | Peptidyl-prolyl cis/trans isomerase that recognizes phosphorylated Ser/Thr-Pro motifs and catalyzes conformational changes; acts as a molecular switch determining pro-survival vs. pro-death outcomes for BAX. |
| [[PINK1\|PINK1]] | Mitochondrial serine/threonine kinase that recruits Parkin to damaged mitochondria to initiate mitophagy. |
| [[Pinosylvin\|Pinosylvin]] | Pinosylvin is a 3,5-dihydroxystilbene, the resveratrol analogue produced by pine trees as a antifungal phytoalexin; it shows modestly greater antifungal and COX activity than resveratrol but very poor oral bioavailability, and it remains a research compound rather than a therapeutic. |
| [[Pioneer Factor\|Pioneer Factor]] | Transcription factor capable of binding and opening closed/repressive chromatin, exemplified in senescence by AP-1, which initiates enhancer opening to drive SASP gene activation. |
| [[PIP5KI\|PIP5KI]] | PIP5KI refers to the type I phosphatidylinositol 4-phosphate 5-kinases (PIP5K1A, PIP5K1B, PIP5K1C) that phosphorylate PtdIns4P to PtdIns(4,5)P2; a distinct type II family (PIP4K2A-C, formerly PIP5K2) makes PI5P instead, and the two are being pursued as cancer targets. |
| [[Piperine\|Piperine]] | Piperine is the pungent alkaloid of black pepper and a bioavailability enhancer that inhibits CYP3A4, P-glycoprotein, and glucuronidation. |
| [[Pirfenidone\|Pirfenidone]] | Pirfenidone is an antifibrotic and anti-inflammatory drug tested as a senotherapeutic candidate in a flavonoid screen, with no significant senolytic activity at 5 μM. |
| [[PKC\|PKC]] | Protein kinase C (PKC) is a family of serine/threonine kinases classified into conventional (α, βI, βII, γ), novel (δ, ε, η, θ), and atypical (ζ, ι/λ) isoforms. PKC signaling regulates Autophag... |
| [[PLA\|PLA]] | Poly(lactic acid) (PLA) is a biodegradable, biocompatible thermoplastic polyester used in polymeric nanoparticles and scaffolds for controlled drug delivery and tissue engineering. |
| [[Plasma Membrane\|Plasma Membrane]] | Overview: Plasma Membrane is a specialized cellular or anatomical structure with defined functions in tissue organization and physiology. |
| [[Plasmacytoid Dendritic Cells\|Plasmacytoid Dendritic Cells]] | A dendritic cell subset that internalizes TFAM-bound mtDNA via RAGE and recognizes it through TLR9, producing inflammatory factors; a route by which mitochondrial DAMPs from senescence trigger innate immunity. |
| [[Plasmin\|Plasmin]] | A broad-spectrum serine protease generated from plasminogen by plasminogen activators (tPA and uPA), central to fibrinolysis, ECM remodelling, and pro-MMP activation. |
| [[Plasminogen\|Plasminogen]] | Inactive zymogen precursor of Plasmin, synthesised in the liver and circulating in plasma; converted to active plasmin by tPA and uPA to initiate fibrinolysis. |
| [[Platelet\|Platelet]] | Anucleate cytoplasmic fragments shed from megakaryocytes that execute primary haemostasis by adhering to exposed subendothelium, degranulating, and aggregating into the primary platelet plug. |
| [[Platelet aggregation\|Platelet aggregation]] | The process by which activated platelets adhere to one another to form a hemostatic plug or pathological thrombus. |
| [[Platelet-Derived Growth Factor\|Platelet-Derived Growth Factor]] | Platelet-derived growth factor (PDGF) is a dimeric growth factor that activates vascular smooth muscle cells; in mitochondria it lowers MFN2 and drives fission. |
| [[Platelets\|Platelets]] | Anucleate cytoplasmic fragments derived from megakaryocytes that mediate hemostasis and thrombosis. |
| [[PLEKHM1\|PLEKHM1]] | PLEKHM1 (pleckstrin homology domain-containing family member 1) is a multi-domain autophagosome/endolysosome tethering effector that binds Rab7, the HOPS tethering complex, and LC3/GABARAP via an LIR motif to drive autophagosome-lysosome and endosome-lysosome fusion; biallelic loss causes an osteolysis syndrome. |
| [[PLGA\|PLGA]] | Poly(lactic-co-glycolic acid) (PLGA) is a biodegradable copolymer used in nanoparticle drug delivery to control release and improve bioavailability of compounds like fisetin. |
| [[PML\|PML]] | Promyelocytic leukemia tumor suppressor protein (TRIM19) that nucleates PML nuclear bodies, the scaffolds to which HIRA relocalizes to drive SAHF formation in senescence and through which SIRT1 regulates p53 and cellular aging. |
| [[Pneumonitis\|Pneumonitis]] | Inflammation of lung parenchyma; a rare but serious noninfectious adverse effect of mTOR inhibitor therapy that mandates drug discontinuation. |
| [[Podophyllotoxin\|Podophyllotoxin]] | Lignan from Podophyllum peltatum that inhibits tubulin polymerization; precursor to etoposide. |
| [[Polymerase chain reaction\|Polymerase chain reaction]] | A DNA amplification method that uses a thermostable DNA polymerase and thermal cycling through denaturation, annealing and extension to make millions of copies of a target sequence from a starting amount as small as a single molecule. |
| [[Polyphenols\|Polyphenols]] | Polyphenols are a category of phytochemicals that are naturally found in plants. |
| [[Pompe Disease\|Pompe Disease]] | A genetic disorder caused by the buildup of glycogen in the body's cells, also known as Glycogen Storage Disease Type II. |
| [[PON1\|PON1]] | Paraoxonase 1 (PON1); calcium-dependent HDL-associated esterase that hydrolyzes oxidized lipids and organophosphates, protecting lipoproteins from oxidative damage and contributing to HDL's anti-atherogenic function. |
| [[Poricoic acid A\|Poricoic acid A]] | Lanostane triterpenoid from Poria cocos that upregulates SIRT3 and promotes beta-catenin K49 deacetylation, attenuating renal fibroblast activation and interstitial fibrosis. |
| [[Post-translational Modification\|Post-translational Modification]] | Covalent modifications of proteins after ribosomal synthesis that rapidly alter protein function, localization, stability, and interactions, expanding the functional proteome far beyond the 20 canonical amino acids. |
| [[Postherpetic Neuralgia\|Postherpetic Neuralgia]] | Chronic neuropathic pain that persists after a herpes zoster rash resolves; the most common complication of shingles. |
| [[Potassium\|Potassium]] | The dominant intracellular cation in mammals, present at roughly 140 mM inside cells against about 4 mM in plasma, and the main determinant of the resting membrane potential; a Western diet supplies roughly 70 mmol per day against a requirement of 2-3 g. |
| [[PP2A\|PP2A]] | Protein phosphatase 2A (PP2A) is a serine/threonine phosphatase that dephosphorylates TFEB at multiple sites (S109, S114, S122) to promote its nuclear translocation. |
| [[PPARα\|PPARα]] | PPARα (Peroxisome Proliferator-Activated Receptor Alpha) is a nuclear receptor transcription factor that regulates Lipid Metabolism, Fatty Acid Oxidation, Ketogenesis, and Inflammation. It is activ... |
| [[PPARγ\|PPARγ]] | Peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor gamma (PPARγ) is a nuclear receptor that regulates fatty acid storage and glucose metabolism. It is a key target for treating Diabetes Mellitus and plays ... |
| [[PQBP1\|PQBP1]] | Polyglutamine binding protein 1, an intranuclear RNA-binding protein that adapts monomeric tau to cGAS to activate cGAS-STING independently of DNA. |
| [[PQQ\|PQQ]] | Pyrroloquinoline quinone, a redox cofactor and mitochondrial-biogenesis promoter that acts synergistically with CoQ10/ubiquinol. |
| [[PRDX6\|PRDX6]] | Peroxiredoxin 6 (PRDX6), a 1-Cys peroxiredoxin with peroxidase, iPLA2, and LPCAT activities; the direct molecular target of apigenin's senomorphic suppression of the SASP in senescent cells. |
| [[Pregnane X Receptor\|Pregnane X Receptor]] | PXR (NR1I2) is a nuclear xenobiotic receptor activated by gut microbial indole-3-propionic acid; regulates detoxification, barrier integrity, vasodilation, and anti-inflammatory gene programs alongside the Aryl Hydrocarbon Receptor. |
| [[Preiss-Handler Pathway\|Preiss-Handler Pathway]] | The de novo biosynthetic route to NAD+ from tryptophan/niacin via quinolinate, named for the Preiss-Handler reaction. |
| [[PREMOTE\|PREMOTE]] | PREMOTE is a multi-center randomized trial showing berberine lowers HbA1c with gut-microbiome shifts, directly linking metabolic benefit to microbiome mechanisms. |
| [[Pro-inflammatory Cytokines\|Pro-inflammatory Cytokines]] | The class of low-molecular-weight secreted proteins whose canonical members IL-1, IL-6 and TNF-alpha drive and amplify innate inflammation, acting through NF-kappaB, JAK-STAT and inflammasome pathways. This is a functional category, not a single protein, and includes both IL-1 family members and interferons. |
| [[Proanthocyanidins\|Proanthocyanidins]] | Proanthocyanidins (oligomeric proanthocyanidins, OPCs) are polymeric/oligomeric flavonoid polyphenols (condensed tannins) built from flavan-3-ol units, with potent antioxidant, vascular, and SIRT1-modulatory anti-aging activity. |
| [[Procyanidin C1\|Procyanidin C1]] | A flavonoid (oligomeric proanthocyanidin) with senotherapeutic (senolytic/senomorphic) activity that extends healthspan and lifespan in mice. |
| [[Progeria\|Progeria]] | Progeria (Hutchinson-Gilford progeria syndrome, HGPS) is a rare genetic disorder characterized by accelerated aging, used in mouse models to study senolytic interventions. |
| [[Progerin\|Progerin]] | Progerin is a truncated, farnesylated version of the Lamin A protein. It is the primary molecular driver of **Hutchinson-Gilford Progeria Syndrome** (HGPS). |
| [[Proline\|Proline]] | Non-essential amino acid that serves as the major nitrogen donor for polyamine synthesis. Proline levels decline with age; supplementation extends lifespan of yeast and C. elegans, and proline may promote longevity through ROS scavenging and effects on senescence. |
| [[Promethazine\|Promethazine]] | Promethazine is a 10-nitrogen-substituted phenothiazine antihistamine/antiemetic; like chlorpromazine it lacks methylene blue-like mitochondrial electron-transfer activity and shows weak neuroprotection. |
| [[Propidium Iodide\|Propidium Iodide]] | A membrane-impermeant DNA dye (PI) that marks cells with ruptured plasma membranes — the workhorse viability counterstain for lytic vs non-lytic death. |
| [[Propionate\|Propionate]] | Propionate is a three-carbon short-chain fatty acid produced by gut microbiota fermentation of fiber; it fuels hepatic gluconeogenesis and modulates appetite, immunity, and inflammation. |
| [[Prostacyclin\|Prostacyclin]] | Prostacyclin (PGI2) is the prostaglandin produced by prostacyclin synthase in endothelium; a potent vasodilator and inhibitor of platelet aggregation that balances thromboxane A2. |
| [[Prostaglandin G₂\|Prostaglandin G2]] | Prostaglandin G2 (PGG2) is the prostaglandin endoperoxide formed by cyclooxygenase dioxygenation of arachidonic acid; the first committed intermediate of the prostanoid cascade and a potent lipid hydroperoxide. |
| [[Prostaglandin H₂\|Prostaglandin H2]] | Prostaglandin H2 (PGH2) is the prostaglandin endoperoxide produced by reduction of PGG2; the common precursor of all prostanoids, including prostaglandins, thromboxane A2, and prostacyclin. |
| [[Prostaglandins\|Prostaglandins]] | Prostaglandins are lipid-derived autacoids produced from arachidonic acid via cyclooxygenase and terminal synthases; local mediators of inflammation, pain, fever, vascular tone, and platelet function. |
| [[PROTAC\|PROTAC]] | Proteolysis-targeting chimera technology proposed to degrade cGAS or STING for therapeutic pathway suppression. |
| [[Protease\|Protease]] | A protease (peptidase) is any enzyme that catalyses hydrolysis of peptide bonds in proteins; the class spans serine, cysteine, aspartic, metallo and threonine catalytic types and governs protein turnover, digestion, clotting, complement and regulated cell death. |
| [[Protein C\|Protein C]] | A vitamin K-dependent plasma serine protease zymogen that acts as the pivot of the natural anticoagulant system, activated on endothelium by the thrombin-thrombomodulin complex to inactivate factors Va and VIIIa and shut down thrombin generation. |
| [[Protein Kinase A\|Protein Kinase A]] | Kinase that phosphorylates DRP1 at Ser637 to inhibit mitochondrial fission. |
| [[Protein Kinase D\|Protein Kinase D]] | PKD, a major sensor of oxidative stress that interacts with NF-κB to modulate SASP gene expression. |
| [[Protein Kinase R\|Protein Kinase R]] | dsRNA-activated kinase that can engage the RIPK1 necrosome and phosphorylate eIF2alpha in necroptotic contexts. |
| [[Protein Restriction\|Protein Restriction]] | Dietary regimen that lowers total protein intake while meeting nutritional needs, promoting metabolic health, reduced senescence, and extended lifespan across species. Its benefits are mediated in part by FGF21 and by restriction of specific essential amino acids. |
| [[Protein Synthesis\|Protein Synthesis]] | Ribosomal translation of mRNA into protein; highly ATP-consuming and coupled to growth signaling. |
| [[Protein Turnover\|Protein Turnover]] | The continuous synthesis and degradation of body proteins. Whole-body protein turnover correlates with resting metabolic rate and contributes to the residual variance in RMR not explained by lean mass. |
| [[Proteinopathy\|Proteinopathy]] | Diseases driven by misfolded and aggregated proteins, sharing cytosolic dsDNA generation as a unifying trigger of cGAS-STING activation. |
| [[Proteobacteria\|Proteobacteria]] | Proteobacteria is a major phylum of Gram-negative bacteria, including many pathogens; its increased abundance in the gut is frequently associated with dysbiosis, inflammation, and aging-related inflammaging. |
| [[Proteostasis\|Proteostasis]] | Protein homeostasis—the networked set of pathways that maintain protein folding, quality control, and turnover; declines with age and is restored by mTORC1 inhibition. |
| [[Proteotoxicity\|Proteotoxicity]] | Definition: Proteotoxicity is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and e... |
| [[Prothrombin\|Prothrombin]] | The vitamin K-dependent zymogen factor II that sits at the head of the common pathway, converted by the prothrombinase complex into thrombin through two specific cleavages at Arg320 and Arg271; its circulating half-life of about 60-72 hours is the basis of the PT/INR assay. |
| [[Proto-lysosomal Tubules\|Proto-lysosomal Tubules]] | Transient tubular membrane extensions that bud from endolysosomes to recycle limiting membrane and content during phagocytic lysosome reformation. |
| [[Protozoa\|Protozoa]] | A convenience grouping of heterotrophic unicellular eukaryotes, most of which move by cilia, flagella or pseudopodia; the medically important members are the apicomplexans (Plasmodium, Toxoplasma, Cryptosporidium) and the kinetoplastid flagellates (Leishmania, Trypanosoma). |
| [[Pruritus\|Pruritus]] | The unpleasant cutaneous sensation that provokes scratching, carried by unmyelinated C fibres and thinly myelinated A-delta fibres to the dorsal horn; unlike pain it is not a defence against tissue injury but a disease in its own right. |
| [[Psoriasis\|Psoriasis]] | Psoriasis is a chronic immune-mediated inflammatory skin disease driven by the IL-23/Th17 axis, causing hyperproliferative, scaly plaques. |
| [[PSTK\|PSTK]] | PSTK (O-phosphoseryl-tRNA kinase) phosphorylates Ser-tRNA[Ser]Sec to O-phosphoseryl-tRNA[Ser]Sec, an intermediate in the biosynthesis of selenocysteine on its tRNA. |
| [[Psychiatric Disorders\|Psychiatric Disorders]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Psychiatric Disorders is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves geneti... |
| [[PTBP1\|PTBP1]] | Polypyrimidine tract-binding protein 1 (PTB, hnRNP I), an RNA-binding protein that acts as a broadly acting repressor of alternative exon inclusion and regulates additional steps of RNA metabolism, including the splicing programmes that govern secretory trafficking gene expression. |
| [[PTEN\|PTEN]] | PTEN (Phosphatase and Tensin homolog) is a powerful tumor suppressor protein that is found in almost all tissues in the body. It acts as a ''brake' on cellular growth and division.' |
| [[Pterostilbene\|Pterostilbene]] | A natural dimethylated stilbenoid polyphenol from blueberries that activates sirtuins and AMPK, mimicking caloric restriction. |
| [[PTPN5\|PTPN5]] | Protein tyrosine phosphatase non-receptor type 5 (STEP); dephosphorylates and activates BAK, serving as a licensing signal for mitochondrial apoptosis. |
| [[Pulmonary Arterial Hypertension\|Pulmonary Arterial Hypertension]] | Progressive vascular disease characterized by elevated pulmonary artery pressure and right heart failure |
| [[Pulmonary Embolism\|Pulmonary Embolism]] | Obstruction of the pulmonary arterial tree by thrombus, most often from a deep vein thrombosis in the leg, producing ventilation-perfusion mismatch and acute right ventricular strain; a leading cause of preventable death and frequently presenting with shock or cardiac arrest. |
| [[Pulmonary Fibrosis\|Pulmonary Fibrosis]] | A chronic, progressive lung disease characterized by scarring (fibrosis) of lung tissue |
| [[Pulmonary Hypertension\|Pulmonary Hypertension]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Pulmonary Hypertension is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genet... |
| [[Pulse Oximetry\|Pulse Oximetry]] | Pulse oximetry is non-invasive estimation of oxygen saturation; methylene blue artifactually underestimates it. |
| [[Pulse Wave Velocity\|Pulse Wave Velocity]] | Pulse wave velocity (PWV) is the speed at which the pressure wave generated by left ventricular ejection travels along the arterial tree. It is the gold standard clinical measure of Arterial Stiffn... |
| [[Putrescine\|Putrescine]] | The simplest of the polyamines, a 1,4-diaminobutane produced by ornithine decarboxylase and used as the aminopropyl acceptor in spermidine synthesis; it is both the rate-limiting entry point into polyamine metabolism and a substrate for N-acetyltransferase and polyamine oxidase. |
| [[PVP\|PVP]] | Polyvinylpyrrolidone (PVP, povidone) is a water-soluble synthetic polymer used as an excipient and nanoparticle stabilizer to improve the dissolution and bioavailability of poorly soluble drugs such as fisetin. |
| [[Pycnogenol\|Pycnogenol]] | Pycnogenol is a standardized extract of French maritime pine (Pinus pinaster) bark, rich in A-type proanthocyanidins plus phenolic acids, used for vascular, antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and skin-aging applications. |
| [[PYCR1\|PYCR1]] | Pyrroline-5-carboxylate reductase 1, the final enzyme in proline biosynthesis, catalyzes the NAD(P)H-dependent conversion of P5C to proline and is upregulated in multiple cancers where it promotes proliferation and tumor aggressiveness. |
| [[Pyruvate\|Pyruvate]] | Three-carbon keto acid that is the end product of glycolysis and the primary mitochondrial fuel, entering mitochondria via the mitochondrial pyruvate carrier (MPC) to feed the TCA cycle. |
| [[Pyruvate Dehydrogenase\|Pyruvate Dehydrogenase]] | Mitochondrial enzyme complex that converts pyruvate to acetyl-CoA, the committed step feeding the TCA cycle. Its activity gates glucose-derived carbon entry into the mitochondrial citrate–acetyl-CoA axis. |
| [[Q-VD-OPh\|Q-VD-OPh]] | A broad-spectrum, non-toxic caspase inhibitor (quinolyl-valyl-O-methylaspartyl-fluorophenoxy) that rescues caspase-dependent apoptosis — female-selective in ischemia models. |
| [[QPRT\|QPRT]] | Quinolinate Phosphoribosyltransferase; enzyme converting quinolinic acid to nicotinic acid mononucleotide in de novo NAD+ biosynthesis via the kynurenine pathway |
| [[Quercetin\|Quercetin]] | Quercetin is a plant Flavonoids found in many fruits, vegetables, and grains. It is commonly used as an Anti-inflammatory and antioxidant supplement. |
| [[Quinolinic Acid\|Quinolinic Acid]] | Quinolinic acid (QA) is a downstream metabolite of the kynurenine pathway and an NMDA receptor agonist that is neurotoxic at elevated concentrations, also serving as a precursor for NAD+ synthesis. |
| [[Quinone\|Quinone]] | Quinones are a class of organic compounds derived from aromatic compounds (such as benzene or naphthalene) by conversion of an even number of -CH= groups into -C(=O)- groups, resulting in a ful... |
| [[Rab8\|Rab8]] | Small GTPase that interacts with the mitophagy receptor Optineurin and is required for retrograde transport of mitochondria-containing autophagosomes in neurons. |
| [[Rac GTPase\|Rac GTPase]] | The Rac GTPases (RAC1, RAC2, RAC3, RHOG) are a subfamily of Rho-family small GTPases of roughly 21-25 kDa. They act as binary GDP/GTP switches, cycle nucleotide state via GEFs, GAPs and GDIs, and drive lamellipodia, membrane ruffles and cell adhesion through WAVE, PAK and NADPH oxidase effectors. |
| [[Rac1\|Rac1]] | Rac1 (Ras-related C3 botulinum toxin substrate 1) is a 21 kDa Rho-family small GTPase and the ubiquitously expressed prototype of the Rac subfamily. It cycles GDP/GTP under control of GEFs, GAPs and RhoGDI, and via PAK, WAVE and NADPH oxidase controls lamellipodia, adhesion, gene expression and the respiratory burst. |
| [[RAD51\|RAD51]] | RAD51 is a recombinase enzyme essential for Homologous Recombination (HR) repair of Double-Strand Breaks and DNA Interstrand Crosslinks. It forms helical filaments on single-stranded DNA, catalyzin... |
| [[Radical-trapping Antioxidant\|Radical-trapping Antioxidant]] | A radical-trapping antioxidant (RTA) is a molecule that terminates peroxidation chain reactions by donating a hydrogen atom to a lipid peroxyl radical, yielding a stabilized radical; endogenous RTAs include ubiquinol (CoQ10), vitamin K hydroquinone, and vitamin E, and synthetic RTAs include ferrostatin-1 and liproxstatin-1. |
| [[Radiosensitizer\|Radiosensitizer]] | Agents that increase the cytotoxic effects of ionizing radiation, particularly in hypoxic tumor cells. |
| [[Radiotherapy\|Radiotherapy]] | The therapeutic use of ionizing radiation to damage tumour DNA; the mechanism is indirect, via radiolysis of water and the resulting radical chemistry that fixes DNA damage, and the tumour kill is governed by the linear-quadratic cell survival relationship and the oxygen enhancement ratio. |
| [[RAF\|RAF]] | RAF kinases (ARAF, BRAF, CRAF) are central to the RAS-RAF-MEK-ERK MAPK cascade; RAF/MEK-dependent oxidative cell death connects to ferroptosis upstream pathways. |
| [[RANK\|RANK]] | RANK (TNFRSF11A) is a 616-residue type I transmembrane TNF receptor family member and the sole signaling receptor for RANKL. It trimerizes upon ligand binding, recruits TRAF6 through its cytoplasmic tail, and drives osteoclast differentiation via NF-κB, MAPK, PI3K-Akt and NFATc1. |
| [[RANKL\|RANKL]] | RANKL (TNFSF11) is a 317-residue type II transmembrane TNF superfamily ligand that trimerizes and binds RANK to drive osteoclast differentiation, activation and survival. It exists as a membrane protein and as soluble isoforms shed by ADAM10/MMP14, and is the target of denosumab. |
| [[Rapalogs\|Rapalogs]] | Rapalogs are analogs and derivatives of rapamycin (sirolimus) that inhibit mTORC1 via the FKBP12-FRB interface; used as immunosuppressants, anticancer agents, and geroprotectors, and like rapamycin can exhibit hormetic dose responses. |
| [[Rapamycin\|Rapamycin]] | In the SASP-Remodeling Aminochrome Complex strategy, Rapamycin acts as a "brake." It ensures that Adrenochrome-induced ROS signals are diverted from pro-inflammatory SASP production toward a quiesc... |
| [[RAW264.7\|RAW264.7]] | An immortalized murine macrophage-like cell line commonly used to study innate immunity, inflammation, and phagocytosis. |
| [[Rb\|Rb]] | Retinoblastoma protein, a master regulator of the G1/S cell cycle checkpoint whose hypophosphorylated state enforces senescence by sequestering E2F transcription factors |
| [[Reactive Oxygen Species\|Reactive Oxygen Species]] | Reactive Oxygen Species (ROS) are highly reactive molecules containing oxygen, formed as natural byproducts of oxygen metabolism. They play dual roles as signaling molecules and agents of oxida... |
| [[Receptor Tyrosine Kinases\|Receptor Tyrosine Kinases]] | Receptor tyrosine kinases (RTKs) are a family of 58 single-pass transmembrane receptors in 20 subfamilies that autophosphorylate on tyrosine after ligand-induced dimerization, generating phosphotyrosine docking sites that feed the MAPK, PI3K-Akt and PLCγ pathways. |
| [[Redox\|Redox]] | Definition: Redox is a scientific concept that describes fundamental principles governing biological or chemical systems. |
| [[Redox Homeostasis\|Redox Homeostasis]] | The dynamic balance between oxidants and antioxidants that maintains cellular signaling and prevents oxidative damage; its erosion underlies aging and disease. |
| [[Redox Potential\|Redox Potential]] | Redox potential (E1/2) quantifies a compound's tendency to accept or donate electrons; the negative redox potentials of methylene blue and toluidine blue O correlate with their mitochondrial electron-carrier and oxygen-consumption enhancement. |
| [[Redox Signaling\|Redox Signaling]] | Definition: Redox Signaling is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and ... |
| [[Redox Vaccination\|Redox Vaccination]] | Redox Vaccination is the hypothesis that sub-toxic exposure to redox-cycling compounds (such as Carbazochrome, a stabilized Adrenochrome derivative) upregulates SIRT3 expression through a retro... |
| [[Regulatory T Cell\|Regulatory T Cell]] | FoxP3+ immunosuppressive T cells maintaining tolerance; induced by TGF-beta, IDO1, and AhR signaling. |
| [[Regulatory T cells\|Regulatory T cells]] | A subset of immunosuppressive CD4+ T lymphocytes (Tregs) that maintain self-tolerance and limit autoimmunity and chronic inflammation; expanded by low-dose mTOR inhibition. |
| [[Relocalization of Chromatin Modifiers (RCM) Hypothesis\|Relocalization of Chromatin Modifiers (RCM) Hypothesis]] | An aging framework proposing that progressive loss of epigenetic information arises from the repeated recruitment of chromatin-modifying proteins (sirtuins, HDACs, PARP1) away from their genomic loci to DNA repair sites. |
| [[Renal Cell Carcinoma\|Renal Cell Carcinoma]] | A kidney cancer that originates in the lining of the proximal convoluted tubule. |
| [[Renal Failure\|Renal Failure]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Renal Failure is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, envir... |
| [[Resolvins\|Resolvins]] | Resolvins are specialized pro-resolving lipid mediators derived from EPA and DHA that actively terminate inflammation and promote tissue repair rather than merely suppressing it. |
| [[Respiratory Burst\|Respiratory Burst]] | The Respiratory Burst is the rapid release of Reactive Oxygen Species (ROS), such as Superoxide anion and Hydrogen Peroxide, from cells like Neutrophils and monocytes as part of the immune response. |
| [[Respiratory Chain Complex I\|Respiratory Chain Complex I]] | Complex I (NADH:ubiquinone oxidoreductase) is the largest ETC enzyme; it oxidizes NADH, reduces ubiquinone, pumps protons, and is a major superoxide source. |
| [[Resting Metabolic Rate\|Resting Metabolic Rate]] | The energy expended by a post-absorptive organism at rest in a thermoneutral environment, excluding activity, thermic effect of food, and growth. RMR is the largest single component of daily energy expenditure and is determined primarily by fat-free mass. |
| [[Resveratrol\|Resveratrol]] | Resveratrol (3,5,4′-trihydroxystilbene) is a polyphenolic stilbene found in grapes, red wine, and other plants. It was identified as the prototypical STACs (STAC) and has been shown to extend lifes... |
| [[Reticulocyte\|Reticulocyte]] | Enucleated but organelle-retaining immature red cell released from marrow; the stage of mitochondrial clearance and the clinical readout of erythropoietic output. |
| [[Retinal Ganglion Cells\|Retinal Ganglion Cells]] | CNS projection neurons vulnerable in glaucoma whose cGAS-STING activation drives cell death and vision loss. |
| [[Retinoic Acid\|Retinoic Acid]] | Active metabolite of vitamin A and potent differentiation inducer used to drive embryonic stem cell lineage commitment. |
| [[Retrograde Signaling\|Retrograde Signaling]] | A pathway of communication from the mitochondria to the cell that influences cellular and organismal activities |
| [[Retrotransposon\|Retrotransposon]] | Mobile genetic elements that copy themselves within the genome via an RNA intermediate; normally silenced by chromatin modifiers, but can be de-repressed by epigenetic dysregulation. |
| [[Reverse Cholesterol Transport\|Reverse Cholesterol Transport]] | The process by which excess cholesterol is transported from peripheral tissues to the liver via HDL; primary anti-atherogenic pathway. |
| [[Reverse Electron Transport\|Reverse Electron Transport]] | Mitochondrial electron backflow from Complex II-derived ubiquinol to Complex I, a major source of superoxide under high membrane potential. |
| [[Reverse transcriptase\|Reverse transcriptase]] | Reverse transcriptase is an RNA-dependent DNA polymerase (EC 2.7.7.49) that synthesizes DNA from an RNA template, the defining enzyme of retroviruses and retrotransposons and the catalytic core of telomerase. |
| [[Rheumatoid Arthritis\|Rheumatoid Arthritis]] | Rheumatoid arthritis (RA) is a long-term, progressive, and disabling autoimmune disease that causes inflammation, swelling, and pain in and around the joints and other body organs. The accumulation... |
| [[RHIM\|RHIM]] | RIP homotypic interaction motif (RHIM) — the amyloid-forming protein interaction module that assembles RIPK1/RIPK3, ZBP1, and TRIF necrosomes in necroptosis. |
| [[RhoA\|RhoA]] | RhoA (Ras homolog family member A) is a 21 kDa Rho-family small GTPase that acts as a GDP/GTP switch downstream of integrins, cadherins and GPCRs. Through ROCK and mDia it assembles stress fibers and focal adhesions, raises actomyosin contractility, and drives cytokinesis. |
| [[Ribonuclease III\|Ribonuclease III]] | Family of double-stranded RNA-specific endoribonucleases, including Drosha and Dicer, that catalyze microRNA and siRNA precursor processing. |
| [[ribonucleotide reductase\|Ribonucleotide reductase]] | Ribonucleotide reductase (RNR) is the sole enzyme that catalyzes the de novo conversion of ribonucleoside diphosphates (NDPs) into deoxyribonucleoside diphosphates (dNDPs), making it the rate-limiting, allosterically regulated committed step of dNTP biosynthesis for DNA replication and repair — and a validated anticancer drug target. |
| [[Ribosomal Protein S6\|Ribosomal Protein S6]] | A component of the 40S ribosomal subunit whose phosphorylation by S6K1 serves as the canonical pharmacodynamic biomarker of mTORC1 activity. |
| [[Ribosome Biogenesis\|Ribosome Biogenesis]] | Definition: Ribosome Biogenesis is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways ... |
| [[Ridaforolimus\|Ridaforolimus]] | Ridaforolimus (AP23573, MK-8669, formerly deforolimus) is a second-generation rapamycin analogue and allosteric mTORC1 inhibitor that failed to gain FDA approval despite improving progression-free survival in the phase III SUCCEED sarcoma trial. |
| [[RIG-I\|RIG-I]] | Retinoic acid-inducible gene I (DDX58), the cytosolic RIG-I-like receptor that detects short 5′-triphosphate double-stranded RNA and signals through MAVS to drive type I interferon and inflammatory gene expression — a mediator of senescence-associated inflammation. |
| [[Rigidity\|Rigidity]] | Parkinsonian motor sign of increased muscle tone from basal ganglia dopamine loss. |
| [[Rilonacept\|Rilonacept]] | Rilonacept is an IL-1 trap fusion protein that binds and neutralizes interleukin-1 alpha and interleukin-1 beta, used in autoinflammatory syndromes. |
| [[RILP\|RILP]] | Rab7-interacting lysosomal protein, a Rab effector composed of an N-terminal dynein-dynactin recruiting module and a C-terminal Rab7-binding coiled coil that forms a dyad; it is the principal minus-end motor adaptor positioning late endosomes and lysosomes perinuclearly. |
| [[Ripoptosome\|Ripoptosome]] | A 2 MDa death-receptor-independent intracellular complex of RIPK1, FADD, caspase-8, caspase-10 and c-FLIP isoforms that forms when cIAPs are depleted and decides between apoptosis and necroptosis. |
| [[RISC\|RISC]] | RNA-induced silencing complex — the Argonaute-containing effector machinery that uses guide miRNAs/siRNAs to repress or cleave target mRNAs. |
| [[RNA Interference\|RNA Interference]] | Conserved gene-silencing mechanism in which dsRNA is processed into small RNAs that guide Argonaute-RISC to repress complementary mRNAs. |
| [[RNA-Binding Proteins\|RNA-Binding Proteins]] | A diverse protein class that binds RNA to regulate processing, and which cGAS scaffolds in the nucleus to maintain homeostasis. |
| [[RNA-seq\|RNA-seq]] | RNA-seq (RNA sequencing) is a high-throughput sequencing method that profiles the complete set of RNA transcripts in a biological sample. It quantifies Gene Expression, detects Alternative Spli... |
| [[RNF168\|RNF168]] | A RING finger E3 ubiquitin ligase that acts second in the RNF8-RNF168 cascade at DNA double-strand breaks, monoubiquitinating histone H2A/H2AX at K13-K15 and extending K63-linked chains to generate the binding surface that recruits 53BP1 and BRCA1. |
| [[RNF185\|RNF185]] | Mitochondrial ubiquitin E3 ligase that regulates autophagy through interaction with BNIP1, using K63-linked ubiquitin chains to recruit p62 for mitophagy. |
| [[ROS\|ROS]] | Reactive Oxygen Species (ROS) are chemically reactive molecules containing oxygen, including Superoxide Radicals, Hydrogen Peroxide, Hydroxyl Radicals, and Singlet Oxygen. They serve dual roles... |
| [[Rotenone\|Rotenone]] | Rotenone is a naturally occurring isoflavonoid pesticide and classic Complex I inhibitor used to model Parkinson's disease through mitochondrial dysfunction and oxidative stress. |
| [[RRM1\|RRM1]] | RRM1 encodes the large (α) catalytic subunit of human ribonucleotide reductase (RNR); its homodimer carries the active site where ribonucleoside diphosphates are reduced to deoxyribonucleoside diphosphates, plus the allosteric specificity/activity sites and the redox-active disulfide re-reduced each cycle by thioredoxin-1 or glutaredoxin. RRM1 overexpression predicts chemoresistance, notably to gemcitabine. |
| [[RRM2\|RRM2]] | RRM2 encodes the small (β) subunit of human ribonucleotide reductase that houses the diferric-tyrosyl radical cofactor driving catalysis; expression is S-phase-restricted via E2F, and the subunit is the direct target of hydroxyurea and triapine, which destroy the radical (or its iron center) to collapse de novo dNTP synthesis. |
| [[RRM2B\|RRM2B]] | RRM2B encodes the p53-inducible small subunit (p53R2) of ribonucleotide reductase that partners with RRM1 to supply deoxyribonucleotides for DNA repair and mitochondrial DNA synthesis in non-cycling cells; loss-of-function mutations cause mitochondrial DNA depletion syndromes, and p53R2 is a defensive arm of the p53 DNA-damage response. |
| [[RSL3\|RSL3]] | A ferroptosis-inducing compound that directly inhibits GPX4 by covalent modification of its catalytic selenocysteine. |
| [[Rspo1\|Rspo1]] | R-spondin 1, an enhancer of Wnt signaling that maintains intestinal epithelial stem cells and injury resistance |
| [[Rubicon\|Rubicon]] | A RUN domain-containing protein that inhibits the Beclin 1–Vps34 autophagy complex and negatively regulates autophagosome formation and endocytic maturation. |
| [[Ruxolitinib\|Ruxolitinib]] | JAK1/2 inhibitor (Jakafi) that reduces SASP cytokines; a senomorphic therapy. |
| [[Ryanodine Receptor\|Ryanodine Receptor]] | Intracellular Ca2+ release channel of the sarcoplasmic/endoplasmic reticulum that supplies cytosolic Ca2+ signals to mitochondria. |
| [[S Phase\|S Phase]] | S Phase (Synthesis Phase) is the phase of the Cell Cycle during which DNA Replication occurs, producing two copies of the genome. It follows G1 Phase and precedes G2 Phase. S Phase is initiated by ... |
| [[S-Nitrosoglutathione Reductase\|S-Nitrosoglutathione Reductase]] | The enzyme that metabolizes S-nitrosoglutathione and regulates protein S-nitrosylation; its reduced activity in senescent cells causes excessive Parkin S-nitrosylation, inhibiting mitophagy and promoting senescence. |
| [[S100A13\|S100A13]] | EF-hand calcium/copper-binding S100 protein that drives IL-1α nuclear translocation and SASP initiation. |
| [[SK61_2\|S6K1/2]] | Ribosomal protein S6 kinase 1 and 2 are AGC-family kinases downstream of mTORC1 that drive translation and exert negative feedback on insulin/PI3K signaling; in the rapamycin-hormesis model their mTORC1-mediated activation has a low Michaelis-Menten constant. |
| [[SAH\|SAH]] | S-adenosylhomocysteine, the immediate product of methylation reactions and a competitive inhibitor of methyltransferases. |
| [[SAHA\|SAHA]] | SAHA (vorinostat) is a hydroxamic-acid pan-HDAC inhibitor and the first FDA-approved histone deacetylase inhibitor, increasing histone acetylation and derepressing gene expression. |
| [[Salicylic acid\|Salicylic acid]] | Beta-hydroxy acid used therapeutically and as a solubility-enhancing vehicle for fenbendazole. |
| [[SAMe\|SAMe]] | SAMe (S-adenosylmethionine) is a naturally occurring compound that is a major Methyl Donor in the body. It is involved in over 100 biochemical reactions. |
| [[Sandfly\|Sandfly]] | Sandfly is a colloquial name covering several unrelated biting dipteran families; the medically important group is the subfamily Phlebotominae (Psychodidae), female vectors of Leishmania and of phlebovirus sandfly fever. |
| [[Sanglifehrin A\|Sanglifehrin A]] | Cyclophilin D-binding MPT inhibitor without calcineurin activity, used to dissect MPT necrosis from necroptosis. |
| [[Saposin-D\|Saposin-D]] | A small lysosomal accessory protein (sphingolipid activator protein) that facilitates substrate presentation to acid ceramidase by extracting ceramide from membranes and enabling access to the enzyme's active site. |
| [[Sarcomere\|Sarcomere]] | The sarcomere is the repeating contractile unit of striated muscle bounded by two adjacent Z-discs, containing interdigitating actin thin filaments and myosin thick filaments plus the titin-based molecular rulers that set filament length and passive elasticity. |
| [[Sarcoplasmic Reticulum\|Sarcoplasmic Reticulum]] | Specialized smooth endoplasmic reticulum of muscle fibers that stores and releases cytosolic calcium via the SERCA pumps and ryanodine receptors, making it the core calcium-handling organelle of excitation-contraction coupling. |
| [[Sarcoptes scabiei\|Sarcoptes scabiei]] | The parasitic mite that causes scabies in humans and animals; the burrowing female lives in the stratum corneum, provoking intense pruritus and a type I hypersensitivity reaction to mite faeces and saliva. |
| [[SARM1\|SARM1]] | Sterile Alpha and TIR Motif Containing 1; an ~80 kDa NAD+-consuming glycohydrolase of the Toll/IL-1 receptor (TIR) family that drives axon degeneration and Wallerian degeneration upon axonal injury by depleting NAD+. |
| [[SARS-CoV-2\|SARS-CoV-2]] | SARS-CoV‑2 (Severe Acute Respiratory Syndrome Coronavirus 2) is a positive-sense single-stranded RNA virus belonging to the Coronaviridae family (genus *Betacoronavirus*). It is the causative a... |
| [[SAT1\|SAT1]] | SAT1 (spermidine/spermine N1-acetyltransferase 1) is a p53-induced polyamine catabolism enzyme that promotes ferroptosis via lipid peroxidation. |
| [[Satellite Cell\|Satellite Cell]] | Satellite cells are Muscle Stem Cell of Skeletal Muscle, located between the Basal Lamina and the Sarcolemma of mature muscle fibers. They are essential for postnatal Muscle Growth, regeneratio... |
| [[Saturated Enzymatic Regime\|Saturated Enzymatic Regime]] | A kinetic regime in which an enzyme-catalyzed interaction operates near Vmax (substrate-saturating), making the reaction rate insensitive to substrate concentration—shown to boost drug-hormesis probability ~10×. |
| [[SB203580\|SB203580]] | A selective p38 MAPK inhibitor used in the apigenin study as a tool to confirm p38 MAPK's role in SASP expression. |
| [[SCAPs\|SCAPs]] | Senescence-cell anti-apoptotic pathways, the pro-survival network that renders senescent cells resistant to apoptosis. |
| [[Schizophrenia\|Schizophrenia]] | Schizophrenia is a concept related to Adrenochrome and catecholamine metabolism. |
| [[Sclerostin\|Sclerostin]] | Osteocyte-secreted glycoprotein that inhibits Wnt/beta-catenin signaling and suppresses bone formation |
| [[Scramblase\|Scramblase]] | Calcium-activated phospholipid scramblases (TMEM16F/ANO6, PLSCR family) that externalize phosphatidylserine — the eat-me signal executed in eryptosis and apoptosis. |
| [[SECISBP2\|SECISBP2]] | SECISBP2 (SBP2) is the SECIS-binding protein that recognizes the SECIS element in selenoprotein mRNA 3′UTRs and, together with eEFSec, directs recoding of UGA stop codons to insert selenocysteine. |
| [[Securin\|Securin]] | Separase inhibitor degraded by APC/C-CDC20 at anaphase onset to allow sister chromatid separation. |
| [[Selective Androgen Receptor Modulators\|Selective Androgen Receptor Modulators]] | Tissue-selective androgen receptor ligands that promote anabolism and muscle mass with reduced androgenic side effects versus testosterone. |
| [[Selective Serotonin Reuptake Inhibitors\|Selective Serotonin Reuptake Inhibitors]] | Selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors (SSRIs) are a major class of antidepressants that raise synaptic serotonin; dangerous with MAO inhibitors. |
| [[Selenium\|Selenium]] | Selenium (Se) is an essential trace element that is incorporated into selenoproteins as the 21st amino acid, selenocysteine (Sec, U), where it confers unique redox properties critical for antio... |
| [[Selenocysteine\|Selenocysteine]] | Selenocysteine (Sec) is the 21st proteinogenic amino acid — a serine analog in which selenium replaces the side-chain oxygen. It is incorporated by recoding the UGA stop codon and serves as the catalytic residue of selenoenzymes (GPx, TrxR, deiodinases) that defend redox homeostasis. |
| [[Selenomethionine\|Selenomethionine]] | The selenium analogue of methionine and the dominant form of selenium in plant foods; it is non-specifically incorporated in place of methionine into body proteins, is preferentially retained in the methionine pool, and is the selenium species used in most selenium supplements. |
| [[Selenoprotein\|Selenoprotein]] | Selenoproteins are proteins that contain selenocysteine (Sec) as a catalytic residue, inserted co-translationally by UGA recoding. The human selenoproteome (~25 members) centers on redox functions — glutathione peroxidases, thioredoxin reductases, and iodothyronine deiodinases. |
| [[Selenoprotein P\|Selenoprotein P]] | Selenoprotein P (SELENOP) is a selenium-rich extracellular glycoprotein that transports selenium to tissues and provides antioxidant defense; it contains multiple selenocysteine residues (up to 10 in humans). |
| [[Senescence-Messaging Secretome\|Senescence-Messaging Secretome]] | Alternative name for the SASP emphasizing its role as a communication network between senescent cells and their microenvironment |
| [[Senolytic Paradox\|Senolytic Paradox]] | The observation that eliminating senescent cells — including oncogene-induced senescent cells in premalignant lesions — can paradoxically promote tumor growth by removing a cell-autonomous cancer barrier. |
| [[SENP\|SENP]] | SENP is the family of SUMO-specific cysteine proteases (SENP1-3 and SENP5-7) that mature SUMO precursors by removing their C-terminal extension and reverse SUMOylation by cleaving SUMO off target proteins. |
| [[SENP1\|SENP1]] | Sentrin/SUMO-specific protease 1; cleaves SUMO from conjugated substrates (deSUMOylation), regulating protein localization, stability, and activity across stress responses. |
| [[SEPHS2\|SEPHS2]] | SEPHS2 (selenophosphate synthetase 2) generates monoselenophosphate from selenide and ATP, providing the activated selenium donor used by SepSecS to make selenocysteine. |
| [[SepSecS\|SepSecS]] | SepSecS (SEPSECS, selenocysteine synthase) converts O-phosphoseryl-tRNA[Ser]Sec into selenocysteine-tRNA[Ser]Sec using monoselenophosphate, completing selenocysteine biosynthesis on the tRNA. |
| [[Sepsis\|Sepsis]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Sepsis is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, environmenta... |
| [[Septic Shock\|Septic Shock]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Septic Shock is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genetic, enviro... |
| [[SERCA\|SERCA]] | SERCA pumps are the sacro/endoplasmic reticulum Ca2+-ATPase family of P-type ATPases encoded by ATP2A1-3, which move cytosolic calcium into the ER/SR lumen against gradient and thereby terminate muscle contraction, buffer cytosolic calcium and shape calcium signalling. |
| [[Serine\|Serine]] | A hydroxyl-bearing amino acid that serves as the primary substrate for mitochondrial one-carbon metabolism via SHMT2, linking amino acid metabolism to nucleotide biosynthesis, NADPH regeneration, and epigenetic regulation — a pathway frequently hijacked by cancer cells. |
| [[Serotonin\|Serotonin]] | Serotonin (5-hydroxytryptamine or 5-HT) is a monoamine Neurotransmitters that plays a key role in regulating mood, sleep, appetite, and digestion. |
| [[Serotonin Receptor\|Serotonin Receptor]] | Serotonin receptors are the GPCR and ligand-gated ion channel targets of serotonin; excessive stimulation underlies serotonin syndrome. |
| [[Serotonin Syndrome\|Serotonin Syndrome]] | Serotonin syndrome is a potentially life-threatening drug reaction caused by excessive serotonergic activity, characterized by autonomic instability, altered mental status, and neuromuscular abnormalities. |
| [[Serotonin-Norepinephrine Reuptake Inhibitors\|Serotonin-Norepinephrine Reuptake Inhibitors]] | Serotonin-norepinephrine reuptake inhibitors (SNRIs) are antidepressants raising synaptic serotonin and norepinephrine; dangerous with MAOIs. |
| [[SERT\|SERT]] | Serotonin transporter (SLC6A4) that clears synaptic serotonin; target of SSRIs and modulator of mood, redox tone, and excitotoxic balance in aging. |
| [[Setanaxib\|Setanaxib]] | Setanaxib (GKT137831) is a first-in-class selective NADPH oxidase NOX1/NOX4 inhibitor developed to reduce ROS-driven fibrosis and inflammation. |
| [[Sex Steroid Ablation\|Sex Steroid Ablation]] | A therapeutic approach involving removal or suppression of sex steroids to restore thymic architecture, increase thymic cellularity, and enhance thymopoiesis. |
| [[SH-SY5Y\|SH-SY5Y]] | A human neuroblastoma cell line differentiated into neuron-like cells, widely used as a dopaminergic neuron model. |
| [[SHARPIN\|SHARPIN]] | Component of the linear ubiquitin chain assembly complex (LUBAC) and a regulator of RIPK1-dependent signaling; spontaneous SHARPIN-deficiency causes chronic proliferative dermatitis in mice that is reversed by TNFR1 deficiency. |
| [[Shingles Vaccine\|Shingles Vaccine]] | Recombinant subunit (glycoprotein E + AS01B adjuvant) or live-attenuated vaccine that prevents herpes zoster and may attenuate age-related inflammaging. |
| [[SHMT2\|SHMT2]] | Serine hydroxymethyltransferase 2, a mitochondrial enzyme that catalyzes the conversion of serine to glycine in one-carbon metabolism, is upregulated in colorectal cancer and promoted by SIRT3-mediated deacetylation. |
| [[Short-chain Fatty Acids\|Short-chain Fatty Acids]] | Short-chain fatty acids (SCFAs) are produced by the gut microbiota through the fermentation of non-digestible carbohydrates, serving as important signaling molecules and energy sources for host cells. |
| [[SHP1\|SHP1]] | Protein tyrosine phosphatase non-receptor type 6 (PTPN6); dephosphorylates Caspase-8 at Tyr380, restoring apoptotic sensitivity in cancer cells. |
| [[SHP2\|SHP2]] | Src homology 2 domain-containing phosphatase 2 (PTPN11) that dephosphorylates Parkin at Tyr143 to activate its E3 ligase. |
| [[Sigma-1 receptor\|Sigma-1 receptor]] | Sigma-1 receptor (S1R) is an ER/chaperone protein abundantly expressed in hepatocytes; its inhibition promotes ferroptosis in hepatocellular carcinoma. |
| [[Silica\|Silica]] | Silica (silicon dioxide) is a crystalline mineral that activates the NLRP3 inflammasome via lysosomal rupture and cathepsin B release, causing chronic inflammatory lung disease. |
| [[Silybin\|Silybin]] | Flavonolignan from milk thistle (Silybum marianum) with hepatoprotective and nephroprotective activity; attenuates cisplatin nephrotoxicity likely via SIRT3-mediated preservation of tubular integrity and mitochondrial bioenergetics. |
| [[Simian Virus 40\|Simian Virus 40]] | Simian Virus 40 (SV40) is a non-enveloped DNA virus belonging to the Polyomaviridae family. Its small (~5.2 kb) circular double-stranded DNA genome has been a foundational model in Molecular Bi... |
| [[Single-Cell RNA-seq\|Single-Cell RNA-seq]] | Single-cell RNA sequencing measures the transcriptome of individual cells rather than bulk tissue, resolving heterogeneous cell populations and states that averaging hides; droplet platforms with molecular barcodes made it routine, but dropout, batch effects and stochastic expression remain its defining analytic problems. |
| [[Sirtuin-Caspase Crosstalk\|Sirtuin-Caspase Crosstalk]] | The bidirectional regulatory loop between sirtuins (NAD+-dependent deacetylases) and caspases (apoptotic executioner proteases) — sirtuins generally suppress caspase-dependent apoptosis, while caspases feed back to cleave and inactivate sirtuins, locking in cell death. |
| [[sjTREC\|sjTREC]] | Single joint T cell receptor excision circle; a molecular marker for recent thymic emigrants and active thymopoiesis, used to quantify thymic output. |
| [[Skeletal Muscle\|Skeletal Muscle]] | Overview: Skeletal Muscle is a specialized cellular or anatomical structure with defined functions in tissue organization and physiology. |
| [[SLC1A5\|SLC1A5]] | SLC1A5 (ASCT2) is a glutamine transporter targeted by miR-137 in melanoma; it supports antioxidant defenses that modulate ferroptosis sensitivity. |
| [[SLC25A1\|SLC25A1]] | Mitochondrial citrate carrier (CIC) that exports citrate from the mitochondrial matrix to the cytosol, where it is converted to acetyl-CoA by ACLY. Upregulated in cellular senescence; its inhibition suppresses the SASP and improves healthspan in aged mice. |
| [[SLC25A22\|SLC25A22]] | SLC25A22 is a mitochondrial inner-membrane glutamate transporter that suppresses ferroptosis; it is stabilized by SIRT3-mediated deacetylation (K83), which prevents its ubiquitination and proteasomal degradation — a key non-enzymatic link between SIRT3 redox control and cell death. |
| [[SLC25A51\|SLC25A51]] | Mitochondrial inner-membrane NAD+ importer that supplies matrix NAD+ for respiration and sirtuin activity. |
| [[SLC3A2\|SLC3A2]] | SLC3A2 (4F2hc/CD98) is the heavy chain of the system Xc- cystine/glutamate antiporter required for trafficking and stability of the catalytic SLC7A11 subunit. |
| [[SLC6A8\|SLC6A8]] | The gene encoding the principal creatine transporter (CT1) on chromosome Xq28; mutations cause creatine transporter deficiency, an X-linked form of cerebral creatine deficiency syndrome associated with intellectual disability, epilepsy, and speech delay. |
| [[SLC7A11\|SLC7A11]] | SLC7A11 (xCT) is the catalytic light chain of the system Xc- cystine/glutamate antiporter and a key determinant of ferroptosis susceptibility. |
| [[SMAD\|SMAD]] | SMAD proteins are the primary intracellular signal transducers of the TGF-beta superfamily. Eight human SMADs fall into three classes: receptor-regulated R-SMADs (1, 2, 3, 5, 8), the common mediator SMAD4, and inhibitory SMADs (6, 7). |
| [[SMAD2\|SMAD2]] | SMAD2 (MADH2) is a receptor-regulated SMAD phosphorylated at its C-terminal SSXS motif by TGF-beta, activin and Nodal type I receptors. Unlike SMAD3 it lacks direct DNA binding because of an exon-3 insert, and it is required for embryonic axis patterning and definitive endoderm specification. |
| [[SMAD4\|SMAD4]] | SMAD4 (MADH4, DPC4) is the sole common-mediator SMAD and obligate partner of all R-SMADs. Loss of both alleles inactivates TGF-beta and BMP signaling and occurs in roughly 55% of pancreatic ductal adenocarcinomas; germline mutations cause juvenile polyposis syndrome. |
| [[SmgGDS\|SmgGDS]] | SmgGDS is an estradiol-induced small-GTPase regulator protecting the female heart against ferritinophagy-mediated ferroptosis. |
| [[Smurf1\|Smurf1]] | HECT-domain E3 ubiquitin ligase that constrains TGF-beta superfamily signalling by ubiquitinating receptor-regulated SMADs; it is a negative regulator of osteoblast differentiation and bone mass, and its loss is embryonic lethal in mice. |
| [[SMURF2\|SMURF2]] | SMAD Ubiquitin Regulatory Factor 2 (SMURF2) — a HECT-family E3 ubiquitin ligase that regulates TGF-β/SMAD signaling and targets SIRT1 for ubiquitination-mediated degradation, suppressing colorectal cancer proliferation. |
| [[SN011\|SN011]] | Small-molecule inhibitor of the cGAS–STING pathway that binds the STING agonist site. Used to test whether STING-dependent signaling mediates the SASP independently of the mitochondrial acetyl-CoA axis. |
| [[SNAP29\|SNAP29]] | SNAP29 is a ubiquitously expressed, soluble Q-SNARE (SNAP-25 family) that binds many syntaxins and, unlike SNAP25, localizes to intracellular membranes rather than the plasma membrane, supplying the v-SNARE half of autophagosome maturation and Golgi SNARE complexes. |
| [[SNAPIN\|SNAPIN]] | Protein that drives retrograde transport of mitochondria from synapses to the soma, coordinating with Parkin-mediated mitophagy to maintain synaptic mitochondrial integrity. |
| [[SNCA\|SNCA]] | Gene encoding α-synuclein, a presynaptic protein whose misfolding and aggregation is central to Parkinson's disease pathology. |
| [[SNEDDS\|SNEDDS]] | Self-nanoemulsifying drug delivery systems (SNEDDS) are anhydrous surfactant-oil preconcentrates that self-emulsify into nanoemulsions upon aqueous dilution, improving oral bioavailability of poorly soluble drugs. |
| [[SOCS3\|SOCS3]] | Suppressor of Cytokine Signaling 3 (SOCS3) is a negative-feedback regulator of cytokine signaling that targets IDO1 for ubiquitination and degradation. |
| [[SOD1\|SOD1]] | Catalytic function: SOD1 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[SOD3\|SOD3]] | Catalytic function: SOD3 is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Sodium Potassium ATPase\|Sodium Potassium ATPase]] | Sodium Potassium ATPase (Na⁺/K⁺-ATPase, Na⁺/K⁺ pump) is a transmembrane ion pump that maintains the Electrochemical Gradient across the Plasma Membrane by transporting three Na⁺ ions out and two K⁺... |
| [[Solid Lipid Nanoparticles\|Solid Lipid Nanoparticles]] | Solid lipid nanoparticles (SLNs) are colloidal carriers made of physiological lipids solid at body temperature, offering controlled release and improved bioavailability of lipophilic drugs like fisetin. |
| [[soluble epoxide hydrolase\|soluble epoxide hydrolase]] | Soluble epoxide hydrolase (sEH/EPHX2), a bifunctional enzyme that degrades cardioprotective epoxy-fatty acids and modulates SIRT3 protein turnover via proteolytic degradation in vascular calcification. |
| [[Soluble Guanylyl Cyclase\|Soluble Guanylyl Cyclase]] | Soluble guanylyl cyclase (sGC) is the intracellular receptor for nitric oxide; NO activates sGC to produce cGMP, driving vasodilation. Methylene blue inhibits sGC downstream of nitric oxide. |
| [[Soluplus\|Soluplus]] | Polyvinyl caprolactam-polyvinyl acetate-polyethylene glycol graft copolymer used for injectable micelles. |
| [[Sorafenib\|Sorafenib]] | Sorafenib is a multi-kinase inhibitor used in advanced hepatocellular carcinoma whose anti-tumor effect is partly mediated by inducing ferroptosis. |
| [[Speech Development\|Speech Development]] | Speech development is the emergence of intelligible spoken language from babbling through first words to complex syntax, a process depending on auditory input, motor planning, myelination and — metabolically — on adequate brain creatine and energy supply. |
| [[Spermidine\|Spermidine]] | Spermidine is a naturally occurring polyamine that is found in all living cells. It is recognized as a potent ''geroprotector'' due to its ability to extend the lifespan of various organisms, inc... |
| [[Spermine\|Spermine]] | The highest polyamine in the putrescine-spermidine-spermine pathway, a tetravalent polycation that binds nucleic acids, scavenges free radicals, and regulates chromatin, translation and innate immune sensing. |
| [[Sphingolipid Metabolism\|Sphingolipid Metabolism]] | The network of pathways synthesizing and degrading sphingolipids (ceramide, sphingomyelin, sphingosine, sphingosine-1-phosphate), central to cell survival, stress signaling, and — newly — ferroptosis sensitivity in senescence. |
| [[Sphingomyelin\|Sphingomyelin]] | A phosphosphingolipid synthesized from ceramide; a structural component of membranes and a reservoir for ceramide generation via sphingomyelinases. |
| [[Sphingosine\|Sphingosine]] | An amino alcohol backbone of sphingolipids; produced by acid ceramidase-mediated cleavage of ceramide and phosphorylated to sphingosine-1-phosphate, a pro-survival signaling lipid. |
| [[Sphingosine-1-phosphate\|Sphingosine-1-phosphate]] | A bioactive sphingolipid signaling lipid phosphorylated from sphingosine; generally pro-survival, pro-proliferative, and anti-apoptotic, counterbalancing ceramide. |
| [[Spinal Cord Injury (SCI)\|Spinal Cord Injury (SCI)]] | Damage to the spinal cord from trauma or ischemia causing loss of motor, sensory, and autonomic function below the lesion; associated with muscle atrophy and neuropathic sequelae. |
| [[Spinal muscular atrophy with progressive myoclonic epilepsy\|Spinal muscular atrophy with progressive myoclonic epilepsy]] | A rare autosomal recessive neurodegenerative disorder caused by hypomorphic ASAH1 mutations with ~5-30% residual acid ceramidase activity, allelic with Farber disease. |
| [[Spliceosome\|Spliceosome]] | The macromolecular machinery that catalyzes pre-mRNA splicing, recruited and regulated by noncanonical nuclear cGAS. |
| [[Squalene synthase\|Squalene synthase]] | Squalene synthase is the first committed enzyme of sterol biosynthesis in the mevalonate pathway and a regulator of ferroptosis sensitivity through downstream lipid products. |
| [[SR-BI\|SR-BI]] | Scavenger receptor class B type I; bidirectional cholesterol transporter on macrophages; mediates cholesterol flux between cells and HDL. |
| [[Src\|Src]] | Proto-oncogenic tyrosine kinase that phosphorylates Caspase-8 at Tyr380, inhibiting apoptosis and promoting migration; frequently activated in cancers. |
| [[SRCAP\|SRCAP]] | SRCAP is an ATP-dependent chromatin remodeler of the SNF2 family that forms the SRCAP complex, the vertebrate Swr1 ortholog responsible for depositing the H2A.Z histone variant into nucleosomes, and also functions as a transcriptional coactivator for CREBBP-dependent steroid receptor signalling. |
| [[SREBP-1c\|SREBP-1c]] | SREBP-1c (sterol regulatory element-binding protein 1c) is a basic helix-loop-helix leucine zipper transcription factor that regulates genes involved in Lipid Synthesis, particularly Fatty acid... |
| [[SREBP2\|SREBP2]] | Sterol regulatory element-binding protein 2; a basic helix-loop-helix leucine zipper transcription factor that activates genes of cholesterol biosynthesis and uptake; negatively regulated by SIRT6. |
| [[SSRP1\|SSRP1]] | SSRP1 is the 80 kDa HMG-box subunit of the FACT chromatin transcription complex, a histone chaperone that disassembles and reassembles H2A-H2B dimers during transcription elongation and replication, and which also recognizes cisplatin-modified DNA. |
| [[Stanniocalcin-1\|Stanniocalcin-1]] | Glycoprotein hormone that orchestrates a hormetic AMPK/SIRT3 axis mitigating oxidative stress and apoptosis; inhibits BNIP3 expression to ameliorate renal injury in diabetic models. |
| [[STAT\|STAT]] | Signal transducer and activator of transcription family mediating JAK-STAT signaling downstream of interferons in necroptotic inflammation. |
| [[STAT1\|STAT1]] | STAT1 is a latent cytoplasmic transcription factor and the canonical effector of type I and type II interferon signaling. JAK phosphorylation at Tyr701 drives reciprocal SH2-phosphotyrosine dimerization, nuclear translocation and binding to GAS elements; STAT1 forms homodimers and STAT1-STAT2 heterodimers. |
| [[STAT3\|STAT3]] | Signal transducer and activator of transcription 3 (STAT3) is a transcription factor which in humans is encoded by the *STAT3* gene. |
| [[STAT6\|STAT6]] | Signal transducer and activator of transcription 6 is the key transcription factor downstream of IL-4 and IL-13 receptor signaling, driving type 2 immune responses. |
| [[Statins\|Statins]] | Statins are a class of lipid-lowering medications that competitively inhibit HMG-CoA reductase — the rate-limiting enzyme in the mevalonate pathway — reducing endogenous cholesterol synthesis. ... |
| [[STEAP3\|STEAP3]] | STEAP3 (SIKE) is the endosomal ferrireductase that converts Fe3+ to Fe2+ for DMT1 transport, feeding the labile iron pool that drives ferroptosis. |
| [[Stem Cell Exhaustion\|Stem Cell Exhaustion]] | Stem cell exhaustion is the age-related decline in the number and function of stem cells, which impairs tissue regeneration and contributes to the aging process. |
| [[Stem Cells\|Stem Cells]] | Undifferentiated cells with self-renewal and differentiation capacity; their exhaustion with age is delayed by mTORC1 inhibition via autophagy, reduced inflammation, and growth-factor induction. |
| [[STING\|STING]] | Stimulator of interferon genes, a signaling protein that plays a key role in innate immunity by sensing cytosolic DNA. |
| [[STING Inhibitors\|STING Inhibitors]] | Experimental small molecules that block STING signaling to reduce neuroinflammation and neuronal death. |
| [[Stomatitis\|Stomatitis]] | Inflammation and ulceration of the oral mucosa, a frequent and dose-limiting adverse effect of mTOR inhibitor therapy. |
| [[Stress Granules\|Stress Granules]] | Cytoplasmic ribonucleoprotein condensates that sequester RNA-binding proteins such as TDP-43 and interface with innate immune signaling. |
| [[Stress-Induced Senescence\|Stress-Induced Senescence]] | Cellular senescence triggered by acute stressors including genotoxic damage, oxidative stress, oncogene activation, and mitochondrial dysfunction. |
| [[Striatum\|Striatum]] | The Striatum (also known as the neostriatum or caudate-putamen) is a subcortical part of the forebrain and a critical component of the Basal Ganglia. It consists of the Caudate Nucleus ... |
| [[Stroke\|Stroke]] | Stroke is a medical emergency caused by interruption of blood supply to the brain, leading to tissue damage and neurological deficits. It is a leading cause of disability and mortality worldwide. |
| [[STX17\|STX17]] | STX17 is a Qa-SNARE syntaxin with a unique double-hairpin C-terminal transmembrane anchor that cycles between the ER-Golgi intermediate compartment and the autophagosome, where it is the essential SNARE for autophagosome-lysosome fusion. |
| [[Substance P\|Substance P]] | An 11-amino-acid tachykinin neuropeptide (Arg-Pro-Lys-Pro-Gln-Gln-Phe-Phe-Gly-Leu-Met-NH2) co-transmitted by nociceptive primary afferents; it acts at the NK1 receptor to transmit pain, drive neurogenic inflammation, and — via the area postrema — mediate emesis. |
| [[Substantia Nigra\|Substantia Nigra]] | The Substantia Nigra (SN) is a critical basal ganglia structure located in the midbrain. Its name, Latin for 'black substance,' is derived from the dark color provided by high concentrations of... |
| [[Successful Aging\|Successful Aging]] | Successful aging is a multidimensional concept encompassing avoidance of disease/disability, maintained physical and cognitive function, and sustained social engagement. |
| [[Succinate Dehydrogenase\|Succinate Dehydrogenase]] | Succinate dehydrogenase (SDH, respiratory complex II), the only enzyme participating in both the TCA cycle and the electron transport chain; a key acetylation node regulated by SIRT3 in the mitochondrial matrix. |
| [[Sulfiredoxin\|Sulfiredoxin]] | ATP-dependent enzyme that reduces hyperoxidized (sulfinic acid) peroxiredoxins back to their active form, restoring antioxidant capacity. |
| [[Sulforaphane\|Sulforaphane]] | Sulforaphane (SFN) is an isothiocyanate compound from cruciferous vegetables (broccoli, Brussels sprouts, cabbage) and one of the most potent known dietary activators of the NRF2 pathway. |
| [[SUMF1\|SUMF1]] | SUMF1 encodes the endoplasmic reticulum enzyme C-alpha-formylglycine-generating enzyme (FGE), which post-translationally converts a cysteine residue into the catalytic formylglycine that all human sulfatases require; SUMF1 mutations cause multiple sulfatase deficiency. |
| [[SUMO\|SUMO]] | SUMO (small ubiquitin-like modifier) is a family of ~11 kDa ubiquitin-like proteins — SUMO1, SUMO2, SUMO3, SUMO4 in humans — covalently attached to lysine residues of target proteins to alter their localization, interactions, and stability, usually without triggering degradation. |
| [[Sunitinib\|Sunitinib]] | Multi-targeted receptor tyrosine-kinase inhibitor (VEGFR/PDGFR/KIT) for RCC and GIST. |
| [[Super-enhancer\|Super-enhancer]] | Large clusters of enhancers that drive high transcription of cell-identity and senescence genes; senescence-activated super-enhancers are enriched in H3K27ac and acetyl marks and direct SASP expression. |
| [[Superoxide\|Superoxide]] | Biochemical properties: Superoxide is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects metabolic f... |
| [[Superoxide Dismutase\|Superoxide Dismutase]] | Superoxide Dismutase (SOD) is an enzyme that alternately catalyzes the dismutation of the Superoxide anion (O₂⁻) radical into ordinary molecular oxygen (O₂) and Hydrogen Peroxide (H₂O₂). |
| [[SUPT16H\|SUPT16H]] | SUPT16H encodes SPT16, the 140 kDa subunit of the FACT chromatin transcription complex; together with SSRP1 it disassembles and reassembles H2A-H2B dimers to permit RNA polymerase II elongation through nucleosomes and functions in replication and DNA repair. |
| [[Suramin\|Suramin]] | Suramin is a medication used to treat African sleeping sickness and Onchocerciasis. It was one of the few drugs available for onchocerciasis before Ivermectin. |
| [[SUV39H1\|SUV39H1]] | Suppressor of variegation 3-9 homolog 1 (KMT1A), the H3K9 trimethyltransferase whose senescence-associated decline licenses H3K9 demethylation and SASP expression. |
| [[SWI-SNF\|SWI-SNF]] | ATP-dependent chromatin-remodeling complex family mutated across cancers and neurodevelopmental disorders. |
| [[SWI-SNF Complex\|SWI-SNF Complex]] | A chromatin-remodeling complex recruited by noncanonical nuclear cGAS via LLPS to regulate transcription. |
| [[Sympathetic Nervous System\|Sympathetic Nervous System]] | The "fight, flight, or freeze" branch of the autonomic nervous system that releases catecholamines and cortisol, lowers HRV, and drives the acute stress response. |
| [[Synaptophysin\|Synaptophysin]] | Synaptic vesicle glycoprotein (SYP) used as a pan-neuronal presynaptic marker reflecting synaptic density and connectivity. |
| [[Synthetic Lethality\|Synthetic Lethality]] | Synthetic lethality is a genetic or pharmacological concept where simultaneous disruption of two genes or pathways causes cell death, while disruption of either alone is tolerated. |
| [[System Xc-\|System Xc-]] | System Xc- is a cystine/glutamate antiporter at the plasma membrane whose activity is required for glutathione synthesis and ferroptosis resistance. |
| [[Systemic Inflammatory Response Syndrome\|Systemic Inflammatory Response Syndrome]] | Sterile systemic inflammation driven by necroptotic DAMP release, as in severe pancreatitis; precursor concept to sepsis criteria. |
| [[Systemic Lupus Erythematosus\|Systemic Lupus Erythematosus]] | A chronic autoimmune disease characterized by loss of self-tolerance, autoantibody production, and multi-organ inflammation driven by immune complex deposition. |
| [[Sytox Green\|Sytox Green]] | A membrane-impermeant cyanine DNA dye for live-cell reporting of membrane rupture and extracellular DNA — the standard NETosis imaging readout. |
| [[T Cell Receptor\|T Cell Receptor]] | A heterodimeric membrane receptor on T cells that recognizes peptide-MHC complexes, initiating adaptive immune responses; TCR gene rearrangement during thymopoiesis generates the diverse receptor repertoire. |
| [[T Lymphocyte\|T Lymphocyte]] | A specialized immune cell that develops in the thymus through thymopoiesis; critical for adaptive immunity, self-tolerance, and immune surveillance. |
| [[T-bet\|T-bet]] | Master transcription factor of Th1 differentiation downstream of IL-12/STAT4; drives interferon-gamma programs. |
| [[TAK1\|TAK1]] | TGFβ-activated kinase 1, a MAP3K that activates p38 MAPK and mTOR signaling to promote SASP. |
| [[Takotsubo Cardiomyopathy\|Takotsubo Cardiomyopathy]] | Acute, reversible left ventricular systolic dysfunction triggered by intense emotional or physical stress, mediated by catecholamine surge, oxidative stress, and lipid peroxidation. |
| [[TALE\|TALE]] | Transcription activator-like effectors, natural DNA-binding repeat arrays from Xanthomonas bacteria that can be reprogrammed to recognize specific DNA sequences; the DNA-binding domain used in mitoTALENs and other gene-editing tools. |
| [[Tamoxifen\|Tamoxifen]] | Selective estrogen receptor modulator (SERM) used in hormone-receptor-positive breast cancer; efficacy modulated by sirtuins |
| [[TARG1\|TARG1]] | TARG1 (OARD1/C6orf130) is a macrodomain ADP-ribosylhydrolase erasing Asp/Glu-MARylation and terminal PAR |
| [[Targeted Therapy\|Targeted Therapy]] | Cancer treatment modality in which drugs are aimed at a specific molecular dependency of a tumour — an oncogene, a receptor kinase, a blood vessel, or an immune checkpoint — rather than at the nonspecific damage of rapidly dividing cells. |
| [[TASCC\|TASCC]] | TOR-autophagy spatial coupling compartment, an mTORC1-autophagy signaling hub in senescent cells that couples amino-acid recycling to sustained SASP translation. |
| [[Tau\|Tau]] | Microtubule-associated protein tau (MAPT) is an intrinsically disordered neuronal protein whose hyperphosphorylation and aggregation drive neurofibrillary tangle formation in Alzheimer's Disease and other tauopathies. |
| [[Tauopathy\|Tauopathy]] | Neurodegenerative diseases characterized by pathological aggregation of the microtubule-associated protein tau. |
| [[Taurine\|Taurine]] | A conditionally essential sulfur-containing amino acid with roles in bile acid conjugation, osmoregulation, calcium handling, antioxidant defense, and mitochondrial translation; declines with age and supports longevity phenotypes when supplemented. |
| [[Tazemetostat\|Tazemetostat]] | Tazemetostat (EPZ-6438) is a first-in-class oral EZH2 inhibitor approved for epithelioid sarcoma and EZH2-mutant follicular lymphoma. |
| [[TBARS\|TBARS]] | Thiobarbituric acid reactive substances — a colorimetric assay measuring malondialdehyde as a biomarker of lipid peroxidation. |
| [[TBK1\|TBK1]] | TANK-binding kinase 1, an enzyme involved in the innate immune response, activated by STING and regulated by TFEB. |
| [[TCA cycle\|TCA cycle]] | Tricarboxylic acid (Krebs) cycle, the central mitochondrial pathway oxidizing acetyl-CoA to CO2 while generating NADH, FADH2, and GTP. |
| [[TDO\|TDO]] | Tryptophan 2,3-dioxygenase (TDO) is a hepatic heme-containing enzyme that catalyzes the same first step in the kynurenine pathway as IDO1 — converting tryptophan to N-formylkynurenine — and is primarily regulated by tryptophan concentration and glucocorticoids. |
| [[TRPC6\|TRPC6]] | Ca2+-permeable transient receptor potential channel 6; mediates Ca2+ entry into human erythrocytes (TRPC4/5 in mouse), a candidate initiation route for eryptosis. |
| [[TDP-43\|TDP-43]] | TAR DNA-binding protein 43, an RNA-binding protein whose mitochondrial mislocalization in ALS/FTD triggers cGAS-STING activation via mtDNA release. |
| [[Telomerase\|Telomerase]] | Catalytic function: Telomerase is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Telomere\|Telomere]] | Repetitive nucleoprotein caps at chromosome ends that protect genomic integrity and shorten with age and cell division. |
| [[Telomere Attrition\|Telomere Attrition]] | Telomere attrition is the process of telomere shortening that occurs with each cell division, eventually leading to cellular senescence or apoptosis. |
| [[Temsirolimus\|Temsirolimus]] | The ester prodrug of sirolimus, an intravenous mTOR inhibitor approved for advanced renal cell carcinoma; it binds FKBP12 to inhibit mTORC1, causing G1 arrest, reduced VEGF synthesis and anti-angiogenic effects. |
| [[Testosterone\|Testosterone]] | Principal male androgen regulating muscle, bone, and metabolic health; decline with age links to redox stress, mitochondrial dysfunction, and cardiovascular risk. |
| [[TET Enzymes\|TET enzymes]] | Catalytic function: TET enzymes is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Tetrahydrobiopterin\|Tetrahydrobiopterin]] | Biochemical properties: Tetrahydrobiopterin is an endogenous metabolite involved in cellular energy metabolism, redox reactions, or biosynthetic pathways. Its cellular concentration reflects me... |
| [[TFAM\|TFAM]] | Mitochondrial transcription factor A, a nuclear-encoded regulator of mitochondrial DNA replication, transcription, and nucleoid architecture; induced by mTOR inhibition to support mitochondrial renewal. |
| [[TFEB\|TFEB]] | Master regulator of the autophagy-lysosome pathway coordinating lysosomal biogenesis and autophagy |
| [[TGFBR1\|TGFBR1]] | The type I TGF-beta receptor, a ligand-activated ser/thr kinase that phosphorylates SMAD2/3 to launch canonical TGF-beta signalling; it is also a BMP receptor, and heterozygous loss-of-function mutations cause Loeys-Dietz syndrome. |
| [[TGFβ\|TGFβ]] | Transforming growth factor beta is an early SASP factor that drives paracrine senescence, EMT, fibrosis, and immune suppression via TGFBR-SMAD2/3 signaling. |
| [[Th1\|Th1]] | Type 1 helper T cells are a CD4+ T cell subset that drives cell-mediated immunity against intracellular pathogens through IFN-gamma production and macrophage activation. |
| [[TH17\|TH17]] | T helper 17 cells are a CD4+ T cell subset differentiated by the SASP factors IL-6 and IL-1β in the presence of TGFβ, linking senescence to adaptive immunity. |
| [[Th2\|Th2]] | Type 2 helper T cells are a CD4+ T cell subset that drives humoral and anti-helminth immunity through IL-4, IL-5, and IL-13 production. |
| [[Thermic Effect of Food\|Thermic Effect of Food]] | The energy cost of digesting, absorbing, and metabolising a meal, also termed diet-induced thermogenesis or the heat increment of feeding. It is proportional to food intake and is influenced by dietary protein content. |
| [[Thermogenesis\|Thermogenesis]] | Heat production by living organisms, arising from basal metabolism, the thermic effect of food, shivering and non-shivering thermogenesis (notably via brown adipose tissue and mitochondrial uncoupling), and physical activity. |
| [[Thioredoxin\|Thioredoxin]] | Thioredoxin (Trx) is an evolutionarily conserved family of small redox-active proteins that reduce oxidized cysteine residues on proteins via a Cys-Gly-Pro-Cys active site. In mammals the family comprises the cytosolic Thioredoxin-1 and mitochondrial Thioredoxin-2, plus domain-containing relatives, functioning as core antioxidant regulators and redox signaling hubs. |
| [[Thioredoxin reductase\|Thioredoxin reductase]] | Thioredoxin reductase (TXNRD) is a family of selenium-dependent NADPH oxidoreductases that recycle oxidized thioredoxin to its active reduced form, supporting the thioredoxin system's antioxidant and redox-signaling functions in cytosol (TXNRD1) and mitochondria (TXNRD2). |
| [[Thioredoxin-1\|Thioredoxin-1]] | Thioredoxin-1 (Trx1, TXN) is a ubiquitously expressed ~12 kDa cytosolic oxidoreductase that maintains thiol redox balance, regulates redox-sensitive signaling via ASK1, NF-κB and HIF-1α, regenerates peroxiredoxins, and is one of the few antioxidant proteins whose systemic overexpression prolongs mouse lifespan and protects the heart. |
| [[Thioredoxin-2\|Thioredoxin-2]] | Thioredoxin-2 (Trx2, TXN2) is the mitochondrial isoform of Thioredoxin-1, a small redox-active oxidoreductase that maintains mitochondrial thiol-redox balance, supports respiration and the antioxidant defense, and inhibits mitochondria-located ASK1 to suppress apoptosis. |
| [[Threonine\|Threonine]] | Essential amino acid supporting protein synthesis, mucin production, and one-carbon metabolism. |
| [[Thrombin\|Thrombin]] | Catalytic function: Thrombin is an enzyme that catalyzes biochemical reactions essential for cellular metabolism and homeostasis. |
| [[Thrombocytopenia\|Thrombocytopenia]] | A hematological condition defined by abnormally low blood platelet counts; a common, usually mild and reversible side effect of chronic mTOR inhibition. |
| [[Thromboembolism\|Thromboembolism]] | Obstruction of a vessel by a clot that has detached from its site of formation and travelled in the bloodstream; the dominant causes are venous thromboembolism (DVT and pulmonary embolism) and arterial thromboembolism (stroke, myocardial infarction). |
