| [[Thromboxane A₂\|Thromboxane A2]] | Thromboxane A2 (TXA2) is the prostanoid produced by thromboxane synthase in platelets; a potent vasoconstrictor and promoter of platelet aggregation that balances prostacyclin. |
| [[Thymic Epithelial Cells\|Thymic Epithelial Cells]] | Stromal cells in the thymus cortex and medulla that form a supportive network essential for T cell development, selection, and tolerance induction. |
| [[Thymic Involution\|Thymic Involution]] | The age-related shrinkage and functional decline of the thymus, characterized by loss of thymic epithelial space, reduced thymopoiesis, and decreased naive T cell output, contributing to immune aging; reversible by interventions including autophagy induction. |
| [[Thymidylate Synthase\|Thymidylate Synthase]] | Folate-dependent enzyme that catalyzes dUMP to dTMP; primary target of 5-fluorouracil. |
| [[Thymopoiesis\|Thymopoiesis]] | The process of T cell development, selection, and maturation within the thymus. |
| [[Thymus\|Thymus]] | Primary lymphoid organ where T cells develop, undergo selection, and are exported to the periphery; undergoes age-related involution. |
| [[TIMP1\|TIMP1]] | Tissue Inhibitor of Metalloproteinases-1, a soluble endogenous inhibitor of MMPs (especially MMP-9 and MMP-12) with roles in ECM homeostasis, fibrosis, and the SASP. |
| [[TIMP2\|TIMP2]] | Soluble tissue inhibitor of metalloproteinases that inhibits multiple MMPs and paradoxically facilitates pro-MMP-2 activation at the cell surface via MT1-MMP. |
| [[TIR domain\|TIR domain]] | The TIR (Toll/IL-1 Receptor) domain is the C-terminal catalytic core of SARM1, harboring the NAD+ glycohydrolase active site whose dimerization drives axonal NAD+ depletion and Wallerian degeneration. |
| [[Tissue Plasminogen Activator\|Tissue Plasminogen Activator]] | Serine protease that converts Plasminogen to Plasmin at fibrin surfaces, initiating clot lysis; the primary thrombolytic agent used clinically (alteplase, tenecteplase). |
| [[Titin\|Titin]] | The largest known protein, encoded by TTN, spanning the half-thick filament of the sarcomere as a chain of 244 folded domains; it acts as a molecular spring, providing passive elasticity and serving as a mechanosensor that transmits stretch to signalling pathways. |
| [[TLR3\|TLR3]] | Toll-like receptor 3, an endosomal pattern-recognition receptor that binds double-stranded RNA and triggers antiviral/inflammatory signaling exclusively through the TRIF adaptor. |
| [[TLR4\|TLR4]] | Toll-like receptor 4, a cell-surface innate immune receptor for LPS that signals through both MyD88 and TRIF adaptors to drive inflammation. |
| [[TLR6\|TLR6]] | Toll-like receptor 6, a cell-surface innate immune receptor that heterodimerizes with TLR2 to recognize diacylated lipopeptides and lipoteichoic acid via MyD88-dependent signaling. |
| [[TLR9\|TLR9]] | Endosomal Toll-like receptor that senses unmethylated CpG DNA and modulates cGAS expression in a context-dependent manner. |
| [[TMG\|TMG]] | Trimethylglycine (betaine), a methyl-donor osmolyte that supports methionine synthesis via betaine-homocysteine S-methyltransferase. |
| [[TNF Signaling\|TNF Signaling]] | The TNF/TNF receptor signaling pathway; a KEGG pathway significantly inhibited by apigenin in senescent cells alongside NF-κB. |
| [[TNF-alpha\|TNF-α]] | Pro-inflammatory cytokine involved in systemic inflammation and the acute phase response. |
| [[TNFR\|TNFR]] | Tumor necrosis factor receptor family mediating TNFR1 death-complex assembly toward necroptosis. |
| [[TNFα\|TNFα]] | Tumor necrosis factor alpha (TNFα) is a pleiotropic pro-inflammatory cytokine produced primarily by activated Macrophages, T Cells, and Natural Killer Cells. It is a master regulator of the inf... |
| [[Tocopherols\|Tocopherols]] | Tocopherols are the four chromanol-based forms of vitamin E (alpha, beta, gamma, delta) distinguished by methyl substitution of the chromanol ring; the principal lipid-phase chain-breaking antioxidants in membranes and lipoproteins. |
| [[Tocotrienols\|Tocotrienols]] | Tocotrienols are the four unsaturated forms of vitamin E (alpha, beta, gamma, delta) carrying an isoprenoid tail with three double bonds; chain-breaking antioxidants with distinct anti-inflammatory, anticancer, and hypocholesterolemic properties. |
| [[Tolcapone\|Tolcapone]] | A potent nitrocatechol-class COMT inhibitor that crosses the blood-brain barrier, used in Parkinson's disease. |
| [[Toll-like Receptor\|Toll-like Receptor]] | A class of proteins that play a key role in the innate immune system by recognizing structurally conserved molecules derived from microbes. |
| [[TLR2\|Toll-like Receptor 2]] | TLR2, an innate immunity sensor upregulated in senescent cells that activates an immunity-regulating SASP. |
| [[Toluidine Blue O\|Toluidine Blue O]] | Toluidine Blue O (TB) is a 3,7-dimethyl phenothiazine dye and the most potent methylene blue analog for neuroprotection, sharing MB's alternative mitochondrial electron-transfer mechanism. |
| [[TOM1\|TOM1]] | Target of Myb1, a VHS/GAT-domain endosomal trafficking adaptor that sorts ubiquitinated membrane receptors to lysosomes, negatively regulates TLR/cytokine signaling, and participates in autophagy; a SIRT5 desuccinylation target linked to autophagic protection in cardiac ischemia. |
| [[TOM20\|TOM20]] | Mitochondrial import receptor on the outer mitochondrial membrane that is targeted by α-synuclein in Parkinson's disease, blocking protein import and triggering mitophagy dysfunction. |
| [[TOP1\|TOP1]] | Topoisomerase 1, whose trapped cleavage complexes (TOP1cc) license cGAS recognition of cytoplasmic chromatin fragments to trigger SASP via cGAS–STING. |
| [[Topoisomerase\|Topoisomerase]] | Topoisomerases are enzymes that regulate DNA supercoiling by cleaving and religating DNA strands; fisetin is a topoisomerase inhibitor, contributing to its anti-cancer activity. |
| [[Topologically Associating Domain\|Topologically Associating Domain]] | Megabase-scale chromosomal domain within which DNA sequences contact each other more frequently than with outside sequences, organizing enhancer-promoter interactions that regulate SASP in senescence. |
| [[TRAF2\|TRAF2]] | TRAF2 (TNF receptor-associated factor 2) is a protein recruited by the MAVS complex to activate an innate immune response following viral infection. |
| [[TRAF3\|TRAF3]] | TNF receptor-associated factor 3, an E3 ubiquitin ligase adaptor that links TLR/TRIF and antiviral receptors to IRF3-mediated interferon induction. |
| [[TRAK1\|TRAK1]] | Mitochondrial transport adaptor protein that forms a complex with OPTN and KIF5B for anterograde axonal mitochondrial transport. |
| [[Transcription\|Transcription]] | Transcription is the first step of gene expression, in which a particular segment of DNA is copied into RNA by the enzyme RNA polymerase. |
| [[Transcriptomic Aging\|Transcriptomic Aging]] | Age-related shifts in gene-expression programs (transcriptome) reflecting dysregulated stress-response, inflammatory, and senescence pathways. |
| [[Transferrin\|Transferrin]] | Transferrin is the major iron-transport glycoprotein that delivers ferric iron to cells and thereby fuels the iron-dependent execution of ferroptosis. |
| [[Transferrin receptor 1\|Transferrin receptor 1]] | Overview: Transferrin receptor 1 is a membrane transport protein that facilitates the movement of molecules across biological membranes. It mediates substrate translocation through conformation... |
| [[TransitID\|TransitID]] | A proximity labeling method developed by Alice Ting's lab for mapping proteome spatial and temporal dynamics by distinguishing proteins transitioning between cellular compartments using orthogonal split-biotin ligases with time-resolved labeling. |
| [[Translocase of the Inner Mitochondrial Membrane\|Translocase of the Inner Mitochondrial Membrane]] | The TIM complex translocates proteins across the inner mitochondrial membrane during import, including PINK1. |
| [[Translocase of the Outer Mitochondrial Membrane\|Translocase of the Outer Mitochondrial Membrane]] | The TOM complex imports nuclear-encoded proteins into mitochondria across the outer membrane, including PINK1. |
| [[Transposable Elements\|Transposable Elements]] | Transposable elements ("jumping genes") are mobile DNA sequences whose age-related derepression drives genomic instability and inflammation. |
| [[Transverse aortic constriction\|Transverse aortic constriction]] | Transverse aortic constriction (TAC) is a surgical pressure-overload model in mice that constricts the transverse aorta between the brachiocephalic and left carotid arteries, producing cardiac hypertrophy that progresses to heart failure; a standard experimental model in cardiovascular and sirtuin research. |
| [[Traumatic Brain Injury\|Traumatic Brain Injury]] | Traumatic brain injury (TBI) is an acquired brain injury from external mechanical force causing transient or prolonged neurological deficits; mild TBI (concussion) is common in sport and military populations. Mitochondria-targeted agents are an active neuroprotection research focus. |
| [[TREM2\|TREM2]] | Triggering receptor expressed on myeloid cells 2, a microglial immunoglobulin-superfamily receptor that gates cGAS-STING/IFN-I signaling and senescence. |
| [[TREX1\|TREX1]] | Three prime repair exonuclease 1, a major cytosolic DNase that degrades cytosolic DNA to prevent cGAS-STING activation and maintain immune tolerance to self-DNA. |
| [[TRF2\|TRF2]] | TRF2 (Telomeric Repeat-binding Factor 2, encoded by TERF2) is a core subunit of the shelterin complex that binds double-stranded TTAGGG repeats and suppresses ATM activation and end-to-end telomere fusions. |
| [[TRIAD\|TRIAD]] | Test of Rapamycin In Aging Dogs — first rigorous pharmacologic geroscience trial with lifespan endpoints outside the lab, in any species. |
| [[Triapine\|Triapine]] | Triapine (3-AP) is an investigational α-(N)-heterocyclic thiosemicarbazone iron chelator that inhibits ribonucleotide reductase by stripping the iron/tyrosyl-radical cofactor of the RRM2 (and RRM2B) small subunits, collapsing de novo dNTP synthesis; studied across phase I–II oncology trials as a chemosensitizer and radiosensitizer, but not FDA-approved. |
| [[Trichostatin A\|Trichostatin A]] | Chemical properties: Trichostatin A is a chemical substance with defined molecular structure, functional groups, and physicochemical properties that determine its biological activity. |
| [[Tricyclic Antidepressant\|Tricyclic Antidepressant]] | Tricyclic antidepressants (TCAs) are an older antidepressant class with serotonergic and anticholinergic effects; dangerous with MAOIs. |
| [[TRIF\|TRIF]] | TIR-domain-containing adaptor protein inducing interferon-β (TRIF/TICAM1), the sole adaptor for TLR3 signaling and a key branch of TLR4 signaling that activates IRF3, NF-κB, and STAT1. |
| [[Triiodothyronine\|Triiodothyronine]] | The biologically active thyroid hormone, produced largely by peripheral deiodination of thyroxine (T4). T3 regulates basal metabolic rate via genomic and non-genomic actions across nearly all tissues. |
| [[Trimethylamine N-oxide\|Trimethylamine N-oxide]] | Gut microbiota-derived metabolite (TMAO) implicated in cardiovascular inflammation; its effects are attenuated by SIRT1 in vascular smooth muscle cells. |
| [[tRNA\|tRNA]] |  |
| [[Troponin\|Troponin]] | Contractile regulatory protein complex (TnC, TnI, TnT) of striated muscle; cardiac troponin I/T are specific biomarkers of myocardial injury. |
| [[TRPM7\|TRPM7]] | TRPM7 is a Mg2+/Ca2+-permeable cation channel fused to an alpha-kinase domain; it is implicated in oxidative-stress and necroptosis-associated membrane compromise, including MLKL-independent routes. |
| [[Tryptophan\|Tryptophan]] | Tryptophan (Trp) is an essential amino acid required for protein synthesis and the precursor for serotonin, melatonin, and NAD+ via the kynurenine pathway. |
| [[TSC\|TSC]] | TSC is the tuberous sclerosis complex, a heterotrimer of TSC1 (hamartin), TSC2 (tuberin) and TBC1D7 that functions as a GTPase-activating protein for Rheb and thereby restrains mTORC1. Loss-of-function variants cause tuberous sclerosis complex disease; mTOR inhibitors are an approved treatment. |
| [[Tuberculosis\|Tuberculosis]] | A chronic granulomatous infection caused by the bacterium Mycobacterium tuberculosis, characterised by caseating granulomas, latent infection in about a quarter of the world's population, and a drug regimen complicated by multi-month multidrug therapy and rising antimicrobial resistance. |
| [[Tubulin\|Tubulin]] | Tubulin is the α/β-heterodimer subunit of microtubules, the dynamic polymers of the cytoskeleton and mitotic spindle; it is the target of microtubule-interfering anticancer agents (benzimidazoles, taxanes, vinca alkaloids) and is deacetylated by the NAD+-dependent sirtuin SIRT2. |
| [[Tumor Antigen\|Tumor Antigen]] | A molecule (mutated, overexpressed, differentiation, or oncofetal) expressed by tumor cells and presented by MHC that is recognized by T cells and underlies immune surveillance and cancer immunotherapy. |
| [[Tumor-Associated Macrophage\|Tumor-Associated Macrophage]] | Tumor-associated macrophages (TAMs) are the most abundant immune cells in many tumors; their immunosuppressive M2 polarization is driven by intra-tumoral metabolic reprogramming, including a SENP1-SIRT3-cholesterol axis that suppresses antitumor CD8+ T-cell immunity. |
| [[TUNEL\|TUNEL]] | Terminal deoxynucleotidyl transferase dUTP nick-end labeling — the standard in situ assay marking DNA fragmentation in apoptosis (and, less specifically, other death programs). |
| [[Tunneling Nanotubes\|Tunneling Nanotubes]] | Actin-based intercellular conduits that transfer cGAS-STING products and signals between CNS cells. |
| [[TXNDC5\|TXNDC5]] | Thioredoxin domain-containing protein 5, a chaperone that synergizes with HSPA8 (HSC70) to drive NF-κB-mediated inflammatory signaling in rheumatoid arthritis fibroblasts. |
| [[TXNIP\|TXNIP]] | Thioredoxin-interacting protein (TXNIP/VDUP1/TBP-2); redox-sensitive negative regulator of thioredoxin and glucose/ROS sensor linking oxidative stress to NLRP3 inflammasome activation, IL-1β maturation, and metabolic dysfunction. |
| [[Type 2 Diabetes\|Type 2 Diabetes]] | Chronic metabolic disorder characterized by insulin resistance and progressive pancreatic beta-cell dysfunction, driving hyperglycemia and multiorgan complications. |
| [[Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus\|Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus]] | Chronic metabolic disorder characterized by insulin resistance, progressive pancreatic beta-cell dysfunction, and hyperglycemia |
| [[Type I Interferon\|Type I Interferon]] | Family of interferons (IFN-alpha/beta) produced downstream of innate immune sensors that drive antiviral and inflammatory transcriptional programs. |
| [[Type I vs Type II Cells\|Type I vs Type II Cells]] | The distinction between type I cells that execute death-receptor apoptosis directly via caspase-8 to caspase-3 and type II cells that require Bid-mediated mitochondrial amplification. |
| [[Tyrosinase\|Tyrosinase]] | Tyrosinase (hTyr) is the rate-limiting copper enzyme of melanin biosynthesis; its overexpression in rodent brain recapitulates age-dependent Neuromelanin accumulation and Parkinson's Disease-like pathology, implicating enzymatic melanogenesis in pigment-driven vulnerability. |
| [[Tyrosine\|Tyrosine]] | Tyrosine is a non-essential amino acid that is the precursor to Catecholamines, including Dopamine, Epinephrine, and Norepinephrine. |
| [[Tyrosine Hydroxylase\|Tyrosine Hydroxylase]] | Tyrosine Hydroxylase (TH) is the rate-limiting enzyme in the biosynthesis of Catecholamines, including Dopamine, Norepinephrine, and Epinephrine. |
| [[Tyrosine Kinase\|Tyrosine Kinase]] | Enzymes that transfer a phosphate group from ATP onto tyrosine residues of substrate proteins, functioning as binary on/off switches in signalling; the group is split into receptor tyrosine kinases and non-receptor cytosolic kinases and constitutes the largest single drug target class in oncology. |
| [[UBC9\|UBC9]] | UBC9 (UBE2I) is the single, obligate E2 conjugating enzyme of the SUMO pathway; it transfers activated SUMO from the SAE1-SAE2 E1 onto substrate lysines, directly recognising the SUMO consensus motif Psi-K-x-E/D and serving as the pathway's principal substrate-recognition module. |
| [[Ubiquinone\|Ubiquinone]] | Ubiquinone (coenzyme Q10, CoQ10) is the oxidized form of a lipophilic electron carrier; in ferroptosis defense it is the FSP1 substrate reduced to ubiquinol, a membrane radical-trapping antioxidant, and a non-sterol product of the mevalonate pathway. |
| [[Ubiquitin\|Ubiquitin]] | Ubiquitin is a conserved 76-amino-acid regulatory protein that tags substrates for proteasomal degradation or signaling; ubiquitinated inclusions mark Proteostasis failure in Neuromelanin-rich neurons of Parkinson's Disease. |
| [[Ubiquitin-like domain\|Ubiquitin-like domain]] | The N-terminal UBL domain of Parkin that is autoinhibitory, engages RING0, and is regulated by phosphorylation to release active Parkin. |
| [[Ubiquitin-Proteasome System\|Ubiquitin-Proteasome System]] | The ubiquitin-proteasome system (UPS) is the primary pathway for selective protein degradation; it regulates TFEB levels via E3 ligases like STUB1 which target phosphorylated TFEB. |
| [[UBN1\|UBN1]] | Ubinuclein 1, the metazoan-specific subunit of the HIRA histone chaperone complex required together with HIRA and ASF1a for SAHF formation in senescence. |
| [[UCHL1\|UCHL1]] | Deubiquitinating enzyme enriched in neurons that is aberrantly recruited to mitochondria in Alzheimer's disease, contributing to excessive mitophagy and synaptic loss. |
| [[UCP1\|UCP1]] | Uncoupling protein 1, the BAT-resident mitochondrial carrier that dissipates the proton gradient as heat, enabling thermogenesis and cold-induced mitohormesis. |
| [[UGA codon\|UGA codon]] | The UGA codon is one of three canonical translation stop signals; in selenoprotein mRNAs it is recoded to specify selenocysteine through the SECIS element, SECISBP2, and eEFSec, enabling insertion of the 21st amino acid. |
| [[UGT1A1\|UGT1A1]] | UGT1A1 is the uridine diphosphate glucuronosyltransferase 1A1 enzyme, a phase II conjugating enzyme in the ER that glucuronidates bilirubin and the active metabolite SN-38; reduced activity causes Gilbert syndrome and predicts irinotecan toxicity. |
| [[UK5099\|UK5099]] | Small-molecule inhibitor of the mitochondrial pyruvate carrier (MPC). Blocks mitochondrial pyruvate import to suppress the pyruvate–citrate–acetyl-CoA axis in senescence and to inhibit mitochondrial-metabolism-dependent cell death such as necroptosis; also has well-documented MPC-independent effects. |
| [[Um34\|Um34]] | Um34 (2′-O-methyluridine at position 34) is a tRNA[Ser]Sec modification installed by FTSJ1 that is required for efficient UGA recoding of selenocysteine and for melanoma metastasis. |
| [[UCP3\|Uncoupling Protein 3]] | A mitochondrial inner-membrane carrier expressed predominantly in skeletal muscle that may participate in fatty-acid handling, mild proton conductance, and reactive oxygen species control. Endurance training down-regulates UCP3 in muscle, linking it to exercise efficiency and resting metabolic rate. |
| [[Unfolded Protein Response\|Unfolded Protein Response]] | A cellular stress response related to the endoplasmic reticulum (ER) stress. |
| [[uPA\|uPA]] | Urokinase plasminogen activator, a serine protease of the SASP that converts plasminogen to plasmin and drives ECM remodeling and invasion. |
| [[uPAR\|uPAR]] | Urokinase plasminogen activator receptor (CD87), a GPI-anchored membrane protein that localizes uPA activity to the cell surface and promotes invasion |
| [[UPRam\|UPRam]] | Unfolded protein response activated by mistargeting of proteins. A stress pathway triggered when mitochondrial proteins are misdirected to other organelles or accumulate in the cytosol. |
| [[UPRmt\|UPRmt]] | The mitochondrial unfolded protein response is a retrograde stress pathway that restores mitochondrial proteostasis and mediates mitohormetic lifespan extension. |
| [[Uric Acid\|Uric Acid]] | Uric acid (UA, 2,6,8-trihydroxypurine) is the end-product of purine metabolism in humans, produced by xanthine oxidase-mediated oxidation of hypoxanthine and xanthine. It functions as both a major endogenous antioxidant in plasma and, at elevated concentrations, a pro-oxidant and crystallizing agent that activates the NLRP3 inflammasome. Hyperuricemia is an independent risk factor for gout, cardiovascular disease, and metabolic syndrome. |
| [[Uric Acid Crystals\|Uric Acid Crystals]] | Monosodium urate (MSU) and uric acid crystals are potent NLRP3 inflammasome activators that cause gouty inflammation through lysosomal rupture and IL-1β secretion. |
| [[Urolithin A\|Urolithin A]] | Urolithin A is a postbiotic metabolite derived from ellagitannins (found in pomegranates and nuts) that has been shown to induce mitophagy and improve muscle function and longevity in various m... |
| [[USP10\|USP10]] | USP10 (Ubiquitin Specific Peptidase 10) is a deubiquitinating enzyme (DUB) that stabilizes SIRT6 by binding its C-terminal region and removing ubiquitin chains, counteracting proteasomal degradation. |
| [[USP30\|USP30]] | Mitochondrial deubiquitinase that opposes Parkin-mediated mitophagy by removing ubiquitin chains from outer mitochondrial membrane proteins. |
| [[UTY\|UTY]] | UTY (Ubiquitously Transcribed TPR Gene on Y Chromosome) is the Y homolog of KDM6A that lacks H3K27 demethylase activity but retains non-catalytic tumor-suppressor function. |
| [[UVRAG\|UVRAG]] | A tumor-suppressor protein that positively regulates autophagy by stabilizing the Beclin 1–Vps34 complex and is also required for endocytic trafficking. |
| [[Valine\|Valine]] | Essential branched-chain amino acid that is fully glucogenic, entering the TCA cycle via succinyl-CoA. Valine restriction reduces body weight, improves metabolic health, reduces hepatic senescence, and extends lifespan of male mice; dysregulated valine catabolism contributes to insulin resistance. |
| [[Valsartan\|Valsartan]] | Valsartan, an angiotensin II receptor blocker used to treat hypertension and heart failure |
| [[VAMP8\|VAMP8]] | VAMP8 (endobrevin) is a broadly expressed v-SNARE that mediates homotypic endosome and lysosome fusion and, in exocrine and endocrine cells, drives granule-to-granule fusion during sequential compound exocytosis. |
| [[Varicella Zoster Virus\|Varicella Zoster Virus]] | Human alphaherpesvirus causing chickenpox (primary infection) and establishing lifelong latency that can reactivate as herpes zoster (shingles). |
| [[Vascular Calcification\|Vascular Calcification]] | Deposition of calcium phosphate minerals in the vascular wall, driving arterial stiffness and cardiovascular mortality, especially in chronic kidney disease |
| [[Vascular Endothelial Injury\|Vascular Endothelial Injury]] | Vascular endothelial injury is direct damage to the endothelium from mechanical trauma, ischemia-reperfusion, oxidative stress, toxins or inflammatory mediators, causing glycocalyx shedding, junction loss, permeability changes and pro-thrombotic/pro-inflammatory conversion of the vessel surface. |
| [[Vascular Inflammation\|Vascular Inflammation]] | Vascular inflammation is the coordinated recruitment and activation of circulating leukocytes into the vessel wall, driven by endothelial and mural-cell NF-kB signalling that induces VCAM-1, ICAM-1, MCP-1 and GM-CSF; it is the shared mechanism of atherosclerosis, aneurysm, heart failure and vasculitis. |
| [[Vascular Remodeling\|Vascular Remodeling]] | Vascular remodeling is the structural change in vessel wall geometry and composition occurring after injury or chronic disease - intimal hyperplasia, adventitial fibrosis, and constrictive or adaptive change in lumen diameter - driven by smooth muscle phenotypic switching, matrix turnover and hemodynamic feedback. |
| [[Vasoconstriction\|Vasoconstriction]] | Vasoconstriction is the narrowing of blood vessels via smooth-muscle contraction, raising vascular resistance and blood pressure; methylene blue induces it in vasoplegic shock. |
| [[Vasodilation\|Vasodilation]] | Vasodilation is the widening of blood vessels via smooth-muscle relaxation, lowering vascular resistance; excessive NO-driven vasodilation underlies vasoplegic shock. |
| [[Vasoplegic Shock\|Vasoplegic Shock]] | Vasoplegic shock (vasoplegia) is refractory hypotension from pathologic vasodilation, commonly after cardiopulmonary bypass or in septic/anaphylactic shock; methylene blue is an off-label rescue therapy. |
| [[Vasopressin\|Vasopressin]] | Posterior-pituitary peptide hormone regulating water homeostasis and vasoconstriction; implicated in brain aging, redox stress, and autophagy modulation. |
| [[Vasopressors\|Vasopressors]] | Vasopressors are agents that raise blood pressure by vasoconstriction or inotropy; first-line for shock before rescue agents like methylene blue. |
| [[VCP\|VCP]] | Valosin-containing protein that induces excessive mitophagy in Huntington's disease by binding to mutant huntingtin, leading to neuronal death. |
| [[VEGFR\|VEGFR]] | Vascular endothelial growth factor receptors (VEGFR-1/2/3) driving angiogenesis; TKI targets. |
| [[Venetoclax\|Venetoclax]] | Venetoclax (ABT-199) is an orally bioavailable, BCL-2-selective BH3 mimetic that occupies the BCL-2 hydrophobic groove and displaces BH3-only activators to trigger mitochondrial outer membrane permeabilization; it is first-line in CLL and standard-of-care partner in AML. |
| [[Ventral Tegmental Area\|Ventral Tegmental Area]] | The Ventral Tegmental Area (VTA) is a group of neurons located close to the midline on the floor of the midbrain. It is a key component of the brain's reward and motivation system. |
| [[Ventricular Remodeling\|Ventricular Remodeling]] | Etiology and pathophysiology: Ventricular Remodeling is a medical condition characterized by specific pathological changes in cellular, tissue, or organ function. Its development involves genet... |
| [[VER155008\|VER155008]] | A selective HSPA8 (HSC70) inhibitor that suppresses the SASP in senescent cells, phenocopying the senomorphic effect of apigenin. |
| [[Verrucomicrobia\|Verrucomicrobia]] | Verrucomicrobia is a phylum of bacteria that are ubiquitous in soil and water, and in the human gut they are represented primarily by the genus Akkermansia. |
| [[Very Low Density Lipoprotein\|Very Low Density Lipoprotein]] | Very Low Density Lipoprotein (VLDL) is an endogenous triglyceride-rich lipoprotein assembled in the liver; glycation and AGE modification promote its adhesion to vessel walls and its progression toward atherosclerotic plaque. |
| [[Vesicle Transport\|Vesicle Transport]] | Vesicle transport is the directed, coat-mediated movement of membrane-bound carriers between organelles and the cell surface — COPI/COPIl at the Golgi, COPII and clathrin at ER exit and endocytosis, with Rab GTPases, tethers and SNAREs specifying which membranes fuse. |
| [[vFLIP\|vFLIP]] | Viral FLICE-inhibitory proteins — DED-containing viral mimics of c-FLIP that block death-receptor apoptosis and rewire NF-kB signaling to keep infected cells alive. |
| [[VHL\|VHL]] | VHL (von Hippel-Lindau) is a tumor suppressor and the substrate receptor of the Cullin-2-RING E3 ubiquitin ligase. Its beta domain binds hydroxylated HIF alpha prolines, marking them for polyubiquitination and proteasomal degradation; loss of VHL constitutively activates HIF and underlies most clear cell renal cell carcinomas. |
| [[Vimentin\|Vimentin]] | Vimentin is a type III intermediate filament protein that provides structural support to the cytoskeleton and is involved in cell integrity, motility, autophagy, vesicle trafficking, and proteostasis. |
| [[Vinblastine\|Vinblastine]] | Vinka alkaloid microtubule-targeting agent that inhibits tubulin polymerization, used as chemotherapy. |
| [[Vincristine\|Vincristine]] | Vincristine is a Vinca alkaloid chemotherapeutic that inhibits microtubule polymerization, arresting mitosis; dose-limiting peripheral neuropathy is characteristic. |
| [[Vitamin A\|Vitamin A]] | Vitamin A is the fat-soluble retinoid family — retinol, retinal, retinoic acid, and retinyl esters — required for vision, epithelial integrity, and retinoic-acid-dependent gene transcription, and a direct radical-trapping anti-ferroptotic nutrient whose provitamin A precursors are the carotenoids beta- and alpha-carotene and beta-cryptoxanthin. |
| [[Vitamin B12\|Vitamin B12]] | Vitamin B12 (cobalamin) is a water-soluble vitamin essential for red blood cell formation, DNA Repair, and neurological function. It is a critical cofactor in the Methylation. |
| [[Vitamin B3\|Vitamin B3]] | Vitamin B3 (niacin) encompasses Nicotinic acid and Nicotinamide (niacinamide). It is the precursor to NAD+ and NADP+, making it essential for cellular energy metabolism, DNA Repair, and Sirtuin... |
| [[Vitamin B6\|Vitamin B6]] | Vitamin B6 refers to a group of six interconvertible compounds: pyridoxine, pyridoxal, Pyridoxamine, and their phosphorylated derivatives. The active form, pyridoxal 5''-phosphate (PLP), serves ... |
| [[Vitamin C\|Vitamin C]] | Vitamin C (ascorbic acid) is a water-soluble vitamin and essential antioxidant that regenerates vitamin E at membrane interfaces, recycles glutathione, and serves as a cofactor for collagen, carnitine, and catecholamine synthesis. |
| [[Vitamin D\|Vitamin D]] | Vitamin D is a group of fat-soluble secosteroids responsible for increasing intestinal absorption of calcium, magnesium, and phosphate. The two major forms are vitamin D₂ (ergocalciferol) and v... |
| [[Vitamin E\|Vitamin E]] | Vitamin E is a group of eight fat-soluble compounds that include four tocopherols and four tocotrienols. It acts as a potent antioxidant. |
| [[Vitamin K\|Vitamin K]] | Vitamin K is a naphthoquinone group (phylloquinone, menaquinone, menadione) whose reduced form (VKH2) acts as a ferroptosis-suppressing radical-trapping antioxidant via the FSP1-driven non-canonical vitamin K cycle. |
| [[Vitamin K Epoxide Reductase\|Vitamin K Epoxide Reductase]] | Vitamin K epoxide reductase is the VKORC1-encoded endoplasmic-reticulum enzyme system that recycles vitamin K epoxide back to the hydroquinone cofactor required by gamma-glutamyl carboxylase; inhibiting it blocks gamma-carboxylation of clotting factors and is the mechanism of warfarin anticoagulation. |
| [[VKORC1\|VKORC1]] | VKORC1 is the 163-residue endoplasmic-reticulum membrane oxidoreductase that recycles vitamin K epoxide back to vitamin K hydroquinone, sustaining gamma-carboxylation of clotting factors, and is the pharmacological target of warfarin. |
| [[VLA-4\|VLA-4]] | VLA-4 is the alpha4beta1 (CD49d/CD29) integrin that couples T-lymphocyte firm adhesion to VCAM-1 on inflamed endothelium and to fibronectin in tissue; blockade with anti-alpha4 antibodies is an established therapy in multiple sclerosis and Crohn disease. |
| [[VTA\|VTA]] | The ventral tegmental area (VTA) is a midbrain nucleus located adjacent to the Substantia Nigra. It contains Dopamine, GABA, and Glutamate neurons and is a core component of the brain's Reward ... |
| [[Wallerian Degeneration\|Wallerian Degeneration]] | Wallerian degeneration is the stereotyped program of distal axonal fragmentation that follows axonal severance from the soma, executed primarily by SARM1-driven catastrophic NAD+ depletion. |
| [[Werner Syndrome\|Werner Syndrome]] | Werner Syndrome (WS) is a rare, autosomal recessive progeroid syndrome characterized by the appearance of premature Aging. Unlike HGPS, which begins in early childhood, Werner Syndrome typicall... |
| [[WI-38\|WI-38]] | A diploid human fetal lung fibroblast cell line derived by Leonard Hayflick in 1962, widely used as a model for replicative senescence and aging research; the cell line in which the ACase-ferroptosis connection was discovered. |
| [[WIPI2\|WIPI2]] | WIPI2 is a seven-bladed beta-propeller phosphoinositide effector that binds PI(3)P via its FRRG motif and recruits the ATG12-ATG5-ATG16L1 complex to the omegasome, licensing LC3/GABARAP lipidation at the phagophore and creating a positive feedback loop with PI3KC3-C1. |
| [[Withaferin A\|Withaferin A]] | Bioactive withanolide from Withania somnifera (ashwagandha) with anti-inflammatory, anti-fibrotic, and anti-tumor activity; exerts hepatic anti-fibrotic effects through SIRT3. |
| [[Wnt signaling\|Wnt Signaling]] | Definition: Wnt Signaling is a fundamental biological process that maintains cellular and organismal homeostasis. It involves coordinated molecular events regulated by signaling pathways and en... |
| [[Wnt-beta-catenin\|Wnt-beta-catenin]] | Canonical Wnt pathway stabilizing beta-catenin for proliferation and stemness programs. |
| [[WNT16B\|WNT16B]] | A Wnt ligand and canonical SASP factor secreted by therapy-damaged stromal cells that promotes cancer therapy resistance; its expression is suppressed by apigenin. |
| [[Wolbachia\|Wolbachia]] | Wolbachia is a genus of maternally inherited endosymbiotic bacteria in the family Ehrlichiaceae (order Rickettsiales) that infects arthropods and filarial nematodes, altering host reproduction and, in filariae, providing essential metabolites as an obligate mutualist. |
| [[Wolman Disease\|Wolman Disease]] | Wolman disease is the infantile form of lysosomal acid lipase deficiency (LAL-D), an autosomal recessive LIPA mutation causing cholesteryl ester and triglyceride accumulation, with vomiting, failure to thrive, adrenal calcification, and death usually within the first year. |
| [[WR-1065\|WR-1065]] | WR-1065 is the free aminothiol (2-[(3-aminopropyl)amino]ethanethiol) formed when alkaline phosphatase hydrolyses amifostine; it is the cytoprotective species responsible for scavenging radiation-induced radicals and for delayed radioprotection via NF-kB-driven induction of manganese superoxide dismutase. |
| [[WTAP\|WTAP]] | Wilms tumor 1-associated protein (WTAP) is a nuclear regulatory subunit of the m6A mRNA methyltransferase complex that binds METTL3 and METTL14, localizes to nuclear speckles, and has m6A-independent roles in alternative splicing and sex determination. |
| [[XBP1\|XBP1]] | X-box binding protein 1 (XBP1), especially its spliced form XBP1s, is a key unfolded-protein-response transcription factor that also regulates hepatic autophagy by activating TFEB; SIRT6 regulates it to alleviate ER stress. |
| [[Xenohormesis\|Xenohormesis]] | Xenohormesis is a biological hypothesis proposing that animals have evolved to sense chemical cues from stressed plants to prepare for worsening environmental conditions. For example, plant molecul... |
| [[XRCC1\|XRCC1]] | XRCC1 is a catalytically inert nuclear scaffold protein that organises DNA single-strand break repair. It assembles PARP1, DNA polymerase beta, polynucleotide kinase, aprataxin and DNA ligase III into a functional repair complex at base excision and nucleotide excision lesions. |
| [[YME1L\|YME1L]] | Inner-membrane ATP-dependent metalloprotease that constitutively cleaves OPA1 to generate S-OPA1. |
| [[YY1\|YY1]] | YY1 is a ubiquitously expressed GLI-Kruppel zinc finger transcription factor that acts as both an activator and a repressor. Its four C-terminal C2H2 zinc fingers and acidic/glycine-rich N-terminal region make it a structural regulator of enhancer-promoter looping and a component of the INO80 chromatin remodelling complex. |
| [[Z-DNA\|Z-DNA]] | Left-handed, noncanonical double-helical DNA conformation recognized by cGAS and ZBP1 as a structural sensor of nucleic-acid stress. |
| [[ZBP1\|ZBP1]] | Cytosolic Z-nucleic-acid sensor that triggers PANoptosis and cell death, including in viral encephalitis via STING/MAVS. |
| [[Zellweger Spectrum Disorders\|Zellweger Spectrum Disorders]] | Peroxisome-biogenesis disorders from PEX gene loss, ranging from Zellweger syndrome to milder variants. |
| [[ZFP36L1\|ZFP36L1]] | Zinc finger protein 36 C3H1-like 1 (TTP-family RNA-binding protein) that degrades AU-rich-element mRNAs; inhibited by MK2 to stabilize SASP transcripts. |
| [[Zharp-99\|Zharp-99]] | Zharp-99 is a small-molecule RIPK3 inhibitor used preclinically to block necroptosis. |
| [[Zinc\|Zinc]] | Zinc (Zn, atomic number 30) is an essential trace element and the second most abundant trace metal in the body, serving as a catalytic or structural cofactor for over 300 enzymes and structurally anchoring zinc-finger DNA-binding domains, with both deficiency and excess being clinically consequential. |
| [[Zinc Finger\|Zinc Finger]] | A conserved protein structural motif in which zinc ions coordinate cysteine and histidine residues to form a compact DNA-binding domain; arrays of zinc fingers are used as programmable DNA-recognition modules in zinc finger nucleases and mitoZFNs. |
| [[Zone 2 Cardio\|Zone 2 Cardio]] | Low-to-moderate aerobic training sustained in the fat-oxidation zone; builds the mitochondrial base and supports recovery on non-hormetic days. |
| [[Zscan4\|Zscan4]] | A transcription factor implicated in feedforward potentiation of the SASP during the transition from acute stress signaling to chronic senescence-associated secretion. |
| [[α-Ketoglutarate\|α-Ketoglutarate]] | α-Ketoglutarate (α-KG) is a key TCA cycle intermediate derived from glutamine and glutamate; serves as a metabolic hub linking carbon metabolism to biosynthesis, epigenetic regulation, and signaling. |
| [[α-tubulin\|α-tubulin]] | Alpha subunit of tubulin heterodimers; principal cytoplasmic deacetylation substrate of SIRT2, linking the sirtuin to microtubule dynamics, mitosis, and the senescence marker phenotype. |
| [[β-Catenin\|β-Catenin]] | β-Catenin is a dual-function protein central to canonical Wnt signaling as a transcriptional coactivator and to cell adhesion as a component of adherens junctions. Its transcriptional activity is suppressed by SIRT1-mediated deacetylation. |
